Meanwhile, this was the first time for many years that Willoughby Hall had been occupied by any other than caretakers; and Fairhaven, to confess the truth, was a trifle ill-at-ease before the modish persons who now tenanted the old mansion; and consoled itself after an immemorial usage by backbiting.

And meanwhile I enjoyed myself tremendously. It was the first time I was ever thrown with people who were unanimously agreed that, after all, nothing is very serious. Mrs. Charteris, of course, was different; but she, like the others, found me divertingly naive and, in consequence, petted and cosseted me. I like petting; and since everyone seemed agreed to regard me as "the Child in the House"—that was Alicia Wade's nickname, and it clung—and to like having a child in the house, I began a little to heighten my very real boyishness. There was no harm in it; and if people were fonder of me because I sat upon the floor by preference, and drolly exaggerated what I really thought, it became a sort of public duty to do these things. So I did, and found it astonishingly pleasant.

===Chinese===
与此同时，这还是多年以来威洛比府头一回住进了看守人以外的什么人；说句实话，面对如今住进这座古老宅邸的一班时髦人物，费尔黑文人心里颇有几分局促；于是便依照亘古相沿的老例，靠背后说人坏话来聊以自慰。

与此同时，我自己则快活到了极点。这是我生平头一回被抛进这样一群人中间：他们异口同声地认定，归根结底，天下没有什么是了不得的大事。查特里斯太太（Mrs. Charteris）当然与此不同；但她也同旁人一样，觉得我天真得有趣，于是便宠我、惯我。我喜欢受宠；既然大家似乎一致把我看作"屋中孩童"（The Child in the House）——这是艾丽西娅·韦德（Alicia Wade）给我起的绰号，而且一叫就叫住了——又都喜欢家里有个孩子，我便开始着手稍稍渲染自己那千真万确的孩子气。这并无害处；倘若因为我偏爱坐在地板上、又滑稽地夸大自己的真实想法，人们便更加喜欢我，那么做这些事情便几乎成了一种公益义务。于是我照办不误，并且发现这样做快活得出奇。

### IV / IV

===Original===
And meanwhile too, John Charteris could never see enough of me, whom, as I today suspect, Charteris was studying conscientiously, to the end that I should be converted into "copy." For me, I was waiting cannily until he should actually ask to see those manuscripts I had brought to Willoughby Hall, and should help me to get them published. So there were two of us. … In any event, it was just three weeks after Stella's marriage that Charteris coaxed me into Fairhaven's Opera House to witness a performance of *Romeo and Juliet*, by the Imperial Dramatic Company.

I went under protest; I had witnessed the butchery of so many dramas within these walls during my college days, that I knew what I must anticipate, I said. I had, as a matter of fact, always enjoyed the Opera House "shows," but I did not wish to acknowledge the harboring of such crude tastes to Charteris. In any event, at the conclusion of the second act—

===Chinese===
与此同时，约翰·查特里斯（John Charteris）对我也是怎么看也看不够——如今我疑心，查特里斯当时是在一丝不苟地对我做研究，好把我加工成"写作素材"。至于我呢，我正狡黠地等着他哪一天当真开口，要看我带到威洛比府来的那几部手稿，等着他帮我设法刊行。所以说，我们俩是一对儿。……总之，就在斯特拉成婚之后刚刚三个星期，查特里斯把我哄进了费尔黑文的歌剧院，去看帝国剧团演出的《罗密欧与朱丽叶》（Romeo and Juliet）。

我是抗议着去的；我说，大学时代我在这四堵墙里目睹过太多戏剧惨遭屠戮，早知道自己该作何指望了。事实上，歌剧院的"玩意儿"我一向是喜欢的，只是不愿向查特里斯承认，自己竟藏着这等粗俗的趣味。总之，到了第二幕落幕的时候——

===Original===
"By Jove!" said I, in a voice that shook a little. "She's a stunner!" I jolted out, as I proceeded to applaud, vigorously, with both hands and feet. "And who would have thought it! Good Lord, who would have thought it!"

Charteris smiled, in that infernally patronizing way he had sometimes. "A beautiful woman, my dear boy—an inordinately beautiful woman, in fact, but entirely lacking in temperament."

===Chinese===
"老天在上！"我用微微发颤的嗓音喊道。"她真是个绝色！"我迸出这一句，一面手脚并用地起劲鼓掌跺脚。"谁想得到呢！我的老天，谁想得到呢！"

查特里斯微微一笑，用的是他有时会摆出的那副可恶的屈尊俯就的架势。"是个美人，我亲爱的孩子——事实上是美得过分的女人，可惜全无气质。"

===Original===
"Temperament!" I scoffed; "what's temperament to two eyes like those? Why, they're as big as golf-balls! And her voice—why, a violin—a very superior violin—if it could talk, would have just such a voice as that woman has! Temperament! Oh, you make me ill! Why, man, just look at her!" I said, conclusively.

Charteris looked, I presume. In any event, the Juliet of the evening stood before the curtain, smiling, bowing to right and left. The citizens of Fairhaven were applauding her with a certain conscientious industry, for they really found Romeo and Juliet a rather dull couple. The general opinion, however, was that Miss Montmorenci seemed an elegant actress, and in some interesting play, like *The Two Orphans* or *Lady Audley's Secret*, would be well worth seeing. Upon those who had witnessed her initial performance, she had made a most favorable impression in *The Lady of Lyons*; while at the Tuesday matinee, as Lady Isabel in *East Lynne*, she had wrung the souls of her hearers, and had brought forth every handkerchief in the house. Moreover, she was very good-looking—quite the lady, some said; and, after all, one cannot expect everything for twenty-five cents; considering which circumstances, Fairhaven applauded with temperate ardor, and made due allowance for Shakespeare as being a classic, and, therefore, of course, commendable, but not necessarily interesting.

===Chinese===
"气质！"我嗤之以鼻，"跟那样一双眼睛比起来，气质算得了什么？嚯，那双眼睛有高尔夫球那么大！还有她的嗓音——嚯，一把小提琴——一把上上品的小提琴——若是会说话，准就是那个女人这样的嗓音！气质！噢，你真叫我恶心！嗨，老兄，你就瞧瞧她吧！"我作了总结。

我想查特里斯是瞧了。总之，当晚的朱丽叶正立在幕前，微笑着，向左右两旁鞠躬。费尔黑文的公民们正带着某种恪尽职守的勤勉为她鼓掌，因为他们实在觉得，罗密欧与朱丽叶是一对相当乏味的情侣。不过，众议一致认为，蒙莫朗西小姐（Montmorenci）看去是位优雅的女演员，倘若换一出有意思的戏，比如《两个孤儿》（The Two Orphans）或者《奥德利夫人的秘密》（Lady Audley's Secret），必定很值得一看。在看过她头一场演出的人们心里，她已经凭《里昂的女郎》（The Lady of Lyons）留下了极好的印象；而在周二的日场上，她扮演《东林怨》（East Lynne）中的伊莎贝尔夫人，更是绞拧住了满场听众的魂灵，把人人的手帕都引了出来。何况她人又生得很美——有人说，十足一位大家闺秀；再说，两毛五分钱的票价，总不能指望样样俱全；考虑到这些情形，费尔黑文便以有节制的热情鼓了掌，并对莎士比亚作了应有的宽宥——他是个经典作家，因此固然可敬可佩，却未必引人入胜。

### V / V

===Original===
"Well?" I queried, when she had vanished. I was speaking under cover of the orchestra—a courtesy title accorded a very ancient and very feeble piano. "Well, and what do you think of her—of her looks, I means? Who cares for temperament in a woman!"

Charteris assumed a virtuous expression. "I don't dare tell you," said he; "you forget I am a married man."

Then I frowned a little. I often resented Charteris's flippant allusion to a wife whom I considered, with some reason, to be vastly too good for her husband. And I considered how near I had come to remaining with the others at Willoughby Hall—for that new game they called bridge-whist! And I decided I would never care for bridge. How on earth could presumably sensible people be content to coop themselves in a drawing-room on a warm May evening, when hardly a mile away was a woman with perfectly unfathomable eyes and a voice which was a love-song? Of course, she couldn't act, but, then, who wanted her to act? I indignantly demanded of my soul.

===Chinese===
"怎么样？"等她隐入幕后，我问道。我说话是借着"乐队"的掩护——那是对一架非常老迈、非常虚弱的钢琴所奉上的一个客气的头衔。"怎么样，你觉得她如何——我是说，她那模样儿？谁在乎女人有没有气质呢！"

查特里斯装出一副道貌岸然的表情。"我可不敢说，"他说，"你忘了，我是个成了家的人。"

于是我微微皱起了眉。查特里斯动辄轻佻地提起他的太太，常叫我着恼——依我看（这看法也不无道理），她实在比她丈夫好出十万八千里。我又想到，自己方才差一点儿就跟其余的人一起留在威洛比府了——为的就是他们叫做"桥牌惠斯特"的那门新牌戏！于是我断定，桥牌这种东西，我一辈子也不会喜欢。在温暖的五月之夜，那些想必通情达理的人们，怎么竟能甘心把自己关在客厅里，而此刻不到一英里之外，便有一位眼睛深不可测、嗓音本身就是一支情歌的女人？当然啦，她不会演戏，可是——谁又要她演戏呢？我愤愤然质问自己的灵魂。

===Original===
One simply wanted to look at her, and hear her speak. Charteris, with his prattle about temperament, was an ass; when a woman is born with such eyes and with a voice like that, she has done her full duty by the world, and has prodigally accomplished all one has the tiniest right to expect of her.

It was impossible she was in reality as beautiful as she seemed, because no woman was quite so beautiful as that; most of it was undoubtedly due to rouge and rice-powder and the footlights; but one could not be mistaken about the voice. And if her speech was that, what must her singing be! I thought; and in the outcome I remembered this reflection best of all.

===Chinese===
人只不过想看着她、听她说话罢了。查特里斯满口气质长气质短，实在是头蠢驴；一个女人，天生这样一双眼睛、这样一副嗓音，她便已对这个世界尽足了本分，人们哪怕抱着最微小的一点资格想向她要求的，她都已挥霍无度地超额办到了。

她本人绝不可能真像台上看来那么美，因为天底下没有一个女人美到那个地步；那份美多半无疑得归功于胭脂、米粉和脚光灯；可是那嗓音，是谁也错不了的。倘若她说话已是这般，她唱起歌来更当如何！我当时这样想；而后来事到临头，我记得最牢的，恰恰就是这一念。

===Original===
I consulted my programme. It informed me, in large type at the end, that Juliet was "old Capulet's daughter," and that the part was played by Miss Annabelle Alys Montmorenci.

And I sighed. I admitted to myself that from a woman who wilfully assumed such a name little could be hoped. Still, I would like to see her off the stage … without all those gaudy fripperies and gewgaws … merely from curiosity. … Then too, they said those actresses were pretty gay. …

===Chinese===
我看了看节目单。它在末尾用大号字体告知我：朱丽叶是"老凯普莱特之女"，该角色由安娜贝勒·艾利斯·蒙莫朗西小姐（Annabelle Alys Montmorenci）饰演。

于是我叹了口气。我暗自承认：一个存心给自己安上这种名字的女人，指望不得。不过，我还是愿意见见她下了台的模样……卸去那一身花里胡哨的绸衫和亮闪闪的零碎……纯粹出于好奇。……再说，人家都说，那种女演员是顶放荡的。……

### VI / VI

===Original===
"A most enjoyable performance," said Mr. Charteris, as we came out of the Opera House. "I have always had a sneaking liking for burlesque."

Thereupon he paused to shake hands with Mrs. Adrian Rabbet, wife to the rector of Fairhaven.

===Chinese===
"一场最赏心悦目的演出，"走出歌剧院时，查特里斯先生说道。"对滑稽戏，我一向怀有一种偷偷的喜爱。"

说着他停下脚步，同阿德里安·拉贝特太太（Mrs. Adrian Rabbet）握手，她是费尔黑文教区牧师的太太。

===Original===
"Such a sad play," she chirped, "and, do you know, I am afraid it is rather demoralizing in its effects on young people. No, of course, I didn't think of bringing the children, Mr. Charteris—Shakespeare's language is not always sufficiently obscure, you know, to make that safe. And besides, as I so often say to Mr. Rabbet, it is sad to think of our greatest dramatist having been a drinking man. It quite depressed me all through the play to think of him hobnobbing with Dr. Johnson at the Tabard Inn, and making such irregular marriages, and stealing sheep—or was it sheep, now?"

I said that, as I remembered, it was a fox, which he hid under his cloak until the beast bit him.

===Chinese===
"多惨的一出戏啊，"她啁啾似的说，"而且你知道吗，我怕它对年轻人的心术颇有几分败坏之虞。不，当然啦，我压根儿没想到要带孩子们来，查特里斯先生——你知道，莎士比亚的语言并不总是晦涩得足以叫人放心。再说了，正如我常常对拉贝特先生说的，一想到我们最伟大的剧作家竟是个贪杯之人，实在叫人痛心。整场戏看下来，一想到他同约翰逊博士在塔巴德客栈称兄道弟，缔结那样不合规矩的婚姻，还偷羊——呃，偷的到底是羊还是别的什么来着？——我就郁闷得不得了。"

我说，据我记忆所及，那是一只狐狸，他把它藏在大氅底下，直到那畜生咬了他一口。

===Original===
"Well, at any rate, it was something extremely deplorable and characteristic of genius, and I quite feel for his wife." Mrs. Rabbet sighed, and endeavored, I think, to recollect whether it was *Ingomar* or *Spartacus* that Shakespeare wrote. "However," she concluded, "they play *Ten Nights in a Barroom* on Thursday, and I shall certainly bring the children then, for I am always glad for them to see a really moral and instructive drama. That reminds me! I absolutely must tell you what Tom said about actors the other day—"

And she did. This led naturally to Matilda's recent and blasphemous comments on George Washington, and her observations as to the rector's dog, and little Adey's personal opinion of Elisha. And so on, in a manner not unfamiliar to fond parents. Mrs. Rabbet said toward the end that it was a most enjoyable chat, although to me it appeared to partake rather of the nature of a monologue. It consumed perhaps a half-hour; and when we two at last relinquished Mrs. Rabbet to her husband's charge, it was with a feeling not altogether unakin to relief.

===Chinese===
"呃，无论如何，那总归是件极其可叹、又极见天才本色的事，我很是同情他的太太。"拉贝特太太叹了口气，随后——我想——还在竭力回想，莎士比亚写的究竟是《英格马尔》（Ingomar）还是《斯巴达克斯》（Spartacus）。"不过，"她收尾道，"星期四他们演《酒馆十夜》（Ten Nights in a Barroom），到时候我一定带孩子们来，因为能看到一出真正有道德、有教益的戏，我总是高兴的。这么一提我倒想起来了！我非得告诉你不可，前几天汤姆说起演员来的那些话——"

她果真讲了。于是自然而然又引到了玛蒂尔达新近关于乔治·华盛顿的大不敬之论、她对牧师家那条狗的观察，还有小阿迪对以利沙（Elisha）的个人见解。如此等等，其套路为慈爱的父母们所司空见惯。临了，拉贝特太太说，这次谈话令人愉快之至，尽管依我看，它毋宁说更近乎一场独白。它耗去了约莫半个钟头；等到我们二人终于把拉贝特太太交还给她的丈夫照管时，心头怀着的那份感觉，已经同如释重负相去无几。

### VII / VII

===Original===
We walked slowly down Fairhaven's one real street, which extends due east from the College for as much as a mile, to end inconsequently in those carefully preserved foundations, which are now the only remnant of a building wherein a number of important matters were settled in Colonial days. There Cambridge Street divides like a Y, one branch of which leads to Willoughby Hall.

Our route from the Opera House thus led through the major part of Fairhaven, which, after an evening of unwonted dissipation, was now largely employed in discussing the play, and turning the cat out for the night. The houses were mostly dark, and the moon, nearing its full, silvered row after row of blank windows. There was an odour of growing things about, for in Fairhaven the gardens are many.

Then it befell that I made a sudden exclamation.

===Chinese===
我们缓步走下费尔黑文唯一一条名副其实的大街。这条街从学院一路正东延伸出去，足有一英里，到头来不了了之地终结在几处精心保存的房基前——那座建筑如今也只剩下这几块房基了，殖民时代曾有若干重大事项在那里面尘埃落定。剑桥街（Cambridge Street）在那里分作一个 Y 形，其中一条岔路通往威洛比府。

如此说来，我们从歌剧院回去的路，便穿过了费尔黑文的大半个城。度过了一个不寻常的纵情行乐的夜晚之后，全城此刻大都正忙着议论这出戏，忙着把猫放出去过夜。家家的屋子多半已熄了灯，将近满月的月亮，把一排又一排空茫的窗子镀上了银。四下里浮动着一股草木生长的气息，因为在费尔黑文，园子是很多的。

就在这时，事有凑巧，我突然失声喊了一下。

===Original===
"Eh?" said Charteris.

"Why, nothing," I explained, lucidly.

It may be mentioned, however, that we were, at this moment, passing a tall hedge of box, set about a large garden. The hedge was perhaps five feet six in height; Charteris was also five feet six, whereas I was an unusually tall young man, and topped my host by a good half-foot.

===Chinese===
"嗯？"查特里斯说。

"哦，没什么，"我明明白白地解释道。

不过不妨在此一提：此刻我们正走过一道高高的黄杨树篱，它环绕着一座大园子。那树篱约莫五英尺六英寸高；查特里斯的身高也是五英尺六英寸，而我却是个身量出奇之高的青年，比我的主人足足高出半英尺。

===Original===
"I say," I observed, after a little, "I'm all out of cigarettes. I'll go back to the drugstore," I suggested, as seized with a happy thought, "and get some. I noticed it was still open. Don't think of waiting for me," I urged, considerately.

"Why, great heavens!" Charteris ejaculated; "take one of mine. I can recommend them, I assure you—and, in any event, there are all sorts, I fancy, at the house. They keep only the rankest kind of domestic tobacco yonder."

"I prefer it," I insisted, "oh, yes, I really prefer it. So much milder and more wholesome, you know. I never smoke any other sort. My doctor insists on my smoking the very rankest tobacco I can get. It is much better for the heart, he says, because you don't smoke so much of it, you know. Besides," I concluded, virtuously, "it is infinitely cheaper; you can get twenty cigarettes all for five cents at some places. I really must economize, I think."

===Chinese===
"我说，"过了一小会儿，我发话道，"我的香烟全抽光了。我要回药店去一趟，"我提议道，仿佛灵机一动，"再买一些。我留意到它还开着门呢。千万别想着等我，"我体贴入微地敦促道。

"哎呀，我的老天爷！"查特里斯脱口喊道，"拿我的抽嘛。我可以向你担保，我这烟尽可推荐——再说，家里我想各样总有的是。那家店里只卖国产烟草里头最冲的那种劣等货。"

"我偏偏喜欢那种，"我执意道，"哦，是的，我真心喜欢。你知道，那种烟要温和得多，也有益健康得多。别的烟我从来不抽。我的医生坚持要我抽能弄到手的最冲的劣等烟。他说，那对心脏好得多，因为那样你就抽不了那么多，你懂的。再说了，"我一脸高洁地总结道，"那还便宜得没法比；有的地方五分钱就能买足二十支。我想，我实在该节俭一点儿了。"

===Original===
Charteris turned, and with great care stared in every direction. He discovered nothing unusual. "Very well!" assented Mr. Charteris; "I, too, have an eye for bargains. I will go with you."

"If you do alive," quoth I, quite honestly, "I devoutly desire that all sorts of unpleasant things may happen to me for not having wrung your neck first."

Charteris grinned. "Immoral young rip!" said he; "I warn you, before entering the ministry, Mr. Rabbet was accounted an excellent shot."

===Chinese===
查特里斯转过身，极其郑重地朝四面八方瞪眼望了一通。他没有发现任何异常。"很好！"查特里斯先生应道，"我也识得捡便宜的门道。我陪你同去。"

"你要是真去，"我万分老实地说，"那我就虔诚地祈愿：老天为罚我没有先拧断你的脖子，叫种种倒霉事都落到我头上来吧。"

查特里斯咧嘴一笑。"缺德的小坏蛋！"他说，"我警告你，拉贝特先生进教会当牧师之前，可是公认的一把神枪。"

===Original===
"Get out!" said I.

And the fervour of my utterance was such that Charteris proceeded to obey. "Don't be late for breakfast, if you can help it," he urged, kindly. "Of course, though, you are up to some new form of insanity, and I shall probably be sent for in the morning, to bail you out of the lockup."

Thereupon he turned on his heel, and went down the deserted street, singing sweetly.

===Chinese===
"滚你的！"我说。

而我这话说得又如此情辞恳切，查特里斯便当真遵命照办了。"早饭别迟到，能不迟就尽量不迟，"他和善地叮嘱道。"当然啦，你反正又在酝酿什么新花样的疯癫，明儿早上多半要派人去请我，把你从拘留所里保释出来。"

说罢他脚跟一转，沿着空无一人的街道走远了，一路婉转地唱着歌。

===Original===
Sang Mr. Charteris:

"Curly gold locks cover foolish brains,

Billing and cooing is all your cheer,

Sighing and singing of midnight strains

Under bonnybells' window-panes.

Wait till you've come to forty year!

===Chinese===
查特里斯先生唱道：

"金色的鬈发盖着愚蠢的脑袋，

唧唧哝哝谈情，便是你全部的快活，

只会在美人儿的窗棂之外，

一声声叹息，唱着午夜的情歌——

且等你活到四十岁再说！

===Original===
"Forty times over let Michaelmas pass,

Grizzling hair the brain doth clear;

Then you know a boy is an ass,

Then you know the worth of a lass,

Once you have come to forty-year."

===Chinese===
"且让米迦勒节过去四十遭，

灰白的头发会把头脑打扫；

那时你才知道小子是草包，

那时你才知道姑娘何等好，

一旦你自己也挨到了四十岁才知晓。"

## VI / 六

He Chats Over a Hedge / 他隔着树篱攀谈

### I / 一

===Original===
Left to myself, I began to retrace my steps. Solitude had mitigated my craving for tobacco in a surprising manner; indeed, a casual observer might have thought it completely forgotten, for I walked with curious leisure. When I had come again to the box-hedge my pace had degenerated, a little by a little, into an aimless lounge. Mr. Robert Etheridge Townsend was rapt with admiration of the perfect beauty of the night.

Followed a strange chance. There was only the mildest breeze about; it was barely audible among the leaves above; and yet—so unreliable are the breezes of still summer nights—with a sudden, tiny and almost imperceptible outburst, did this treacherous breeze lift Mr. Townsend’s brand-new straw hat from his head, and waft it over the hedge of trim box-bushes. This was unfortunate, for, as has been said, the hedge was a tall and sturdy hedge. So I peeped over it, with disconsolate countenance.

===Chinese===
剩下我独自一个，我便循着原路往回走。独处竟以惊人的效力缓释了我的烟瘾；说实在的，随便哪个漫不经心的路人都会当我把它忘了个一干二净，因为我走得闲散得出奇。待我又走回那道黄杨树篱跟前，我的步子已经一点一点地堕落成一种漫无目的的闲荡。罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森（Robert Etheridge Townsend）先生正心醉神迷，叹赏着这夜色十全十美的光华。

随后便发生了一桩离奇的巧事。四下里本只有一缕最轻最柔的微风，在头顶的枝叶间勉强可闻；然而——夏日静夜的风偏偏就是这样靠不住——这阵促狭的风忽然小小地、几乎无从察觉地发作起来，竟把汤森先生那顶崭新的草帽从头上掀起，一路飘送过了那道修剪齐整的黄杨树篱。这可大为不妙，因为——如前所述——那是一道又高又结实的树篱。于是我满脸怏怏，从篱顶上探头张望。

### II / 二

===Original===
“Beastly awkward,” said I, as meditatively; “I’d give a great deal to know how I’m going to get my hat back without breaking through the blessed hedge, and rousing the house, and being taken for a burglar, may be—”

“It is terrible,” assented a quite tranquil voice; “but if gentlemen *will* venture abroad on such terrible nights—”

“Eh?” said I. I looked up quickly at the moon; then back toward the possessor of the voice. It was peculiar I had not noticed her before, for she sat on a rustic bench not more than forty feet away, and in full view of the street. It was, perhaps, the strangeness of the affair that was accountable for the great wonder in my soul; and the little tremor which woke in my speech.

“—so windy,” she complained.

===Chinese===
「真是别扭透顶，」我作沉思状地说；「我情愿破费一大笔，只消知道我究竟怎样才能把帽子取回来——既不必撞破这该死的树篱，也不必惊动这一家子，更不至于被人当成个夜盗，没准儿真会当成夜盗——」

「是可怕呀，」一个十分安闲的声音附和道；「只是绅士们偏要挑这种可怕的夜晚出门游荡——」

「呃？」我说。我急忙抬头望了望月亮；又回过头去望那嗓音的主人。说来奇怪，我先前竟不曾留意到她，因为她分明坐在一张粗木长椅上，离我至多四十英尺，而且正对着街面，一望即知。也许，正是这桩事情本身的离奇，才害得我灵魂里生出那般大大的惊异，也害得我的话音里醒来一丝轻轻的颤动。

「——这风刮得也实在厉害，」她抱怨道。

===Original===
“Er—ah—yes, quite so!” I agreed, hastily.

“I am really afraid that it must be a tornado. Ah,” she continued, emotion catching at her voice, “heaven help all poor souls at sea! How the wind must whistle through the cordage! how the marlinspikes must quiver, and the good ship reel on such a night!” She looked up at a cloudless sky, and sighed.

“Er h’m!” I observed.

For she had come forward and had held out my hat toward me, and I could see her very plainly now; and my mouth was making foolish sounds, and my heart was performing certain curious and varied gymnastics which could not, by any stretch of the imagination, be included among its proper duties, and which interfered with my breathing.

===Chinese===
「呃——啊——对，正是正是！」我慌忙附和。

「我真怕这准是一场龙卷风。啊，」她接着说，激动得嗓音发哽，「上天保佑海上一切可怜的魂灵吧！这样的夜里，风准在船索间呜呜地尖啸！索针怕不都要簌簌打颤，好端端的船也要踉跄打晃了！」她抬头望着一碧无云的夜空，叹了口气。

「呃哼！」我评了一句。

原来她已走上前来，正把我的帽子朝我递来，此刻我能把她看得清清楚楚了；我的嘴里正发出种种傻气之声，而我的心呢，正在表演一套古怪而花样繁多的体操——这套动作任凭怎样穿凿附会，也归不进它的正当职责里去——而且还妨碍起我的呼吸来。

### III / 三

===Original===
“Didn’t I know it—didn’t I know it?” I demanded of my soul, and my pulses sang a paean; “I knew, with that voice, she couldn’t be a common actress—a vulgar, raddled creature out of a barn! You not a gentlewoman! Nonsense! Why—why, you’re positively incredible! Oh, you great, wonderful, lazy woman, you are probably very stupid, and you certainly can’t act, but your eyes are black velvet, and your voice is evidently stolen from a Cremona, and as for your hair, there must be pounds of it, and, altogether, you ought to be set up on a pedestal for men to worship! There is just one other woman in the whole wide world as beautiful as you are; and she is two thousand years old, and is securely locked up in the Louvre, and belongs to the French Government, and, besides, she hasn’t any arms, so that even there you have the advantage!”

Indeed, Miss Annabelle Alys Montmorenci was of much the same large, placid type as the Venus of Milo, nor were the upper portions of the two faces dissimilar. Miss Montmorenci’s lips, however, were far more curved, more buxom, and were, at the present moment, bordered by an absolutely bewildering assemblage of dimples which the statue may not boast.

===Chinese===
「我岂不是早就知道么——岂不是早就知道么？」我质问着自己的灵魂，周身的脉搏齐声唱起一曲凯歌；「我早知道，有那样的嗓音，她绝不可能是寻常的女戏子——绝不是谷仓里滚出来的那种粗俗的、脂残粉褪的货色！你不是大家闺秀？胡说！哎呀——哎呀，你简直叫人难以置信！哦，你这高大、奇妙、慵懒的女人，你多半蠢得很，你也笃定不会演戏，可你的眼睛是黑丝绒，你的嗓音分明是从一把克雷莫纳（Cremona）名琴上偷来的，至于你的头发，怕不有论磅称的分量——总而言之，你早该被供上基座，好教男人们顶礼膜拜！普天之下，统共只有另外一个女人同你一样美；而她已经是两千岁的人了，被牢牢锁在卢浮宫（Louvre）里，归法国政府所有；再说，她连两条胳膊都没有——这样看来，即便在这一点上，你也占着上风！」

说真的，安娜贝尔·艾莉丝·蒙特莫伦西小姐（Annabelle Alys Montmorenci），正是同米洛的维纳斯（Venus of Milo）一模一样的那种丰腴安详的类型，两张脸的上半部也无甚分别。只是蒙特莫伦西小姐的嘴唇，曲线远为弯浓，远为丰润，而且此刻唇边正环拥着一簇足以令人神魂颠倒的笑靥——这却是那座雕像所不能夸口的。

### IV / 四

===Original===
“I really think,” said Miss Montmorenci, judicially, “that it would be best for you to seek some shelter from this devastating wind. It really is not safe, you know, in the open. You might be swept away, just as your hat was.”

“The shelter of a tree—” I began, looking doubtfully into the garden, which had any number of trees.

“The very thing,” she assented. “There is a splendid oak yonder, just half a block up the street.” And she graciously pointed it out.

I regarded it with disapproval. “Such a rickety old tree,” I objected, sulkily.

===Chinese===
「我当真觉得，」蒙特莫伦西小姐俨然裁事一般地说，「你顶好还是找个地方躲避躲避这场摧枯拉朽的大风。要知道，露天里实在不安全。弄不好你会被卷走的，就跟你的帽子一个样。」

「找棵树遮一遮——」我开口道，一面迟疑地朝园子里张望，那园中多的是树。

「正是呢，」她赞同道。「那边就有一棵极好的橡树，沿街往上不过半条街的路程。」她还落落大方地指给我看。

我满不以为然地打量着它。「这么一棵摇摇欲坠的老树，」我赌气似的提出异议。

===Original===
Followed a silence. She bent her head to one side, and looked up at me. She was now grave with a difference. “A strolling actress isn’t supposed to be very particular, is she?” asked Miss Montmorenci. “She wouldn’t object to a man’s coming by night and trying to scrape acquaintance with her—a man who wouldn’t think of being seen with her by day? She would like it, probably. She—she’d probably be accustomed to it, wouldn’t she?” And Miss Montmorenci smiled.

And I, on a sudden, was abjectly ashamed of myself. “Why, you can’t think that of me!” I babbled. “I—oh, don’t think me that sort, I beg of you! I’m not—really, I’m not, Miss Montmorenci! But I admired you so much tonight—I—oh, of course, I was very silly and very presumptuous, but, really, you know—”

I paused for a little. This was miles apart from the glib talk I had designed.

“My name is Robert Townsend,” I then continued; “I am staying at Mr. Charteris’s place, just outside of Fairhaven. And I am delighted to meet you, Miss Montmorenci. So now, you see, we have been quite properly introduced, haven’t we? And, by the way,” I suggested, after a moment’s meditation, “there is a very interesting old college here—old pictures, records, historical association and suchlike. I would like to inspect it, vastly. Can’t I call for you in the morning. We can do it together, if you don’t mind, and if you haven’t already seen it. Won’t you, Miss Montmorenci? You really ought to see King’s College, you know; it is quite famous, because I was educated there, and no end of other interesting things have happened within its venerable confines.”

===Chinese===
接着是一阵沉默。她把头偏向一侧，抬眼望着我。此刻她的庄重已是另一种庄重。「照说，一个流动剧团的女演员，总不至于太挑剔吧，是不是？」蒙特莫伦西小姐问道。「一个男人夜里跑来，想方设法同她攀谈搭讪——一个白天决计不肯让人瞧见他同她走在一起的男人——她总不至于见怪吧？她多半还乐意呢。她——她多半早就习以为常了，对不对？」说罢，蒙特莫伦西小姐莞尔一笑。

而我，霎时间羞愧得无地自容。「哎呀，你怎么能这么看我！」我语无伦次地说。「我——哦，千万别把我当成那种人，求求你了！我不是——真的，我不是，蒙特莫伦西小姐！只是今晚我实在太过倾慕你了——我——哦，当然，我又蠢又放肆透了，可是，真的，你要知道——」

我稍稍顿了顿。这同我预先打好腹稿的那套伶牙俐齿，相差不啻十万八千里。

「我的名字叫罗伯特·汤森，」接着我继续说道；「我眼下正住在查特里斯先生府上，就在费尔黑文城外。蒙特莫伦西小姐，能结识你，我真是高兴极了。这么着，你瞧，咱们这也算得上经过体体面面的正式引见了，不是吗？对了，」我沉吟片刻，又提议道，「这儿有一所非常有意思的老学堂——古画啦，档案啦，历史掌故啦，诸如此类。我巴不得去瞻仰瞻仰呢，想得不得了。明儿上午我去接你，咱们一道去逛逛，行不行？只要你不嫌弃，又还没去看过的话。去不去嘛，蒙特莫伦西小姐？说真的，你理该去看看国王学院；它是大大有名呢——一来我本人就是在那儿受的教育，二来在那座古意盎然的院墙之内，还发生过数不清的趣事。」

===Original===
She had drawn close to the hedge. “You really mean it?” she asked. “You would walk through the streets of this Fairhaven with me—with a barnstormer, with a strolling actress? You’d be afraid!” she cried, suddenly; “oh, yes, you talk bravely enough, but you’d be afraid, of course, when the time came! You’d be afraid!”

I had taken the hat, but my head was still uncovered. “I don’t think,” said I, reflectively, “that I am afraid of many things, somehow. But of one thing I am certainly not afraid, and that is of mistaking a good woman for—for anything else. Their eyes are different somehow,” I haltingly explained, as to myself; then I smiled. “Shall we say eleven o’clock?”

Miss Montmorenci laid one hand upon the hedgetop and slowly twisted off four box-leaves what while I waited. “I—I believe you,” she said, in meditation; “oh, yes, I believe you, somehow, Mr. Townsend. But we rehearse in the morning, and there is a matinee every day, you know, and—and there are other reasons—” She paused, irresolutely. “No,” said Miss Montmorenci, “I thank you, but—good night.”

“Oh, I say! am I never to see any more of you?”

===Chinese===
她已挨近了树篱。「你是当真的？」她问道。「你肯陪我走过这费尔黑文的大街小巷——陪一个跑码头的戏子，陪一个流动剧团的女演员？你会害怕的！」她忽然叫道；「哦，是啊，你话说得够英勇，可真到了那个时辰，你自然会害怕的！你会害怕的！」

帽子我已经接了过来，头上却还空着。「我想，」我若有所思地说，「不知怎么，我害怕的东西并不多。可有一样我笃定不怕：那就是把一个好女人错认成——错认成别的任何什么。她们的眼睛总有些不一样，」我吞吞吐吐地解释着，像是自言自语；随后我笑了。「咱们就定在十一点钟，如何？」

蒙特莫伦西小姐把一只手搭上篱顶，在我等候的当口，慢条斯理地拧下了四片黄杨树叶。「我——我相信你，」她沉吟着说；「哦，是的，不知怎么，我信你，汤森先生。只是我们上午要排戏，每天午后还有一场日场，你是知道的，再说——再说还有别的缘故——」她踌躇不决地顿住了。「不，」蒙特莫伦西小姐说，「多谢你了，可是——晚安。」

「哦，喂！这么说，我就再也见不着你了？」

===Original===
A century or so of silence now. Her deliberation seemed endless.

At last: “Matinees and rehearsal keep us busy by day. But I am boarding here for the week, and—and I rest here in the garden after the evening performance. It is cool, it—it is like a glass of water after taking rather bitter medicine. And you aren’t a bad sort, are you? No; you look too big and strong and clean, Mr. Townsend. And, besides, you’re just a boy—”

“In that case,” cried Mr. Townsend, “I shall say goodnight with a light heart.” And I turned to go.

“A moment—” said she.

“An eternity,” I proffered.

===Chinese===
这回是约莫一个世纪的沉默。她的斟酌仿佛没有穷尽。

她终于开口：「日场和排戏把白天都占满了。可我这个礼拜就在这儿搭伙寄宿，而且——而且每晚散了戏，我总要到这园子里歇一歇。这儿凉爽，这——这就像服下一剂相当苦的药之后，再喝上一杯清水。你也不算个坏家伙，是不是？不坏；你瞧着太高大、太结实、太干净了，汤森先生。再说，你不过是个大孩子——」

「既然如此，」汤森先生喊道，「我便可以怀着轻松的心情道晚安了。」说罢我便转身要走。

「且慢——」她说。

「等上一个永世也行，」我慨然应道。

===Original===
“Promise me,” she said, “that you will not come again this week to the Opera House.”

My brows were raised a trifle. “I adore the drama,” I pleaded.

“And I loathe it. And I act very badly—hopelessly so,” said Miss Montmorenci, with an indolent shrug; “and, somehow, I don’t want you to see me do it. Why did you mind my calling you a boy? You *are*, you know.”

So I protested I had not minded it at all; and I promised. “But at least,” I said, triumphantly, “you can’t prevent my remembering Juliet!”

===Chinese===
「答应我，」她说，「这个礼拜，你可别再到歌剧院（Opera House）来了。」

我的眉梢微微扬起。「我酷爱戏剧，」我央告道。

「可我厌恶它。而且我演得糟透了——糟得无可救药，」蒙特莫伦西小姐懒懒地耸了耸肩；「而且，不知怎么，我不想让你看见我演成那样。我管你叫大孩子，你何必介意呢？你本来就是个孩子，你知道的。」

于是我分辩说自己毫不介意；并且应承了下来。「不过至少，」我得意扬扬地说，「你总拦不住我惦念朱丽叶（Juliet）吧！」

===Original===
She said of course not, only I was not to be silly.

“And therefore,” quoth I, “Juliet shall be remembered always.” I smiled and waved my hand. “Au revoir, Signorina Capulet,” said I.

And I took my departure. My blood rejoiced, with a strange fervor, in the summer moonlight. It was good to be alive.

===Chinese===
她说自然拦不住，只是不许我犯傻。

「因此之故，」我道，「朱丽叶必将被我永志不忘。」我微笑着挥了挥手。「再会了，卡普莱特小姐（Signorina Capulet），」我说。

于是我便动身离去。我的血液在这夏夜的月色里，怀着一种奇异的狂热欢欣鼓舞。活着，真好。

## VII / 七

He Goes Mad in a Garden / 他在园中发了疯

### I / 一

===Original===
"And, oh, but it is good to be with you again, Signorina!" cried I, as I came with quick strides into the moonlit garden. I caught both her hands in mine, and laughed like an ineffably contented person. There was nothing very subtle about the boy that then was I; at worst, he overacted what he really felt; and just at present he was pleased with the universe, and he saw no possible reason for concealing the fact.

It was characteristic, also, that she made no pretence at being surprised by my coming. She was expecting me and she smiled very frankly at seeing me. Also, in place of the street dress of Tuesday, she wore something that was white and soft and clinging, and left her throat but half concealed. This, for two reasons, was sensible and praiseworthy; one being that the night was warm, and the other that it really broadened my ideas as to the state of perfection which it is possible for the human throat to attain.

===Chinese===
「啊，又同你在一起，可真是好呀，小姐（Signorina）！」我一面迈着轻快的步子走进月色朗照的园子，一面喊道。我把她的双手一把握在我手里，像个快活得难以言喻的人似的笑起来。当年的我，那个小伙子，身上实在没有什么细致含蓄的地方；往坏处说，他不过是把真心实意演过了头；而眼下，他对整个宇宙心满意足，又实在看不出有什么理由要隐瞒这一点。

同样能见出她性子的是，她毫不装模作样，假装因我到来而吃惊。她本就是在等我，见了我便十分坦然地微笑。而且，她换下了星期二那身外出穿的衣裙，换了件又白又软、贴着身子的什么衣裳，喉颈只遮住了一半。这事出于两重缘故，可谓既明智又可嘉：一来夜气温暖；二来它着实开阔了我的眼界，教我知道，人的喉颈原来可能臻于何等完善的境地。

### II / 二

===Original===
"So you don't like my stage-name?" she asked, as I sat down beside her. "Well, for that matter, no more do I."

"It doesn't suit you," I protested—"not in the least. Whereas, you might be a Signorina Somebody-or-other, you know. You are dark and stately and—well, I can't tell you all the things you are," I complained, "because the English language is so abominably limited. But, upon the whole, I am willing to take the word of the playbill—yes, I am quite willing to accept you as Signorina Capulet. She had a habit of sitting in gardens at night, I remember. Yes," I decided, after reflection, "I really think it highly probable that you are old Capulet's daughter. I shall make a point of it to pick a quarrel as soon as possible, with that impertinent, trespassing young Montague. He really doesn't deserve you, you know."

Unaccountably, her face saddened. Then, "Signorina? Signorina?" she appraised the title. "It *is* rather a pretty name. And the other is horrible. Yes, you may call me Signorina, if you like."

===Chinese===
「这么说，你不喜欢我的艺名？」我在她身旁坐下时，她问道。「呃，说到这个，我自己也不喜欢呢。」

「这名字配不上你，」我抗议道——「半点儿也配不上。可话说回来，你分明当得起『意大利某家某户的一位小姐』，这你是知道的。你生得黝黑、端庄，还有——唉，你到底是何等样人，我简直没法一样样说给你听，」我抱怨道，「因为英语这东西贫乏得可恨。不过，总的说来，我愿意相信戏单上的话——是的，我十分乐意承认，你就是卡普莱特小姐（Signorina Capulet）。我记得，她就有个夜里坐在园子里的习惯。对了，」我思忖之后断言，「我确实认为，你十有八九真就是老凯普莱特的女儿。我这就打定主意，要尽早同那个放肆无礼、擅闯园子的蒙太古家小子寻衅吵上一架。说真的，他实在配不上你，你是知道的。」

不知怎的，她的脸黯淡了下来。随后：「小姐？小姐？」她掂量着这个名目。「这称呼倒真有几分好听。另外那个名字才可怕呢。好吧，你若喜欢，就管我叫小姐好了。」

### III / 三

===Original===
She would not tell me her real name. She was unmarried—this much she told me, but of her past life, her profession, or of her future she never spoke. "I don't want to talk about it," she said, candidly. "We play for a week in Fairhaven, and here, once off the stage, I intend to forget I am an actress. When I am on the stage," she added, in meditative wise, "of course everyone knows I am not."

===Chinese===
她不肯把她的真名告诉我。她尚未嫁人——这一层她是对我说了的；可她过去的生涯、她的行当，或是她的将来，她从来不提。「我不想谈这些，」她坦率地说。「我们在费尔黑文演一个礼拜的戏；在这儿，只要一下了台，我便打算忘掉自己是个女演员。至于在台上的时候嘛，」她又若有所思地补了一句，「人人自然都知道我并不是。」

===Original===
I laughed. I found her very satisfying; she was not particularly intelligent, perhaps, but then I was beginning to consider clever women rather objectionable creatures. There was a sufficiency of them among the Charteris house-party—Alicia Wade, for instance, and Pauline Ashmeade and Cynthia Chaytor—and I thought of them almost resentfully. The world had accorded them not exactly what they most wanted, perhaps, but, at least, they had its luxuries; and they said sharp, cynical things about the world in return. In a woman's mouth epigrams were as much out-of-place as a meerschaum pipe.

Here, on the contrary, was a woman whom the world had accorded nothing save hard knocks, and she regarded it, upon the whole, as an eminently pleasant place to live in. She accepted its rebuffs with a certain large calm, as being all in the day's work. There was, no doubt, some good and sufficient reason for these inconveniences; not for a moment, however, did she puzzle her handsome head in speculating over this reason. She was probably too lazy. And the few favours the world accorded her she took thankfully.

===Chinese===
我笑了。我觉得她着实令人惬意；她也许算不得特别聪慧，可我那时正开始觉得，聪明的女人是相当惹人厌的东西。查特里斯家的乡居宾客里，这一号人已经很够了——比如艾丽西娅·韦德（Alicia Wade），还有保利娜·阿什米德（Pauline Ashmeade）、辛西娅·查托（Cynthia Chaytor）——我想起她们来，几乎带着怨气。世界给她们的，或许并不恰恰是她们最想要的东西，但至少，世间的种种奢华她们样样有份；作为回报，她们便对这个世界说些尖酸刻薄的俏皮话。警句出在女人口中，就同海泡石烟斗衔在女人嘴里一样不相宜。

而眼前这一个，恰恰相反：世界给过她的，除了连连的碰磕，什么也没有；可是在她看来，这世界总的说来仍是个顶顶宜人的居所。她以一种雍容的镇定承受世间的冷遇，只当那是分内的日常营生。这些不如意，无疑自有其充分而正当的缘由；只是片刻也不见她为推究这缘由去劳烦她那颗漂亮的脑袋。她多半是太懒了。而世界偶尔施与她的那一点儿恩惠，她都心怀感激地领受。

===Original===
"You see," she explained to me—this was on Thursday night, when I found her contentedly eating cheap candy out of a paper bag—"the world is really very like a large chocolate drop; it's rather bitter on the outside, but when you have bitten through, you find the heart of it sweet. Oh, how greedy!—you've taken the last candied cherry, and I am specially fond of candied cherries!" And indeed, she looked frankly regretful as I munched it.

I thought her adorable; and in exchange for that last candied cherry I promised her some of the new books—*David Harum* certainly, and, *When Knighthood Was in Flower*, because everybody was reading it, and *Mr. Dooley*, because they said this young fellow Dunne was nearly as funny as Bill Nye. …

===Chinese===
「你瞧，」她向我解释道——这是星期四晚上，我撞见她正心满意足地捧着一只纸袋吃廉价糖果——「这个世界呀，实在很像一颗大颗的巧克力糖：外皮相当苦，可你一口咬穿，便发现里头的芯子是甜的。哎呀，好馋！——你把最后一颗蜜饯樱桃拿了去，我偏偏顶喜欢蜜饯樱桃！」而当我嚼着那颗樱桃时，她脸上当真明明白白现出惋惜之色。

我觉得她可爱极了；作为对那最后一颗蜜饯樱桃的报偿，我答应送她几本新书——必定有《大卫·哈勒姆》（David Harum），还有《骑士之花盛开时》（When Knighthood Was in Flower），因为人人都在读；再有《杜利先生》（Mr. Dooley），因为人们说，邓恩（Dunne）这个年轻家伙，差不多有比尔·奈（Bill Nye）那么逗乐。……

### IV / 四

===Original===
In fact, the moon seemed to shine down each night upon that particular garden in a more and more delightful and dangerous manner. And I being a fairly normal and healthy young man, the said moonshine affected me in a fashion which has been peculiar to moonshine since Noah was a likely stripling; my blood appeared to me, at times, to leap and bubble in my veins as if it had been some notably invigorating and heady tipple; and my heart was unreasonably contented, and I gave due thanks for this woman who had come to me unsullied through the world's gutter. For she came unsullied; there was no questioning that.

I pictured her in certain execrable rhymes as the Lady in *Comus*, moving serene and unafraid among a rabble of threatening, bestial shapes. And I rejoiced that there were women like this in the world—brave, wholesome, unutterably honest women, whose very lack of cleverness—oh, subtle appeal to my vanity!—demanded a gentleman's protection.

===Chinese===
事实上，月亮仿佛一夜比一夜更迷人、也更凶险地朗照着那一座园子。而我呢，是个相当正常、相当健康的年轻人，那月光便以这样一种方式作用于我——这方式，打从挪亚还是个精壮少年的时候起，就一直是月光独有的专利。我的血液，在我自己看来，时而仿佛在血管里腾跃起沫，宛如某种格外滋补、格外上头的佳酿；我的心没来由地满足，我还为这个女人郑重其事地谢了恩——她从世间的阴沟里一路穿行而来，到我身边时依旧纤尘不染。因为她来时确乎纤尘不染；这一点无可置疑。

我曾在几首糟糕透顶的诗行里，把她写成《科玛斯》（Comus）里的那位女郎，在一群面目狰狞、兽形魍魉的包围之中，安详前行，无所畏惧。我又庆幸世上竟有这样的女人——勇敢、健全、诚实得无以复加的女人；她们那恰恰缺失的聪明——啊，这对我的虚荣心是何等精妙的奉承！——正要求着一位绅士的保护。

===Original===
As has been said, I was a well-grown lad, but when I thought in this fashion I seemed to myself, at a moderate computation, ten feet in height—and just the person, in short, who would be an ideal protector.

Thus far my callow meditations. My course of reasoning was perhaps faulty, but then there are, at twenty-one, many processes more interesting and desirable than the perfecting of a mathematical demonstration. And so, for a little, my blood rejoiced with a strange fervour in the summer moonlight, and it was good to be alive.

===Chinese===
如前所说，我是个身量挺拔的少年；可每当这样想着，我便觉得自己——往保守里估算——足有十英尺高，总而言之，正是做一名理想保护人的不二人选。

我那羽翼未丰的玄思，至此为止。我这一套推演或许站不住脚，可是人在二十一岁上，比起修完一道数学证明来，还有许许多多更有趣、更可心的过程可走呢。于是有一阵子，我的血液便怀着一种奇异的狂热，在夏夜的月色里欢欣鼓舞，而活着，真好。

### V / 五

===Original===
Thursday was the twenty-third of the month, so upon that afternoon I wrote to Bettie Hamlyn, in far-off Colorado.

It was a lengthy letter. It told her of how desolate her garden was and of how odd Fairhaven seemed without her. It told how I had half changed my mind, and would probably not go to Europe with Mr. Charteris, after all. Bettie had been at pains, in the letter I was answering, to expatiate upon her hatred of Charteris, whom she had never seen. My letter told her, in fine, of a variety of matters. And it ended:

===Chinese===
星期四是当月的二十三号，于是那天下午，我给远在科罗拉多的贝蒂·哈姆林写了封信。

那是一封长信。信里告诉她，她的园子何等荒凉，缺了她的费尔黑文看上去又何等古怪。信里还说，我的心事已变了一半，多半终究不会随查特里斯先生去欧洲了。在我正作复的那封信里，贝蒂曾不惮辞费地铺陈她对查特里斯的憎恶——尽管她连他的面也不曾见过。总而言之，我这封信对她讲了形形色色的事情。信的结尾是：

===Original===
I went to the Opera House on Monday. But that, like everything else, isn't the same without you, dear. The woman who played Juliet was, I believe, rather good-looking, but I scarcely noticed her in worrying over the pitiful circumstance that the Apothecary and the Populace of Verona had only one pair of shoes between them. Besides, Mercutio kept putting on a bathrobe and insisting he was Friar Laurence. … I would write more about it, if I had not almost used up all my paper. There is just room to say—

===Chinese===
星期一我去歌剧院看了戏。可是亲爱的，那地方同其余的一切一样，没有你便不是原先那个味儿了。演朱丽叶的那个女人，我相信，生得还算标致，可我几乎没顾上瞧她，只顾着为那桩可怜的境况发愁：维罗纳的卖药人和维罗纳的众百姓，两个合起来才共有一双鞋。再说，茂丘西奥还总是往身上披一件浴袍，一口咬定自己就是劳伦斯神父。……我本想再多写些，只可惜信纸差不多用尽了。剩下的空白刚好够说一句——

### VI / 六

===Original===
This was, as I have stated, on Thursday afternoon. Upon the following evening—

"And why not?" I demanded, for the ninth time.

===Chinese===
如我方才所说，这是星期四下午的事。到了第二天晚上——

「为什么不可以呢？」我质问道，这已是第九回了。

===Original===
But she was resolute. "Oh, it is dear of you!" she cried; "and I—I do care for you—how could I help it? But it can't be—it can't ever be," she repeated wearily; and then she looked at me, and smiled a little. "Oh, boy, boy! dear, dear boy!" she murmured, half in wonder, "how foolish of you and—how dear of you!"

"And why not?" said I—for the tenth time.

===Chinese===
可她心意已决。「哦，你真好！」她喊道；「而且我——我确实喜欢你——教我怎能不喜欢呢？可是不成——永远也不能成，」她倦倦地重复道；随后她望着我，微微一笑。「哦，孩子，孩子！亲爱的、亲爱的孩子！」她半带惊叹地喃喃道，「你多么傻，又——多么贴心！」

「为什么不可以呢？」我说——这已是第十回了。

===Original===
She gave a sobbing laugh. "Oh, the great, brave, stupid boy!" she said, and, for a moment, her hand rested on my hair; "he doesn't know what he is doing—ah, no, he doesn't know! Why, I might hold you to your word! I might sue you for breach of promise! I might marry you out of hand! Think of that! Why I am only a strolling actress, and fair game for any man—any man who isn't particular," she added, with the first trace of bitterness I had ever observed in her odd, throaty voice. "And you would marry me—you! you would give me your name, you would make me your wife! You have actually begged me to be your wife, haven't you? Ah, my brave, strong, stupid Bobbie, how many women must love you—women who have a right to love you! And you would give them all up for me—for me, you foolish Bobbie, whom you haven't known a week! Ah, how dear of you!" And she caught her breath swiftly, and her voice broke.

"Yes," I brazenly confessed; "I really believe I would give them all up—every blessed one of them—for you." I inspected her, critically, and then smiled. "And I don't think that I would be deserving any very great credit for self sacrifice, either, Signorina."

===Chinese===
她发出一声噙着哽咽的笑。「哦，这个高大、勇敢、愚蠢的孩子！」她说，有一瞬，她的手轻轻停在我的头发上；「他不知道自己在做什么——啊，不，他不知道！嗐，我说不定会逼你兑现你说过的话呢！我说不定会到法庭告你负心毁约呢！我说不定会索性当场嫁给你呢！想想看吧！我不过是个跑码头的女演员，什么男人都可以拿我当一件名正言顺的猎物——只要那男人不挑不拣，」她补了一句，那是我头一回在她那副奇异的、喉音沉沉的嗓音里听出些许苦涩。「而你竟要娶我——你！你竟要把你的姓氏给我，要我当你的妻子！你当真恳求过我做你的妻子，是不是？啊，我勇敢、健壮、愚蠢的博比（Bobbie），该有多少女人爱着你——那些有资格爱你的女人！而你竟肯为了我把她们统统舍弃——为了我，你这个傻博比，你认识我总共还不满一个礼拜呢！啊，你真好！」说到这里，她猛地抽了一口气，嗓音随之破裂了。

「是的，」我厚着脸皮承认；「我确实相信，为了你，我会把她们统统舍弃——一个不落，一个不留。」我挑剔地把她端详了一番，随后笑了。「而且我并不认为，这算得上什么了不得的自我牺牲，小姐。」

===Original===
"My dear," she answered, "it pleases you to call me old Capulet's daughter—but if I were only a Capulet, and you a Montague, don't you see how much easier it would be? But we don't belong to rival families, we belong to rival worlds, to two worlds that have nothing in common, and never can have anything in common. They are too strong for us, Bobbie—my big, dark, squalid world, that you could never sink to, and your gay little world which I can never climb to—your world that would have none of me, even if—even *if*—" But the condition was not forthcoming.

"The world," said I, in an equable tone—"My dear, I may as well warn you I am shockingly given to short and expressive terms, and as we are likely to see a deal of each other for the future, you will have to be lenient with them—accordingly, I repeat, the world may be damned."

===Chinese===
「亲爱的，」她答道，「你乐意见我说自己是老凯普莱特的女儿——可我若真是个凯普莱特，你若真是个蒙太古，你难道看不出，事情会好办多少么？可我们并不属于两个敌对的家族，我们属于两个敌对的世界，属于两个毫无共同之处、也永不会有共同之处的世界。同它们相比，我们太弱小了，博比——我这个广大、黑暗、卑污的世界，你永远沉沦不进去；你那个欢快的小世界，我也永远攀不上去——你的世界容不下我，哪怕——哪怕——」可那后半句终究没有说出来。

「至于那个世界，」我用平心静气的口吻说——「亲爱的，我不妨预先警告你，我这人糟糕透顶地爱用些简短有力的话儿，既然往后咱们少不得要多多彼此厮守，你只得对它们宽容些——因此之故，我再说一遍，世界就让它见鬼去吧。」

===Original===
And I laughed, in unutterable content. "Have none of you!" I cried. "My faith, I would like to see a world which would have none of you! Ah, Signorina, it is very plain to me that you don't realize what a beauty, what a—a—good Lord, what an unimaginative person it was that invented the English language! Why, you have only to be seen, heart's dearest—only to be seen, and the world is at your feet—my world, to which you belong of rights; my world, that you are going to honour by living in; my world, that in a little will go mad for sheer envy of blundering, stupid, lucky me!" And I laughed her to scorn.

There was a long silence. Then, "I belonged to your world once, you know."

"Why, of course, I knew as much as that."

"And yet—you never asked—"

===Chinese===
我怀着难以言喻的满足，放声笑了。「他们谁都不要你！」我喊道。「我的老天，我倒真想见识见识一个容不下你的世界！啊，小姐，我再明白不过：你根本不知道自己是个怎样的美人，怎样的——怎样的——老天爷，当初发明英语的那个人，想象力可真是贫乏得可以！嗐，你只消让人瞧上一眼，心肝儿——只消让人瞧上一眼，世界便拜倒在你脚下了——我的世界，你本来就理应属于它；我的世界，你即将住进去、为它增光；我的世界，过不了多久，就要为了我这个笨手笨脚、蠢头蠢脑、偏又鸿运当头的家伙，妒忌得发狂！」随后我笑着，把她嘲弄了一番。

接着是长长的沉默。随后：「我从前也是属于过你的世界的，你知道。」

「嗐，那还用说，这个我自然是知道的。」

「可是——你从来不问——」

===Original===
"Ah, Signorina, Signorina!" I cried; "what matter? Don't I know you for the bravest, tenderest, purest, most beautiful woman God ever made? I doubt you—I! My word!" said I, and stoutly, "that *would* be a pretty go! You are to tell me just what you please," I went on, almost belligerently, "and when and where you please, my lady. And I would thank you," I added, with appropriate sternness, "to discontinue your pitiful and transparent efforts to arouse unworthy suspicions as to my future wife. They are wasted, madam—utterly wasted, I assure you."

===Chinese===
「啊，小姐，小姐！」我喊道；「那又有什么要紧？难道我不知道你么——上帝造下的女人里，数你最勇敢、最温柔、最纯洁、最美丽？我怀疑你——我！哎呀！」我说，而且说得斩钉截铁，「那可真要闹笑话了！你爱告诉我什么，就告诉我什么，」我几乎剑拔弩张地继续道，「爱什么时候讲、爱在哪儿讲，一律悉听尊便，我的小姐。还有，在下要谢过您了，」我以恰如其分的严峻补上一句，「请莫再枉费那些又可怜、又一望即穿的气力，妄图唤起对我未来妻子的种种不该有的疑心。那是白费劲，夫人——彻头彻尾的白费劲，我向您保证。」

===Original===
"Oh, Bobbie, Bobbie!" she sighed; "you are such a beautiful baby! Give me time," she pleaded weakly.

And, when I scowled my disapproval, "Only till tomorrow—only a little, little twenty-four hours. And promise me, you won't speak of this—this crazy nonsense again tonight. I must think."

===Chinese===
「哦，博比，博比！」她叹道；「你真是个漂亮的大娃娃！给我一点儿时间吧，」她软弱无力地恳求道。

见我皱起眉头、面有难色，她又道：「只到明天——只消小小的二十四个钟头。你答应我，今儿晚上再别提起这件——这件疯话了。我得想一想。」

===Original===
"Never!" said I, promptly; "because I couldn't be expected to keep such an absurd promise," I complained, in indignation.

"And you look so strong," she murmured, with evident disappointment—"so strong and firm and—and—admirable!"

===Chinese===
「万万不答应！」我应声答道；「谁也别指望我守住这么个荒唐的诺言，」我愤愤然抱怨道。

「可你瞧着多结实呀，」她喃喃道，明显地大失所望——「这么结实，这么坚定，还——还——令人钦佩！」

===Original===
So I promised at once. And I kept the promise—that is, I did subsequently refer to the preferable and proper course to pursue in divers given circumstances "when we are married"; but it was on six occasions only, and then quite casually—and six times, as I myself observed, was, all things considered, an extremely moderate allowance and one that did great credit to my self-control.

===Chinese===
于是我当即答应了。而且我信守了诺言——也就是说，后来在形形色色的场合，我确实又提起过几回「等咱们结了婚」在种种情形之下该怎样行事才更妥善、更得体；但统共只有六回，而且每回都纯属顺口一提——而我自家也看得分明，通盘算来，六回这个限额已是克制到了极点，足以为我的自制力大大地增光。

### VII / 七

===Original===
"And besides, why *not*?" I said—for the eleventh time.

"There are a thousand reasons. I am not your equal, I am just an ostensible actress—Why, it would be your ruin!"

===Chinese===
「再说，究竟为什么不呢？」我说——这已是第十一回了。

「理由有一千条。我配不上你，我不过是个名义上的女演员——嗐，那会毁了你的！」

===Original===
"My dear Mrs. Grundy, I confess that, for the moment, your disguise had deceived me. But now I recognize your voice."

===Chinese===
「亲爱的格伦迪太太（Mrs. Grundy），我承认，方才一时之间，您这副伪装真把我骗过去了。可如今我听出您的嗓音了。」

===Original===
She laughed a little. "And after all," the grave voice said, which was, to me at least, the masterwork of God, "after all, hasn't one always to answer Mrs. Grundy—in the end?"

"Why, then, you disgusting old harridan," said I, "I grant you it is utterly impossible to defend my behaviour in this matter, and, believe me, I don't for an instant undertake the task. To the contrary, I agree with you perfectly—my conduct is most thoughtless and reprehensible, and merits your very severest condemnation. For look you, here is a young man, well born, well-bred, sufficiently well endowed with this world's goods, in short, an eminently eligible match, preparing to marry an 'ostensible actress' a year or two his senior—why, of course you are—and of whose past he knows nothing—absolutely nothing. Don't you shudder at the effrontery of the minx? Is it not heartbreaking to contemplate the folly, the utter infatuation of the misguided youth who now stands ready to foist such a creature upon the circles of which your ladyship is a distinguished ornament? I protest it is really incredible. I don't believe a word of it."

===Chinese===
她轻轻笑了。「说到底，」那副庄重的嗓音说道——这嗓音，至少在我听来，是上帝的登峰造极之作——「说到底，人到头来，难道不总得向格伦迪太太交账么——终究总得交账？」

「既然这样，那么，您这个叫人作呕的老妖婆，」我说，「我向您认了：要为我在此事里的行为辩护，是万万办不到的；而且请您相信，我片刻也不曾打算承揽这桩差事。恰恰相反，我全然同意您的高见——我的行止极其轻率、极其应受斥责，理当领受您最最严厉的谴责。您瞧啊：这里有一个青年，出身好，教养好，尘世间的财产也足够殷实，总而言之，是门好得无以复加的亲事，如今却打算娶一个比他年长一两岁的『名义上的女演员』——嗐，您自然是的——而且对这个女人的过去一无所知——彻头彻尾地一无所知。您不为这小贱人的厚颜无耻打寒战么？想到那个执迷不悟的年轻人即将把这样一件货色硬塞进您的社交圈子——您这位夫人俨然正是那圈子里一件华贵的饰物——想到他的愚顽、他昏了头的痴恋，岂不叫人肝肠寸断？我抗议，这实在叫人难以置信。我一个字也不信。」

===Original===
"I cannot quite believe it, either, Bobbie—"

"But you see, he loves her. You, my dear madam, blessed with a wiser estimation of our duties to society, of the responsibilities of our position, of the cost of even the most modest establishment, and, above all, of the sacredness of matrimony and the main chance, may well shrug your shoulders at such a plea. For, as you justly observe, what, after all, is this love? only a passing madness, an exploded superstition, an irresponsible ignis fatuus flickering over the quagmires and shallows of the divorce court. People's lives are no longer swayed by such absurdities; it is quite out of date."

===Chinese===
「我自己也还是不能全信呢，博比——」

「可是您瞧，他爱她呀。而您呢，亲爱的夫人，上苍赋有您更明智的鉴识——关于我们对社会负有的义务，关于我们这地位肩负的职责，关于哪怕最俭省的一份家业也要多少开销，尤其是关于婚姻的神圣和终身利害的盘算——对这样一个托词，您尽可以耸耸肩膀。因为，正如您方才中肯指出的，这爱情，说到底，算得了什么？不过是一阵过眼的疯狂，一种早经戳破的迷信，一缕不负责任的鬼火（ignis fatuus），在离婚法庭的泥沼和浅滩之上明灭摇曳。人们的生活再也不受这类荒唐东西的左右了；它早就过时啦。」

===Original===
"Yes; you are joking, Bobbie, I know; yet it is really out of date—"

"But I protest, loudly, my hand upon my heart, that it is true; people no longer do mad things for love, or ever did, in spite of lying poets; any more than the birds mate in the spring, or the sun rises in the morning; popular fallacies, my dear madam, every one of them. You and I know better, and are not to be deceived by appearances, however specious they may be. Ah, but come now! Having attained this highly satisfactory condition, we can well afford to laugh at all our past mistakes—yes, even at our own! For let us be quite candid. Wasn't there a time, dear lady, before Mr. Grundy came a-wooing, when, somehow, one was constantly meeting unexpected people in the garden, and, somehow, one sat out a formidable number of dances during the evening, and, somehow, the poets seemed a bit more plausible than they do today? It was very foolish, of course—but, ah, madam, there *was* a time—a time when even our staid blood rejoiced with a strange fervour in the summer moonlight, and it was good to be alive! Come now, have you the face to deny it—Mrs. Methuselah?"

===Chinese===
「是；你在说笑呢，博比，这我知道；可它确实过时了——」

「可是我抗议——高声抗议，手按着心口抗议——那是真的：人们再也不会为爱情干疯事了，或者不如说从来不曾干过，任凭那些诗人撒谎；正如鸟儿到了春天并不求偶，太阳到了清晨并不升起一样；亲爱的夫人，这些统统是流俗的谬见。您同我都是明白人，任凭表象何等似是而非，也休想骗过咱们。啊，得啦，咱们如今既已修到这般称心满意、通明透彻的境地，尽可以笑话往日的一切错处了——是的，连咱们自家的错处也一并笑话！因为，让咱们彻底坦白一回吧。亲爱的夫人，在格伦迪先生登门求亲之前，难道不曾有过那样一段时光么：不知怎么，人总在园子里撞见些不期而遇的人；不知怎么，一个晚上的舞，总要坐着相伴歇掉好几支；不知怎么，诗人们的话，比起今天来似乎也可信那么几分？那当然愚蠢得很——可是，啊，夫人，那样一段时光毕竟是有过的——在那段时光里，连咱们古井无波的血液，也曾怀着一种奇异的狂热，在夏夜的月色里欢欣鼓舞过，而活着，真好！来吧，您可有脸面否认么——玛土撒拉太太（Mrs. Methuselah）？」

===Original===
"It has not been quite bad to be alive, these last few hours—"

"And, oh, my dear, how each of us will look back some day to this very moment! And we are wasting it! And I have not any words to tell you how I love you! I am just a poor, dumb brute!" I groaned.

===Chinese===
「这几个钟头活着的滋味，倒也着实不坏——」

「啊，亲爱的，有朝一日，你我将会怎样回望眼下的这一刻啊！而咱们竟在虚掷它！我竟寻不出一言半语来告诉你，我多么爱你！我就是一头可怜的、哑口无言的畜生！」我呻吟道。

===Original===
Then very tenderly she began to talk with me in a voice I cannot tell you of, and concerning matters not to be recorded.

And still she would not promise anything; and I would give an arm, I think, could it replevin all the idiotic and exquisite misery I knew that night.

===Chinese===
随后，她十分温柔地同我攀谈起来，用的那种嗓音，我无法向你说起；谈的那些事情，也不便记载于此。

而她依旧什么也不肯应承；我想，倘若交出一条胳膊便能赎回那一夜我所尝尽的又痴又甜的苦楚，这条胳膊我是肯给的。

## VIII / 八

He Duels with a Stupid Woman / 他与一个愚蠢的女人决斗

### I / 一

===Original===
Yet I approached the garden on Saturday night with an elated heart. This was the last evening of the engagement of the Imperial Dramatic Company. Tomorrow the troupe was to leave Fairhaven; but I was very confident that the leading lady would not accompany them, and by reason of this confidence, I smiled as I strode through the city of Fairhaven, and hummed under my breath an inane ditty of an extremely sentimental nature.

As I bent over the little wooden gate, and searched for its elusive latch, a man came out of the garden, wheeling sharply about the hedge that, until this, had hidden him; and simultaneously, I was aware of the mingled odour of bad tobacco and of worse whiskey. Well, she would have done with such people soon! I threw open the gate, and stood aside to let him pass; then, as the moon fell full upon the face of the man, I gave an inarticulate, startled sound.

===Chinese===
然而，星期六晚上，我走向那座园子时，心头是飞扬的。这是帝国剧团（Imperial Dramatic Company）此轮演出季的最后一个夜晚。明天剧团就要离开费尔黑文；可我笃定得很，那位头牌女主角是绝不会随团同去的，也正凭着这份笃定，我大步穿过费尔黑文的街市，一路含笑，还在喉咙底下哼着一支伤感透顶的痴傻小调。

我正俯在那扇小木门前，摸索那神出鬼没的门闩，一个男人从园子里走了出来，急急绕过那道此前一直把他遮着的树篱；与此同时，我闻到了劣等烟草和更劣等的威士忌混在一处的气味。哼，反正她很快就要同这号人物一刀两断了！我一把推开园门，侧身站开，让他过去；随后，月光满满地落在那人的脸上，我发出了一声含混的、吃惊的声音。

===Original===
“Fine evening, sir,” suggested the stranger.

“Eh?” said I; “eh? Oh, yes, yes! quite so!” Afterward I shrugged my shoulders, and went into the garden, a trifle puzzled.

===Chinese===
「晚上真不错啊，先生。」那陌生人搭讪道。

「呃？」我说；「呃？哦，对，对！正是！」随后我耸了耸肩，走进园子，心里有点儿犯迷糊。

### II / 二

===Original===
I found her beneath a great maple in the heart of the enclosure. It was a place of peace; the night was warm and windless, and the moon, now come to its full glory, rode lazily in the west through a froth of clouds. Everywhere the heavens were faintly powdered with stardust, but even the planets seemed pale and ineffectual beside the splendour of the moon.

The garden was drenched in moonshine—moonshine that silvered the unmown grass-plots, and converted the white rosebushes into squat-figured wraiths, and tinged the red ones with dim purple hues. On every side the foliage blurred into ambiguous vistas, where fireflies loitered; and the long shadows of the nearer trees, straining across the grass, were wried patterns scissored out of blue velvet. It was a place of peace and light and languid odours, and I came into it, laughing, the possessor of an over-industrious heart and of a perfectly unreasoning joy over the fact that I was alive.

===Chinese===
我在园子正中一株大枫树下找到了她。那是一片安谧之地；夜温暖无风，月亮如今已臻全盛，懒洋洋地在西天行驶，穿过一团泡沫似的云絮。四方的天空都薄薄敷了一层星尘，可就连诸行星，在这轮明月的华彩旁边，也显得苍白而乏力。

园子浸透了月光——那月光给未加修剪的草坪镀上银色，把白色的蔷薇丛化作一个个矮墩墩的幽灵，又给红的蔷薇蒙上一层幽暗的紫晕。四面八方，枝叶都晕染成影影绰绰的深景，萤火虫在其间徜徉；近处树木的长影挣扎着横过草地，恰似从蓝丝绒上铰出的一幅幅歪斜花样。这是一片安谧、光明、气息慵懒的所在，而我笑着走进来，胸中揣着一颗过分勤勉的心，还为着自己活着这一桩事实，怀着一腔全然不讲道理的欢喜。

===Original===
“I say,” I observed, as I stretched luxuriously upon the grass beside her, “you put up at a shockingly disreputable place, Signorina.”

“Yes?” said she.

“That fellow who just went out,” I explained—“do you know the police want his address, I think? No,” I continued, after consideration, “I am sure I’m not mistaken—that is either Ned Lethbury, the embezzler, or his twin-brother. It’s been five years since I saw him, but that is he. And that,” said I, with proper severity, “is a sample of the sort of associate you prefer to your humble servant! Ah, Signorina, Signorina, I am a tolerably worthless chap, I admit, but at least I never forged and embezzled and then skipped my bail! So you had much better marry me, my dear, and say goodbye to your peculating friends. But, deuce take it! I forgot—I ought to notify the police or something, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
「我说，」我在她身旁的草地上舒舒服服地伸展开来，开口道，「小姐（Signorina），你落脚的这户人家，可不体面得吓死人。」

「是吗？」她说。

「刚走出去的那个家伙，」我解释道——「你可知道，我看警察正打听他的住址呢。不，」我斟酌了一下，又接着说，「我敢保自己没认错——那不是挪用公款的内德·莱思伯里（Ned Lethbury），就是他的孪生兄弟。我有五年没见他了，可那就是他。而这一位，」我以恰如其分的严正说道，「正好给你那宁可舍了鄙人也要结交的朋友做了一个标本！唉，小姐，小姐，我承认自己是个顶没出息的家伙，可我至少从不伪造文书、挪用公款，然后再弃保潜逃！所以呀，亲爱的，你还是嫁给我好得多，同你那班手脚不干净的朋友道一声再见。可是，见鬼！我倒忘了——我想，我总该去报警什么的。」

===Original===
She caught my arm. Her mouth opened and shut again before she spoke. “He—he is my husband,” she said, in a toneless voice. Then, on a sudden, she wailed: “Oh, forgive me! Oh, my great, strong, beautiful boy, forgive me, for I am very unhappy, and I cannot meet your eyes—your honest eyes! Ah, my dear, my dear, do not look at me like that—you don’t know how it hurts!”

The garden noises lisped about us in the long silence that fell. Then the far-off whistling of some home going citizen of Fairhaven tinkled shrilly through the night, and I shuddered a bit.

===Chinese===
她一把抓住我的胳膊。她张了张嘴，又合上，这才开口。「他——他是我丈夫。」她说，嗓音平淡得没有一丝起伏。随即，她忽然恸哭起来：「哦，饶恕我吧！哦，我高大、强壮、漂亮的男孩，饶恕我吧，因为我非常不幸，我不敢迎上你的眼睛——你那双诚实的眼睛！啊，亲爱的，亲爱的，别那样看着我——你不知道这有多叫人疼！」

在随之落下的长长静默里，园中的种种声息在我们四周咿呀低语。随后，费尔黑文某个归家市民的口哨远远传来，尖细地叮叮划过夜色，我不由得微微打了个寒噤。

===Original===
“I don’t understand,” I commenced, strangely quiet. “You told me—”

“Ah, I lied to you! I lied to you!” she cried. “I didn’t, mean to—hurt you. I did not know—I couldn’t know—I was so lonely, Bobbie,” she pleaded, with wide eyes; “oh, you don’t know how lonely I am. And when you came to me that first night, you—why, you spoke to me as the men I once knew used to speak. There was respect in your voice, and I wanted that so; I hadn’t had a man speak to me like that for years, you know, Bobbie. And, boy dear, I was so lonely in my squalid world—and it seemed as if the world I used to know was calling me—your world, Bobbie—the world I am shut out from.”

===Chinese===
「我不明白，」我开口道，出奇地平静。「你告诉过我——」

「啊，我骗了你！我骗了你！」她喊道。「我不是存心——要伤你。我不知道——我不可能知道——我太孤单了，博比（Bobbie），」她睁大眼睛央告道；「哦，你不知道我有多孤单。头一晚你到我身边来的时候，你——哎呀，你同我说话的那个样子，就像我从前认识的那些男人惯常的那样。你的话音里有敬重，我多想要这个啊；你知道么，博比，多少年来，没有一个男人那样同我说过话了。而且，亲爱的男孩，在我这污糟的世界里，我孤单透了——那时候，仿佛我从前的世界在召唤我——你的世界，博比——那个把我关在外头的世界。」

===Original===
“Yes,” I said; “I think I understand.”

“And I thought for a week—just to peep into it, to be a lady again for an hour or two—why, it didn’t seem wicked, then, and I wanted it so much! I—I knew I could trust you, because you were only a boy. And I was hungry—*so* hungry for a little respect, a little courtesy, such as men don’t accord strolling actresses. So I didn’t tell you till the very last I was married. I lied to you. Oh, but you don’t understand, this stupid, honest boy doesn’t understand anything except that I have lied to him!”

===Chinese===
「是的，」我说；「我想我明白。」

「我只打算过一个礼拜——只消往那世界里偷看一眼，重新做上一两个钟头的大家闺秀——哎呀，那时候这算不得什么罪过，可我又多么想要！我——我知道我信得过你，因为你不过是个大孩子。而我饿——就为一点点敬重、一点点礼数，我*那样*地饿，男人们是不肯把这种东西施舍给跑码头的女演员的。所以直到最后一刻，我都没告诉你我成了亲。我骗了你。哦，可你不懂，这个又蠢又老实的男孩什么都不懂，只懂我骗了他！」

===Original===
“Signorina,” I said, again, and I smiled, resolutely, “I think I understand.” I took both her hands in mine, and laughed a little. “But, oh, my dear, my dear,” I said, “you should have told me that you loved another man; for you have let me love you for a week, and now I think that I must love you till I die.”

“Love him!” she echoed. “Oh, boy dear, boy dear, what a Galahad it is! I don’t think Ned ever cared for anything but Father’s money; and I—why, you have seen him. How *could* I love him?” she asked, as simply as a child.

===Chinese===
「小姐，」我又说了一遍，毅然微笑着，「我想我明白。」我把她的双手握进我手里，轻轻笑了一声。「可是，哦，亲爱的，亲爱的，」我说，「你原该告诉我，你爱着另一个男人；因为你已由着我爱了你整整一个礼拜，而如今我想，我恐怕要一直爱你，爱到我死。」

「爱他！」她应声道。「哦，亲爱的男孩，亲爱的男孩，好一位加拉哈德（Galahad）呀！我不认为内德除了父亲的钱，还曾把心上过别的什么；而我——哎呀，你也见过他了。我*怎么可能*爱他呢？」她问，简单得像个孩子。

===Original===
I bowed my head. “And yet—” said I. Then I laughed again, somewhat bitterly. “Don’t let’s tell stories, Mrs. Lethbury,” I said; “it is kindly meant, I know, but I remember you now. I even danced with you once, some seven years ago—yes, at the Green Chalybeate. I remember the night, for a variety of reasons. You are Alfred Van Orden’s daughter; your father is a wealthy man, a very wealthy man; and yet, when your—your husband disappeared you followed him—to become a strolling actress. Ah, no, a woman doesn’t sacrifice everything for a man in the way you have done, unless she loves him.”

I caught my breath. Some unknown force kept tugging down the corners of my mouth, in a manner that hampered speech; moreover, nothing seemed worth talking about. I had lost her. That was the one thing which mattered.

===Chinese===
我垂下头。「可是——」我说。随即我又笑了，笑得有几分苦。「咱们就别编故事了吧，莱思伯里太太，」我说；「我知道这是出于好意，可我如今记起你来了。七年前我甚至同你跳过一回舞——对，就在绿铁泉（Green Chalybeate）。那一晚我记得，缘由有好几重。你是阿尔弗雷德·范·奥登（Alfred Van Orden）的女儿；你父亲是个有钱人，非常有钱的人；然而当年，你的——你丈夫失踪了，你竟跟着他走了——落到做了个跑码头的女演员。啊，不，一个女人若不是爱着一个男人，断不会像你这样，为他把一切都牺牲掉。」

我屏住呼吸。某种不知名的力量一味往下拽我嘴角的两个角，拽得说话都碍事；再说，仿佛再没有什么事情值得开口谈起。我已经失去她了。这才是唯一要紧的事。

===Original===
“Why, of course, I went with him,” she assented, a shade surprised; “he was my husband, you know. But as for loving—no, I don’t think Ned ever really loved me,” she reflected, with puckering brows. “He took that money for—for another woman, if you remember. But he is fond of me, and—and he *needs* me.”

I did not say anything; and after a little she went on, with a quick lift of speech.

“Oh, what a queer life we have led since then! You can’t imagine it, my dear. He has been a tavern-keeper, a drummer—everything! Why, last summer we sold rugs and Turkish things in Atlantic City! But he is always afraid of meeting someone who knows him, and—and he drinks too much. So we have not got on in the world, Ned and I; and now, after three years, I’m the leading lady of the Imperial Dramatic Company, and he is the manager. I forgot, though—he is advance-agent this week, for he didn’t dare stay in Fairhaven, lest some of the men at Mr. Charteris’s should recognize him, you know. He came back only this evening—”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，我当然跟他走了，」她表示同意，略带一分诧异；「要知道，他是我丈夫啊。至于爱嘛——不，我不认为内德当真爱过我，」她蹙着眉头思忖。「你如果还记得，他挪那笔钱，是为着——为着另一个女人。不过他喜欢我，而且——而且他*需要*我。」

我没有作声；过了一小会儿，她接着往下说，话音轻快地扬了起来。

「哦，打那以后我们过的多古怪的日子呀！你想都想不到的，亲爱的。他做过酒馆掌柜，做过旅行推销员——什么都做过！哎呀，去年夏天，我们还在大西洋城（Atlantic City）卖地毯和土耳其货色呢！可他老是怕撞见认得他的人，而且——而且他酒喝得太凶。所以我们一直没能发迹，内德和我；如今，过了三年，我当上了帝国剧团的头牌女主角，他当上了经理。不过我倒忘了——这个礼拜他出去做先遣代理人了，因为他不敢待在费尔黑文，你知道的，生怕查特里斯先生府上的哪位先生把他认出来。他今天傍晚才回来——」

===Original===
She paused for a moment; a wistful quaver crept into her speech. “Oh, it’s queer, it’s queer, Bobbie! Sometimes—sometimes when I have time to think, say on long Sunday afternoons, I remember my old life, every bit of it—oh, I do remember such strange little details! I remember the designs on the bread and butter plates, and all the silver things on my desk, and the plank by my door that always creaked and somehow never got fixed, and the big, shiny buttons on the coachman’s coat—just trifles like that. And—and they hurt, they hurt, Bobbie, those little, unimportant things! They—grip my throat.”

She laughed, not very mirthfully. “Then I am like the old lady in the nursery rhyme, and say, Surely, this can’t be I. But it is I, boy dear—a strolling actress, a barnstormer! Isn’t it queer, Bobbie? But, oh, you don’t know half—”

===Chinese===
她停了片刻；一缕怅惘的颤音悄悄爬进她的话里。「哦，真古怪，真古怪，博比！有时候——有时候等我得空想一想，比如说在漫长的星期天下午，我就记起我从前的日子，一点一滴全记起来——哦，我记得的尽是些古怪的小细节！我记得面包黄油碟上的花纹，记得我书桌上那些银器，记得我房门口那块老是吱呀作响、可不知怎么始终没人来修的地板，还有马车夫外衣上那些亮闪闪的大纽扣——尽是诸如此类的小玩意儿。可是——可是它们扎人，它们扎人啊，博比，那些无足轻重的小东西！它们——扼住我的喉咙。」

她笑了，笑得并不怎么欢。「于是我便成了童谣里那位老太太，口口声声说：断乎不会是我吧，断乎不会。可这就是我呀，亲爱的男孩——一个跑码头的女演员，一个走乡串镇的戏子！这多古怪呀，博比？可是，哦，你连一半都不知道——」

===Original===
I was remembering many things. I remembered Lethbury, a gross man, superfluously genial, whom I had never liked, although I recalled my admiration of his whiskers. I recollected young Amelia Van Orden, not come to her full beauty then, the bud of girlhood scarce slipped; and I remembered very vividly the final crash, the nine days’ talk over Lethbury’s flight in the face of certain conviction—by his father-in-law’s advice (as some said) who had furnished and forfeited heavy bail for the absconder. Oh, the brave woman who had followed! Oh, the brave, foolish woman! And, for the action’s recompense, he was content to exhibit her to yokels, to make of her beauty an article of traffic. Heine was right; there is an Aristophanes in heaven. And then hope blazed.

===Chinese===
我想起了许许多多的事。我想起莱思伯里，一个臃肿的男人，和气得过了量，我从来就不喜欢他，虽然我还记得当年自己对他的连鬓胡须颇为倾倒。我记起年轻的阿梅莉亚·范·奥登（Amelia Van Orden），那时她的美尚未全开，少女的蓓蕾几乎还未褪蒂；我更历历在目地记起最后那场崩塌，记起莱思伯里在定罪成局的前夕亡命他乡、惹起的那场轰动一时的九日之谈——据说（有人这么说）还是他岳父给出的主意，老人家为这个卷款逃亡者垫付了巨额保金，又白白地折没了它。哦，那跟着他走的勇敢女人！哦，那勇敢而愚蠢的女人！而作为这一义举的报偿，他竟心安理得，拿她向乡愚们展览，把她的美貌做成一件买卖的货色。海涅说得对；天上果然坐着一位阿里斯托芬（Aristophanes）。于是，希望熊熊地烧了起来。

===Original===
“Your husband,” I said, quickly, “he does not love you? He—he is not faithful to you?”

“No,” she answered; “there is a Miss Fortescue—she plays second parts—”

===Chinese===
「你丈夫，」我赶忙说，「他不爱你？他——他对你不忠？」

「不，」她答道；「有一位福特斯库小姐（Fortescue）——她演二路角色——」

===Original===
“Ah, my dear, my dear!” I cried, with a shaking voice; “come away, Signorina—come away with me! He *doesn’t* need you—and, oh, my dear, I need you so! You can get your divorce and marry me. Ah, Signorina, come away—come away from this squalid life that is killing you, to the world you are meant for, to the life you hunger for! Come back to the clean, lighthearted world you love, the world that is waiting to pet and caress you just as it used to do—our world, Signorina! You don’t belong here with—with the Fortescues. You belong to us.”

===Chinese===
「啊，亲爱的，亲爱的！」我喊道，嗓音发颤；「跟我走吧，小姐——跟我一起走吧！他*并不*需要你——而，哦，亲爱的，我多么需要你！你可以离婚，然后嫁给我。啊，小姐，走吧——离开这桩正把你折磨至死的污糟生活，到那个生来属于你的世界里去，到你所渴望的那种生活里去！回到你爱的那个干净、无忧无虑的世界里来吧，那个世界正等着宠你疼你，一如它从前那样——我们的世界，小姐！你不属于这里，不同——不同福特斯库之流在一起。你属于我们。」

===Original===
I sprang to my feet. “Come now!” said I. “There’s Anne Charteris; she is a good woman, if ever lived one. She used to know you, too, didn’t she? Well, then, come with me to her, dearest—and tonight! You shall see your father tomorrow. Your father—why, think how that old man loves you, how he has longed for you, his only daughter, all these years. And I?” I spread out my hands, in the tiniest, impotent gesture. “I love you,” I said, simply. “I cannot do without you, heart’s dearest.”

===Chinese===
我一跃而起。「这就走吧！」我说。「有安妮·查特里斯（Anne Charteris）在；世上只要还有好女人，她便是一个。她从前也认得你，不是吗？那么好了，跟我一同去见她，最亲爱的——就在今晚！明天你就去见你父亲。你父亲——哎呀，想想那位老人家多爱你，想想这些年来他多想你，他唯一的女儿。还有我呢？」我摊开双手，做了一个极其细微、无可奈何的手势。「我爱你，」我简单地说。「没有你，我活不成，我心上最亲爱的人。」

===Original===
Impulsively, she rested both hands upon my breast; then bowed her head a little. The nearness of her seemed to shake in my blood, to catch at my throat, and my hands, lifted for a moment, trembled with desire of her.

“You don’t understand,” she said. “I am a Catholic—my mother was one, you know. There is no divorce for us. And—and besides, I’m not modern. I am very old-fashioned, I suppose, in my ideas. Do you know,” she asked, with a smile upon the face which lifted confidingly toward me, “I—I *really* believe the world was made in six days; and that the whale swallowed Jonah, and that there is a real purgatory and a hell of fire and brimstone. You don’t, do you, Bobbie? But I do—and I promised to stay with him till death parted us, you know, and I must do it. I am all he has. He would get even worse without me. I—oh, boy dear, boy dear, I love you so!” And her voice broke, in a great, choking sob.

===Chinese===
她一时情动，把双手按在我胸上；随后微微垂下头去。她的贴近仿佛在我血液里震颤，仿佛扼住我的喉咙；我的双手举起了片刻，因对她的渴望而颤抖。

「你不明白，」她说。「我是天主教徒——要知道，我母亲就是。我们是没有离婚这一说的。再——再说，我这个人不新派。我想，在观念上我是非常老派的。你知道吗，」她把脸信赖地仰向我，脸上带着笑，问道，「我——我*当真*相信这个世界是六天里造出来的；相信鲸鱼吞下了约拿；相信真有炼狱，真有一个烧着硫磺烈火的地狱。你不信，是不是，博比？可我信——而且我答应过要陪伴他，直到死亡把我们分开，你知道的，我必须做到。他所有的，就只剩我了。没有我，他还会变得更坏。我——哦，亲爱的男孩，亲爱的男孩，我多么爱你！」她的嗓音终于断掉，迸作一声巨大的、哽咽的抽泣。

===Original===
“A promise—a promise made by an ungrown girl to a brute—a thief—!”

“No, dear,” she answered, quietly; “a promise made to God.”

===Chinese===
「一句诺言——一个没成年的姑娘对一个畜生——一个窃贼——许下的诺言——！」

「不，亲爱的，」她静静地回答；「那是对上帝许下的诺言。」

===Original===
And looking into her face, I saw love there, and anguish, and determination. It seemed monstrous, but of a sudden I knew with a dull surety; she loved me, but she thought she had no right to love me; she would not go with me. She would go with that drunken, brutish thief.

And I suddenly recalled certain clever women—Alicia Wade, Pauline Ashmeade, Cynthia Chaytor—the women of that world wherein I was novitiate; beyond question, they would raise delicately penciled eyebrows to proclaim this woman a fool—and to wonder.

They would be right, I thought. She was only a splendid, tenderhearted, bright-eyed fool, the woman that I loved. My heart sickened as her folly rose between us, an impassable barrier. I hated it; and I revered it.

===Chinese===
我望进她的脸，看见上面有爱，有痛楚，也有决绝。这似乎骇人听闻，可我突然间怀着一种木然的笃定知道了：她爱我，却认为自己没有权利爱我；她不会跟我走。她要跟那个酗酒的、畜生一般的窃贼走。

我又猛然想起几位聪明女人——艾丽西娅·韦德（Alicia Wade）、保利娜·阿什米德（Pauline Ashmeade）、辛西娅·查托（Cynthia Chaytor）——我在其中不过还是个见习生的那个世界里的女人们；毫无疑问，她们会把描画精致的眉梢轻轻一挑，宣告这个女人是个傻瓜——并且还要为之纳罕一番。

她们说得对，我想。她不过是一个光彩照人的、软心肠的、眼睛明亮的傻瓜，我所爱的这个女人。她的愚痴在我们中间升起，筑成一道不可逾越的屏障，我的心为之作呕。我恨它；我又敬它。

===Original===
Thus we two stood silent for a time. The wind murmured above in the maples, lazily, ominously. Then the gate clicked, with a vicious snap that pierced the silence like the report of a distant rifle. “That is probably Ned,” she said wearily. “I had forgotten they close the barrooms earlier on Saturday nights. So goodbye, Bobbie. You—you may kiss me, if you like.”

So for a moment our lips met. Afterward I caught her hands in mine, and gripped them close to my breast, looking down into her eyes. They glinted in the moonlight, deep pools of sorrow, and tender—oh, unutterably tender and compassionate.

But I found no hope there. I lifted her hand to my lips, and left her alone in the garden.

===Chinese===
我们两个就这样默默站了一阵。风在头顶的枫树间低语，懒懒的，又透着不祥。随后，园门咔哒一响，那一声狠戾的脆响刺破寂静，像远方一声步枪的枪响。「多半是内德，」她疲惫地说。「我倒忘了，星期六晚上酒馆关门关得早。那么再会吧，博比。你——你若愿意，可以吻我。」

于是我们的唇相触了片刻。随后我握住她的双手，紧紧攥在我胸口，低头望进她的眼睛。那双眼在月光里莹莹闪动，是两泓深深的愁潭，又温柔——哦，温柔与怜惜得难以言传。

可我在那里找不到希望。我把她的手举到唇边，便留下她独自一个，在园子里。

### III / 三

===Original===
Lethbury was fumbling at the gate.

“Such nuishance,” he complained, “havin’ gate won’t unlock. Latch mus’ got los’—po’ li’l latch,” murmured Mr. Lethbury, plaintively—“all ’lone in cruel worl’!”

I opened the gate for him, and stood aside to let him pass toward his wife.

===Chinese===
莱思伯里正在门口胡乱摸索。

「真讨嫌，」他抱怨道，「这门就是打不开。门闩准是丢了——可怜的小门闩，」莱思伯里先生可怜巴巴地咕哝着——「孤零零一个，扔在这狠心的世上！」

我替他开了门，侧身站开，让他朝他妻子那边走过去。

## IX / 九

He Puts His Tongue in His Cheek / 他把舌头抵在了腮帮里

### I / 一

===Original===
It was not long before John Charteris knew of the entire affair, for in those days I had few concealments from him: and the little wizened man brooded awhile over my misery, with an odd wistfulness.

===Chinese===
约翰·查特里斯不久便知道了事情的全部，因为那些日子里，我对他几乎没有什么隐瞒：于是这个干瘦矮小的男人，便对着我的苦楚出了一会儿神，带着一种古怪的怅惘。

===Original===
“I remember Amelia Van Orden perfectly,” he said—“now. I ought to have recognized her. Only, she was never, in her best days, the paragon you depict. She sang, I recollect; people made quite a to-do over her voice. But she was very, very stupid, and used to make loud shrieking noises when she was amused, and was generally reputed to be ‘fast.’ I never investigated. Even so, there was not any real doubt as to her affair, in any event, with Anton von Anspach, after that night the sleigh broke down—”

===Chinese===
「阿梅莉亚·范·奥登这个人，我记得清清楚楚，」他说——「如今全记起来了。我本该认出她来的。只是，她纵在最好的年月里，也从来不是你所描绘的那种十全美人。她唱过歌，这我记得；人们为她的嗓音着实喧腾过一阵。可她非常非常蠢，高兴起来惯会发出尖声的叫嚷，风评又素来是个『轻佻』女人。我没有去查究过。即便如此，无论如何，打雪橇坏在半路的那一夜起，她同安东·冯·安斯巴赫（Anton von Anspach）的那档子事，便没有什么真值得怀疑的了——」

===Original===
“Oh, spare me all those ancient Lichfield scandals! She is an angel, John, if there was ever one.”

“In your eyes, doubtless! So your heart is broken. Yet do you not realize that not a month ago you were heartbroken over Stella Musgrave? Child, I repeat, I envy you this perpetual unhappiness, for I have lost, as you will presently lose, the capacity of being quite miserable.”

===Chinese===
「哦，那些利奇菲尔德老掉牙的风流公案，你饶了我吧！她是个天使，约翰，倘若世上真有过天使的话。」

「在你眼里，自然是！这么说，你的心碎了。可你难道不明白，不出一个月之前，你还为斯特拉·马斯格罗夫心碎过么？孩子，我再说一遍，我羡慕你这没完没了的伤心，因为那种痛彻愁苦的本事，我已经失掉了，你不久也要失掉。」

===Original===
“But, John, it seems as if there were nothing left to live for, now—”

“At twenty-one! Well, certainly, at that age one loves to think of life as being implacable. But you will soon discover that she is merely inconsequential, and that none of her antics are of lasting importance; and you will learn to smile a deal more often than you weep or laugh.”

===Chinese===
「可是约翰，如今这世上，仿佛再没有什么可活的了——」

「二十一岁就说出这种话！唉，当然了，那个年纪的人，就爱把生活看作铁面无情的对头。可你很快就会发现，她不过是漫不经心、没个长性，她那些胡闹没有一桩有长久的重要性；你还会学会多笑，笑的回数远比哭和大笑来得多。」

===Original===
Then we talked of other matters. It was presently settled that Charteris was to take me abroad with him that summer; and with the thorough approval of my mother.

===Chinese===
随后我们谈起别的事情。不久便定下来：那年夏天查特里斯带我出国；我母亲也满口赞成。

===Original===
“Mr. Charteris will be of incalculable benefit to you,” she told me, “in introducing you to the very best people, all of whom he knows, of course, and besides you are getting to look older than I, and it is unpleasant to have to be always explaining you are only my stepson, particularly as your father never married anybody but me, though, heaven knows, I wish he had. Of course you will be just as wild as your father and your Uncle George. I suppose that is to be expected, and I daresay it will break my heart, but all I ask of you is please to keep out of the newspapers, except of course the social items. And if you *must* associate with abandoned women, please for my sake, Robert, don’t have anything to do with those who can prove that they are only misunderstood, because they are the most dangerous kind.”

===Chinese===
「查特里斯先生引你去结识顶顶上等的人物——他们他自然全都认得——对你会有说不尽的好处；再说，你瞧着已渐渐比我显老，总得一遍又一遍向人解释你不过是我的继子，实在叫人不痛快，何况你父亲除我之外压根儿谁也没娶过——虽然老天知道，我倒宁愿他娶过。当然喽，你将来准同你父亲和你乔治叔叔一样荒唐。我想这也是意料中事，而且我敢说这会伤透我的心；可是我对你的全部请求，就是千万别上报——社交消息栏自然除外。还有，你倘若非要同不正经的女人来往不可，看在我的份上，罗伯特，千万别沾那些能证明自己『只是不被人理解』的女人，因为那一种最危险。」

===Original===
I kissed her. “Dear little mother, I honestly believe that when you get to heaven you will refuse to speak to Mary Magdalen.”

“Robert, let us remember the Bible says, ‘in my Father’s house are many mansions,’ and of course nobody would think of putting me in the same mansion with her.”

It was well-nigh the last conversation I was to hold with my mother; and I was to remember it with an odd tenderness. …

===Chinese===
我吻了她。「亲爱的小妈妈，我实实在在相信，你到了天堂，是不肯同抹大拉的马利亚（Mary Magdalen）讲话的。」

「罗伯特，咱们可要记得，圣经上说，『在我父的家里有许多住处』，自然，谁也不会想到把我和她安置在同一处宅子里。」

那差不多是我同母亲最后一次谈话；日后想起它来，我心里总带着一种奇异的温柔。……

### II / 二

===Original===
Upon the doings of myself in Europe during the ensuing two years I prefer to dwell as lightly as possible. I had long anticipated a sojourn in divers old-world cities; but the London I had looked to find was the London of Dickens, say, and my Paris the Paris of Dumas, or at the very least of Balzac. It is needless to mention that in the circles to which the, quite real, friendship of John Charteris afforded an entry I found little that smacked of such antiquity. I had entered a world inhabited by people who amused themselves and apparently did nothing else; and I was at first troubled by their levity, and afterward envious of it, and in the end embarked upon sedulous attempt to imitate it. I continued to be very boyish; indeed, I found myself by this in much the position of an actor who has made such a success in one particular role that the public declines to patronize him in any other.

===Chinese===
此后两年间我在欧洲的种种行状，我宁可尽量轻轻带过。我久已盼着到旧大陆的各色古城里盘桓些时日；可我指望寻见的伦敦，是——譬如说——狄更斯笔下的伦敦，我的巴黎是大仲马笔下的巴黎，退一万步也得是巴尔扎克笔下的。不必多说，凭着约翰·查特里斯那份千真万确的交情为我叩开的那些圈子，里头几乎寻不出什么带着这般古意的东西。我闯进了一个世界，里头住满了寻欢作乐、而且显然此外什么也不干的人们；起初我为他们的轻浮而惶惶不安，继而又嫉妒起它来，末了便一头扎进勤勤恳恳的模仿里去。我照旧满身孩子气；说真的，我由此发觉自己的处境，同一个演员颇有些相仿：他在一个特定的角色上红得那般彻底，以致观众再也不肯赏光看他演任何别的戏。

### III / 三

===Original===
It was during this first year abroad that I wrote *The Apostates*, largely through the urging of John Charteris.

“You have the ability, though, that dances most gracefully in fetters. You will never write convincingly about the life you know, because life is, to you, my adorable boy, a series of continuous miracles, to which the eyes of other men are case-hardened. Write me, then, a book about the past.”

===Chinese===
正是在出国的头一年，我写了《背教者》（The Apostates），多半出于约翰·查特里斯的撺掇。

「不过你倒有那样的本事：戴着镣铐，也跳得一出顶曼妙的舞。你所熟悉的当代生活，你是永远写不像的，因为生活，在我可爱的孩子你看来，是一连串绵延不绝的奇迹，而旁人的眼睛对这奇迹早已结了硬茧。那么，替我写一本关于过去的书吧。」

===Original===
“I have thought of it,” said I, “for being over here makes the past seem pretty real, somehow. Last month when I was at Ingilby I was on fire with the notion of writing something about old Ormskirk—my mother’s ancestor, you know. And since I’ve seen what’s left of Bellegarde I have wanted to write about his wife’s people too—the dukes and vicomtes of Puysange, or even about the great Jurgen. You see, I am just beginning to comprehend that these are not merely characters in Lowe’s and La Vrilliere’s books, but my flesh and blood kin, like Uncle George Bulmer—”

===Chinese===
「我也想过这个，」我说，「因为不知怎么，人一到这边，过去便显得相当真切起来。上个月我住在英吉尔比（Ingilby）的时候，一心火烧火燎，想写点什么老奥姆斯柯克（Ormskirk）的事——你知道，那是我母亲的先祖。而自从见过贝勒加德（Bellegarde）剩下的残迹，我又想写写他妻子那一族——普伊桑日（Puysange）的历代公爵和子爵，甚至写写那位了不起的尤尔根（Jurgen）。你瞧，我才刚刚开始明白：这些不只是劳（Lowe）和拉弗里利耶尔（La Vrilliere）书里的人物，还是我的骨肉至亲，就像乔治·布尔默（George Bulmer）叔叔——」

===Original===
“And for that reason you want to write about them! You would, though; it is eminently characteristic. Well, then, why should you not immortalize the persons who had the honor of begetting you—oh, most handsome and most naive of children!—by writing your very best about them?”

“Because to succeed—not only among the general but with the ‘cultured few,’ God save the mark!—it is now necessary to write not badly but abominably.”

===Chinese===
「而恰恰为着这个缘故，你才想写他们！换了你准会如此；这十足是你的本色。那么好吧，何不倾尽你的绝技去写他们，教那些有幸把你生养下来的人物流芳百世呢——啊，天下最俊美、最天真的孩子！」

「因为要想成功——不但在大众里头，还要博得『少数有教养者』的青睐，老天爷饶恕这个名目！——如今须要做到的，不是写得不坏，而是写得糟糕透顶。」

===Original===
“What would you demand, then, of a book?”

I meditated. “What one most desiderates in the writings of today is clarity, and beauty, and tenderness and urbanity, and truth.”

===Chinese===
「那么，你要求一本书做到些什么呢？」

我沉吟片刻。「在今日的写作里，人们最渴求的是明晰，是美，是温柔与儒雅，还有真实。」

===Original===
“Not a bad recipe, upon the whole, though I would stipulate for symmetry and distinction also—Write the book!”

“Ah,” said I, “but this is the kind of book I wish to read when, of course, the mood seizes me. It is not at all the sort of book, though, I would elect to write. The main purpose of writing any book, I take it, is to be read; and people simply will not read a book when they suspect it of being carefully written. That sort of thing gets on a reader’s nerves; it’s too much like watching a man walk a tightrope and wondering if he won’t slip presently.”

===Chinese===
「总的来说，这张方子不坏，只是我还要添上匀称与不凡——写这本书吧！」

「啊，」我说，「可是这一类书，是我兴之所至时愿意读的；却压根儿不是我愿意动笔去写的那一类。据我看，写任何一本书的主旨，总归是给人读；而人们一旦疑心一本书是惨淡经营写出来的，便干脆不肯读它了。那一套叫读者神经发紧；太像看人走钢丝，一路悬着心，生怕他下一刻便要失脚。」

===Original===
“Oh, ‘people!’” Charteris flung out, in an extremity of scorn. “Since time was young, a generally incompetent humanity has been willing to pardon anything rather than the maddening spectacle of labour competently done. And they are perfectly right; it is abominable how such weak-minded persons occasionally thrust themselves into a world quite obviously designed for persons who have not any minds at all. But I was not asking you to write a ‘bestseller.’”

===Chinese===
「哦，『人们』！」查特里斯把这个词嗤了出来，轻蔑到了极处。「打从时间尚且年幼的时候起，这个大体上事事无能的人类，向来什么都肯宽恕，唯独不能容忍一桩干得利落漂亮的活计摆到眼前，教他们抓狂。他们也全然有理：这些头脑孱弱的家伙，时不时硬要挤进一个明摆着为全无头脑者设计的世界里来，真是可恶之至。不过，我并没有请你写什么『畅销书』。」

===Original===
“No, you were asking me to become an Economist, and be one of ‘the few rare spirits which every age providentially affords,’ and so on. That is absolute and immoral nonsense. When you publish a novel you are at least pretending to supply a certain demand; and if you don’t endeavour honestly to supply it, you are a swindler, no more and no less. No, it is all very well to write for posterity, if it amuses you, John; personally, I cannot imagine what possible benefit you will derive from it, even though posterity *does* read your books. And for myself, I want to be read and to be a power while I can appreciate the fact that I *am* a sort of power, however insignificant. Besides, I want to make some money out of the blamed thing. Mother is a dear, of course, but, like all the Bulmers, with age she is becoming tightfisted.”

===Chinese===
「没有，你是请我去做一个经济学家派，做『每个时代都蒙上天安排下的少数几个罕见才智之士』里头的一个，等等等等。那是一派彻头彻尾、伤天害理的胡言。你出版一部小说，至少是在装模作样地供应某种需求；你若不肯老老实实去供应它，你便是个骗子，不多也不少。不，为后世写作自无不可，倘若那能给你解闷，约翰；只是就我个人而论，我想象不出你能从中捞到什么好处，哪怕后世当真读你的书。至于我自己，我要趁还来得及领会『我也算一号人物』——哪怕微不足道——这个事实的时候，被人阅读，做个有权势的人。再说，我还想从这劳什子身上赚几个钱。母亲当然是个可人儿，可她同布尔默家所有的人一样，上了年纪，手也越攥越紧了。」

===Original===
“And Esau—” Charteris began.

“Yes—but that’s Biblical, and publishing a book is business. People say to authors, just as they do to tailors: ‘I want such and such an article. Make it and I’ll pay you for it.’ Now, your tailor may consider the Imperial Roman costume more artistic than that of today, and so may you in the abstract, but if he sent home a toga in place of a pair of trousers, you would discontinue dealing with him. So if it amuses you to make togas, well and good; I don’t quarrel with it; but, personally, I mean to go into the gents’ furnishing line and to do my work efficiently.”

===Chinese===
「还有以扫（Esau）——」查特里斯开了腔。

「是啊——可那是圣经里的事，出书却是生意。人们对作家说的话，同他们对裁缝说的一模一样：『我要某式某样的货色。做出来，我付你钱。』喏，你的裁缝也许认为罗马帝国的衣冠比今日的行头更艺术，你往抽象里想也未尝不可以这么看；可他若给你送回家一件托加袍来顶替一条裤子，你从此便不再同他打交道了。所以，做托加袍倘若能让你快活，很好很好；我无意同你抬杠；只是就我而论，我打算投身男装服饰这一行，并且把我的活计干得干净利落。」

===Original===
“Yes—but with your tongue in your cheek.”

“It is the one and only attitude,” I sweetly answered, “in which to write if you indeed desire to be read with enjoyment.” And presently I rose and launched upon

A Defence of That Attitude

===Chinese===
「是啊——只不过你写的时候，舌头是抵在腮帮子里的。」

「那是独一无二的态度，」我甜甜地答道，「倘若你当真指望人家读得兴味盎然，便唯有以此态度来写作。」随后我便站起身来，就此放言——

为那种态度一辩

===Original===
“The main trouble with you, John Charteris, is that you will never recover from being fin de siècle. Yes, you belong to that queer dying nineteenth century.
