Uncle George whistled softly. “ ‘Honourable young gentleman,’ ” he murmured, as to himself, “ ‘desires to meet attractive and innocent young lady. Object: to learn how to be idyllic in three-hundred pages.’ ”

===Chinese===
乔治叔叔轻轻打了个呼哨。「『一位体面的青年绅士，』」他自言自语似的低声道，「『欲结识迷人而天真的年轻淑女一名。目的：学会如何在三百页篇幅之内写成田园牧歌。』」

===Original===
There was no commentary upon his text.

===Chinese===
对他这段高文，无人作评。

===Original===
“I say,” queried Mr. Bulmer, “do you think this sort of thing is fair to the girl? Isn’t it a little cold-blooded?”

===Chinese===
「我说，」布尔默先生问道，「你觉不觉得这种事对那姑娘不大公道？这岂不有几分冷血么？」

===Original===
“Respected nunky, you are at times very terribly the man in the street! Anyhow, I leave the Green Chalybeate tomorrow in search of *As the Coming of Dawn*.”

===Chinese===
「受人敬重的老叔，您有时候实在可怕得很，活脱脱是个街头巷尾的寻常俗人！不管怎样，我明天就要离开绿铁泉，去寻访《恰似黎明降临》了。」

===Original===
“Look here,” said Mr. Bulmer, rising, “if you start on a tour of the country, looking for assorted dawns and idylls, it will end in my abducting you from some rustic institution for the insane. You take a liver-pill and go to bed! I don’t promise anything, mind, but perhaps about the first I can manage a little cheque if only you will make oath on a few Bibles not to tank up on it in Lichfield. The transoms there,” he added unkindlily, “are not built for those full rich figures.”

===Chinese===
「你听着，」布尔默先生站起身来，「你若当真要周游全国，四处搜罗五花八门的黎明与田园诗，到头来免不了由我把你从哪家乡下疯人院里搭救出来。快吃一粒肝药丸，上床睡觉去！你给我记住，我什么也不应承；不过到一号前后，兴许我能设法对付出一张小小的支票——只要你肯对几部圣经起誓，不用这笔钱在利奇菲尔德痛饮一场。那儿的门楣横窗，」他又刻薄地添了一句，「可不是照着您这样富态丰满的身材建造的。」

### II / 二

===Original===
Next morning, I notified the desk-clerk, and, quite casually, both the newspaper correspondents, that the Green Chalybeate was about to be bereft of the presence of a distinguished novelist. Then, as my train did not leave till night, I resolved to be bored on horseback, rather than on the golf-links, and had Guendolen summoned, from the stable, for a final investigation of the country roads thereabouts.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，我知会了柜台接待员，又以全然若无其事的姿态知会了那两位报纸记者：绿铁泉即将痛失一位杰出小说家的驾临。随后，由于火车要到夜里才开，我决意把这份烦闷改在马背上去消受，而不去高尔夫球场，便吩咐从马厩里唤出格温多琳（Guendolen），对这一带的乡间道路做最后一次勘察。

===Original===
Guendolen this afternoon elected to follow a new route; and knowing by experience that any questioning of this decision could but result in undignified defeat, I assented. Thus it came about that we circled parallel to the boardwalk, which leads uphill to the deserted Royal Hotel, and passed its rows of broken windows; and went downhill again, always at Guendolen’s election; and thus came to the creek, which babbled across the roadway and was overhung with thick foliage that lisped and whispered cheerfully in the placid light of the declining sun. It was there that the germ of *As the Coming of Dawn* was found.

===Chinese===
这天下午，格温多琳裁定取道一条新路线；经验告诉我，对这项裁决提出任何质询，结局都只能是一场不体面的败仗，我便应允了。于是我们兜的圈子恰好与那条木板步道平行——步道一路朝山上通去，直通那座被废弃的皇家旅馆（Royal Hotel）——路过了它一排排破碎的窗子；随后又一路下坡，处处循着格温多琳的裁决；就这样来到了那条小溪边。溪水淙淙地横过路面，头顶浓荫四合，在西斜太阳恬静的光里，欢快地咬着舌子窸窣低语。《恰似黎明降临》的胚芽，便是在那里寻获的。

===Original===
For I had fallen into a reverie over the deplorable obstinacy of my new heroine, who declined, for all my labours, to be unsophisticated; and taking advantage of this, Guendolen had twitched the reins from my hand and proceeded to satisfy her thirst in a manner that was rather too noisy to be quite good form. I sat in patience, idly observing the sparkling reflection of the sunlight on the water. I was elaborating a comparison between my obstinate heroine and Guendolen. Then Guendolen snorted, as something rustled through the underbrush, and turning, I perceived a Vision.

===Chinese===
原来我正陷入出神，为我那位新女主角可叹的固执发愁——任凭我百般经营，她始终不肯变得天真未凿；格温多琳趁这个空子，从我手里一扯扯走了缰绳，径自打起水来，那动静未免太响，算不得十分得体。我耐心坐着，闲看日光在水面闪烁的粼粼倒影，心中正细细推敲一个比拟，把我这位固执的女主角同格温多琳相提并论。忽然灌木丛中窸窣一响，格温多琳打了个响鼻；我回过头去，便看见了一位幻象（Vision）。

===Original===
The Vision was in white, with a profusion of openwork. There were blue ribbons connected with it. There were also black eyes, of the almond-shaped, heavy-lidded variety that I had thought existed only in Lely’s pictures, and great coils of brown hair which was gold where the chequered sunlight fell upon it, and two lips that were inexpressibly red. I was filled with pity for my tired horse, and a resolve that for this once her thirst should be quenched.

===Chinese===
那幻象一身白衣，镂空轻纱层叠繁复。周身还连缀着几条蓝缎带。还有一双黑眼睛——杏核形、眼帘沉沉的那一种，我原以为只存在于莱利（Lely）的画里；还有一大盘一大盘的棕色头发，斑驳的日光落上去，便泛出金色；还有两片嘴唇，红得无从言说。我心里霎时充满对我这匹疲惫坐骑的怜悯，并立下决断：就这一回，定要叫她饮个畅快。

===Original===
Thereupon, I lifted my cap hastily; and Guendolen scrambled to the other bank, and spluttered, and had carried me well past the Iron Spring, before I announced to the evening air that I was a fool, and that Guendolen was describable by various quite picturesque and derogatory epithets. And I smiled.

===Chinese===
于是我匆匆掀了掀帽檐；格温多琳蹬蹬爬上对岸，甩着水沫，一路把我驮过了铁泉（Iron Spring）老远，我才对着暮色里的空气宣布：我是个傻瓜；而格温多琳呢，尽可以用各色相当传神又相当不客气的形容词来描摹她。于是我笑了。

===Original===
“Now, Robert Etheridge Townsend, you writer of books, here is a subject made to your hand!” And then:

“Only ’twixt the light and shade
Floating memories of my maid
Make me pray for Guendolen.”

After this we retraced our steps. I was peering anxiously about the roadway.

===Chinese===
「好啊，罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森，你这写书的人，现成有个题目送到你手上来了！」接着便是：

「唯有在光与影之间，
我那姑娘飘浮的忆念，
教我为格温多琳祈愿。」

这之后，我们循原路折回。我一路焦灼地打量着路面。

===Original===
“Pardon me,” said I, subsequently; “but *have* you seen anything of a watch—a small gold one, set with pearls?”

“Heavens!” said the Vision, sympathetically, “what a pity! Are you sure it fell here?”

“I don’t seem to have it about me,” I answered, with cryptic, but entire veracity. I searched about my pockets, with a puckered brow. “And as we stopped here—”

===Chinese===
「恕我冒昧，」稍后我开口道；「您*可曾*见到一块怀表——金质的小表，镶着珍珠的？」

「天哪！」幻象满怀同情地说，「多可惜！您敢肯定它是掉在这儿了么？」

「我身上似乎不见它的踪影，」我答道，话玄而句句属实。我皱着眉头，浑身上下摸索口袋。「方才我们又恰好在这里停过——」

===Original===
I looked inquiringly into the water.

“From this side,” observed the Vision, impersonally, “there is less glare from the brook.”

===Chinese===
我探询地往水里望去。

「从这一边看，」幻象以事不关己的口吻评点道，「溪水的反光要小一些。」

===Original===
Having tied Guendolen to a swinging limb, I sat down contentedly in these woods. The Vision moved a little, lest I be crowded.

“It might be further up the road,” she suggested.

===Chinese===
我把格温多琳拴上一条摇曳的横枝，便心满意足地在这些树中间坐了下来。幻象往旁边挪了挪，惟恐挤着了我。

「表兴许掉在更靠前头的路上，」她提示道。

===Original===
“Oh, I must have left it at the hotel,” I observed.

“You might look—” said she, peering into the water.

“Forever!” I assented.

===Chinese===
「哦，那我准是把它落在旅馆里了，」我说。

「您可以找找看——」她说着，眼睛往水里端详。

「找上一辈子！」我欣然应道。

===Original===
The Vision flushed, “I didn’t mean—” she began.

“But I did,” quoth I—“and every word of it.”

===Chinese===
幻象红了脸。「我不是那个意思——」她开口道。

「我可是那个意思，」我道——「而且字字当真。」

===Original===
“Why, in that case,” said she, and rose to her feet, “I’d better—” A frown wrinkled her brow; then a deep, curved dimple performed a similar office for her cheek. “I wonder—” said she.

===Chinese===
「嗐，既然如此，」她说着，站起身来，「我还是——」一道蹙纹拢上她的眉心；随即一记深深的、弯弯的酒涡，又给她面颊当起了同样的差。「我纳闷——」她说。

===Original===
“Why, you would be a boldfaced jig,” said I, composedly; “but, after all there is nobody about. And, besides—for I suspect you of being one of the three dilapidated persons in veils who came last night—we are going to be introduced right after supper, anyway.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，您这岂不成了个厚脸皮的轻佻丫头，」我从容自若地说；「不过话说回来，四下里一个人也没有。再说——因为我疑心您正是昨儿夜里那三位蒙面纱、形容狼狈的人物之一——横竖晚饭一过，自会有人把我们引见。」

===Original===
The Vision sat down. “You mentioned your sanatorium?” quoth she.

“The Asylum of Love,” said I; “discharged—under a false impression—as cured, and sent to paradise.

===Chinese===
幻象坐了回去。「您方才提到您的疗养院？」她道。

「爱情疯人院，」我说；「业已获准出院——出于一场误会——认定为痊愈，并发往天堂。」

===Original===
“Oh!” said I, defiant, “but it *is*!”

===Chinese===
「哦！」我说，语气不服，「可这里*就是*！」

===Original===
She looked about her. “The woods *are* rather beautiful,” she conceded, softly.

“They form a quite appropriate background,” said I. “It is a veritable Eden, before the coming of the snake.”

===Chinese===
她环顾四周。「这林子*确实*秀美，」她柔声让步道。

「它们构成一方再相宜不过的背景，」我说。「这里是一座十足地道的伊甸园，时在蛇到来之前。」

===Original===
“Before?” she queried, dubiously.

“Undoubtedly,” said I, and felt my ribs, in meditative wise. “Ah, but I thought I missed something! We participate in a historic moment. This is in Eden immediately after the creation of—Well, but of course you are acquainted with that famous bull about Eve’s being the fairest of her daughters?”

===Chinese===
「之前？」她将信将疑地问。

「毫无疑问，」我说，一面若有所思地摸了摸自己的肋骨。「啊，我说怎么方才觉得缺了点什么！我们正躬逢一个历史性的时刻。此刻身在伊甸园中，正是那位——嗯，不过，『夏娃是她女儿们当中最美的一位』，这句著名的悖谬妙语，您自然是听说过的？」

===Original===
“It is *quite* time,” said she, judicially, “for me to go back to the hotel, before—since we are speaking of animals—your presence here is noticed by one of the squirrels.”

===Chinese===
「*实在*到时候了，」她以法官断案的口吻说，「我该回旅馆去了，赶在——既然我们正谈论动物——您在此地的行藏，被哪一只松鼠察觉之前。」

===Original===
“It is not good,” I pleaded, “for man to be alone.”

“I have heard,” said she, “that—almost anyone can cite scripture to his purpose.”

===Chinese===
「那人独居不好，」我央告道。

「我听说过，」她说，「——几乎随便什么人，都能把圣经引得合乎自己的目的。」

===Original===
I thrust out a foot for inspection. “No suggestion of a hoof,” said I; “and not the slightest odour of brimstone, as you will kindly note; and my inoffensive name is Robert Townsend.”

===Chinese===
我伸出一只脚来，供人查验。「毫无蹄子的嫌疑，」我说；「也绝无一丝硫磺的气味，敬希明鉴；至于在下这无害的姓名，乃是罗伯特·汤森。」

===Original===
“Of course,” she submitted, “I could never think of making your acquaintance in this irregular fashion; and, therefore, of course, I could not think of telling you that my name is Marian Winwood.”

“Of course not,” I agreed; “it would be highly improper.”

===Chinese===
「自然，」她顺水推舟地说，「我决计想不到以这样不合规矩的方式同您结交；因此呢，我自然也决计想不到告诉您，我的名字叫玛丽安·温伍德（Marian Winwood）。」

「自然想不到，」我表示赞同；「那可大大有失体统。」

===Original===
“—And it is more than time for me to go to supper,” she concluded again, with a lacuna, as it seemed to me, in the deduction.

===Chinese===
「——而且，我去用晚饭的时辰也早已过了头了，」她再度作结道——依我看，这番推论里缺了一环。

===Original===
“Look here!” I remonstrated; “it isn’t anywhere near six yet.” I exhibited my watch to support this statement.

“Oh!” she observed, with wide, indignant eyes.

“I—I mean—” I stammered.

She rose to her feet.

===Chinese===
「您听我说！」我抗议道；「眼下离六点还早得很呢。」我出示怀表，以佐此说。

「哦！」她说，眼睛睁得溜圆，满是嗔意。

「我——我是说——」我结结巴巴。

她站起身来。

===Original===
“—I will explain how I happened to be carrying two watches—”

===Chinese===
「——我愿解释解释，我怎么恰好随身带着两块怀表——」

===Original===
“I do not care to listen to any explanations. Why should I?”

===Chinese===
「我不想听什么解释。凭什么要听？」

===Original===
“—upon,” I firmly said, “the third piazza of the hotel. And this very evening.”

===Chinese===
「——就在，」我斩钉截铁地说，「旅馆三楼的游廊上。就在今天晚上。」

===Original===
“You will not.” And this was said even more firmly. “And I hope you will have the kindness to keep away from these woods; for I shall probably always walk here in the afternoon.” Then, with an indignant toss of the head, the Vision disappeared.

===Chinese===
「您休想。」这一句说得越发斩钉截铁。「还望您行个好，从此躲开这片树林；因为我往后每天下午，多半总要在此地散步。」说罢，幻象嗔然把头一甩，倏忽不见了。

### III / 三

===Original===
I whistled. Subsequently I galloped back to the hotel.

===Chinese===
我打了个呼哨。随后便策马疾驰，奔回旅馆。

===Original===
“See here!” said I, to the desk-clerk; “how long does this place keep open?”

“Season closes latter part of September, sir.”

===Chinese===
「喂，你听着！」我对柜台接待员说；「这地方还开到什么时候？」

「本季度到九月下旬截止，先生。」

===Original===
I told him I would need my rooms till then.

===Chinese===
我告诉他，我的房间要用到那个时候。

## XVII / 十七

He Provides Copy / 他供出了素材

### I / 一

===Original===
So it was Uncle George Bulmer who presently left the Green Chalybeate, to pursue Mrs. Chaytor with his lawless arts. I stayed out the season.

===Chinese===
于是，不多几日便离开绿铁泉的，是乔治·布尔默叔叔——他追着查托太太去了，随身带去他那套无法无天的手段。我则留了下来，把整个避暑季住满。

===Original===
Now I cannot conscientiously recommend the Green Chalybeate against your next vacation. Once very long ago, it was frequented equally for the sake of gaiety and of health. In the summer that was Marian’s the resort was a beautiful and tumbledown place where invalids congregated for the sake of the nauseous waters—which infallibly demolish a solid column of strange maladies I never read quite through, although it bordered every page of the writing-paper you got there from the desk-clerk—and a scanty leaven of persons who came thither, apparently, in order to spend a week or two in lamenting “how very dull the season is this year, and how abominable the fare is.”

===Chinese===
如今，要我凭良心举荐绿铁泉作你下一个度假的去处，我办不到。很久很久以前，人们来此，为寻欢与为养身各占一半。到了属于玛丽安的那个夏天，这处胜地是一派美丽而颓败的光景：病人们为那令人作呕的矿泉水汇聚于此——那水包治不误，能一举摧毁长长一栏稀奇古怪的病症，那一栏我从来不曾读完过，尽管它印满了你在那边从账房领班手里领来的每一页信纸——另有寥寥一撮人，瞧那意思，跑到这里来，专为花上一两个礼拜，哀叹「今年这避暑季多么乏味，伙食又多么可憎」。

===Original===
But for one I praise the place, and I believe that Marian Winwood also bears it no ill-will. For we two were very happy there. We took part in the “subscription euchers” whenever we could not in time devise an excuse which would pass muster with the haggard “entertainer.” We danced conscientiously beneath the pink and green icing of the ballroom’s ceiling, with all three of the band playing “Hearts and Flowers”; and with a dozen “chaperones”—whom I always suspected of taking in washing during the winter months—lined up as closely as was possible to the door, as if in preparation for the hotel’s catching fire any moment, to give us pessimistic observal. And having thus discharged our duty to society at large, we enjoyed ourselves tremendously.

===Chinese===
可是说到我自己，我却要夸这地方一句，而且我相信，玛丽安·温伍德（Marian Winwood）对它也并无恶感。因为我们两个在那里过得非常快活。每逢来不及编出一项哄得过那位形容枯槁的「司仪」的托辞，我们便去参加「认购尤克牌赛」。我们在舞厅顶棚那粉绿相间的糖霜底下，一丝不苟地跳舞，乐队全体三位乐师奏着《心与花》；一打「陪媪」——我一直疑心她们入了冬便靠替人浆洗度日——排得离门越近越好，挨成一列，仿佛随时预备着旅馆起火，好对我们作悲观的检阅。如此这般，对大社会尽完了本分，我们便纵情作乐起来。

===Original===
For instance, we would talk over the book I was going to write in the autumn. That was the main thing. Then one could golf, or drive, or—I blush to write it even now—croquet. Croquet, though, is a much maligned game, as you will immediately discover if you ever play it on the rambling lawn of the Chalybeate, about six in the afternoon, say, when the grass is greener than it is by ordinary, and the shadows are long, and the sun is well beneath the treetops of the Iron Bank, and your opponent makes a face at you occasionally, and on each side the old, one-storied cottages are builded of unusually red bricks and are quite ineffably asleep.

===Chinese===
比如，我们会把我打算在秋天动笔的那部书细细谈论一番。这才是头等大事。然后呢，可以打高尔夫，可以驱车兜风，或者——即便此刻落笔，我还要脸红——打槌球。不过，槌球是一门蒙冤已深的游戏，只要你在绿铁泉那片漫展无际的草坪上打过一回，你立刻便会承认——譬如说，下午六点光景，草色比寻常更绿，影子拖得老长，太阳已经落到铁岸（Iron Bank）的树冠底下去了，你的对手时不时朝你做个鬼脸，两旁那些老式的单层小屋，砌着出奇红的砖，睡得说不出的酣沉。

===Original===
Or again there is always the creek to divert yourself in. Once I caught five crawfishes there, while Marian waited on the bank; and afterward we found an old tomato-can and boiled them in it, and they came out a really gorgeous crimson. This was the afternoon that we were Spanish Inquisitors. … Oh, believe me, you can have quite a good time at the Chalybeate, if you set about it in the proper way.

===Chinese===
再不然，那条小溪也永远可供你消遣。有一回，我在那儿捉到五只小龙虾，玛丽安在岸上守候；随后我们寻着一只旧番茄罐头，把它们煮在里面，煮出来真是一派华丽丽的绯红。那正是我们扮西班牙宗教裁判官的那个下午。……哦，相信我，只要你按正当的路子来办，在绿铁泉也尽可以过上一段好时光。

### II / 二

===Original===
Only it is true that sometimes, when it rained, say, with that hopeless insistency which, I protest, is unknown anywhere else in the world; and when Marian was not immediately accessible, and cigarettes were not quite satisfactory, because the entire universe was so sodden that matches had to be judiciously coaxed before they would strike; and when if you happened to be writing a fervid letter to Rosalind Jemmett, let us say, the ink would not dry for ever so long:—why, it is true that in these circumstances you would feel a shade too like the wicked Lord So-and-So of a melodrama to be comfortable.

===Chinese===
只不过，有时候确也难免如此——譬如说，碰上下雨，那种没救的缠绵劲儿，我敢声辩，普天之下再无第二处见识过；又碰上玛丽安一时寻不着人，烟抽得又不甚惬意，因为整个宇宙湿得那样通透，火柴须得好生哄劝、循循善诱，方肯着火；又碰上——比方说——你正给罗瑟琳·杰米特写一封炽热的情书，那墨水偏生许久不干：——呃，确也难免，处在这般境况里，你会觉得自己活脱脱是情节剧里那位作恶的某某勋爵，像得叫人不大自在。

===Original===
Yet even in these circumstances, reason told me that the Book was the main thing, that the girl would be thoroughly over the affair by November at latest, and that at the cost of a few inconsequent tears, she would have meanwhile immeasurably obliged posterity. And I knew that no man may ever write in perdurable fashion save by ruthlessly converting his own life into “copy,” since of other persons’ lives he can, at most, reproduce but the blurred and misinterpreted by-ends, by reason of almost any author’s deplorable lack of omniscience. Yes, the Book was the main thing; and yet the girl—knowingly to dip my pen into her heart as into an inkstand was not, at best, chivalric. …

===Chinese===
然而即便在这般境况里，理智仍旧告诉我：那部书才是头等大事；到十一月，至迟到十一月，这姑娘准会把这段情事彻底熬过去；况且只消赔上几滴无关宏旨的眼泪，她便同时给后世帮了一个无可估量的大忙。我也知道，人要想写出传之久远的文字，唯有狠起心肠，把自己的生活炼成「稿件素材」，因为旁人的生活，他至多只能复现其中模糊的、遭人曲解的枝节——缘由在于，几乎任何一位作家，都可悲地缺乏全知全能。是啊，那部书才是头等大事；可是那位姑娘——存心把笔尖浸进她的心，如同浸进一只墨水瓶，这无论往好里说，也算不得骑士风范。……

===Original===
“But the Book!” said I. “Why, I must be quite idiotically in love to think of letting that Book perish!” And I viciously added: “Confound the pretty simpleton!” …

===Chinese===
「可是那部书！」我说。「嗐，我准是害了失心疯的相思，才会起念头让那部书夭折！」接着我又恶狠狠补上一句：「这漂亮的小傻瓜，真该死！」……

### III / 三

===Original===
So the book was builded, after all, a little by a little. Hardly an evening came when after leaving Marian I had not at least one excellent and pregnant jotting to record in my notebook. Now it would be just an odd turn of language, or a description of some gesture she had made, or of a gown she had worn that day; and now a simile or some other rather good figure of speech which had popped into my mind when I was making love to her.

===Chinese===
于是，那部书终究一点一点地垒了起来。几乎夜夜如此：离开玛丽安之后，我的笔记本里总添得上至少一条精彩而富于孕意的札记。这一回，也许只是她说话的一个别致腔调，或是描下她某个手势、她当天穿的某条裙子；下一回，又是一个明喻，或旁的什么颇为高妙的修辞，是我在向她求爱时冷不丁蹦进脑子里的。

===Original===
Nor had I any difficulty in preserving nearly all she said to me, for Marian was never a chatterbox; yet her responses had, somehow, that long-sought tang it wasn’t in me to invent for any imaginary young woman who must be, for the sake of my new novel, quite heels over head in love.

===Chinese===
她对我说的话，我也几乎句句留得住，毫不费力，因为玛丽安从来不是个碎嘴子；可她那些应对，不知怎的，自有一股我寻觅已久的韵味——要我凭空捏造，是万万捏不出的，哪怕对象是我那部新小说里那位务须爱得神魂颠倒的虚构小姐。

===Original===
And I began to see that Bettie was right, as usual. I had portrayed Gillian Hardress pretty well in *Afield*; but by and large, I had always written about women as though they were “pterodactyls or some other extinct animal, which you had never seen, but had read a lot about.”

And now, in looking over my notes, I knew, and my heart glowed to know, that I was not about to repeat the error.

===Chinese===
我也渐渐看出，贝蒂说得对，一如既往地对。在《旷野》（*Afield*）里，我把吉莲·哈德雷斯描摹得还算像样；可大体而论，我写起女人来，向来只当她们是「翼手龙，或是旁的什么绝了种的动物，你从来没见过，只读过许多关于它们的书」。

而此刻，翻检着我的札记，我知道——知道得心头发热——我眼看不会再重蹈这个覆辙了。

===Original===
So the Book was builded, after all, a little by a little. And a little by a little the summer wore on; and in the lobby of the Main Hotel was hung the beautiful Spirit of the Falls poster of the Buffalo Exposition; and we talked of Oom Paul Krüger, and Shamrock II, and the Nicaragua Canal, and lanky Bob Fitzsimmons, and the Boxer outrages; and we read *To Have and to Hold* and *The Cardinal’s Snuff Box*, and thought it droll that the King of England was not going to call himself King Albert, after all.

===Chinese===
于是，那部书终究一点一点地垒了起来。夏天也一寸一寸地磨蚀下去；大旅馆（Main Hotel）的门厅里，挂起了布法罗博览会那张漂亮的《瀑布之灵》海报；我们谈论保罗·克吕格尔大叔（Oom Paul Krüger），谈论「三叶草二号」，谈论尼加拉瓜运河、瘦长条儿的鲍勃·菲茨西蒙斯，还有义和团的暴行；我们读《执手偕老》（*To Have and to Hold*）和《红衣主教的鼻烟盒》（*The Cardinal’s Snuff Box*），还觉得英国国王到头来不打算自称阿尔伯特国王，这事怪有趣味。

===Original===
And then came the news of how the President had been shot, “with a poisoned bullet,” and a week of contradictory bulletins from the Milburn House in Buffalo. And there were panicky surmises raised everywhere as to “what these anarchists may do next,” so that Maggio was mobbed in Columbus, and Emma Goldman in Chicago; and Colonel Roosevelt was found, after days of search, on Mt. Marcy in the Adirondacks, and was told in the heart of a forest that tomorrow he would be at the head of a nation. And the country’s guidance was entrusted to a mere lad of forty-three, with general uneasiness as to what might come of it; and the dramatic tale of Colonel Roosevelt’s taking of the oath of office was in that morning’s paper; and Marian and I were about to part.

===Chinese===
随后便传来了总统遇刺的消息——「中的是一颗毒弹」——接着是布法罗米尔本宅（Milburn House）里发出的一整个礼拜自相矛盾的病情公告。四面八方都掀起了惶惶的揣测，猜「这帮无政府主义者下一步会干出什么」，于是马焦（Maggio）在哥伦布市遭了围攻，爱玛·戈德曼（Emma Goldman）在芝加哥也遭了围攻；寻了多日，人们才在阿迪朗达克山中的马西峰上寻着罗斯福上校，在密林深处告诉他：明天，他便要执掌一个国家了。国家的舵柄，就这样交到一位才四十三岁的小子手里，举国惴惴，不知往后会闹出什么来；罗斯福上校宣誓就职的那段富于戏剧性的报导，就印在那天的晨报上；而我同玛丽安，也就要分手了。

### IV / 四

===Original===
“It will be dreadful,” sighed she; “for we have to stay a whole week longer, and I shall come here every afternoon. And there will be only ghosts in the woods, and I shall be very lonely.”

“Dear,” said I, “is it not something to have been happy? It has been such a wonderful summer; and come what may, nothing can rob us now of its least golden moment. And it is only for a little.”

===Chinese===
「那可真要命，」她叹道；「因为我们还得再待上整整一个礼拜，而我天天下午都会到这儿来。树林里就只剩些鬼魂了，我会非常孤单的。」

「亲爱的，」我说，「曾快活过一场，这难道不算一桩收获？这个夏天何其美妙；而且不管怎样，如今谁也抢不走它最微小的一寸黄金时光了。再说，分别不过短短一晌。」

===Original===
“You will come back?” said she, half-doubtingly.

“Yes,” I said. “You wonderful, elfin creature, I shall undoubtedly come back—to your real home, and claim you there. Only I don’t believe you do live in Aberlin—you probably live in some great, gnarled oak hereabouts; and at night its bark uncloses to set you free, and you and your sisters dance out the satyrs’ hearts in the moonlight. Oh, I know, Marian! I simply *know* you are a dryad—a wonderful, laughing, clear-eyed dryad strayed out of the golden age.”

===Chinese===
「你会回来的吧？」她说，将信将疑。

「会的，」我说。「你这奇妙的、小精灵似的人儿，我准定会回来——回到你真正的家，在那儿把你认领。只是我不信你当真住在阿伯林（Aberlin）——你多半就住在这一带某棵虬结的老橡树里；夜里，树皮张开，放你出来，你同你的姐妹们在月光底下跳舞，把萨堤洛斯们的心都跳丢了。哦，我知道，玛丽安！我*就是知道*，你是一位德律阿得斯（dryad）——一位了不起的、爱笑的、目光澄澈的树中仙女，从黄金时代里走散出来的。」

===Original===
“What a boy it is!” she said. “No, I am only a really and truly girl, dear—a rather frightened girl, with very little disposition to laughter, just now. For you are going away—Oh, my dear, you have meant so much to me! The world is so different since you have come, and I am so happy and so miserable that—that I am afraid.” An infinitesimal handkerchief went upward to two great, sparkling eyes, and dabbed at them.

“Dear!” said I. And this remark appeared to meet the requirements of the situation.

===Chinese===
「这孩子气的一个人啊！」她说。「不，我不过是一个如假包换的姑娘，亲爱的——一个此刻相当害怕的姑娘，眼下简直没什么心思发笑。因为你要走了——哦，亲爱的，你于我的意义何其重大！自从你来了，世界全然换了个样，我又这样快活、又这样痛苦，快活得——痛苦得——叫我害怕。」一方小得不能再小的手帕升上去，凑到两只盈盈闪光的大眼睛上，轻轻沾了沾。

「亲爱的！」我说。而这一句，看来正好应付了眼前的场面。

===Original===
There was a silence now. We sat in the same spot where I had first encountered Marian Winwood. Only this was an autumnal forest that glowed with many gem-like hues about us; and already the damp odour of decaying leaves was heavy in the air. It was like the Tosti thing translated out of marine terms into a woodland analogue. The summer was ended; but *As the Coming of Dawn* was practically complete.

It was not the book that I had planned, but a far greater one which was scarcely mine. There was no word written as yet. But for two months I had viewed life through Marian Winwood’s eyes; day by day, my half-formed, tentative ideas had been laid before her with elaborate fortuitousness, to be approved, or altered, or rejected, just as she decreed; until at last they had been welded into a perfect whole that was a Book, bit by bit, we had planned it, I and she; and, as I dreamed of it as it would be in print, my brain was fired with exultation, and I defied my doubt and I swore that the Book, for which I had pawned a certain portion of my self-respect, was worth—and triply worth—the price which had been paid. … This was in Marian’s absence.

===Chinese===
这时是一阵沉默。我们坐在我初遇玛丽安·温伍德的那个老地方。只是此刻，四下里已是一座秋日的森林，闪烁着种种宝石般的色泽；腐叶的潮气已然沉沉浮在空中。这就像托斯蒂（Tosti）的那支歌，从海洋的辞句里译出，换成了树林里的对应物。夏天结束了；而《恰似黎明降临》（*As the Coming of Dawn*）也差不多大功告成。

它不是我原先谋划的那部书，而是一部远为伟大的书——伟大得几乎算不得我的。书里还一个字没写。可是整整两个月，我一直透过玛丽安·温伍德的眼睛观看人生；我那些半成形、试着步的构想，日复一日，煞费苦心地装作出于偶然，摆到她面前，听凭她发落：认可，修改，或者否决；直到它们终于锻造成一个完美的整体——一部书。一点一点地，我们把这部书谋划了出来，我，和她；而当我梦想着它印成铅字的模样，我的头脑便燃起一阵狂喜，我向我的疑心叫阵，我赌咒说，这部书——我为它典当了自己自尊里的一份——值这笔代价，值三倍这笔代价。……——而这，是在玛丽安不在跟前的时候。

===Original===
“Dear!” said she. …

Her eyes were filled with a tender and unutterable confidence that thrilled me like physical cold. “Marian,” said I, simply, “I shall never come back.”

The eyes widened a trifle, but she did not seem to comprehend.

===Chinese===
「亲爱的！」她说。……

她的眼里盛满一种温柔而不可名状的信任，叫我浑身一激灵，像受了实打实的寒气。「玛丽安，」我平平常常地说，「我永远不会回来了。」

那双眼睛微微睁大了些，可她似乎并没有听懂。

===Original===
“Have you not wondered,” said I, “that I have never kissed you, except as if you were a very holy relic or a cousin or something of that sort?”

“Yes,” she answered. Her voice was quite emotionless.

===Chinese===
「你难道不曾纳闷过么，」我说，「我从来不曾吻过你——除了那样一种吻法，仿佛你是一件极神圣的圣髑，或者一位表亲，或者诸如此类的什么？」

「纳闷过，」她答道。她的声音平静得没有一丝波澜。

===Original===
“And yet—yet—” I sprang to my feet. “Dear God, how I have longed! Yesterday, only yesterday, as I read to you from the verses I had made to other women, those women that are colourless shadows by the side of your vivid beauty—and you listened wonderingly and said the proper things and then lapsed into dainty boredom—*how* I longed to take you in my arms, and to quicken your calm blood a little with another sort of kissing. You knew—you must have known! Last night, for instance—”

“Last night,” she said, very simply, “I thought—And I hoped you would.”

===Chinese===
「可是——可是——」我霍地跳了起来。「亲爱的上帝啊，我是怎样地渴望过！就在昨天，就在昨天，我为你诵读我写给别的女子的诗篇——比起你鲜活的明艳，那些女子只是些褪了色的影子——你听着，满脸惊奇，说着得体的话，随后便陷入一种精致的倦怠——我*何等*渴望把你搂进怀里，用另一种吻，让你平静的血液稍稍加快一点。你知道的——你一定知道的！就拿昨夜来说——」

「昨夜，」她说，说得非常简单，「我想过——我也盼过你会的。」

===Original===
“What a confession for a nicely brought up girl! Well! I didn’t. And afterward, all night, I tossed in sick, fevered dreams of you. I am mad for love of you. And so, once in a while I kiss your hand. Dear God, your hand!” My voice quavered, effectively.

“Yes,” said she; “still, I remember—”

===Chinese===
「一位家教良好的姑娘，竟作这样的自白！好啦！我没有。而之后整整一夜，我在关于你的病态而滚烫的梦里翻来覆去。我为对你的爱发了狂。所以，我只是隔三差五，吻一吻你的手。亲爱的上帝啊，你的手！」我的嗓音颤抖起来，颤得恰如其分。

「是啊，」她说；「不过，我还记得——」

===Original===
“I have struggled; and I have conquered this madness—for a madness it is. We can laugh together and be excellent friends; and we can never, never be anything more. Well! we have laughed, have we not, dear, a whole summer through? Now comes the ending. Ah, I have seen you puzzling over my meaning before this. You never understood me thoroughly; but it is always safe to laugh.”

She smiled; and I remember now it was rather as Mona Lisa smiles.

===Chinese===
「我挣扎过；我也征服了这疯狂——因为这确是疯狂。我们可以一同欢笑，做极好的朋友；而我们永远、永远不能超出这个。好啦！我们笑过了，不是么，亲爱的，笑了一整个夏天？如今，结局到了。啊，我早先就见你为我的用意纳闷过。你从来不曾彻底懂过我；不过，笑，总归是万全的。」

她微微一笑；如今回想起来，那笑法很有些像蒙娜丽莎（Mona Lisa）。

===Original===
“For we can laugh together—that is all. We are not mates. You were born to be the wife of a strong man and the mother of his sturdy children; and you and your sort will inherit the earth and make the laws for us weaklings who dream and scribble and paint. We are not mates. But you have been very kind to me, Marian dear. So I thank you and say goodbye; and I pray that I may never see you after today.”

There was a sub-tang of veracity in my deprecation of an unasked-for artistic temperament; the thing is very often a nuisance, and was just then a barrier which I perceived plainly; and with equal plainness I perceived the pettier motives that now caused me to point it out as a barrier to Marian. My lips curled half in mockery of myself, as I framed the bitter smile I felt the situation demanded; but I was fired with the part I was playing; and half-belief had crept into my mind that Marian Winwood was created, chiefly, for the purpose which she had already served.

===Chinese===
「因为我们能一同欢笑——仅此而已。我们不是同路人。你生来要做一位强者的妻子，做他那些结实儿女的母亲；你和你这一类人，将承受地土，为我们这些做梦的、涂写的、画画儿的弱者立法。我们不是同路人。可你一直待我极好，亲爱的玛丽安。所以我谢过你，道一声别；我祈求上天，让我今日之后永远不再见你。」

我这番对自己那份没人讨要的艺术家脾气的贬抑，其中倒也掺着一丝真实的余味；这东西十有八九是个累赘，而彼时彼刻正是一道屏障，这一点我看得明明白白；同样明明白白，我也看见驱使我向玛丽安把它指认为屏障的，是那些更琐小的动机。我牵起半边嘴角，半是自嘲，勾勒出我认定这场面所需的那副苦笑；可我正为我扮演的角色烧得发热；一种半信半疑的念头，已经悄悄爬进我心里：玛丽安·温伍德之被造出来，主要就是为了她已经派上的那个用场。

===Original===
I regarded her, in fine, as through the eyes of future readers of my biography. She would represent an episode in my life, as others do in that of Byron or of Goethe. I pitied her sincerely; and, under all, what moralists would call my lower nature, held in leash for two months past, chuckled, and grinned, and leaped, at the thought of a holiday.

She rose to her feet. “Goodbye,” said she.

“You—you understand, dear?” I queried, tenderly.

===Chinese===
归根结底，我是隔着后世读者的眼睛在打量她——后世那些要读我传记的读者。她将构成我生平中的一段插曲，正如别的女子在拜伦（Byron）或歌德（Goethe）的生平中各占一段。我诚心诚意地怜悯她；而在这一切底下，我的——照道德家的说法——下等天性，那条被皮带拴了两个月的东西，一想到假期，便低声窃笑，龇牙咧嘴，蹦跳起来。

她站起身来。「别了，」她说。

「你——你明白么，亲爱的？」我温存地问。

===Original===
“Yes,” she answered; “I understand—not what you have just told me, for in that, of course, you have lied. That Jemmett girl and her money is at the bottom of it all, of course. You didn’t want to lose her, and still you wanted to play with me. So you were pulled two ways, poor dear.”

“Oh, well, if that is what you think of me—!”

“You see, you are not an uncommon type—a type not strong enough to live life healthily, just strong enough to dabble in life, to trifle with emotions, to experiment with other people’s lives. Indeed, I am not angry, dear; I am only—sorry; for you have played with me very nicely indeed, and very boyishly, and the summer has been very happy.”

===Chinese===
「明白，」她答道；「我明白——不是明白你方才告诉我的那套话，因为在那套话里，你自然是撒了谎。那位杰米特家的小姐，还有她的钱，自然才是一切的根底。你不想失掉她，却又想同我玩耍。于是你被两头拉扯着，可怜的亲爱的。」

「哦，也罢，你既然这样看我——！」

「你要知道，你这样的人并不罕见——这一类人，不够强健，无力健全地过日子，却又刚刚够强，能在人生里趟趟水，把感情当儿戏，拿旁人的性命做实验。说真的，我并不生气，亲爱的；我只是——难过；因为你同我玩耍，玩得实在漂亮，十足的孩子气，而这个夏天，也一直很快活。」

### V / 五

===Original===
I returned to Lichfield and wrote *As the Coming of Dawn*.

I spent six months in this. My work at first was mere copying of the book that already existed in my brain; but when it was transcribed therefrom, I wrote and rewrote, shifted and polished and adorned until it seemed I would never have done; and indeed I was not anxious to have done with any labour so delightful.

===Chinese===
我回到利奇菲尔德，写出了《恰似黎明降临》。

这件事我花了六个月。起初，我的工作不过是誊写那部早已存在于我头脑里的书；可等它誊完，我便写了又写，改了又改，腾挪、打磨、装点，直弄到仿佛永远脱不了稿；而且说实在的，同这样一桩甘美的劳作，我原本也不急于了断。

===Original===
Particularly did I rejoice in the character for which Marian Winwood had posed. Last summer’s notebook here came into play; and now, for once, my heroine was in no need of either shoving or prompting. She did things of her own accord, and I was merely her scribe …

I would vaingloriously protest, just to myself, that the love scenes in this story were the most exquisite and, with all that, the most genuine love scenes I knew of anywhere. “By God!” I would occasionally say with Thackeray; “I *am* a genius!”

===Chinese===
尤其叫我得意的是玛丽安·温伍德充任模特的那个人物。去年夏天的那本札记簿，此时派上了用场；而这一回，破天荒地，我的女主人公既不用推搡，也不用提词。她自作主张地行事，我不过是她的书记……

我会扬扬自得地抗议——只对自己抗议——说这个故事里的爱情场面，是我平生所知最精妙的，而且，尽管这般精妙，也是最真挚的爱情场面。「上帝作证！」我不时要学一句萨克雷（Thackeray）；「我*果然*是个天才！」

===Original===
Besides, the story of the book, I knew, was novel and astutely wrought; its progress caught at once and teased your interest always, so that having begun it, most people would read to the end, if only to discover “how it all came out.” I knew the book, in fine, could hardly fail to please and interest a number of people by reason of its plot alone.

I ought to have been content with this. But I had somehow contracted an insane notion that a novel is the more enjoyable when it is adroitly written. In point of fact, of course, no man who writes with care is ever read with pleasure; you may toil through a page or two perhaps, but presently you are noting how precisely every word is fitted to the thought, and later you are noting nothing else. You are insensibly beguiled into a fidgety-footed analysis of every clause, which fatigues in the outcome, and by the tenth page you are yawning.

But I did not comprehend this then. And so I fashioned my apt phrases, and weighed my synonyms, and echoed this or that vowel very skilfully, I thought, and alliterated my consonants with discretion. In fine, I did not overlook the most meticulous device of the stylist; and I enjoyed it. It was a sort of game; and they taught me at least, those six delightful months, that a man writes admirable prose not at all for the sake of having it read, but for the more sensible reason that he enjoys playing solitaire.

===Chinese===
再者，我知道这部书的故事立意新颖、编织机巧；它的进展一上来便攫住你，又始终撩拨着你的兴味，以致凡是开了头的人，多半会一气读到末尾，哪怕只为查明「这一切到头来是怎样的结局」。总而言之，我知道这部书，单凭情节，便几乎万无一失，能讨得一批人的欢心、勾起一批人的兴味。

我本该以此满足了。可我不晓得怎么，染上了一个发疯的念头，以为小说写得越精巧，读来便越有味。事实的真相当然是：凡字斟句酌的作者，从来没有人读得快乐；你或许还能苦撑一两页，可不消多时，你便开始留意每个字同意思扣得何等严丝合缝，再往后，你留意的便只剩这个了。你不知不觉被诱进一种坐立不安的分析里，逐个从句地解剖下去，到头来只落得疲乏，读到第十页，你已经在打哈欠了。

可我当时不明白这个。于是我锻造我熨帖的词句，掂量我的同义词，自以为元音的呼应运用得极见功夫，辅音的头韵也安排得极有分寸。总而言之，文体家最一丝不苟的种种机关，我没有一样漏掉；而我乐在其中。这是一种游戏；而那六个月的美妙光阴至少教会了我：一个人写出可钦佩的散文，压根不是为了叫人读，倒是出于一个更明白事理的缘由——他玩单人纸牌戏，玩得高兴。

===Original===
I led a hermit’s life that winter; and I enjoyed that too. Night, after all, is the one time for writing, particularly when you are inane enough to hanker after perfected speech, and so misguided as to be the slave of the “right word.” You sit alone in a bright, comfortable room; the clock ticks companionably; there is no other sound in the world except the constant scratching of your pen, and the occasional far-off puffing of a freight-train coming into Lichfield; there is snow outside, but before your eyes someone, that is not you exactly, arranges and redrills the scrawls which will bring back the sweet and languid summer and remarshal all its pleasant trivialities for anyone that chooses to read through the printed page, although he read two centuries hence, in Nova Zembla. …

===Chinese===
那个冬天，我过着隐士的日子；这我也喜欢。说到底，夜里才是写作的唯一时辰，尤其当你蠢到渴慕圆熟的语言，又迷误到甘做「恰切字眼」的奴隶。你独自坐在一间明亮舒适的屋子里；时钟滴答，是个好伴；世上别无声息，只有你笔尖不停的沙沙，间或有一列货车驶进利奇菲尔德，在远处噗噗喘气；窗外是雪，可是在你眼前，有一个人——不恰好是你的那个人——正把那些潦草的字迹重新整练成行，它们将唤回那个甜美而慵懒的夏天，为任何一个肯把这印成的书页读下去的人，把它全部可爱的琐事重新集结列队——哪怕他读到时已是两个世纪之后，身在新地岛（Nova Zembla）。……

===Original===
Then you dip into an Unabridged, and change every word that has been written, for a better one, and do it leisurely, rolling in the mouth, as it were, the flavour of every possible synonym, before decision. Then you reread, with a corrective pen in hand the while, and you venture upon the whole to agree with Mérimée that it is preferable to write one’s own books, since those of others are not, after all, particularly worth reading in comparison.

And by this time the windows are pale blue, like the blue of a dying flame, and you peep out and see the sparrows moving like rather poorly made mechanical toys about the middle of the deserted street, where there is neither light nor shade. The colour of everything is perfectly discernible, but there is no lustre in the world as yet, though yonder the bloat sun is already visible in the blue and red east, which is like a cosmic bruise; and upon a sudden you find it just possible to stay awake long enough to get safely into bed. …

===Chinese===
然后你翻开一部足本大词典，把写下的每个字都改换成更好的一个字，改得从容不迫，仿佛把每一个备选同义词的滋味，都先在嘴里含一遍，再下决断。然后你重读，手里始终握着一支批改的笔，而大抵算来，你倾向于同意梅里美（Mérimée）：还是写自己的书更要紧，因为旁人的书，两相比较之下，终究不特别值得一读。

而到这时，窗子已泛出淡蓝，像一朵将熄火焰的蓝；你探头外望，看见麻雀在空街的当心挪动，活像一批做工拙劣的机械玩具——那条街上无光也无影。样样东西的颜色都辨别得清清楚楚，可世上还没有一丝光泽，虽然那边，浮肿的太阳已经在蓝红两色的东天里露了头，那东天像一块宇宙级的瘀伤；冷不丁地，你发现当真还撑得住，刚好够清醒着爬进床里去。……

### VI / 六

===Original===
Thus I dandled the child of my brain for a long while, and arrayed it in beautiful and curious garments, adorning each beloved notion with far-sought words that had a taste in the mouth, and would one day lend an aroma to the printed page; and I rejoiced shamelessly in that which I had done. Then it befell that I went forth and sought the luxury of a Turkish bath, and in the morning, after a rubdown and an ammonia cocktail, awoke to the fact that the world had been going on much as usual, that winter.

Young Colonel Roosevelt seemed not to have wrecked civilization, after all, according to the morning *Courier-Herald*, despite that Democratic paper’s colorful prophecies last autumn in the vein of Jeremiah. To the contrary, Major-General McArthur was testifying before the Senate as to the abysmal unfitness of the Filipinos for self-government; the Women’s Clubs were holding a convention in Los Angeles; there had been terrible hailstorms this year to induce the annual ruining of the peach-crop, and the submarine *Fulton* had exploded; the *California Limited* had been derailed in Iowa, and in Memphis there was some sort of celebration in honor of Admiral Schley; and the Boer War seemed over; and Mr. Havemeyer also was before the Senate, to whom he was making it clear that his companies were in no wise responsible for sugar having reached the unprecedentedly high price of four and a half cents a pound.

The world, in short, in spite of my six months’ retiring therefrom, seemed to be getting on pleasantly enough, as I turned from the paper to face the six months’ accumulation of mail.

===Chinese===
就这样，我把这个我头脑的孩子搂在怀里逗弄了许久，给它穿上美丽而奇特的衣裳，用千辛万苦寻来的、口里含得有滋味的字眼，装点每一个心爱的意念——这些字眼，有朝一日会给印成的书页添上一缕芳香；我为自己所做的一切，无耻地欢欣鼓舞。后来出了这样一桩事：我出门去享受了一次土耳其浴，第二天早晨，一番搓擦、一杯氨水鸡尾酒下肚，我醒悟过来：这个冬天，世界原来一直在照常运转。

据当天早晨的《信使先驱报》（*Courier-Herald*）说，年轻的罗斯福上校终究似乎不曾把文明搞垮——尽管去年秋天，这家民主党的报纸满纸耶利米（Jeremiah）式的花花绿绿的预言。恰恰相反：麦克阿瑟少将正在参议院作证，证明菲律宾人全然不配自治，不配到了深不见底的地步；妇女俱乐部们正在洛杉矶开大会；今年已降过几场可怕的雹灾，照例促成了桃子收成一年一度的毁灭；「富尔顿号」潜艇炸了；「加州特快号」列车在艾奥瓦出了轨；孟菲斯那边，为向施莱海军上将致敬，正办着某种庆典；布尔战争看似收场了；哈弗迈耶先生也在参议院里，正竭力澄清：糖价涨到每磅四分半钱这史无前例的高位，同他的几家公司绝无半点干系。

总而言之，尽管我从这世界上退隐了六个月，它看来照旧过得相当滋润——当我从报纸上抬起眼，面对那积攒了六个月的一堆邮件时。

### VII / 七

===Original===
A few weeks later, I sent for Mr. George Bulmer, and informed him of his avuncular connection with a genius; and waved certain typewritten pages to establish his title.

Subsequently I read aloud divers portions of *As the Coming of Dawn*, and Mr. Bulmer sipped Chianti, and listened.

===Chinese===
几个礼拜后，我差人请来乔治·布尔默先生，向他通报：他如今同一位天才沾上了叔侄的渊源；还挥着几页打字稿，以确立他这份名分。

随后，我高声诵读《恰似黎明降临》的若干段落，布尔默先生一边啜着基安蒂酒（Chianti），一边听。

===Original===
“Look here!” he said, suddenly; “have you seen *The Imperial Votaress*?”

I frowned. It is always annoying to be interrupted in the middle of a particularly well-balanced sentence. “Don’t know the lady,” said I.

“She is advertised on half the posters in town,” said Mr. Bulmer. “And it is the book of the year. And it is your book.”

===Chinese===
「喂！」他猛不丁地说；「你看过《帝国女祭司》（*The Imperial Votaress*）没有？」

我皱起眉头。在一句安排得格外匀称的句子正中间被人打断，终究叫人恼火。「不认识这位女士，」我说。

「城里半数的海报都在替她做广告，」布尔默先生说。「而且它是年度之书。而且，它是你的书。」

===Original===
At this moment I laid down my manuscript. “I *beg* your pardon?” said I.

“Your book!” Uncle George repeated firmly; “and scarcely a hair’s difference between them, except in the names.”

“H’m!” I observed, in a careful voice. “Who wrote it?”

“Some female woman out west,” said Mr. Bulmer. “She’s a George Something-or-other when she publishes, of course, like all those authorines when they want to say about mankind at large what less gifted women only dare say about their sisters-in-law. I wish to heaven they would pick out some other Christian name when they want to cut up like pagans. Anyhow, I saw her real name somewhere, and I remember it began with an *S*—Why, to be sure! it’s Marian Winwood.”

“Amaimon sounds well,” I observed; “Lucifer, well; Larbason, well; yet they are devils’ additions, the names of fiends: but—Marian Winwood!”

“Dear me!” he remonstrated. “Why, she wrote *A Bright Particular Star*, you know, and *The Acolytes*, and lots of others.”

The author of *As the Coming of Dawn* swallowed a whole glass of Chianti at a gulp.

===Chinese===
这时我放下了手稿。「我*请您*再说一遍？」我说。

「你的书！」乔治叔叔斩钉截铁地重复道；「两下里几乎连一根头发丝的差别都没有，除了人名。」

「唔！」我用一种字斟句酌的声口说。「谁写的？」

「西部那边的一个女人家，」布尔默先生说。「她出书的时候，署的自然是『乔治某某』，跟那一票女文豪一个路数——待到要对着全人类信口开河，说些连天分差些的女人只敢拿来讲自家妯娌的话，便都用这等名号。她们既要撒野撒得像个异教徒，我只求上天开恩，教她们另挑一个教名。反正，我在什么地方见过她的真名，记得打头是个 *S*——咦，可不是嘛！是玛丽安·温伍德。」

「亚迈蒙听着响亮，」我说；「路西法，响亮；拉巴森，也响亮；可这些都是魔鬼的添头、妖孽的名号：然而——玛丽安·温伍德！」

「哎呀呀！」他规劝道。「她写过《明亮而独特的星》（*A Bright Particular Star*）的，你知道，还有《侍僧们》（*The Acolytes*），还有好些别的。」

《恰似黎明降临》的作者，把满满一杯基安蒂酒一仰而尽。

===Original===
“Of course,” I said, slowly, “I cannot, in my rather peculiar position, run the risk of being charged with plagiarism—by a Chinese-eyed mental sneak-thief. …”

Thereupon I threw the manuscript into the open fire, which my preference for the picturesque rendered necessary, even in May.

===Chinese===
「当然，」我慢慢地说，「处在我这相当微妙的地位上，我可担不起被人指控剽窃的风险——被一个眯缝眼的文贼。……」

说罢，我把手稿扔进了敞开的炉火——我对写意画境的偏好，使这一手势成为必需，哪怕时当五月。

===Original===
“Oh, look here!” my uncle cried, and caught up the papers. “It is infernally good, you know! Can’t you—can’t you fix it—and—er—change it a bit? Typewriting is so expensive these days that it seems a pity to waste all this.”

I took the manuscript and replaced it firmly among the embers. “As you justly observe,” said I, “it is infernally good. It is probably a deal better than anything else I shall ever write.”

“Why, then—” said Uncle George.

“Why, then,” said I, “the only thing that remains to do is to read *The Imperial Votaress*.”

===Chinese===
「哦，喂喂！」我叔叔叫起来，一把抢出那些纸页。「这书写得好得要命，你知道！你就不能——就不能补救补救——再——呃——改动那么一点儿？如今打字这样贵，把这些全糟蹋了，怪可惜的。」

我接过手稿，毅然把它重新安放回余烬中间。「正像你公允指出的，」我说，「它是好得要命。它多半比我此生再写出的任何东西，都要好上一大截。」

「那么，这——」乔治叔叔说。

「那么，这，」我说，「剩下的唯一一件事，便是去读《帝国女祭司》。」

### VIII / 八

===Original===
And I read it with an augmenting irritation. Here was my great and comely idea transmuted by “George Glock”—which was the woman’s foolish pen-name—into a rather clever melodrama, and set forth anyhow, in a hit or miss style that fairly made me squirm. I would cheerfully have strangled Marian Winwood just then, and not upon the count of larceny, but of butchery.

“And to cap it all, she has assigned her hero every pretty speech I ever made to her! I honestly believe the rogue took shorthand jottings on her cuffs. “There is a land where lovers may meet face to face, and heart to heart, and mouth to mouth”—why, that’s the note I wrote her on the day she wasn’t feeling well!”

Presently, however, I began to laugh, and presently sitting there alone, I began to applaud as if I were witnessing a play that took my fancy.

“Oh, the adorable jade!” I said; and then: “George Glock, forsooth! *George Dandin, tu l’ as voulu.*”

===Chinese===
我读着读着，火气越读越旺。瞧啊，我那宏大而姣好的构想，被「乔治·格洛克」——这是那女人愚蠢的笔名——点化成了一出相当机巧的情节剧，而且信笔写来，那副东一榔头西一棒槌的笔路，直看得我浑身扭动。那一刻，我简直乐意亲手掐死玛丽安·温伍德——罪名不取偷窃，而取屠戮。

「最气人的是，她把我从前对她说过的每一句漂亮话，全数派给了她笔下的男主角！我实实在在相信，这泼皮把速记记在了自己的袖口上。『有一方土地，情人们得以面对面、心贴心、唇对唇地相会』——嗐，这正是她那天身子不爽利时，我写给她的字条！」

然而，过了一忽儿，我笑了起来；又过一忽儿，独自坐在那儿的我，竟鼓起掌来，仿佛看的是一出正中下怀的戏。

「哦，这可人心疼的小滑头！」我说；接着又道：「亏她还是『乔治·格洛克』！真是『乔治·当丹，你自找的』（*George Dandin, tu l’as voulu*）。」

### IX / 九

===Original===
Naturally I put the entire affair into a short story. And—though even to myself it seems incredible—Miss Winwood wrote me within three days of the tale’s appearance, a very indignant letter.

For she was furious, to the last exclamation point and underlining, about my little magazine tale. … “Why don’t you stop writing, and try plumbing or butchering or traveling for scented soap? *You can’t write!* If you had the light of creation you wouldn’t be using my material.” …

—Which caused me to reflect forlornly that I had wasted a great deal of correct behavior upon Marian, since any of the more intimately amorous advances which I might have made, and had scrupulously refrained from making, would very probably have been regarded as raw “material,” to be developed rather than shocked by. …

===Chinese===
自然，我把这整桩事写成了一篇短篇小说。而——虽说连我自己都觉得难以置信——小说发表不出三天，温伍德小姐便给我来了一封信，一封怒气冲天的信。

因为对于我那篇登在杂志上的小故事，她简直怒透了——从第一个字怒到最后一个惊叹号、每一条下划线，无一不怒。……「你干吗不撂下写作，改行去修水管、去宰猪、或者去推销香皂？*你根本不会写！*你若真有创造的光华，也不至于来盗用我的素材。」……

——这话引得我凄凄然反思起来：我在玛丽安身上，白白糟蹋了那么一大堆端正的行止；因为凡是我本可以做出、却出于审慎一概克制的那些更亲昵的情热之举，十之八九，都会被她看作生的「素材」，只消埋头开发，断不至于大惊小怪。……

## XVIII / 十八

He Spends an Afternoon in Arden / 他在亚登消磨了一个下午

### I / 一

===Original===
I had, in a general way, intended to marry Rosalind Jemmett so soon as I had completed *As the Coming of Dawn*; but in the fervour of writing that unfortunate volume, I had at first put off a little, and then a little longer, the answering of her last letter, because I was interested just then in writing well and not particularly interested in anything else; and I had finally approximated to forgetfulness of the young lady's existence.

===Chinese===
大体而言，我原打算一写完《恰似黎明降临》（*As the Coming of Dawn*）便迎娶罗瑟琳·杰米特；可是在伏案疾书那部倒霉书卷的一腔热忱里，回她最后一封信这件事，我起初搁下了一小会儿，随后又搁得更久了一些——因为那时候我一心只在把文章写好上头，对旁的一切都不怎么上心；到末了，这位年轻小姐尚在人世这桩事，我已忘得八九不离十。

===Original===
Now, however, my thoughts harked back to her; and I found, upon inquiry, that Rosalind had spent all of May and a good half of April in Lichfield, in the same town with myself, and was now engaged to Alfred Chaytor﻿—an estimable person, but popularly known as "Sissy" Chaytor.

===Chinese===
然而到了这时候，我的心思又转回到她身上；一打听才知道，罗瑟琳整个五月、连同足足半个四月，都待在利奇菲尔德，同我住在一座城里，如今已同阿尔弗雷德·查托（Alfred Chaytor）订了婚——那是一位可敬的人物，只是人人都管他叫「娘娘腔」查托。

### II / 二

===Original===
And this gave an additional whet to my intentions. So I called upon the girl, and she, to my chagrin, received me with an air of having danced with me some five or six times the night before; our conversation was at first trivial and, on her part, dishearteningly cordial; and, in fine, she completely baffled me by not appearing to expect any least explanation of my discourteous neglect. This, look you, when I had been at pains to prepare a perfectly convincing one.

===Chinese===
而这一来，倒把我的意图磨得更利了些。于是我去拜访这位姑娘。孰料——真叫我懊恼——她接待我的那副神气，仿佛昨儿晚上不过同我跳过五六支舞；我们的谈话起初尽是些琐碎，她那一边更是殷勤得叫人心灰；到头来，她竟丝毫不想指望我对那回失礼的怠慢作出哪怕一丁点解释，把我整个儿闹了个没辙。你要知道，这可全然是在我煞费苦心、备好了一套天衣无缝的说辞之后啊。

===Original===
It must be conceded I completely lost my temper; shortly afterward neither of us was speaking with excessive forethought; and each of us languidly advanced a variety of observations which were more dexterous than truthful. But I followed the intractable heiress to the Moncrieffs that spring, in spite of this rebuff, being insufferably provoked by her unshakable assumptions of my friendship and of nothing more.

===Chinese===
必须承认，我完完全全动了火；不多时，我们两个说话都不再带多少先见了；各人有气无力地抛出种种高见，句句机巧有余，诚实不足。可尽管吃了这记钉子，那年春天我依旧追着这位桀骜难驯的女继承人去了蒙克里夫家（the Moncrieffs）——只因她铁打不动地认定我们之间是交情、并且仅止于交情，这份笃定把我撩拨得难以忍受。

### III / 三

===Original===
It was perhaps a week later she told me: "This, beyond any reasonable doubt, is the Forest of Arden."

"But where Rosalind is is always Arden," I said, politely. Yet I made a mental reservation as to a glimpse of the golf-links, which this particular nook of the forest afforded, and of a redheaded caddy in search of a lost ball.

===Chinese===
大约过了一个礼拜，她对我说：「此地毋庸置疑、千真万确，正是亚登森林（the Forest of Arden）。」

「可是罗瑟琳在哪儿，哪儿便永远是亚登，」我彬彬有礼地说。只是我心里暗自存了个保留：这片林子的这个角落里，望得见一角高尔夫球场，还有一个正满地寻球的赤发球童。

===Original===
But beyond these things the sun was dying out in a riot of colour, and its level rays fell kindlily upon the gaunt pines that were thick about us two, converting them into endless aisles of vaporous gold.

There was primeval peace about; an evening wind stirred lazily above, and the leaves whispered drowsily to one another over the waters of what my companion said was a "brawling loch," though I had previously heard it reviled as a particularly treacherous and vexatious hazard. Altogether, I had little doubt that we had reached, in any event, the outskirts of Arden.

===Chinese===
只是撇开这些不谈，太阳正在一场色彩的狂欢里燃尽它自己，平射的余晖温存地洒落在我们两人四周密密丛丛、清瘦挺拔的松树上，把它们化作一道道没入雾金深处的无尽长廊。

四下里是一派太古的安宁；晚风在高处懒懒地拂动，树叶在一泓流水之上昏昏欲睡地窃窃私语——那水，我的同伴称它是「喧腾的小湖」，虽然我从前只听人骂它是个格外阴险、格外恼人的水障碍。总而言之，无论如何我们确已抵达亚登的外缘，这一点我几乎没有疑心。

===Original===
"And now," quoth she, seating herself on a fallen log, "what would you do if I were your very, very Rosalind?"

"Don't!" I cried in horror. "It wouldn't be proper! For as a decent self-respecting heroine, you would owe it to Orlando not to listen."

"H'umph!" said Rosalind. The exclamation does not look impressive, written out; but, spoken, it placed Orlando in his proper niche.

===Chinese===
「那么，」她道，在一根倒木上坐了下来，「倘若我做了你亲亲爱爱的罗瑟琳，你待要怎办？」

「使不得！」我惶然叫道。「这可不成体统！你身为一位体面的、自重的女主角，对奥兰多（Orlando）负有义务，这话万万听不得。」

「哼！」罗瑟琳说。这一声感叹写在纸面上毫不起眼；可从口中说出来，便把奥兰多安放进了他应有的壁龛。

===Original===
"Oh, well," said I, and stretched myself at her feet, full length﻿—which is supposed to be a picturesque attitude﻿—"why quarrel over a name? It ought to be Gamelyn, anyhow; and, moreover, by the kindness of fate, Orlando is golfing."

Rosalind frowned, dubiously.

===Chinese===
「哦，也罢，」我说着，在她脚边直挺挺舒展开来——据说这是个别有画意的姿势——「何必为一个名字拌嘴？何况明明该叫加梅林（Gamelyn）；再者，承命运的美意，奥兰多打高尔夫球去了。」

罗瑟琳皱起眉来，将信将疑。

===Original===
"But golf is a very ancient game," I reassured her. Then I bit a pine-needle in two and sighed. "Foolish fellow, when he might be﻿—""

"Admiring the beauties of nature," she suggested.

===Chinese===
「可高尔夫本是一项非常古老的游戏，」我宽她的心道。随后我把一根松针咬成两截，叹了口气。「傻家伙，明明大可以——」

「欣赏大自然的美呀，」她进言道。

===Original===
Just then an impudent breeze lifted a tendril of honey-coloured hair and toyed with it, over a low, white brow﻿—and I noted that Rosalind's hair had a curious coppery glow at the roots, a nameless colour that I have never observed anywhere else.﻿ ...

"Yes," said I, "of nature."

"Then," queried she, after a pause, "who are you? And what do you in this forest?"

===Chinese===
恰在此时，一阵没规矩的微风掀起她一绺蜜色的发卷，在一方低平白皙的额头上同它嬉戏——我还留意到，罗瑟琳的头发在发根处透着一层奇异的赤铜微光，一种我从未在别处见过的、叫不出名字的颜色。……

「是啊，」我说，「大自然的美。」

「那么，」隔了片刻，她问道，「你又是何人？在这林中做甚营生？」

===Original===
"You see," I explained, "there were conceivably other men in Arden﻿—""

"I suppose so," she sighed, with exemplary resignation.

"﻿—For you were," I reminded her, "universally admired at your uncle's court﻿—and equally so in the forest. And while Alfred﻿—or, strictly speaking, Gamelyn, or, if you prefer it, Orlando﻿—is the great love of your life, still﻿—""

"Men are so foolish!" said Rosalind, irrelevantly.

===Chinese===
「你要知道，」我解释道，「亚登之中，想必也还有别的男人——」

「大概是有的吧，」她叹道，叹出一副堪作表率的顺从。

「——因为想当年，」我提醒她，「在你叔父的宫廷里，你受尽众人爱慕——到了林子里，也一样受尽爱慕。而阿尔弗雷德——严格说来是加梅林，你若喜欢，叫奥兰多也使得——虽是你此生的挚爱，可是——」

「男人就是这么傻！」罗瑟琳说，答非所问。

===Original===
"﻿—it did not prevent you﻿—""

"Me!" cried she, indignant.

"You had such a tender heart," I suggested, "and suffering was abhorrent to your gentle nature."

===Chinese===
「——这却并不妨碍你——」

「我！」她叫了起来，义形于色。

「你心肠是那样软，」我进言道，「而受苦受难，又为你温柔的天性所深恶。」

===Original===
"I don't like cynicism, sir," said she; "and inasmuch as tobacco is not yet discovered﻿—""

"It is clearly impossible that I am smoking," I finished; "quite true."

"I don't like cheap wit, either," said Rosalind. "You," she went on, with no apparent connection, "are a forester, with a good crossbow and an unrequited attachment﻿—say, for me. You groan and hang verses and things about on the trees."

===Chinese===
「我不喜欢愤世嫉俗的刻薄话，先生，」她说，「况且烟草又还没有被人发现——」

「那便恕我此刻分明不可能在抽烟，」我替她把话说完，「一点不错。」

「廉价的俏皮话，我也不喜欢，」罗瑟琳说。「你呢，」她接着道，同前言没有半点明显的干系，「是个林中人，有一张上好的弩，还有一段得不到回应的爱慕——比方说，对我。你长吁短叹，还把诗啊什么的东西，挂得四下的树上都是。」

===Original===
"But I don't write verses﻿—any longer," I amended. "Still how would this do﻿—for an oak, say﻿—

"I found a lovely centre-piece

Upon the supper-table,

But when I looked at it again

I saw I wasn't able,

And so I took my mother home

And locked her in the stable."

===Chinese===
「可我不写诗了——早就不写了，」我修正道。「不过看看这首如何——比方说，题给一株橡树——

我在晚餐桌上，

发现一件可爱的桌心摆饰，

可待我再瞧它一眼，

便知道自己力不能及，

于是我领了母亲回家，

把她锁进了马厩里。」

===Original===
She considered that the plot of this epic was not sufficiently inevitable. It hadn't, she lamented, a quite logical ending; and the plot of it, in fine, was not, somehow, convincing.

===Chinese===
她品鉴之下认为，这首史诗的情节，必然性还嫌不足。她痛心地指出，它缺一个相当合乎逻辑的结尾；总而言之，这情节不知怎的，就是不能令人信服。

===Original===
"Well, in any event," I optimistically reflected, "I am a nickel in. If your dicta had emanated from a person in Peoria or Seattle, who hadn't bothered to read my masterpiece, they would have sounded exactly the same, and the clipping-bureau would have charged me five cents. Maybe I can't write verses, then. But I am quite sure I can groan." And I did so.

===Chinese===
「呃，不管怎么说，」我乐观地思忖，「这五分镍币我算是省下了。你这些高论，倘若出自皮奥里亚（Peoria）或西雅图（Seattle）哪位懒得读我大作的人之口，听来也会一模一样，而剪报公司还要收我五分钱。如此说来，兴许我当真不会写诗。可有一项我深信不疑：唉声叹气，我在行。」于是我当真叹了一声。

===Original===
"It sounds rather like a foghorn," said Rosalind, still in the critic's vein; "but I suppose it is the proper thing. Now," she continued, and quite visibly brightening, "you can pretend to have an unrequited attachment for me."

"But I can't﻿—" I decisively said.

"Can't," she echoed. It has not been mentioned previously that Rosalind was pretty. She was especially so just now, in pouting. And, therefore, "﻿—pretend," I added.

She preserved a discreet silence.

===Chinese===
「听上去倒更像一声雾笛，」罗瑟琳说，评论家的兴头犹自未减；「不过我料想，这也是应有之义。现在，」她接着说，眼睛分明亮了起来，「你可以假装怀着一段得不到回应的爱慕，对象是我。」

「可我不能——」我斩钉截铁地说。

「不能，」她应声念了一遍。前文还不曾提到，罗瑟琳生得标致。而此刻她正噘着嘴，标致得格外分明。于是——「——假装总行，」我补了一句。

她谨慎地保持了沉默。

===Original===
"Nor," I continued, with firmness, "am I a shambling, nameless, unshaven denizen of Arden, who hasn't anything to do except to carry a spear and fall over it occasionally. I will no longer conceal the secret of my identity. I am Jaques."

"You can't be Jaques," she dissented; "you are too stout."

"I am well-built," I admitted, modestly; "as in an elder case, sighing and grief have blown me up like a bladder; yet proper pride, if nothing else, demands that my name should appear on the programme."

===Chinese===
「再者，」我毅然接着说，「我也不是亚登里那种走路拖沓、无名无姓、不修胡子的居民，除了扛一杆长矛、偶尔再被它绊上一跤，便再无事可做。我再不能隐瞒自己身份的秘密了。我是杰奎斯（Jaques）。」

「你当不得杰奎斯，」她持异议道，「你太富态了。」

「我是身板富态，」我谦逊地承认，「正如古时一桩先例，叹息与悲伤把我吹胀得像一只膀胱；然而自尊——哪怕撇开旁的一切不谈——也要求我的名字印上节目单。」

===Original===
"But would Jaques be the sort of person who'd﻿—?"

"Who wouldn't be?" I asked, with appropriate ardour. "No, depend upon it, Jaques was not any more impervious to temptation than the rest of us; and, indeed, in the French version, as you will find, he eventually married Celia."

"Minx!" said she; and it seemed to me quite possible that she referred to Celia Reindan, and my heart glowed.

===Chinese===
「可杰奎斯会是那种人么，居然肯——？」

「哪种人不肯呢？」我问，热忱拿捏得恰到好处。「不，你只管相信，杰奎斯抵挡诱惑的能耐，并不比我们其余人高明多少；何况，在那部法文改写本里——你日后自会查到——他到头来娶了塞莉娅（Celia）。」

「小狐媚子！」她说；而依我看，她说的大有可能正是塞莉娅·赖因丹（Celia Reindan），于是我的心头一片暖热。

===Original===
"And how," queried Rosalind, presently, "came you to the Forest of Arden, good Jaques?"

I groaned once more. "It was a girl," I darkly said.

"Of course," assented Rosalind, beaming as to the eyes. Then she went on, and more sympathetically: "Now, Jaques, you can tell me the whole story."

"Is it necessary?" I asked.

"Surely," said she, with sudden interest in the structure of pine-cones; "since for a long while I have wanted to know all about Jaques. You see Mr. Shakespeare is a bit hazy about him."

===Chinese===
「那么，」过了一忽儿，罗瑟琳问道，「好杰奎斯，你是怎么流落到这亚登森林来的呢？」

我又叹了一声。「是为了一个姑娘，」我幽幽地说。

「那自然，」罗瑟琳应道，眼里焕发出光彩。随后她接着说，口吻多了几分体恤：「来，杰奎斯，你可以把始末原委都讲给我听了。」

「有这个必要么？」我问。

「自然有，」她说，突然对松球的构造大感兴趣；「因为好久以来，我一直想知道关于杰奎斯的一切。你要知道，莎士比亚先生写到他，未免有点语焉不详。」

===Original===
"*So!*" I thought, triumphantly.

And aloud, "It is an old story," I warned her, "perhaps the oldest of all old stories. It is the story of a man and a girl. It began with a chance meeting and developed into a packet of old letters, which is the usual ending of this story."

Rosalind's brows protested.

"Sometimes," I conceded, "it culminates in matrimony; but the ending is not necessarily tragic."

I dodged exactly in time; and the pine-cone splashed into the hazard.

===Chinese===
「*着啊！*」我心想，得意扬扬。

出了声，我便预先知会她：「这是个古老的故事，兴许是一切古老故事里最古老的一个。它讲一个男人和一个姑娘。它以一场萍水相逢开场，发展成一扎旧书信——这故事通常的收梢，便是这个。」

罗瑟琳的双眉提出了抗议。

「有时候，」我让步道，「它也会以结成连理登峰；不过结局倒不一定非是悲剧。」

我躲闪得恰逢其时；那只松球扑通一声，落进了水障碍里。

===Original===
"It happened," I continued, "that, on account of the man's health, they were separated for a whole year's time before﻿—before things had progressed to any extent. When they did progress, it was largely by letters. That is why this story ended in such a large package.

"Letters," Rosalind confided, to one of the pines, "are so unsatisfactory. They mean so little."

===Chinese===
「事情是这样的，」我接着讲，「由于男方身体欠安，在——在事情还没有多少长进之前，他们足有整整一年天各一方。后来事情果真长进了，长进又多半是靠书信。所以这故事才收梢在那么大的一包东西上。

「书信啊，」罗瑟琳向其中一株松树吐露心曲，「实在寡淡得很。它们的分量太轻了。」

===Original===
"To the man," I said, firmly, "they meant a great deal. They brought him everything that he most wished for﻿—comprehension, sympathy, and, at last, comfort and strength when they were sore needed. So the man, who was at first but half in earnest, announced to himself that he had made a discovery. 'I have found,' said he, 'the great white love which poets have dreamed of. I love this woman greatly, and she, I think, loves me. God has made us for each other, and by the aid of her love I will be pure and clean and worthy even of her.' You have doubtless discovered by this stage in my narrative," I added, as in parenthesis, "that the man was a fool."

===Chinese===
「对那男人，」我语气坚定地说，「它们的分量却重得很。它们带给了他他最渴盼的一切——理解，怜惜，还有末了在他痛感需要之时送到的好言安慰与气力。于是那男人——起初不过三分认真——向自己宣告，他做成了一桩发现。『我找到了，』他说，『诗人们梦寐以求的那场洁白伟大的爱。我深爱这个女人，而她，我想，也爱我。上帝造我们，原是为了彼此；我仗着她的爱相助，定要变得纯洁、干净，乃至配得上她。』讲到我叙述的这里，」我以插叙的口气补了一句，「你无疑已经发现：那男人是个傻瓜。」

===Original===
"Don't!" said Rosalind.

"Oh, he discovered it himself in due time﻿—but not until after he had written a book about her. *As the Coming of Dawn* the title was to have been. It was﻿—oh, just about her. It tried to tell how greatly he loved her. It tried﻿—well, it failed of course, because it isn't within the power of any writer to express what the man felt for that girl. Why, his love was so great﻿—to him, poor fool!﻿—that it made him at times forget the girl herself, apparently. He didn't want to write her trivial letters. He just wanted to write that great book in her honour, which would *make* her understand, even against her will, and then to die, if need be, as Geoffrey Rudel did. For that was the one thing which counted﻿—to make her understand﻿—" I paused, and anyone could see that I was greatly moved. In fact, I was believing every word of it by this time.

===Chinese===
「别说啦！」罗瑟琳说。

「哦，到了时候他自己也会发现的——只是那已是在他为她写完一部书之后了。书名原拟作《恰似黎明降临》。那书是——哦，写的全然就是她。它竭力要说出他爱她爱得何等深。它竭力——唉，它当然失败了，因为那男人对那姑娘的情意，本就不在任何作家表达的权能之内。你想，他的爱大到了——在他这可怜的傻瓜看来！——竟时不时叫他把那姑娘本人都忘了，明摆着的。他不想给她写些不痛不痒的信。他只想写那部献给她的鸿篇，好*叫*她明白，哪怕违逆她的本意，然后倘若必要，便像杰弗里·吕德尔（Geoffrey Rudel，为远方之爱渡海赴死的行吟诗人）那样死掉。因为要紧的只有一桩——叫她明白——」我顿住了，任谁看得出，我心潮翻涌。事实上，讲到这时，我已对自己说的每一个字深信不疑。

===Original===
"Oh, but who wants a man to *die* for her?" wailed Rosalind.

"It is quite true that one infinitely prefers to see him make a fool of himself. So the man discovered when he came again to bring his foolish book to her﻿—the book that was to make her understand. And so he burned it﻿—in a certain June. For the girl had merely liked him, and had been amused by him. So she had added him to her collection of men﻿—quite a large one, by the way﻿—and was, I believe, a little proud of him. It was, she said, rather a rare variety, and much prized by collectors."

===Chinese===
「哦，可谁要一个男人为*她*去死呀？」罗瑟琳哀声道。

「一点不假：人无不宁可看他自出其丑。那男人把那部愚蠢的书带去给她——那部要叫她明白的书——的时候，便把这一层看了个透。于是他把书烧了——在某一个六月里。因为那姑娘不过是喜欢他，拿他取过乐。于是她把他添进了她收藏的男人堆里——顺便说一句，那批藏品相当可观——而且，我相信，还为他有点得意。据她说，这是相当稀见的一个品种，收藏家们珍视得很。」

===Original===
"And how was *she* to know?" said Rosalind; and then, remorsefully: "Was it a very horrid girl?"

"It was not exactly repulsive," said I, as dreamily, and looking up into the sky.

There was a pause. Then someone in the distance﻿—a forester, probably﻿—called "Fore!" and Rosalind awoke from her reverie.

"Then﻿—?" said she.

===Chinese===
「可*她*又怎么知道呢？」罗瑟琳说；随即又悔意绵绵地补了一句：「那姑娘很可恶么？」

「倒也说不上面目可憎，」我说，恍恍惚惚的，眼睛望着天。

有一阵停顿。随后远处什么人——多半是个林中人——喊了一声「看球！」，罗瑟琳这才从遐想里醒转来。

「后来呢——？」她说。

===Original===
"Then came the customary Orlando﻿—oh, well! Alfred, if you like. The name isn't altogether inappropriate, for he does encounter existence with much the same abandon which I have previously noticed in a muffin. For the rest, he was a nicely washed fellow, with a sufficiency of the medieval equivalents for bonds and rubber-tired buggies and country places. Oh, yes! I forgot to say that the man was poor﻿—also that the girl had a great deal of common sense and no less than three longheaded aunts. And so the girl talked to the man in a commonsense fashion﻿—and after that she was never at home."

===Chinese===
「后来便来了例行登场的那位奥兰多——哦，也罢！就叫阿尔弗雷德，随你喜欢。这名字倒也并非全然不贴切，因为他迎面撞上人生时的那股不管不顾，同我先前在一块松饼身上留意到的，如出一辙。至于其余，他是个洗得干干净净的家伙，债券、胶轮马车、乡间宅邸的中世纪等价物，样样不缺。哦，对了！我忘了说那男人穷——也忘了说那姑娘极有常识，还有足足三位精明的姑妈。于是那姑娘便以常识的口吻，同那男人谈了一次——自此以后，她便永远不在家了。」

===Original===
"Never?" said Rosalind.

"Only that time they talked about the weather," said I. "So the man fell out of bed just about then, and woke up and came to his sober senses."

"He did it very easily," said Rosalind, almost as if in resentment.

"The novelty of the process attracted him," I pleaded. "So he said﻿—in a perfectly sensible way﻿—that he had known all along it was only a game they were playing﻿—a game in which there were no stakes. That was a lie. He had put his whole soul into the game, playing as he knew for his life's happiness; and the verses, had they been worthy of the love which caused them to be written, would have been among the great songs of the world. But while the man knew at last that he had been a fool, he was swayed by a manlike reluctance against admitting it. So he laughed﻿—and lied﻿—and broke away, hurt, but still laughing."

===Chinese===
「永远？」罗瑟琳说。

「只有那一回，他们谈了谈天气，」我说。「于是那男人差不多就在那时从床上跌了下来，醒了过来，恢复了清醒的神智。」

「他跌得未免太容易了，」罗瑟琳说，几乎像是带着怨气。

「是这套跌法的新奇吸引了他，」我辩解道。「于是他说——用一种十分明白晓事的态度——他早知道他们玩的不过是一场游戏——一场没有彩头的游戏。那是撒谎。他早把整个灵魂押了上去，心里清楚这一局赌的是他一生的幸福；而那些诗，倘若当得起催生它们的那场爱，本该跻身于世间伟大的歌章之列。只是那男人虽然末了明白了自己是个傻瓜，却又受一股男人气的执拗支使，不肯认账。于是他笑了——撒了谎——脱身走开，受了伤，却还在笑。」

===Original===
"You hadn't mentioned any verses before," said Rosalind.

"I told you he was a fool," said I. "And, after all, that is the entire story."

Then I spent several minutes in wondering what would happen next. During this time I lost none of my interest in the sky. I believed everything I had said: my emotions would have done credit to a Romeo or an Amadis.

===Chinese===
「你先前可没提起过什么诗呀，」罗瑟琳说。

「我不是告诉过你，他是个傻瓜么，」我说。「说到底，全部的故事便是这些。」

随后我花了好几分钟，揣想接下来会发生什么。这段时间里，我对头顶天空的兴趣不减分毫。我说过的话，我句句都信：我这一腔情热，拿去放在罗密欧（Romeo）或阿马迪斯（Amadis，骑士传奇中的完美骑士）身上，也足为他们增光。

===Original===
"The first time that the girl was not at home," Rosalind observed, impersonally, "the man had on a tan coat and a brown derby. He put on his gloves as he walked down the street. His shoulders were the most indignant﻿—and hurt things she had ever seen. Then the girl wrote to him﻿—a strangely sincere letter﻿—and tore it up."

"Historical research," I murmured, "surely affords no warrant for such attire among the rural denizens of tranquil Arden."

"You see," continued Rosalind, oblivious to interruption, "I know all about the girl﻿—which is more than you do."

"That," I conceded, "is disastrously probable."

===Chinese===
「那姑娘头一回不在家的那一趟，」罗瑟琳用置身事外的口吻评述道，「那男人穿一件棕黄色外衣，戴一顶褐色圆顶礼帽。他顺着街一路走，一路戴手套。他的双肩，是她生平所见最义愤填膺——也最受伤的一副。随后那姑娘给他写了信——一封真诚得出奇的信——又把它撕掉了。」

「史学考据，」我喃喃道，「想必拿不出半点凭据，证明太平安宁的亚登乡野间的居民，穿过这等装束。」

「你要知道，」罗瑟琳接着说，全不理会这打岔，「那姑娘的事我全知道——比你知道的还全。」

「那一点，」我让步道，「只怕不幸是实——实得糟糕透顶。」

===Original===
"When she realised that she was to see the man again﻿—*Did* you ever feel as if something had lifted you suddenly hundreds of feet above rainy days and cold mutton for luncheon, and the possibility of other girls' wearing black evening dresses, when you wanted yours to be the only one in the room? Well, that is the way she felt at first, when she read his note. At first, she realised nothing beyond the fact that he was nearing her, and that she would presently see him. She didn't even plan what she would wear, or what she would say to him. In an indefinite way, she was happier than she had ever been before﻿—or has been since﻿—until the doubts and fears and knowledge that give children and fools a wide berth came to her﻿—and *then* she saw it all against her will, and thought it all out, and came to a conclusion."

===Chinese===
「等她明白自己又要见到那男人了——你*可曾*有过那样的感觉：仿佛有什么东西忽然把你高高举起，举到几百英尺之上，把阴雨天、把午餐的冷羊肉、把别的姑娘也可能穿黑晚礼服的种种危险，一齐踩在了脚下——而你要的，是你的那一袭成为满堂唯一的一袭？嗳，她初读他那张便条时，起初便是那样一副心境。起初，她什么都没有意识到，只意识到他正在向她走近，她转眼就要见到他。她连穿什么、同他说什么，都没有盘算。朦朦胧胧地，她比从前任何时候都快乐——或者不如说，比此后任何时候都快乐——直到那起疑虑、恐惧与见识——孩子们和傻瓜们向来绕道远避的那一种——寻上了她——到*那时*，她违逆着本心看清了全部，把前后想了个透，得出了结论。」

===Original===
I sat up. There was really nothing of interest occurring overhead.

"They had played at loving﻿—lightly, it is true, but they had gone so far in their letter writing that they could not go backward﻿—only forward, or not at all. She had known all along that the man was but half in earnest﻿—believe me, a girl always knows that, even though she may not admit it to herself﻿—and she had known that a love affair meant to him material for a sonnet or so, and a well-turned letter or two, and nothing more. For he was the kind of man that never quite grows up. He was coming to her, pleased, interested, and a little eager﻿—in love with the idea of loving her﻿—willing to meet her halfway, and very willing to follow her the rest of the way﻿—if she could draw him. And what was she to do? Could she accept his gracefully insulting semblance of a love she knew he did not feel? Could they see each other a dozen times, swearing not to mention the possibility of loving﻿—so that she might have a chance to reimpress him with her blondined hair﻿—it *is* touched up, you know﻿—and small talk? And﻿—and *besides*﻿—"

===Chinese===
我坐起身来。头顶上实在没有什么可看的景致。

「他们玩过恋爱的把戏——固然玩得轻，但书信往还已走出了那么远，退是退不回去了——只能向前，或者索性作罢。她从头到尾都知道，那男人不过三分认真——信我一句，这种事姑娘永远知道，哪怕她不肯对自己承认——她也知道，一场风流韵事，在他那里不过是一两首十四行诗的素材，外添一两封圆润得体的信，如此而已。因为他正是那种永远长不大的男人。他正向她走来，欢欢喜喜，饶有兴味，还带着一点急切——爱的是『爱她』这个主意——情愿半途迎她，更情愿她若引得动，便一路随她走到底。可她又能怎样呢？她明知他并无其情，难道还能领受他那份姿态优雅、实则辱人的爱的空壳么？他们能一而再、再而三地见面，赌咒发誓绝口不提爱的可能——好让她得个机会，用她那染出来的金发——你要知道，那颜色是补染过的——和寒暄妙语，再把他迷一次么？还有——还有*再者*——」

===Original===
"It is the duty of every young woman to consider what she owes to her family," said I, absentmindedly. Rosalind Jemmett's family consists of three aunts, and the chief of these is Aunt Marcia, who lives in Lichfield. Aunt Marcia is a portly, acidulous and discomposing person, with eyes like shoe-buttons and a Savonarolan nose. She is also a well-advertised philanthropist, speaks neatly from the platform, and has wide experience as a patroness, and extreme views as to ineligibles.

===Chinese===
「每一位青年女子，都有义务想想自己对家庭负有什么，」我心不在焉地说。罗瑟琳·杰米特的家庭由三位姑妈组成，其中为首的是玛西娅姑妈（Aunt Marcia），她住在利奇菲尔德。玛西娅姑妈身躯富态，口舌尖刻，是位叫人惶窘的人物，一双眼睛像两粒皮鞋纽扣，鼻子是萨伏那洛拉（Savonarola）式的。她还是一位广告做得十足的慈善家，登台演讲吐属利落，做女赞助人阅历极深，至于什么样的男子够不上资格，她的见解更是激烈得很。

===Original===
Rosalind flushed somewhat. "And so," said she, "the girl exercised her common sense, and was nervous, and said foolish things about new plays, and the probability of rain﻿—to keep from saying still more foolish things about herself; and refused to talk personalities; and let him go, with the knowledge that he would not come back. Then she went to her room, and had a good cry. Now," she added, after a pause, "you understand."

"I do not," I said, very firmly, "understand a lot of things."

"Yet a woman would," she murmured.

This being a statement I was not prepared to contest, I waved it aside. "And so," said I, "they laughed; and agreed it was a boy-and-girl affair; and were friends."

===Chinese===
罗瑟琳微微红了脸。「于是，」她说，「那姑娘行使了她的常识；她心里发慌，尽说些新上演的戏、天会不会下雨之类的傻话——为的是不说出关于她自己的、更傻的话来；她拒绝谈论私事；然后放他走了，心里明白他不会再来了。随后她回到自己房里，痛痛快快哭了一场。这下，」她顿了顿，又补上一句，「你明白了。」

「我不明白，」我说，语气十分坚定，「好多事情我都不明白。」

「女人却明白，」她低声道。

这句话我无意争辩，便挥手把它撇开了。「于是，」我说，「他们笑了；一致同意这是一场男孩女孩间的儿女情事；做了朋友。」

===Original===
"It was the best thing﻿—" said she.

"Yes," I assented﻿—"for Orlando."

"﻿—and it was the most sensible thing."

"Oh, eminently!"

This seemed to exhaust the subject, and I lay down once more among the pine-needles.

===Chinese===
「那是最妥善的一步——」她说。

「是啊，」我附和道——「对奥兰多而言。」

「——也是最明白事理的一步。」

「哦，登峰造极！」

这话题似已被搜刮一空，我便在松针之间重新躺下了。

===Original===
"And that," said Rosalind, "was the reason Jaques came to Arden?"

"Yes," said I.

"And found it﻿—?"

"Shall we say﻿—Hades?"

"Oh!" she murmured, scandalised.

===Chinese===
「这么说，」罗瑟琳说，「杰奎斯就是为了这个，才来到亚登的？」

「正是，」我说。

「并且发现此地——？」

「咱们不妨说——是冥府（Hades）？」

「哦！」她低低惊呼一声，闻言骇然。

===Original===
"It happened," I continued, "that he was cursed with a good memory. And the zest was gone from his little successes and failures, now there was no one to share them; and nothing seemed to matter very much. Oh, he really was the sort of man that never grows up! And it was dreary to live among memories of the past, and his life was now somewhat perturbed by disapproval of his own folly and by hunger for a woman who was out of his reach."

"And Rosalind﻿—I mean the girl﻿—?"

"She married Orlando﻿—or Gamelyn, or Alfred, or Athelstane, or Ethelred, or somebody﻿—and, whoever it was, they lived happily ever afterward," I said, morosely.

Rosalind pondered over this denouement for a moment.

===Chinese===
「偏偏他又受了记性太好的诅咒。他那些小小的成败得失，如今没了人分享，滋味便都散尽了；什么都显得无关紧要。哦，他当真是那种永远长不大的男人！守着往昔的记忆过活，本已够凄清，如今他的日子，又被对自己蠢行的悔厌、又被对一个高不可攀的女人的渴念，搅得寝食难安。」

「那罗瑟琳——我是说那姑娘——呢？」

「她嫁了奥兰多——或者加梅林，或者阿尔弗雷德，或者阿特尔斯坦（Athelstane），或者埃塞尔雷德（Ethelred），或者随便哪一位——而且不管嫁的是谁，从此他们都过上了幸福的生活，」我郁郁地说。

罗瑟琳把这结局细细咀嚼了片刻。

===Original===
"Do you know," said she, "I think﻿—""

"It's a rather dangerous practice," I warned her.

Rosalind sighed, wearily; but in her cheek at about this time occurred a dimple.

===Chinese===
「你可知道，」她说，「我想——」

「这可是个相当危险的习气，」我警告她。

罗瑟琳倦倦地叹了口气；只是大约就在这个当口，她的脸颊上浮起了一个酒涡。

===Original===
"﻿—I think that Rosalind must have thought the play very badly named."

"*As You Like It*?" I queried, obtusely.

"Yes﻿—since it wasn't, for her."

It is unwholesome to lie on the ground after sunset.

===Chinese===
「——我想，罗瑟琳一定觉得，那出戏的名字起得坏透了。」

「《皆大欢喜》（*As You Like It*）么？」我故作不解地问。

「正是——因为对她来说，并无欢喜。」

日落后躺在地上，于身体是有损害的。

### IV / 四

===Original===
"I had rather a scene with Alfred yesterday morning. He said you drank, and gambled, and were always running after﻿—people, and weren't in fine, a desirable person for me to know. He insinuated, in fact, that you were a villain of the very deepest and non-crocking dye. He told me of instances. His performance would have done credit to Ananias. I was *mad*! So I gave him his old ring back, and told him things I can't tell *you*﻿—no, not just yet, dear. He is rather like a muffin, isn't he?" she said, with the lightest possible little laugh﻿—"particularly like one that isn't quite done."

===Chinese===
「昨天早上，我同阿尔弗雷德闹了一场不小的风波。他说你酗酒，赌钱，还一天到晚追在——人后头跑，总而言之，算不得一个我该结交的正派人物。他甚至影射说，你是个黑得最深、又永不掉色的恶棍。他还举了实例。他这一场演出，足以为亚拿尼亚（Ananias，圣经里以撒谎闻名的角色）增光生色。把我*气疯了*！于是我把他那枚旧戒指还给了他，还对他说了些话——那些话我还不能告诉你——不行，眼下还不行，亲爱的。他倒挺像一块松饼，你说是不是？」她说着，发出一声再轻巧不过的小笑——「尤其像一块没烤透的。」

===Original===
"Oh, Rosalind," I babbled, "I mean to prove that you were right. And I *will* prove it, too!"

And indeed I meant all that I said﻿—just then.

Rosalind said: "Oh, Jaques, Jaques! what a child you are!"

===Chinese===
「哦，罗瑟琳，」我絮絮叨叨地说，「我要证明你是对的。而且我*一定*会证明给你看！」

而我说这话，句句确是真心——至少在当时。

罗瑟琳说：「哦，杰奎斯，杰奎斯！你可真是个孩子！」

## XIX / 十九

He Plays the Improvident Fool / 他做了一回不顾将来的傻子

### I / 一

===Original===
Now was I come near to the summit of my desires, and advantageously betrothed to a girl with whom I was, in any event, almost in love; but I presently ascertained, to my dismay, that sophisticated, “proper” little Rosalind was thoroughly in love with me, and always in the back of my mind this knowledge worried me.

===Chinese===
如今，我已行近欲望之巅，还订下了一门于我有利的婚约，未婚妻是一位不管怎么说我也算得上几乎爱着的姑娘；可是不多时我便查明——查明得叫我懊丧——那位深谙世故、「合乎体统」的小罗瑟琳，竟彻头彻尾地爱上了我；而这桩认知总盘踞在我心底后方，隐隐地煎熬着我。

===Original===
Imprimis, she persisted in calling me Jaques, which was uncomfortably reminiscent of that time wherein I was called Jack. Yet my objection to this silly nickname was a mischancy matter to explain. There was no way of telling her that I disliked anything which reminded me of Gillian Hardress, without telling more about Gillian than would be pleasant to tell. So Rosalind went on calling me Jaques; and I was compelled to put up with a trivial and unpremeditated, but for all that a daily, annoyance; and I fretted under it.

===Chinese===
其一，她固执地管我叫杰奎斯，这不免教人难堪地想起我被人唤作杰克的那段时日。然而，要向她解释我为何嫌恶这个愚蠢的昵称，偏偏又是一桩凶险难办的事。我没法告诉她：凡是教我想起吉莲·哈德雷斯的东西，我一概嫌恶——除非把吉莲的底细也一并倒出，而那些底细倒了是不会叫谁痛快的。于是罗瑟琳只管杰奎斯、杰奎斯地叫下去；我也只得忍受这桩虽细碎、虽无心、却无日无之的烦扰，并在它底下暗自焦灼。

===Original===
Item, she insisted on presenting me with all sorts of expensive knickknacks, and being childishly grieved when I remonstrated.

“But I have the money,” Rosalind would say, “and you haven’t. So why shouldn’t I? And besides, it’s really only selfishness on my part, because I like doing things for you, and *if* you liked doing things for me, Jaques, you’d understand.”

===Chinese===
其二，她执意要送我各色昂贵的玩意儿，我一规劝，她便孩子气地伤心起来。

「可是钱在我手里，」罗瑟琳总要这样说，「又不在你手里。所以我为什么不能送？再说了，这实在只是我自私，因为我喜欢为你做事呀，*倘若*你也喜欢为我做事，杰奎斯，你就会明白了。」

===Original===
So I would eventually have to swear that I did like “doing things” for her; and it followed﻿—somehow﻿—that in consequence she had a perfect right to give me anything she wanted to.

===Chinese===
于是到头来，我总不得不起誓，说我确实喜欢为她「做事」；随后——也不知怎么一来——便顺理成章地推出：她因此享有了十足的权利，凡她想给我的，尽可以给我。

===Original===
And this too fretted me, mildly, all the summer I spent at Birnam Beach with Rosalind and with the opulent friends of Rosalind’s aunt from St. Louis.﻿ ﻿… They were a queer lot. They all looked so unspeakably new; their clothes were spick and span, and as expensive as possible, but that was not it; even in their bathing suits these middle-aged people﻿—they were mostly middle-aged﻿—seemed to have been very recently finished, like animated waxworks of middle-aged people just come from the factory. And they spent money in a continuous careless way that frightened me.

===Chinese===
这桩事也教我微微焦灼，贯穿了整个夏天——我同罗瑟琳、同她姑妈那班来自圣路易斯（St. Louis）的阔绰朋友，在伯南海滩（Birnam Beach）一同消磨的那个夏天。……那是一伙怪人。他们个个看上去新得难以言表；衣裳笔挺鲜亮，贵得无以复加，可毛病还不在此；就连穿着泳装的时候，这些年届中年的男男女女——他们多半年届中年——也像是方才出厂、最后一道工序才上完似的，像一尊尊刚从工厂里送出来的中年人活蜡像。他们花钱那种源源不断、毫不在意的气派，看得我心惊肉跳。

===Original===
But I was on my very best, most dignified behavior; and when Aunt Lora presented me as “one of the Lichfield Townsends, you know,” these brewers and breweresses appeared to be properly impressed. One of them﻿—actually﻿—“supposed that I had a coat-of-arms”; which in Lichfield would be equivalent to “supposing” that a gentleman possessed a pair of trousers. But they were really very thoughtful about never letting me pay for anything; in this regard there seemed afoot a sort of friendly conspiracy.

So the summer passed pleasantly enough; and we bathed, and held hands in the moonlight, and danced at the Casino, and rode the merry-go-round, and played ping-pong, and read *Dorothy Vernon of Haddon Hall*﻿—which was much better, I told everybody, than that idiotic George Glock book, *The Imperial Votaress*. And we drank interminable suisses, and it was all very pleasant.

===Chinese===
不过我端出了自己最得体、最庄重的行止；待到罗拉姑妈（Aunt Lora）把我引荐为「利奇菲尔德的汤森家的一员，你知道」，这班酿啤酒的老爷太太们似乎适当地肃然起敬了。其中一位——居然——「猜想我还有一枚纹章」；这话搁在利奇菲尔德，就等于「猜想」一位绅士还有一条裤子。可是他们确确实实体贴入微，从不让我破费一文；在这一节上，仿佛正有一场友善的密谋在进行着。

就这样，那个夏天过得还算惬意；我们游泳，在月光里携着手，在卡西诺游乐宫（Casino）跳舞，骑旋转木马，打乒乓球，还读《哈顿庄园的多萝西·弗农》（*Dorothy Vernon of Haddon Hall*）——我逢人便说，这可比乔治·格洛克那本愚蠢透顶的《帝国女祭司》强得多了。我们还喝了没完没了的瑞士冰饮（suisses），总之一切都十分惬意。

===Original===
Yet always in the rear of my mind was stirring restively the instinct to get back to my writing; and these sedately frolicsome benevolent people﻿—even Rosalind﻿—plainly thought that “writing things” was just the unimportant foible of an otherwise fine young fellow.

===Chinese===
然而在我心底后方，那股回到写作上去的本能，始终不曾安分，总在攒动着；而这些端庄嬉游、和蔼可亲的人物——连罗瑟琳也在内——明明白白把「写东西」只看作一个旁的方面都无可挑剔的青年人身上，一桩无关紧要的小癖好。

### II / 二

===Original===
And in September Rosalind came to visit her Aunt Marcia in Lichfield, to get clothes and all other matters ready for our wedding in November; and Lichfield, as always, made much of Rosalind, and she had the honor of “leading” the first Lichfield German with Colonel Rudolph Musgrave. My partner at that dance was the Marquise d’Arlanges.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
到了九月，罗瑟琳来利奇菲尔德探望她的玛西娅姑妈，置办嫁衣，把我们十一月婚礼的一应事宜筹备停当；利奇菲尔德一如既往地对罗瑟琳大加抬举，她还荣享殊荣，同鲁道夫·马斯格罗夫上校（Colonel Rudolph Musgrave）一道「领衔」了利奇菲尔德的头一场德国舞会（German）。那晚我的舞伴，是达朗热侯爵夫人。……

===Original===
I was seeing a deal of the Marquise d’Arlanges. She was Stella’s only sister, as you may remember, and was that autumn paying a perfunctory visit to her parents﻿—the second since her marriage.

===Chinese===
那阵子，我同达朗热侯爵夫人过从甚密。你或许记得，她是斯特拉唯一的妹妹，那年秋天正回娘家作一趟照例的敷衍省视——出嫁以来这是第二回。

===Original===
I shall not expatiate, however, concerning Madame la Marquise. You have doubtless heard of her. For Lizzie has not, even yet, found a time wherein to be idle; she has been busied since the hour of her birth in acquiring first, plain publicity, and then social power, and every other amenity of life in turn. I had not the least doubt that she will eventually die an empress.

===Chinese===
不过，关于这位侯爵夫人，我不打算多费笔墨。她的名头你无疑早有耳闻。因为莉齐直到今天，还不曾找到过一段可以赋闲的光阴；打从落地的时辰起，她就一直忙着置办——先置办平平常常的公众名声，再置办社交场上的权势，然后挨着次序，把人生的其余种种乐事一一置办齐备。我毫不怀疑，她最终将以女皇之尊辞世。

===Original===
She was at this time still well upon the preferable side o! thirty, and had no weaknesses save a liking for gossip, cigarettes, and admiration. Lizzie was never the woman to marry a Peter Blagden. Once Stella was settled, Lizzie Musgrave had sailed for Europe, and eventually had arrived at Monaco with an apologetic mother, several letters of introduction, and a Scotch terrier; and had established herself at the Hôtel de la Paix, to look over the “available” supply of noblemen in reduced circumstances. Before the end of a month Miss Musgrave had reached a decision, had purchased her Marquis, much as she would have done any other trifle that took her fancy, and had shipped her mother back to America. Lizzie retained the terrier, however, as she was honestly attached to it.

===Chinese===
这时候，她仍旧稳稳当当站在三十岁那道坎可取的一侧，除了偏爱闲话、香烟和受人仰慕之外，别无弱点。莉齐从来不是那种肯嫁给一个彼得·布拉格登的女人。斯特拉的事一经定妥，莉齐·马斯格罗夫便扬帆去了欧洲，几经辗转，带着一位赔着小心的母亲、几封介绍信和一条苏格兰梗犬，抵达摩纳哥（Monaco），在和平旅馆（Hôtel de la Paix）安顿下来，着手盘点那批家道中落、「现货可供」的贵族男子。不出一个月，马斯格罗夫小姐便拿定了主意，买下了她的侯爵——那份从容，同她买下任何一件入了眼的小玩意儿并无二致——随即将母亲装船打发回了美国。不过莉齐留下了那条梗犬，因为她对它是真心疼爱。

===Original===
Her marriage had been happy, and she found her husband on further acquaintance, as she told me, a mild-mannered and eminently suitable person, who was unaccountably addicted to playing dominoes, and who spent a great deal of money, and dined with her occasionally. In a sentence, the marquise was handsome, “had a tongue in her head,” and, to utilise yet another ancient phrase, was as hard as nails.

===Chinese===
她的婚姻是美满的；而且相处日久，她发觉——她亲口对我说——她丈夫是个性情温良、极其合宜的人物，嗜好玩多米诺骨牌，嗜得没来由，花起钱来挥金似土，偶尔还陪她共进一次晚餐。一句话，这位侯爵夫人容貌标致，「脑壳里长着一条舌头」，而且——再借一句古话说——硬得像钉子。

===Original===
And yet there was a family resemblance. Indeed, in voice and feature she was strangely like an older Stella; and always I was cheating myself into a half-belief that this woman I was talking with was Stella; and Lizzie would at least enable me to forget, for a whole half-hour sometimes, that Stella was dead.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
然而姐妹之间，终究有几分相像。说实在的，论嗓音、论眉目，她都奇异地像一个年长几岁的斯特拉；同这个女人说话时，我总在自欺，骗得自己半信半疑，只觉眼前人便是斯特拉；有莉齐在，至少有时能教我整整半个钟头，忘掉斯特拉已经死了。……

### III / 三

===Original===
“I must thank you,” I said, one afternoon, when I arose to go, “for a most pleasant dream of﻿—what we’ll call the Heart’s Desire. I suppose I have been rather stupid, Lizzie; and I apologise for it; but people are never exceedingly hilarious in dreams, you know.”

===Chinese===
「我得谢谢您，」一天下午，我起身告辞时说，「谢谢您赐下一场顶惬意的梦——梦见的是什么，我们姑且称之为心之渴望吧。我估摸着自己一直相当沉闷，莉齐；为此我向您道歉；不过人在梦里，您知道，是从来不曾特别兴高采烈的。」

===Original===
She said, very gently: “I understand. For I loved Stella too. And that is why the room is never really lighted when you come. Oh, you stupid man, how could I have *helped* knowing it﻿—that all the love you have made to me was because you have been playing I was Stella? That knowledge has preserved me, more than once, my child, from succumbing to your illicit advances in this dead Lichfield.”

===Chinese===
她非常温柔地说：「我明白。因为我也爱斯特拉。这就是为什么你来的时候，这间屋子从来不曾真正点过灯。哦，你这个愚蠢的男人，我怎么可能看不出——你向我做下的那一切求爱，无非因为你在拿我扮演斯特拉。正是这一点，我的孩子，不止一次保全了我，教我没有屈从于你在这座死气沉沉的利奇菲尔德，对我发起的种种名不正言不顺的进攻。」

===Original===
