It was within a month of this other disaster that Jasper Hardress came to America, accompanied by his wife. They planned a tour of the States, which they had not visited in seven years, and more particularly, as his forerunning letter said, they meant to investigate certain mining properties which Hardress had acquired in Montana. So, not unstirred by trepidations, I met them at the pier.

===Chinese===

距这又一桩灾祸发生不出一个月，贾斯珀·哈德雷斯（Jasper Hardress）便携妻子来到美国。他们打算周游合众国一番——这片国土他们已有七年不曾踏访——而且，照他那封打前站的信里所说，他们尤其要去察看哈德雷斯在蒙大拿（Montana）置下的几处矿业产业。于是我到码头去迎接他们，心里并非不忐忑。

===Original===

For I was already in New York, in part to see a volume of my short stories through the press﻿—which you may or may not have read, in its elaborate “gift-book” form, under the title of *The Aspirants*﻿—and in part about less edifying employments. I was trying to forget Elena, and in Lichfield it was not possible to induce such forgetfulness without affording unmerited pleasure for gabbling busybodies.﻿ ﻿… It was not in me to apologise, except in a letter, where the wording and interminable tinkering with phraseology would enable me to forget it was I who was apologising, until a bit of nearly perfect prose was safely mailed; and I knew she would not read any letter from me, because Elena comprehended that I always persuaded her to do what I prompted, if only she listened to me.

===Chinese===

因为我当时已在纽约，一来是要盯着一册短篇小说集付印——装帧成考究的「礼品书」、题作《渴慕者》（*The Aspirants*）的那一册，你读过也罢、没读过也罢——二来是奔着些不那么上台面的营生。我在设法忘掉埃莱娜（Elena）；而在利奇菲尔德，这样的遗忘是没法促成的，除非肯白白赏给那班喋喋不休的多事之徒一场不该得的快活。……赔罪这桩事，我天生做不来，除非是写在信里：在信里，遣词、造句、无休无止地推敲措辞，能教我忘了赔罪的正是我本人，直到一小篇近乎完美的散文安然付邮；而我也知道，我的任何信件她一封也不会读，因为埃莱娜心里明白：只要她肯听我的，我总能说动她去做我要她做的事。

===Original===

As it was, I talked that morning for an hour or more with fat Jasper Hardress.﻿ ﻿… Even now I find the two errands which brought him to America of not unlaughable incongruity.

===Chinese===

就这样，那天上午，我同肥胖的贾斯珀·哈德雷斯谈了一个多钟头。……直到今天，我仍觉得他带到美国来的这两桩差事，彼此之不伦不类，倒也并非叫人笑不出来。

### II / 二

===Original===

For, first, he came as an agent of the Philomatheans, who were endeavouring to secure official recognition by the churches of America and England of a revised translation of, in any event, the New Testament.

===Chinese===

因为，头一桩，他是以菲洛马西安学会（Philomatheans）代表的名义来的；这个学会正力争美英两国教会正式承认一部修订译本——无论如何，至少是《新约》的修订译本。

===Original===

He told me of a variety of buttressing reasons﻿—which I suppose are well-founded, though I must confess I never investigated the matter. He told me how the Authorised Version was a paraphrase, abounding in confusions and in mistranslations from the Greek of Erasmus’s New Testament, which, as the author confessed, “was rather tumbled headlong into the world than edited.” And he told me how the edition of Erasmus itself was hastily prepared from careless copies of inaccurate transcriptions of yet further copies of divers manuscripts of which the oldest dates no further back than the fourth century, and is in turn, most probably, just a liberal paraphrase, as all the others are, of still another manuscript.

===Chinese===

他向我摆出了一堆佐证的理由——我想总归有根有据，虽然我必须承认，此事我从不曾查考。他告诉我，钦定本（Authorised Version）不过是一部转述意译，舛误丛生，处处是从伊拉斯谟（Erasmus）的希腊文《新约》误译而来；而那部书，用作者自己的话说，「与其说是编纂成书，不如说是被一头栽进世间来的」。他还告诉我，伊拉斯谟那一版本身，也是仓促赶成的——所据的副本漫不经心，抄自转录失真的誊录本，而那些誊录本，又录自形形色色手稿的再抄本；诸般手稿之中，年代最早的也上溯不过四世纪，而且十之八九，它也同其余所有抄本一样，多半只是又一部手稿的一番放任意译罢了。

===Original===

So that the English version, as I gathered, may be very fine English, but has scarcely a leg left, when you consider it as a safe foundation for superiority, or pillorying, or as a guide in conduct.

===Chinese===

于是照我的领会，这部英译本作为英文，或许十分精美；可倘若要拿它当作一处牢靠的根基——凭以自居高明、动辄把人绑上示众架——或当作一部立身处世的指南，那它便几乎连一条腿也剩不下了。

===Original===

I suspect, however, that Jasper Hardress somewhat overstated the case, since on this subject he was a fanatic. To me it seemed rather quaint that Hardress or anybody else should be bothering about such things.

===Chinese===

不过我疑心，贾斯珀·哈德雷斯把事情说得有些过甚其词，因为在这个题目上，他原是个狂热之徒。至于我，只觉得哈德雷斯——或者随便什么人——竟肯为这类事情操心，未免相当古怪。

===Original===

And as he feelingly declaimed concerning the great Uncials, and explained why in this particular verse the Ephraem manuscript was in the right, whereas to probe the meaning of the following verse we clearly must regard the Syriac version as of supreme authority, I could well understand how at one period or another his young wife must inevitably have considered him in the light of a rather tedious person.

===Chinese===

待到他慷慨激昂地朗声讲论起那几部赫赫有名的安色尔大字抄本（Uncials），解释为何到了这一节经文，以法莲抄本（Ephraem manuscript）独得其正，而要探明下一节的义理，我们显然又必须奉叙利亚文译本（Syriac version）为至高权威——我便很能明白，他的年轻妻子无论早晚，总难免有一阵子，要把他看作一个相当乏味的人物了。

===Original===

And I told him that it hardly mattered, because the true test of a church-member was the ability to believe that when the Bible said anything inconvenient it really meant something else.

===Chinese===

于是我告诉他，这实在无关紧要，因为考验一个教会门徒的真正标准，便是这样一份本领：坚信每逢《圣经》说出什么不方便的话来，它的真意必定另有所指。

===Original===

But actually I was not feeling over-cheerful, because Jasper’s second object in coming to America was to leave his wife in Sioux City, so that she could secure a divorce from him, on quite un-Scriptural grounds. Hardress told me of this at least without any excitement. He did not blame her. He was too old for her, too stolid, too dissimilar in every respect, he said. Their marriage had been a mistake, that was all﻿—a mismating, as many marriages were. She wanted to marry someone else, he rather thought.

===Chinese===

可实际上，我心里并不怎样轻快，因为贾斯珀来美国的第二桩目的，是把妻子留在苏城（Sioux City），好让她在那边办妥同他离婚的手续——所据的理由，则全然不合《圣经》。这桩事，哈德雷斯至少是心平气和对我讲的。他并不怪她。他同她年纪悬殊太大，人太木讷，无论哪一头都太不相像——他是这么说的。他们的婚姻本是一桩错，仅此而已——同许多婚姻一样，是错配的一对。她想要另嫁别人——他大致是这样想的。

===Original===

And “Oh, Lord! yes!” I inwardly groaned. “She probably does.”

===Chinese===

而「哦，主啊！可不！」我在心里呻吟。「她多半是想嫁的。」

===Original===

Aloud I said: “But the Bible﻿—Yes, I *am* provincial at bottom. It’s because I always think in nigger-English and translate it when I talk. It was my Mammy, you see, who taught me how to think﻿—and in our nigger-English, what the Bible says is true. Why, Jasper, even this Revised Version of yours says flatly that a man﻿—”

===Chinese===

出了声，我说的却是：「可是《圣经》——是啊，我骨子里*确是*个乡下佬。因为我一向用黑鬼英语思想，开口说话时再把它翻译过来。你要知道，教会我思想的，原是我的奶妈——而在我们的黑鬼英语里，《圣经》说的话就是真的。嗐，贾斯珀，连你们那部修订译本也白纸黑字地说，一个男人——」

===Original===

“Child, child!” said Jasper Hardress, and he patted my hair, and I really think it crinkled under his touch, “when you grow up﻿—if indeed you ever do﻿—you will find that a man’s feeling for his wife and the mother of his children, is not altogether limited by what he has read in a book. He wants﻿—well, just her happiness.”

===Chinese===

「孩子，孩子！」贾斯珀·哈德雷斯说着，拍了拍我的头发——我真觉得那头发在他掌下蜷缩起来——「等你长大了——倘若你当真长得大的话——你会发现，一个男人对他妻子、对他儿女生母的那份情分，原不全然受什么书本的限制。他要的——唉，无非是她幸福。」

===Original===

I looked up without thinking; and the aspect of that gross and unattractive man humiliated me. He had reached a height denied to such as I; and inwardly I cursed and envied this fat Jasper Hardress.﻿ ﻿… I would have told him everything, had not the waiter come just then.

===Chinese===

我不假思索地抬眼望去；这个肥硕而不讨喜的男人那副面目，羞辱了我。他已抵达一个我这样的人无缘企及的高度；我在心里又诅咒又艳羡这个肥胖的贾斯珀·哈德雷斯。……若不是侍者恰好那时走过来，我本会把一切和盘托给他的。

### III / 三

===Original===

And the same afternoon I was alone with Gillian Hardress, for the first time in somewhat more than two years. We had never written each other; I had been too cautious for that; and now when the lean, handsome woman came toward me, murmuring “Jack﻿—” very tenderly﻿—for she had always called me Jack, you may remember﻿—I raised a hand in protest.

===Chinese===

当天下午，我便同吉莲·哈德雷斯（Gillian Hardress）单独在一处了，这是两年多以来的头一回。我们彼此从不曾通过信；为着这个，我谨慎得过了头；此刻，这个清瘦而俊美的女人朝我走来，柔声低唤「杰克——」，唤得极其温柔——你或许记得，她一向是唤我杰克的——我举起一只手，表示抗议。

===Original===

“No﻿—that is done with, Jill. That is dead and buried now, my dear.”

===Chinese===

「不——那桩事完了，吉尔（Jill）。它如今已经入土埋葬了，亲爱的。」

===Original===

She remained motionless; only her eyes, which were like chrysoberyls, seemed to grow larger and yet more large. There was no anger in them, only an augmenting wonder.

===Chinese===

她一动不动；只有她那双金绿宝石似的眼睛，似乎越睁越大，大得再不能更大。里面没有怒意，只有一层愈积愈深的惊诧。

===Original===

“Ah, yes,” she said at last, and seemed again to breathe; “so that is dead and buried﻿—in two years.” Gillian Hardress spoke with laborious precision, like a person struggling with a foreign language, and articulating each word to its least sound before laying tongue to its successor.

===Chinese===

「啊，是啊，」她终于开口，仿佛这才重新呼吸起来；「原来那桩事已入土埋葬了——只用两年。」吉莲·哈德雷斯说话吃力而精确，像一个在同外语较劲的人，每个字都要吐尽它最细微的音响，才肯把舌头挪上后一个字。

===Original===

“Yes! we have done with each other, once for all,” said I, half angrily. “I wash my hands of the affair, I clean the slate today. I am not polite about it, and﻿—I am sorry, dear. But I talked with your husband this morning, and I will deceive Jasper Hardress no longer. The man loves you as I never dreamed of loving any woman, as I am incapable of loving any woman. He dwarfs us. Oh, go and tell him, so that he may kill us both! I wish to God he would!”

===Chinese===

「是啊！我们彼此一刀两断，一了百了，」我说，带着几分火气。「这桩事我洗手不干了，今天我就把旧账一笔勾销。我说话不客气，而且——对不住，亲爱的。可今天上午我同你丈夫谈过了，我再也不愿欺骗贾斯珀·哈德雷斯。这个男人爱你，爱到我做梦也不曾梦见的境地，爱到我根本无能为力——对任何女人都爱不出来的境地。他使我们两个都成了侏儒。哦，去告诉他吧，好让他把我们双双杀掉！我巴不得他真这么干！」

===Original===

Mrs. Hardress said: “You have planned to marry. It is time the prodigal marry and settle down, is it not? So long as we were in England it did not matter, except to that Faroy girl you seduced and flung out into the streets﻿—”

===Chinese===

哈德雷斯太太说：「你打算娶亲了。浪子也到了成家安顿的年纪了，不是么？先前我们在英国，这原不相干——只是苦了那个被你勾引、又被你一脚踹到街上去的法罗伊（Faroy）家姑娘——」

===Original===

“I naturally let her go when I found out﻿—”

===Chinese===

「我发觉之后，自然只好由她去了——」

===Original===

“As if I cared about the creature! She’s done with. But now we are in America, and Mr. Townsend desires no entanglements just now that might prevent an advantageous marriage. So he is smitten﻿—very conveniently﻿—with remorse.” Gillian began to laugh. “And he discovers that Jasper Hardress is a better man than he. Have I not always known that, Jack?”

===Chinese===

「仿佛我在乎那个造物似的！她的事早了结了。可如今我们到了美国，汤森先生眼下不想沾什么纠葛，免得碍了一门有利可图的亲事。于是他懊悔之情发作了——发作得真是恰到好处。这一点我不是从头到尾一清二楚么，杰克？」吉莲笑了起来。

===Original===

Now came a silence. “I cannot argue with you as to my motives. Let us have no scene, my dear﻿—”

===Chinese===

接着来了一阵沉默。「我的动机如何，我无法同你辩驳。我们还是别闹一场吧，亲爱的——」

===Original===

“God keep us respectable!” the woman said; and then: “No; I can afford to make no scene. I can only long to be omnipotent for just one instant that I might deal with you, Robert Townsend, as I desire﻿—and even then, heaven help me, I would not do it!” Mrs. Hardress sat down upon the divan and laughed, but this time naturally. “So! it is done with? I have had my dismissal, and, in common justice, you ought to admit that I have received it not all ungracefully.”

===Chinese===

「上帝保佑我们体面！」这个女人说；随即又道：「不；我闹不起这一场。我只能渴慕全能那么一瞬，好照我的心思发落你，罗伯特·汤森——而即便到了那时，上天可怜见，我也下不去手！」哈德雷斯太太在卧榻上坐下，笑了起来，这一回却是自然而然。「这么说，完了？我领了逐客令；而凭一句公道话，你也该承认，这道逐客令我接得不算不体面。」

===Original===

“From the first,” I said, “you have been the most wonderful woman I have ever known.” And I knew that I was sincerely fond of Gillian Hardress.

===Chinese===

「打从一开始，」我说，「你便是我平生所知最不可思议的女人。」而且我知道，我对吉莲·哈德雷斯是真心怀着眷爱的。

===Original===

“But please go now,” she said, “and have a telegram this evening that will call you home, or to Kamchatka, or to Ecuador, or anywhere, on unavoidable business. No, it is not because I loathe the sight of you or for any melodramatic reason of that sort. It is because, I think, I had fancied you to be not completely self-centred, after all, and I cannot bear to face my own idiocy. Why, don’t you realize it was only yesterday you borrowed money from Jasper Hardress﻿—some more money!”

===Chinese===

「不过现在请你走吧，」她说，「今晚务必收到一封电报，把你召回家去，或者召到堪察加（Kamchatka），召到厄瓜多尔（Ecuador），召到随便什么地方，去办一桩推不开的公务。不，这不是因为我见了你的模样就憎恶，也不是那一类情节剧式的缘故。是因为——我想——我原还当你到底不曾自私到彻头彻尾；如今要我直面自己的痴愚，我受不住。嗐，你难道不明白，就在昨天，你还向贾斯珀·哈德雷斯开口借钱来着——又借了一笔！」

===Original===

“Well, but he insisted on it: and I owed it to you to do nothing to arouse his suspicions﻿—”

===Chinese===

「嗐，那是他执意要借给我的：而我为了你，也理应不做任何引他起疑的事——」

===Original===

“And I don’t hate you even now! I wish God would explain to me why He made women so.”

===Chinese===

「而即便此刻，我也不恨你！但愿上帝肯向我解释，他为什么把女人造成了这副模样。」

===Original===

“You accuse me of selfishness,” I cried. “Ah, let us distinguish, for there is at times a deal of virtue in this vice. A man who devotes himself to any particular art or pursuit, for instance, becomes more and more enamoured of it as time wears on, because he comes to identify it with himself; and a husband is fonder of his wife than of any other woman﻿—at least, he ought to be﻿—not because he considers her the most beautiful and attractive person of his acquaintance, but because she is the one in whom he is most interested and concerned. He has a proprietary interest in her welfare, and she is in a manner part of himself. Thus the arts flourish and the home-circle is maintained, and all through selfishness.”

===Chinese===

「你指控我自私，」我嚷道。「啊，我们且分辨分明，因为这件恶德里头，有时蕴着偌多的美德。譬如，一个献身于某门艺术、某项追求的人，年深月久，会对它一往情深，与日俱增，因为他渐渐把它认作了自身；一个丈夫爱妻子胜过爱任何别的女人——至少，理当如此——也并非因为他认定她是平生识得的人里最美貌、最动人的那一个，而是因为她正是他兴味最浓、牵挂最深的那一个。她的福祉里有他一份业主的权益，而她在某种意义上，原是他自身的一部分。于是诸般艺术昌隆，家庭圈子得以维系——全凭自私。」

===Original===

I snapped my fingers airily; I was trying, of course, to disgust her by my callousness. And it appeared I had almost succeeded.

===Chinese===

我轻飘飘地打了个响指；自然，我是想拿这份凉薄去恶心她。而看样子，我几乎就要得手了。

===Original===

“Please go!” she said.

===Chinese===

「请你走吧！」她说。

===Original===

“But surely not while we are as yet involved in a question of plain logic? You think selfishness a vice. None the less you must concede that the world has invariably progressed because, upon the whole, we find civilisation to be more comfortable than barbarism; and that a wholesome apprehension of the penitentiary enables many of us to rise to deaconships. Why, deuce take it, Jill! I may endow a hospital because I want to see my name over the main entrance, I may give a beggar a penny because his gratitude puts me in a glow of benevolence that is cheap at the price. So let us not rashly declare that selfishness is a vice, and﻿—let us part friends, my dear.”

===Chinese===

「可我们既然还卷在一道纯粹的逻辑问题里，总不至于说走就走吧？你把自私看作恶德。但你仍不能不承认：世界所以一往无前，是因为大体说来，我们发觉文明比野蛮住着适意；也不能不承认：对监狱怀一份健全的忌惮，使我们中间许多人得以荣升执事。嗐，去他的，吉尔！我兴许会捐建一座医院，只为想瞧见自己的名字悬在大门正上方；我兴许会施给乞丐一个便士，只因他那一声道谢，能教我周身腾起一阵乐善好施的暖意——这价钱再便宜不过。所以我们不要轻率断言自私是恶德，还有——让我们好聚好散吧，亲爱的。」

===Original===

And I assumed possession of the thin hands that seemed to push me from her in a species of terror, and I gallantly lifted them to my lips.

===Chinese===

于是我攫住了那双瘦手——它们仿佛怀着一种惊怖，正要把我从她身边推开——又殷勤地把它们举到了唇边。

===Original===

The ensuing event was singular. Gillian Hardress turned to the door of her bedroom and brutally, as with two bludgeons, struck again and again upon its panels with clenched hand. She extended her hands to me, and everywhere their knuckles oozed blood. “You kissed them,” she said, “and even today they liked it, and so they are not clean. They will never again be clean, my dear. But they were clean before you came.”

===Chinese===

接下来的一幕，异乎寻常。吉莲·哈德雷斯转身朝着卧室的门，抡起两只拳头，像抡着两根棍棒，一下又一下狠狠砸在门板上。随后她把双手伸给我：处处指节，都渗着血。「你吻过它们，」她说，「直到今天它们还是喜欢的，所以它们不干净了。它们永远不会再干净了，亲爱的。可你来之前，它们原是干净的。」

===Original===

Then Gillian Hardress left me, and where she had touched it, the brass door knob of her bedroom door was smeared with blood.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

随后吉莲·哈德雷斯离我而去；在她触过的地方，她卧室门上那只黄铜门钮，抹着一道道血污。……

### IV / 四

===Original===

When I had come again to Lichfield I found that in the brief interim of my absence Elena Barry-Smith, without announcement, had taken the train for Washington, and had in that city married Warwick Risby. This was, I knew, because she comprehended that, if I so elected, it was always in my power to stop her halfway up the aisle and to dissuade her from advancing one step farther.﻿ ﻿… “I don’t know *how* it is!﻿—” she would have said, in that dear quasi-petulance I knew so well.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

待我重回利奇菲尔德，才发现就在我离去的短短间隙里，埃莱娜·巴里-史密斯（Elena Barry-Smith）不曾声张，径自上了开往华盛顿（Washington）的火车，并在那座城里嫁给了沃里克·里斯比（Warwick Risby）。我明白，这是因为她心里雪亮：只要我愿意，我随时有能力在圣坛走道半途拦住她，劝她半步也不再向前。……「我也说不清*怎么*就到了这一步！——」她准会这样说，带着那一腔我熟得不能再熟的、半真半假的可爱嗔怪。……

===Original===

But as it was, I met the two one evening at the Provises’, and with exuberant congratulation. Then straddling as a young Colossus on the hearthrug, and with an admonitory forefinger, I proclaimed to the universe at large that Mrs. Risby had blighted my existence and beseeched for Warwick some immediate and fatal and particularly excruciating malady. In fine, I was abjectly miserable the while that I disarmed all comment by being quite delightfully boyish for a whole two hours.

===Chinese===

然而事实是，一天晚上，我在普罗维斯家（the Provises’）遇见了他们夫妇，还热热闹闹地道了贺。随后我像个年轻的巨像，叉开双腿立在炉毯上，伸出一根训诫的手指，向普天之下宣告：里斯比太太断送了我的一生，并替沃里克祈求一种即刻发作、致命、而且痛楚格外钻心的恶疾。总而言之，整整两个钟头，我一面凄惨得无以复加，一面又做出一副乖觉讨喜的大孩子模样，缴了旁人一切议论的械。

===Original===

I must record it, though, that Mrs. Vokins patted my hand when nobody else was looking, and said: “Oh, my dear Mr. Bob, I wish it had been you! You was always the one I liked the best.” For that, in view of every circumstance, was humorous, and hurt as only humour can.

===Chinese===

不过有一笔，我还是得记下：趁旁人都不看的时候，沃金斯太太（Mrs. Vokins）拍了拍我的手，说：「哦，我亲爱的鲍勃先生，要是娶的是你就好了！你一向就是我顶顶喜欢的那一个。」因为这话，统观种种情由，实在幽默，也只有幽默才伤人——伤得这样入骨。

===Original===

So in requital, on the following morning, I mailed to Mrs. Risby some verses. This sounds a trifle like burlesque; but Elena had always a sort of superstitious reverence for the fact that I “wrote things.” It would not matter at all that the verses were abominable; indeed, Elena would never discover this; she would simply set about devising an excellent reason for not showing them to anybody, and would consider Warwick Risby, if only for a moment, in the light of a person who, whatever his undeniable merits, had neither the desire nor the ability to write “poetry.” And, though it was hideously petty, this was precisely what I desired her to do.

===Chinese===

于是作为报偿，第二天上午，我给里斯比太太寄去了几行诗。这话听上去有几分像滑稽戏；可是埃莱娜对我「会写东西」这一桩事实，向来怀着一种近乎迷信的崇敬。诗写得恶劣与否，根本无关紧要；说实在的，埃莱娜永远发现不了这一点；她只会着手筹划一个极佳的理由，好不把它们拿给任何人看；还会把沃里克·里斯比——哪怕只是片刻工夫——看作这样一号人：此人纵有种种无可否认的长处，写「诗」的心思与本事，却既无其愿、亦无其能。而这虽然卑劣得骇人，恰恰正是我指望她做的事。

===Original===

So I dispatched to her a sonnet-sequence which I had originally plagiarized from the French of Theodore Passerat in honour of Stella. I loathed sending Stella’s verses to anyone else, somehow; but, after all, my one deterrent was merely a romantic notion; and there was not time to compose a new set. Moreover, “your eyes are blue, your speech is gracious, but you are not she; and I am older﻿—and changed how utterly!﻿—I am no longer I, you are not you,” and so on, was absolutely appropriate. And Elena most undoubtedly knew nothing of Theodore Passerat. And Stella, being dead, could never know what I had done.

===Chinese===

于是我给她寄去了一组十四行诗——那原是我从泰奥多尔·帕瑟拉（Theodore Passerat）的法文里剽窃来、为颂赞斯特拉（Stella）而用的。把斯特拉的诗寄给旁人，不知怎的，我深为憎恶；可说到底，拦着我的无非是一个浪漫的念头；而新写一组，又来不及了。何况——「你的眼睛是蓝的，你的谈吐温文，可你不是她；而我老了——而且变得多么彻底！——我不再是我，你也不再是你」，连同诸如此类，实在是再贴切不过。而泰奥多尔·帕瑟拉为何许人，埃莱娜无疑一无所知。而斯特拉已经死了，永远不会知道我干下的事。

===Original===

So I sent the verses, with a few necessitated alterations, to the address of Mrs. Warwick Risby.

===Chinese===

于是我把那几首诗略作几处势在必行的改动，寄往了沃里克·里斯比太太的地址。

### V / 五

===Original===

I had within the week, an unsigned communication which, for a long while afterward, I did not comprehend. It was the photograph of an infant, with the photographer’s address scratched from the cardboard and without of course any decipherable postmark; and upon the back of the thing was written: “His has been the summer air, and the sunshine, and the flowers; and gentle ears have listened to him, and gentle eyes have been upon him. Let others eat his honey that please, so that he has had his morsel and his song.”

===Chinese===

不出一个礼拜，我收到一封不具名的函件——此后很长一段时日，我都参不透它。那是一帧婴儿的照片，照相馆的地址已从卡纸上刮去，自然也不带任何辨认得出的邮戳；那张卡片的背面写着：「夏日之风属于过他，阳光属于过他，繁花属于过他；温柔的耳朵倾听过他，温柔的眼睛注视过他。谁乐意，就让谁去吃他的蜜——只要他自己享到了那一口，唱完了那一曲。」

===Original===

I thought it was a joke of some sort.

===Chinese===

我起先只当它是某种玩笑。

===Original===

Then it occurred to me that this might be﻿—somehow﻿—Elena’s answer. It was an interpretation which probably appealed to the Supernal Aristophanes.

===Chinese===

随后我才想到，这或许——不知怎的——正是埃莱娜的回答。这样一种解读，多半正合天上那位阿里斯托芬（Aristophanes）的心意。

## XXIII / 二十三

He Reviles Destiny and Climbs a Wall / 他咒骂命运，翻过一道墙

### I / 一

===Original===
But now the spring was come again, and, as always at this season, I was pricked with vague longings to have done with roofs and paven places. I wanted to be in the open. I think I wanted to fall in love with somebody, and thereby somewhat to prolong the daily half-minute, immediately after awakening in the morning, during which I did not think about Elena Risby.

===Chinese===
如今春天又来了；每逢这个时节，我照例会被一阵隐约的渴望刺着，想同屋顶和铺石的地面做个了断。我想到旷野里去。我想，我是想爱上什么人，好借此把每天清晨初醒之后的那半分钟稍稍拉长一些——在那半分钟里，我还不曾想起埃莱娜·里斯比（Elena Risby）。

===Original===
I was bored in Lichfield. For nothing of much consequence seemed, as I yawned over the morning paper, to be happening anywhere. The Illinois Legislature had broken up in a free fight, a British square had been broken in Somaliland, and at the Aqueduct track Alado had broken his jockey’s neck. A mob had chased a negro up Broadway: Russia had demanded that China cede the sovereignty of Manchuria; and Dr. Lyman Abbott was explaining why the notion of equal suffrage had been abandoned finally by thinking people.

Such negligible matters contributed not at all to the comfort or the discomfort of Robert Etheridge Townsend; and I was pricked with vague sweet longings to have done with roofs and paven places. If only I possessed a country estate, a really handsome Manor or a Grange, I was reflecting as I looked over the “Social Items,” and saw that Miss Hugonin and Colonel Hugonin had reopened Selwoode for the summer months.﻿﻿

===Chinese===
我在利奇菲尔德闷得发慌。因为当我打着哈欠翻晨报的时候，天底下似乎没有一处正发生着什么大事。伊利诺伊州议会在一场全武行里散了场；英军的一个方阵在索马里兰被击破；阿奎达克（Aqueduct）赛马场上，「阿拉多」（Alado）摔断了它骑师的脖子。一伙暴民在百老汇追赶一个黑人；俄国要求中国割让满洲的主权；莱曼·阿博特（Lyman Abbott）博士则在解释，平等选举权这一观念，何以已被有思想的人们最终舍弃。

这类鸡毛蒜皮的时闻，对罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森的安适或不安适，没有半分贡献；而我依旧被隐约而甜美的渴望刺着，想同屋顶和铺石的地面做个了断。我一边翻检「社交简闻」栏——看见休戈宁小姐（Miss Hugonin）同休戈宁上校已重开塞尔伍德（Selwoode）消夏——一边暗自思量：倘若我也有上一处乡间产业、一座真正气派的庄园大宅或田庄，该有多好。……

===Original===
So I decided I would go to Gridlington, whither Peter Blagden had forgotten to invite me. He was extremely glad to see me, though, to do him justice. For Peter—by this time the inheritor of his unlamented uncle’s estate—had, very properly, developed gout, which is, I take it, the time-honoured appendage of affluence and, so to speak, its trademark; and was, for all his wealth, unable to get up and down the stairs of his fine house without, as we will delicately word it, the display and, at times, the overtaxing of a copious vocabulary.

===Chinese===
于是我决定去格里灵顿（Gridlington）——彼得·布拉格登忘了请我去的那个地方。不过说句公道话，他见了我还是欢喜得很。因为彼得——此际已是他那无人哀悼的叔父的遗产继承人——非常得体地害起了痛风；依我看，痛风是富足自古传下的附属品，也可以说，是富足的商标。而彼得尽管家财万贯，在他那所华宅里上下楼梯，却不能不——咱们文雅些措辞——搬弄出一大串词汇，有时还免不了透支。

### II / 二

===Original===
I was at Gridlington entirely comfortable. It was spring, to begin with, and out of doors in spring you always know, at twenty-five, that something extremely pleasant is about to happen, and that She is quite probably around the very next turn of the lane.

Moreover, there was at Gridlington a tiny private garden which had once been the recreation of Peter Blagden’s aunt (dead now twelve years ago), and which had remained untended since her cosseting; and I in nature took charge of it.

===Chinese===
我在格里灵顿过得说不出的舒坦。首先，那是春天；而春天里待在户外，人到了二十五岁，总有数：有一桩极惬意的事就要发生了，而「她」多半就在小巷再拐过去的那个弯儿上。

再者，格里灵顿有一座小小的私家花园，从前曾是彼得·布拉格登那位姑妈的消遣（她如今已死去十二年），自打她那份娇宠的功夫搁下，便一直无人料理；我顺天应人，把它接掌了过来。

===Original===
There was in the place a wilding peach-tree, which I artistically sawed into shape and pruned and grafted, and painted all those profitable wounds with tar; and I grew to love it, just as most people do their children, because it was mine. And Peter, who is a person of no sensibility, wanted to ring for a servant one night, when there was a hint of frost and I had started out to put a bucket of water under my tree to protect it. I informed him that he was irrevocably dead to all the nobler sentiments, and went to the laundry and got a washtub.

===Chinese===
园子里有一株野生桃树，我以艺术家的手段把它锯出了体形，又加修剪、又加嫁接，再把那一处处有利可图的伤口涂上焦油；我渐渐爱上了它，正如多数人爱自己的孩子，因为它是我自己的。而彼得——一个全无感受力的人——有天夜里竟要摇铃唤仆人：那晚天有些下霜的兆头，我正要动身去给我的树底下放一桶水护着它。我知会他说，他对一切较高尚的情感而言，已是不可挽回地死了；说罢便径去洗衣房，拎来一只洗衣盆。

===Original===
Peter was not infrequently obtuse. He would contend, for instance, that it was absurd for any person to get so gloriously hot and dirty while setting out plants, when that person objected to having a flower in the same room. For Peter could not understand that a cut flower is a dead or, at best, a dying thing, and therefore to considerate people is just so much abhorrent carrion; and denied it would be really quite as rational to decorate your person or your dinner table with the severed heads of chickens as with those of daffodils.

===Chinese===
彼得不时会犯迟钝。譬如，他会振振有词地争辩：一个人既连屋里摆一朵花都嫌恶，栽种花木时却又热得、脏得那样轰轰烈烈，岂不荒唐。因为彼得不明白，剪下来的花是一件死物，往宽里说也至多是一件垂死之物，所以在体贴的人眼里，它不过是又一堆可憎的腐肉；他又不肯承认：拿剁下来的鸡头装点你的身子或餐桌，同拿黄水仙的头颅来装点，实在同样合乎理性。

===Original===
“But that is only because you are not particularly bright,” I told him. “Oh, I suppose you can’t help it. But why make *all* the actions of your life so foolish? What good do you get out of having the gout, for instance?”

===Chinese===
「不过，那只是因为你脑子不算特别灵光，」我告诉他说。「哦，我想这也怨不得你。可你何苦把自己一辈子*所有*的举动都弄得这么蠢呢？比方说，害痛风，你到底落着什么好了？」

===Original===
Whereupon Mr. Blagden desired to be informed if I considered those with-various-adjectives-accompanied twinges in that qualified foot to be a source of personal pleasure to the owner of the very-extensively-hiatused foot. In which case, Mr. Blagden felt at liberty to express his opinion of my intellectual attainments, which was of an uncomplimentary nature.

===Chinese===
于是布拉格登先生想要领教一下：依我看，在那一只被形容词限定着的脚里，那些被各式定语簇拥着的阵痛，是否算得那位「空白留得极阔」的脚的主人的乐趣之源。倘若果真如此，布拉格登先生便觉得尽可放言，谈谈他对我的才智造诣的看法——而那看法是不带恭维的。

===Original===
“Because, you know,” I pursued, equably, “you wouldn’t have the gout if you did not habitually overeat yourself and drink more than is good for you. In consequence, here you are at thirty-two with a foot the same general size and shape as a hayrick, only rather less symmetrical, and quite unable to attend to the really serious business of life, which is to present me to the heiress. It is a case of vicarious punishment which strikes me as extremely unfair. You have made of your stomach a god, Peter, and I am the one to suffer for it. You have made of your stomach,” I continued, venturing aspiringly into metaphor, “a brazen Moloch, before which you are now calmly preparing to immolate my prospects in life. You ought to be ashamed of yourself, Peter!”

===Chinese===
「因为，你要知道，」我心平气和地接着说，「倘若你不是惯常吃得过量、喝得过了养生的分寸，你本不至于害痛风。结果呢，你才三十二岁，一只脚的尺寸与形状已同一座干草垛大体相仿，只是对称稍逊，而且全然无力去操办人生真正正经的大事——那就是把我引见给那位女继承人。这是一桩转嫁旁人的惩罚，在我看来极其不公。你把自己的胃供成了一位神，彼得，受罪的却是我。你把你的胃，」我接着说，一面斗胆向比喻的高处进发，「供成了一尊黄铜铸的摩洛（Moloch），此刻正预备在它跟前，从容不迫地把我的前程宰了祭神。你该为自己害臊，彼得！」

===Original===
Mr. Blagden’s next observation was describable as impolite.

===Chinese===
布拉格登先生接下来说的那句话，堪以「失礼」二字形容。

===Original===
“Fate, too,” I lamented, in a tragic voice, “appears to have entered into this nefarious conspiracy. Here, not two miles away, is one of the greatest heiresses in America—clever, I am told, beautiful, I am sure, for I have yet to discover a woman who sees anything in the least attractive about her—and, above all, with the Woods millions at her disposal. Why, Peter, Margaret Hugonin is the woman I have been looking for these last three years. She is, to a hair, the sort of woman I have always intended to make unhappy. And I can’t even get a sight of her! Here are you, laid up with the gout, and unable to help me; and yonder is the heiress, making a foolish pretence at mourning for the old curmudgeon who left her all that money, and declining to meet people. Oh, but she is a shiftless woman, Peter! At this very moment she might be getting better acquainted with me; at this very moment, Peter, I might be explaining to her in what points she is utterly and entirely different from all the other women I have ever known. And she prefers to immure herself in Selwoode, with no better company than her father, that ungodly old retired colonel, and a she-cousin, somewhere on the undiscussable side of forty—when she might be engaging me in amorous dalliance! That Miss Hugonin is a shiftless woman, I tell you! And Fate—oh, but Fate, too, is a vixenish jade!” I cried, and shook my fist under the nose of an imaginary Lachesis.

===Chinese===
「还有命运，」我换上一副悲声哀叹道，「看来也加入了这桩穷凶极恶的同谋。瞧，不出两英里，便住着全美最富有的女继承人之一——聪明，是听人说的；美丽，我则敢担保，因为我至今还不曾发现哪一个女人，能从她身上瞧出一星半点动人之处——而最要紧的是，伍兹家的百万家财归她任意支配。哎呀，彼得，玛格丽特·休戈宁（Margaret Hugonin）正是我这三年以来寻寻觅觅的那个女人。她分毫不差，正是我一向打定主意要叫她不幸的那一号女人。可我竟连看她一眼都办不到！这边是你，害痛风躺着，帮不上忙；那边是那位女继承人，为那个把全部家财留给她的老吝啬鬼装出一副愚蠢的居丧姿态，又推托着不肯见客。哦，她可真是个没算计的女人，彼得！此时此刻，她本可以同我多加亲近；此时此刻，彼得，我本可以向她逐条讲明，在哪几点上，她同我平生识得的其余一切女人全然不同、彻底两样。她却宁可把自己幽禁在塞尔伍德，身边寻不出更好的伴儿，除了她父亲——那位不敬神明的退役老上校——和一位女表亲，年岁嘛，在不便细究的四十开外某处——而这会儿，她本可以同我恩爱调笑！我告诉你，那位休戈宁小姐是个没算计的女人！还有命运——哦，命运，命运也是个爱撒泼的贱货！」我喊道，朝着一位凭空想象出来的拉刻西斯（Lachesis，命运三女神之一）鼻子底下晃了晃拳头。

===Original===
“You appear,” said Peter, drily, “to be unusually well-informed as to what is going on at Selwoode.”

===Chinese===
「说起塞尔伍德的动静，」彼得干巴巴地说，「你未免消息灵通得异乎寻常。」

===Original===
“You flatter me,” I answered, as with proper modesty. “You must remember that there are maids at Selwoode. You must remember that my man Byam, is—and will be until that inevitable day when he will attempt to blackmail me, and I shall kill him in the most lingering fashion I can think of—that Byam is, I say, something of a diplomatist.”

===Chinese===
「您过奖了，」我应道，谦逊得恰到好处。「您得记得，塞尔伍德有的是侍女。您得记得，我的仆人拜亚姆（Byam）是——而且在那一桩难免的日子到来之前都会是，那一日他终将试图敲诈我，而我会用想得出的最缠绵的死法结果了他——我是说，拜亚姆这个人，多少算得一位外交家。」

===Original===
Mr. Blagden regarded me with disapproval.

===Chinese===
布拉格登先生满不以为然地打量着我。

===Original===
“So you’ve been sending your nigger cousin over to Selwoode to spy for you! You’re a damn cad, you know, Bob,” he pensively observed. “Now most people think that when you carry on like a lunatic you’re simply acting on impulse. I don’t. I believe you plan it out a week ahead. I sometimes think you are the most adroit and unblushing looker-out for number one I ever knew; and I can’t for the life of me understand why I don’t turn you out of doors.”

===Chinese===
「原来你一直在支使你那黑鬼表亲往塞尔伍德跑，替你当探子！你是个十足的下流坯，你要知道，鲍勃，」他若有所思地说。「大多数人以为，你像疯子一般胡闹的时候，不过是凭一时冲动行事。我可不这么想。我相信你是提前一个礼拜就盘算好了。我有时觉得，你是我平生仅见的手脚最利索、脸皮最厚、专替『一号先生』打算的家伙；而我怎么也想不明白，自己为什么还不把你撵出门去。」

===Original===
“I don’t know where you picked up your manners,” said I, reflectively, “but it must have been in devilish low company. I would cut your acquaintance, Peter, if I could afford it.” Then I fell to pacing up and down the floor. “I incline, as you have somewhat grossly suggested, to a certain favouritism among the digits. And why the deuce shouldn’t I? A fortune is the only thing I need. I have good looks, you know, of a sort; ah, I’m not vain, but both my glass and a number of women have been kind enough to reassure me on this particular point. And that I have a fair amount of wits my creditors will attest, who have lived promise-crammed for the last year or two, feeding upon air like chameleons. Then I have birth—not that good birth ensures anything but bad habits though, for you will observe that, by some curious freak of nature, an old family-tree very seldom produces anything but wild oats. And, finally, I have position. I can introduce my wife into the best society; ah, yes, you may depend upon it, Peter, she will have the privilege of meeting the very worst and stupidest and silliest people in the country on perfectly equal terms. You will perceive, then, that the one desirable thing I lack is wealth. And this I shall naturally expect my wife to furnish. So, the point is settled, and you may give me a cigarette.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道你这一套礼数是打哪儿捡来的，」我若有所思地说，「不过想必是在下流得可以的交际场里。倘若我出得起这份代价，彼得，我早同你绝交了。」随后我起身，在屋里踱来踱去。「我承认，正如你那番不大文雅的暗示，我在数目字中间，确有偏心的一两位。可我到底为什么就不该偏心呢？一笔家财，是我唯一缺的东西。我有一副过得去的相貌，你是知道的；啊，我并不虚荣，不过我的镜子和好些位女人，都已好心地在这一特定的要点上，向我再三担保过了。至于我有相当分量的头脑，我的债主们可以作证——这一两年来，他们全凭塞得满满的期许过活，像变色龙一般以空气果腹。其次，我有出身——虽说好出身除了坏习气，什么也担保不了，因为你会观察到，出于造化某种古怪的恶作剧，一棵古老的家谱树，几乎从来结不出别的，只结野燕麦——少年人的荒唐勾当。最后，我有地位。我能把我的妻子引进最上流的社交界；啊，是的，你只管放心，彼得，她将享有一桩特权：同全国最坏、最蠢、最傻的那些人物，在绝对平等的条件下往来。于是你可以看出，我所缺的唯一一件可欲之物，便是财富。而这一项，我自然指望我的妻子来供应。所以，事情就这么定了，你可以给我一支烟了。」

===Original===
Peter handed me the case, with a snort. “You are a hopelessly conceited ass,” Mr. Blagden was pleased to observe, “for otherwise you would have learned, by this, that you’ll, most likely, never have the luck of Charteris, and land a woman who will take it as a favour that you let her pay your bills. God knows you’ve angled for enough of ’em!”

===Chinese===
彼得哼了一声，把烟盒递给我。「你是一头无可救药的自大蠢驴，」布拉格登先生欣然点评道，「不然的话，你到这会儿总该学乖了：你多半永远交不上查特里斯那样的好运，钓不到一个肯把替你付账视作领受恩宠的女人。天知道你朝多少人下过竿子！」

===Original===
“You are painfully coarse, Peter,” I pointed out, with a sigh. “Indeed, your general lack of refinement might easily lead one to think you owed your millions to your own thrifty industry, or some equally unpleasant attribute, rather than to your uncle’s very commendable and lucrative innovation in the line of—well, I remember it was something extremely indigestible, but, for the moment, I forget whether it was steam-reapers or a new sort of pickle. Yes, in a great many respects, you are hopelessly parvenuish. This cigarette-case, for instance—studded with diamonds and engraved with a monogram big enough for a coach-door! Why, Peter, it simply reeks with the ostentation of honestly acquired wealth—and with very good tobacco, too, by the way. I shall take it, for I am going for a walk, and I haven’t any of my own. And some day I shall pawn this jewelled abortion, Peter—pawn it for much fine gold; and upon the proceeds I shall make merriment for myself and for my friends.” And I pocketed the case.

===Chinese===
「你粗俗得叫人难受，彼得，」我叹口气指出。「说实在的，你这一身普遍的缺乏教养，很容易叫人以为，你那几百万是靠你自己勤俭的营生、或某种同样叫人败兴的品性挣来的，而不是得自你叔父在——嗯，那个行当里一项颇为可嘉又颇为生财的革新；哦，我记得那是种极难消化的东西，只是眼下我记不清，究竟是蒸汽收割机，还是一种新式的腌菜了。是的，在许许多多方面，你都暴发户气得无可救药。就拿这只烟盒来说——缀满钻石，錾着一行花押，大得够装点一扇马车的车门！哎呀，彼得，它通身散发着正道挣来的财富那股炫耀气——顺带一句，还混着上好烟草的气味。我这就把它带上了，因为我要去散个步，自己却一支烟也没有。而且有朝一日，我要把这件镶宝的怪胎当掉，彼得——当它一大笔足赤的金子；再拿那笔进项，给我自己、给我的朋友们，好好张罗一场快活。」说着，我把烟盒揣进了口袋。

===Original===
“That’s all very well,” Peter growled, “but you needn’t try to change the subject. You know you *have* angled after any number of rich women who have had sense enough, thank God, to refuse you. You didn’t use to be—but now you’re quite notoriously good-for-nothing.”

===Chinese===
「话说得倒好听，」彼得低吼道，「可你休想岔开话头。你自己知道，你*分明*朝数不清的有钱女人抛过竿子，而她们，托上天的福，还算是脑子够用，把你回绝了。你从前并不——可如今你是个尽人皆知的一无是处。」

===Original===
“It is the one blemish,” said I, sweetly, “upon an otherwise perfect character. And it is true,” I continued, after an interval of meditation, “that I have, in my time, encountered some very foolish women. There was, for instance, Elena Barry-Smith, who threw me over for Warwick Risby; and Celia Reindan, who had the bad taste to prefer Teddy Anstruther; and Rosalind Jemmett, who is, very inconsiderately, going to marry Tom Gelwix, instead of me. These were staggeringly foolish women, Peter, but while their taste is bad, their dinners are good, so I have remained upon the best of terms with them. They have trodden me under their feet, but I am the long worm that has no turning. Moreover, you are doubtless aware of the axiomatic equality between the fish in the sea and those out of it. I hope before long to better my position in life. I hope—Ah, well, that would scarcely interest you. Good morning, Peter. And I trust, when I return,” I added, with chastening dignity, “that you will evince a somewhat more Christian spirit toward the world in general, and that your language will be rather less reminiscent of the bloodstained buccaneer of historical fiction.”

===Chinese===
「那是一处唯一的瑕疵，」我甜甜地说，「瑕在一副其余方面都完美无缺的品性上。而且也是实情，」我沉思片刻，又接着说，「我平生确实遭遇过一些蠢得可以的女人。比如埃莱娜·巴里-史密斯，她甩了我，跟了沃里克·里斯比；比如塞莉娅·赖因丹，她品位欠佳，偏偏相中了特迪·安斯特瑟（Teddy Anstruther）；再比如罗瑟琳·杰米特，她极不体谅人心，眼看就要嫁给汤姆·盖尔威克斯（Tom Gelwix），而不是我。这几位都是蠢得骇人的女人，彼得，不过她们的品位虽坏，餐桌却好，所以我同她们始终保持着顶好的交情。她们把我踩在了脚底下，可我是一条永无翻身之日的长虫。再者，海里的鱼同捞出了海的鱼之间那条公理式的平等，你无疑早有耳闻。我希望不久便把我的人生境遇改善一步。我希望——啊，罢了，那话头想必引不起你的兴致。早上好，彼得。还有，我相信，等我回来的时候，」我补上一句，带着一副诲人式的庄严，「你对普天之下的世人，会多显出几分基督徒的精神，你的谈吐，也会少几分历史小说里那血债斑斑的海盗气味。」

===Original===
“You’re a grinning buffoon,” said Peter. “You’re a fat Jack-pudding. You’re an ass. Where are you going, anyway?”

===Chinese===
「你是个咧着嘴傻笑的丑角，」彼得说。「你是条肥嘟嘟的『杰克布丁』（Jack-pudding，旧时滑稽戏里的丑角）。你是头蠢驴。反正，你到底要去哪儿？」

===Original===
“I am going,” said I, “to the extreme end of Gridlington. Afterward I am going to climb the wall that stands between Gridlington and Selwoode.”

“And after that?” said Peter.

I gave a gesture. “Why, after that,” said I, “fortune will favour the brave. And I, Peter, am very, very brave.”

===Chinese===
「我要去，」我说，「格里灵顿最远的尽头。随后，我要去翻那道立在格里灵顿与塞尔伍德之间的墙。」

「翻过之后呢？」彼得说。

我做了个手势。「嗐，翻过之后，」我说，「命运女神自会眷顾勇者。而我，彼得，是非常、非常勇敢的。」

===Original===
Then I departed, whistling. In view of all my memories it had been strangely droll to worry Peter Blagden into an abuse of marrying for money. For this was on the twenty-eighth of April, the anniversary of the day that Stella had died, you may remember.﻿

===Chinese===
随后我吹着口哨动身了。念及我全部的记忆，把彼得·布拉格登撩拨得破口大骂起「为钱结婚」来，实在是异样地好笑。因为这一天是四月二十八日——你或许记得，正是斯特拉去世的那个日子的周年。……

### III / 三

===Original===
And a half-hour subsequently, true to my word, I was scaling a ten-foot stone wall, thickly overgrown with ivy. At the top of it I paused, and sat down to take breath and to meditate, my legs meanwhile bedangling over an as flourishing Italian garden as you would wish to see.

===Chinese===
半小时之后，我不失信约，正攀着一道十英尺高的石墙，墙面上爬满了厚厚的常春藤。到了墙顶，我歇了歇，坐下来喘气、出神，两条腿悬在墙那边晃荡着——底下是一座意大利式的花园，繁盛得再合你心意不过。

===Original===
“Now, I wonder,” I queried, of my soul, “what will be next? There is a very cheerful uncertainty about what will be next. It may be a spring-gun, and it may be a bulldog, and it may be a susceptible heiress. But it is apt to be—No, it isn’t,” I amended, promptly; “it is going to be an angel. Or perhaps it is going to be a dream. She can’t be real, you know—I am probably just dreaming her. I would be quite certain I was just dreaming her, if this wall were not so humpy and uncomfortable. For it stands to reason, I would not be fool enough to dream of such unsympathetic iron spikes as I am sitting on.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我倒要问问，」我探问自己的灵魂，「接下来会是什么？接下来是什么，这里面有一种令人愉快的没准儿。也许是一杆弹簧枪，也许是一条斗牛犬，也许是一位情窦易开的女继承人。但它多半会是——不，不对，」我当即改口；「它多半会是一位天使。要不然，多半会是一场梦。她不可能是真人，你知道——多半我只是正梦见她。倘若这道墙不是这样疙疙瘩瘩、硌得难受，我几乎要笃定我只是梦见她了。因为按理说，我总不至于蠢到梦见自己正坐在这样不讲情面的铁刺上。」

===Original===
“Perhaps you are not aware,” hazarded a soprano voice, “that this is private property?”

===Chinese===
「也许您有所不知，」一个女高音似的嗓音小心试探道，「此处乃是私家产业？」

===Original===
“Why, no,” said I, very placidly; “on the contrary I was just thinking it must be heaven. And I am tolerably certain,” I commented further, in my soul, “that you are one of the more influential seraphim.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，倒真不知，」我十分平和地说；「恰恰相反，我方才正在想，这里必定是天堂。而且我颇有几分把握，」我在灵魂里进一步点评道，「您是众撒拉弗中较有势力的一位。」

===Original===
The girl had lifted her brows. She sat upon a semicircular stone bench, some twenty feet from the wall, and had apparently been reading, for a book lay open in her lap. She now inspected me, with a sort of languid wonder in her eyes, and I returned the scrutiny with unqualified approval in mine.

===Chinese===
那姑娘已扬起了眉。她坐在一张半圆形的石凳上，离墙约莫二十英尺，先前显然在读书，因为一本摊开的书正搁在她膝头。此刻她端详起我来，眼里带着几分慵懒的讶异；而我回敬这道端详时，眼里盛着的是全然的赞许。

===Original===
And in this I had reason. The heiress of Selwoode was eminently good to look upon.

===Chinese===
而这一点上，我自有道理。塞尔伍德的这位女继承人，是格外出众地耐看的。

## XXIV / 二十四

He Reconciles Sentiment and Reason / 他调停了情感与理性

### I / 一

===Original===
So I regarded her for a rather lengthy interval, considering meanwhile, with an immeasurable content how utterly and entirely impossible it would always be to describe her.

===Chinese===
就这样，我打量了她好一阵子，一面打量，一面怀着一份无可计量的满足，思忖着要描摹她，将是何等彻底、何等干净地绝无可能——而且永远绝无可能。

===Original===
Clearly, it would be out of the question to trust to words, however choicely picked, for, upon inspection, there was a delightful ambiguity about every one of this girl’s features that defied such idiotic makeshifts. Her eyes, for example, I noted with a faint thrill of surprise, just escaped being brown by virtue of an amber glow they had; what colour, then, was I conscientiously to call them?

===Chinese===
显然，把指望寄托在字眼上——哪怕挑得再精——是问也不必问的：因为细细端详起来，这位姑娘的每一处容貌，都带着一种可人的含混，公然不把这等愚蠢的权宜之物放在眼里。譬如她的眼睛，我怀着一阵轻微的惊喜注意到，只因其间浮着一层琥珀的光，便差一点点算不得褐色了；那么，凭良心说，我该唤它们作什么颜色才好？

===Original===
And her hair I found a bewildering, though pleasing, mesh of shadow and sunlight, all made up of multitudinous graduations of some anonymous colour that seemed to vary with the light you chanced to see it in, through the whole gamut of bronze and chestnut and gold; and where, pray, in the bulkiest lexicon, in the very weightiest thesaurus, was I to find the adjective which could, if but in desperation, be applied to hair like that without trenching on sacrilege? … For it was spring, you must remember, and I was twenty-five.

===Chinese===
至于她的头发，我发现那是一张令人迷乱、却不失悦目的网，由暗影与日光织成，通体是某种无名颜色的千层万级，仿佛随你碰巧在何种光里看它，便在青铜、栗色与金黄的整列音阶间流转不定；那么请问，在最厚的一部大词典里、在最沉的一部类语大典里，我又上哪儿找得出一个形容词，哪怕情急之下，加得给这样一头头发而不至近于亵渎呢？……须知那正是春天，你记得，而我那年二十五岁。

===Original===
So that in my appraisal, you may depend upon it, her lips were quickly passed over as a dangerous topic, and were dismissed with the mental statement that they were red and not altogether unattractive. Whereas her cheeks baffled me for a time—but always with a haunting sense of familiarity—till I had, at last, discovered they reminded me of those little tatters of cloud that sometimes float about the setting sun—those irresolute wisps which cannot quite decide whether to be pink or white, and waver through their tiny lives between the two colours.

===Chinese===
于是在我的估量里——这一点你只管放心——她的双唇很快便被当作一个危险话题匆匆绕过，只用心里一句话打发了：它们是红的，而且并非全然不动人。她的双颊倒叫我为难了一阵——心里始终萦绕着一种似曾相识之感——直到末了我才发现，它们令我想起落日四围有时飘浮的那些小小云絮——那些游移不定的云丝，拿不准究竟该做粉红还是该做白，便在短短一生里，于两色之间摇曳不定。

### II / 二

===Original===
To this effect, then, I discoursed with my soul, what time I sat upon the wall-top and smiled and kicked my heels to and fro among the ivy. By and by, though, the girl sighed.

===Chinese===
于是我便如此这般，同我的灵魂讲论起来，一面讲，一面坐在墙头顶上，含着笑，两只脚跟在常春藤间前前后后地晃荡。不过过了不多时，姑娘叹了口气。

===Original===
“You are placing me in an extremely unpleasant position,” she complained, as if wearily. “Would you mind returning to your sanatorium and allowing me to go on reading? For I am interested in my book, and I can’t possibly go on in any comfort so long as you elect to perch up there like Humpty-Dumpty, and grin like seven dozen Cheshire cats.”

===Chinese===
「你正把我搁在一个极不痛快的境地，」她抱怨道，语气象是倦倦的。「可否请你回你的疗养院去，容我接着读书？我这本书正读到兴头上，只要你打定主意像个矮胖子（Humpty-Dumpty）似的蹲踞在那上头，又咧着嘴笑得赛过七打柴郡猫（Cheshire cats），我便无论如何也读不安稳。」

===Original===
“Now, that,” I spoke, in absent wise, “is but another instance of the widely prevalent desire to have me serve as scapegoat for the sins of all humanity. I am being blamed now for sitting on top of this wall. One would think I wanted to sit here. One would actually think,” I cried, and raised my eyes to heaven, “that sitting on the very humpiest kind of iron spikes was my favorite form of recreation! No—in the interests of justice,” I continued, and fell into a milder tone, “I must ask you to place the blame where it more rightfully belongs. The injuries which are within the moment being inflicted on my sensitive nature, and, incidentally, upon my not overstocked wardrobe, I am willing to pass over. But the claims of justice are everywhere paramount. Miss Hugonin, and Miss Hugonin alone, is responsible for my present emulation of Mohammed’s coffin, and upon that responsibility I am compelled to insist.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，这个么，」我心不在焉地说，「不过是又一桩例证，说明那种巴望拿我充当全人类罪孽替罪羊的普遍世风。这会儿我又为坐在这堵墙顶上挨起埋怨来了。人家怕只道是我自己乐意坐在这儿呢。人家简直要当真以为，」我扬起眼来望着老天，嚷道，「坐在这世上顶疙疙瘩瘩的一等铁刺尖上，正是我最心爱的消遣！不——为了公道起见，」我接着往下说，语气放缓和了些，「我必须请你把罪责放回它更名正言顺的归属处去。我这颗敏感的天性此刻正遭受的种种戕害——顺带说一句，还有我那件并不富余的行头正遭受的戕害——我愿意概不追究。可是公道的要求，无论在何处都高于一切。休戈宁（Hugonin）小姐，而且唯有休戈宁小姐，须为我眼下这般效法穆罕默德的悬棺担待责任，而这份责任，我是不得不坚持的。」

===Original===
“May one suggest,” she queried gently, “that you are probably—mistaken?”

===Chinese===
「可否容人进一言，」她轻轻地问道，「您多半是——弄错了？」

===Original===
I sketched a bow. “Recognising your present point of view,” said I, gallantly, “I thank you for the kindly euphemism. But may one allowably demonstrate the fallacy of this same point of view? I thank you: for silence, I am told, is proverbially equal to assent. I am, then, one Robert Townsend, by birth a gentleman, by courtesy an author, by inclination an idler, and by lucky chance a guest of Mr. Peter Blagden, whose flourishing estate extends indefinitely yonder to the rear of my coattails. My hobby chances to be gardening. I am a connoisseur, an admirer, a devotee of gardens. It is, indeed, hereditary among the Townsends; a love for gardens runs in our family just as a love for gin runs in less favoured races. It is with us an irresistible passion. The very founder of our family—one Adam, whom you may have heard of—was a gardener. Owing to the unfortunate loss of his position, the family since then has sunken somewhat in the world; but time and poverty alike have proven powerless against our horticultural tastes and botanical inclinations. And then,” cried I, with a flourish, “and then, what follows logically?”

===Chinese===
我虚虚地鞠了一躬。「体察到您眼下的视角，」我殷勤地说，「我谢谢您这句好心的委婉语。不过，可否容我摆一摆道理，证明这同一个视角实在站不住脚？多谢默许：因为人家告诉我，俗话早已说透，沉默即等于默认。那么，在下便是罗伯特·汤森其人：论出身，是位绅士；论虚衔，是位作家；论天性，是个闲人；论侥幸造化，是彼得·布拉格登先生的座上客——他那份兴旺的产业，就在我燕尾后摆的再那头，漫无际涯地铺展过去。在下的癖好，偏巧是园艺。我是园林的鉴赏家，园林的赞叹者，园林的信徒。这在汤森家，委实是祖传的：爱园林的心思在我们这一族里代代相承，恰如爱金酒的心思在那几家没这样蒙福的门第里代代相承。这在我们是一股抗拒不了的热情。我们这一族的开山鼻祖——一位亚当（Adam），您想必听说过——本人便是园丁。只因他不慎丢了那份差事，家族打那以后在世间便略见式微；然而任是时光还是贫穷，在我们的园艺趣味与草木癖好面前，都已证明了无能为力。那么，」我意气飞扬地嚷道，「那么，接下去顺理成章的该是什么？」

===Original===
“Why, if you are not more careful,” she languidly made answer, “I am afraid that, owing to the laws of gravitation, a broken neck is what follows logically.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，你要是再不当心些，」她懒懒地作答，「我怕多亏了万有引力定律，接下去顺理成章的便是摔断脖子了。」

===Original===
“You are a rogue,” I commented, in my soul, “and I like you all the better for it.”

===Chinese===
「你是个促狭鬼，」我在心里评点道，「而且正因这一层，我愈发喜欢你了。」

===Original===
Aloud, I stated: “What follows is that we can no more keep away from a creditable sort of garden than a moth can from a lighted candle. Consider, then, my position. Here am I on one side of the wall, and with my peach-tree, to be sure—but on the other side is one of the most famous masterpieces of formal gardening in the whole country. Am I to blame if I succumb to the temptation? Surely not,” I argued; “for surely to any fair-minded person it will be at once apparent that I am brought to my present very uncomfortable position upon the points of these very humpy iron spikes by a simple combination of atavism and injustice—atavism because hereditary inclination draws me irresistibly to the top of the wall, and injustice because Miss Hugonin’s perfectly unreasonable refusal to admit visitors prevents my coming any farther. Surely, that is at once apparent?”

===Chinese===
出声的部分，我则申明如下：「顺理成章接下去的是：见了像样的园子，我们便如飞蛾见了点亮的蜡烛，再没法子躲开。那么，请设身处地替我想一想。墙这边是我——不消说，还有我的桃树——可墙那一边，却是全国数一数二的规则式园林杰作之一。倘若我缴械于这诱惑，难道该怪我么？断乎不该，」我论证道；「因为任何心存公道的人，必定一眼便看得分明：我之所以落得眼下这个极不舒服的境地、坐上这些疙疙瘩瘩的铁刺尖，无非是返祖与不公二者的简单合谋——说是返祖，因为遗传的癖好拽着我，不由分说，直拽上墙头；说是不公，因为休戈宁小姐全然不近情理、拒不见客，害得我再无法前进一步。这总是一眼便看得分明的吧？」

===Original===
But now the girl yielded to my grave face, and broke into a clear, rippling carol of mirth. She laughed from the chest, this woman. And perched in insecure discomfort on my wall, I found time to rejoice that I had finally discovered that rarity of rarities, a woman who neither giggles nor cackles, but has found the happy mean between these two abominations, and knows how to laugh.

===Chinese===
可是这会儿，姑娘到底受不住我这张一本正经的脸，迸出一串清亮如流泉的欢笑来。这个女人笑，是从胸腔里笑出来的。而我蹲在自家墙头那摇摇欲坠、硌得难受的高位上，竟还有闲情庆幸：自己到底发现了奇中之奇——一个女人，既不嗤嗤傻笑，也不嘎嘎怪笑，却在这两大可憎之间寻得了中庸之道，深谙笑的艺术。

===Original===
“I have heard of you, Mr. Townsend,” she said at last. “Oh, yes, I have heard a deal of you. And I remember now that I never heard you were suspected of sanity.”

===Chinese===
「我听说过您，汤森先生，」她末了说道。「哦，是的，您的事我听了不老少。而且我这会儿想起来了：我可从来不曾听说，有谁疑心过您神志健全。」

===Original===
“Common sense,” I informed her, from my pedestal, “is confined to that decorous class of people who never lose either their tempers or their umbrellas. Now, I haven’t any temper to speak of—or not at least in the presence of ladies—and, so far, I have managed to avoid laying aside anything whatever for a rainy day; so that it stands to reason I must possess uncommon sense.”

===Chinese===
「常识，」我在墙头高位上开导她道，「是专属于那一类规行矩步之士的：他们从不发脾气，也从不丢雨伞。至于我呢，脾气谈不上有——至少在女士们跟前谈不上——而且直到如今，我总算不曾为着下雨天，预先撂下攒过任何一样东西；由此顺理推想，我怀有的必然是非常之识。」

===Original===
“If that is the case,” said the girl, “you will kindly come down from that wall and attempt to behave like a rational being.”

===Chinese===
「倘若当真如此，」姑娘说，「那就请你从那墙上下来，试着行止得像个有理性的生物吧。」

===Original===
I was down—as the phrase runs—in the twinkling of a bedpost. On which side of the wall, I leave you to imagine.

===Chinese===
我下来了——照那句俗话说的，一根床柱还没来得及眨眼的工夫。至于下在了墙的哪一边，就留给你去想象了。

===Original===
“—For I am sure,” the girl continued, “that I—that Margaret, I should say—would not object in the least to your seeing the gardens, since they interest you so tremendously. I’m Avis Beechinor, you know—Miss Hugonin’s cousin. So, if you like, we will consider that a proper introduction, Mr. Townsend, and I will show you the gardens, if—if you really care to see them.”

===Chinese===
「——因为我确信，我——呃，该说玛格丽特（Margaret）——准不会有一丝一毫介意您来游园，既然您对园子的兴味这样浓。我是艾维斯·比奇诺（Avis Beechinor），您知道——休戈宁小姐的表妹。那么，您若愿意，咱们便不妨把方才这番话当作一场正式的引见，汤森先生，由我领您游园——倘若——倘若您当真想看的话。」

===Original===
My face, I must confess, had fallen slightly. Up to this moment, I had not a suspicion but that it was Miss Hugonin I was talking to: and I now reconsidered, with celerity, the information Byam had brought me from Selwoode.

===Chinese===
我必须承认，我的脸微微沉了下去。直到此刻，我从未起过半点疑心，一直以为同我说话的正是休戈宁小姐：于是我此刻赶紧把拜亚姆从塞尔伍德（Selwoode）捎回来的消息，重新掂量了一遍。

===Original===
“For, when I come to think of it,” I reflected, “he simply said she was older than Miss Hugonin. I embroidered the tale so glibly for Peter’s benefit that I was deceived by my own ornamentations. I had looked for corkscrew ringlets and false teeth agleam like a new bathtub in Miss Hugonin’s cousin—not an absolutely, supremely, inexpressibly unthinkable beauty like this!” I cried, in my soul. “Older! Why, good Lord, Miss Hugonin must be an infant in arms!”

===Chinese===
「因为细想起来，」我反省道，「他分明只说了她比休戈宁小姐年长。这段话我为了说给彼得听，添枝加叶编得那样顺溜，到头来竟连自己也着了自家花巧的道。我原只当休戈宁小姐这位表妹，该顶着螺旋卷的发圈，一口假牙亮得赛过新浴缸——哪里想得到，竟是这样一位绝对、超绝、难以言传、不可思议的美人！」我在心里喊道。「年长！嗐，老天爷，那位休戈宁小姐岂不还得裹在襁褓里！」

===Original===
But my audible discourse was prefaced with an eloquent gesture. “If I’d care!” I said. “Haven’t I already told you I was a connoisseur in gardens? Why, simply look, Miss Beechinor!” I exhorted her, and threw out my hands in a large pose of admiration. “Simply regard those yew-hedges, and parterres, and grassy amphitheatres, and palisades, and statues, and cascades, and everything—*everything* that goes to make a formal garden the most delectable sight in the world! Simply feast your eyes upon those orderly clipped trees and the fantastic patterns those flowers are laid out in! Why, upon my word, it looks as if all four books of Euclid had suddenly burst into blossom! And you ask me if I would *care*! Ah, it is evident *you* are not a connoisseur in gardens, Miss Beechinor!”

===Chinese===
不过我出声的那篇议论，先配了一个雄辩的手势。「还问我想不想！」我说。「我不是早已告诉过您，我是园林鉴赏家了么？哎呀，您只管看呀，比奇诺小姐！」我劝勉着她，双手往开处一撒，摆出一个大大的赞叹架势。「您只管瞧瞧那些紫杉树篱、花坛、草坡圆场、篱栅、雕像、叠瀑，还有一切——*一切*造就一座规则式园林、使它成为世上最可心悦目之景致的东西！您只管把那些修剪得整整齐齐的树木、把那些花卉铺排出的奇幻纹样，饱览个够！哎呀，说句实在话，这模样，简直像欧几里得（Euclid）的全四卷书忽然一齐开了花！而您倒还问我*想不想*！啊，显而易见，*您*才不是园林鉴赏家呢，比奇诺小姐！」

===Original===
And I had started on my way into this one, when the girl stopped me.

===Chinese===
而我已然抬步，要进这园子里去了，姑娘却叫住了我。

===Original===
“This must be yours,” she said. “You must have spilled it coming over the wall, Mr. Townsend.”

===Chinese===
「这准是您的，」她说。「翻墙的时候，您准是把它弄掉了，汤森先生。」

===Original===
It was Peter’s cigarette-case.

===Chinese===
那是彼得的香烟盒。

===Original===
“Why, dear me, yes!” I assented, affably. “Do you know, now, I would have been tremendously sorry to lose that? It is a sort of present—an unbirthday present from a quite old friend.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，可不是嘛！」我和颜悦色地应承道。「您可知道，真要丢了它，我会心疼得不轻？这算得上一件礼物——一位相交颇久的老朋友送的一件『非生日礼物』。」

===Original===
She turned it over in her hand.

===Chinese===
她把它在手里翻过来掉过去。

===Original===
“It’s very handsome,” she marvelled. “Such a pretty monogram! Does it stand for Poor Idiot Boy?”

===Chinese===
「真讲究，」她啧啧称奇。「这么漂亮的花押！它代表的是『可怜的白痴男孩』（Poor Idiot Boy）么？」

===Original===
“Eh?” said I. “P.I.B., you mean? No, that stands for Perfectly Immaculate Behaviour. My friend gave it to me because, he said, I was so good. And—oh, well, he added a few things to that—partial sort of a friend, you know—and, really—Why, really, Miss Beechinor, it would embarrass me to tell you what he added,” I protested, and modestly waved the subject aside.

===Chinese===
「嗯？」我说。「您是说 P.I.B.？不，它代表的是『完美无瑕的品行』（Perfectly Immaculate Behaviour）。我朋友把它送我，是因为——他说——我这人太『好』了。而且——哦，这个——他在那句话后头还添了几句——您知道，是位有几分偏私的朋友——而且，说真的——哎呀，说真的，比奇诺小姐，他添的那几句，说出来我实在难为情，」我分辩道，随即谦逊地一挥手，把这个话题拂到了一旁。

===Original===
“Now that,” my meditations ran, “is the absolute truth. Peter did tell me I was good. And it really would embarrass me to tell her he added ‘for-nothing.’ So, this far, I have been a model of veracity.”

===Chinese===
「方才这番话，」我心里的念头这样淌着，「可是不折不扣的实话。彼得确实说过我『好』。而他添上的那半句『好得一无是处』，说给她听，我也确实要难为情。所以，到这一步为止，我一直是个诚实的楷模。」

===Original===
Then I took the case—gaining thereby the bliss of momentary contact with a velvet-soft trifle that seemed, somehow, to set my own grosser hand a-tingle—and I cried: “Now, Miss Beechinor, you must show me the pergola. I am excessively partial to pergolas.”

===Chinese===
随后我接过烟盒——由此赢得了片刻的至福：指尖同一件天鹅绒般绵软的小物什轻轻一触，那触感不知怎的，竟把我这只粗蠢的手都触得微微发麻——我便嚷道：「好了，比奇诺小姐，您务必领我去看藤架了。我对藤架偏爱得实在过了分。」

===Original===
And in my soul, I wondered what a pergola looked like, and why on earth I had been fool enough to waste the last three days in bedeviling Peter, and how under the broad canopy of heaven I could ever have suffered from the delusion that I had seen a really adorable woman before today.

===Chinese===
而在我心底，我正纳闷着：藤架究竟是个什么模样？自己究竟犯了什么傻，竟把过去整整三天，全耗在捉弄彼得上头？还有，普天之下、朗朗乾坤，我从前怎么会害那样一场错觉，竟以为自己今天之前，就见识过一位真正可爱的女人？

### III / 三

===Original===
But, “She is entirely too adorable,” I reasoned with myself, some three-quarters of an hour later. “In fact, I regard it as positively inconsiderate in any impecunious young person to venture to upset me in the way she has done. Why, my heart is pounding away inside me like a trip-hammer, and I am absolutely lightheaded with goodwill and charity and benevolent intentions toward the entire universe! Oh, Avis, Avis, you know you hadn’t any right to put me in this insane state of mind!”

===Chinese===
可是，「她也未免太可爱了，」约莫三刻钟之后，我这样同自己评理。「说实在的，任何一个身无分文的年轻人，斗胆把我搅得像她搅的这样天翻地覆，我只认为简直是不体谅人。哎呀，我这颗心在腔子里怦怦地撞，活像一柄汽锤；而且我满腔满怀都是好意、慈善和对全宇宙的仁厚宏愿，简直晕晕乎乎、头轻脚飘！哦，艾维斯，艾维斯，你自己知道，你根本无权把我弄成这副痴狂的心性！」

===Original===
I was, at this moment, retracing my steps toward the spot where I had climbed the wall between Gridlington and Selwoode, but I paused now to outline a reproachful gesture in the direction from which I came.

===Chinese===
此时此刻，我正循原路往回走，走向早先翻墙的地方——格里灵顿（Gridlington）与塞尔伍德之间的那堵墙——可我又停住脚，朝来路的方向，虚描了一个责备的手势。

===Original===
“What do you mean by having such a name?” I queried, sadly. “Avis! Why, it is the very soul of music, clear, and sweet and as insistent as a bird-call, an unforgettable lyric in four letters! It is just the sort of name a fellow cannot possibly forget. Why couldn’t you have been named Polly or Lena or Margaret, or something commonplace like that, Avis—dear?”

===Chinese===
「你长这么一个名字，是什么意思？」我悲切切地质问道。「艾维斯！哎呀，这名字简直是音乐的灵魂，清朗，甜美，又像一声鸟啼那样萦萦不去，是四个字母写就的一首忘不掉的抒情诗！这正是任凭哪个家伙也断然忘它不掉的那种名字。当初怎么不给你取名叫波莉，叫莉娜，叫玛格丽特，或者随便什么平庸些的名字呢，艾维斯——亲爱的？」

===Original===
And the juxtaposition of these words appealing to my sense of euphony, I repeated it, again and again, each time with a more relishing gusto. “Avis dear! dear Avis! dear, *dear* Avis!” I experimented. “Why, each one is more hopelessly unforgettable than the other! Oh, Avis dear, why are you so absolutely and entirely unforgettable all around? Why do you ripple all your words together in that quaint fashion till it sounds like a brook discoursing? Why did you crinkle up your eyes when I told you that as yet unbotanised flower was a *Calycanthus arithmelicus*? And why did you pout at me, Avis dear? A fellow finds it entirely too hard to forget things like that. And, oh, dear Avis, if you only knew what nearly happened when you pouted!”

===Chinese===
这两个字眼并作一处，恰恰悦了我耳朵的音感，我便一遍又一遍地念它，一遍比一遍咂摸得有味。「艾维斯，亲爱的！亲爱的艾维斯！亲爱的、*亲爱的*艾维斯！」我试验着。「哎呀，一个比一个更无可救药地忘不掉！哦，亲爱的艾维斯，你怎么会这样方方面面、彻头彻尾，教人怎么也忘不掉？你怎么把字字句句那样古怪地串成一道涟漪，听上去像一条小溪在侃侃而谈？我告诉你那朵尚未经植物学著录的花是 *Calycanthus arithmelicus*（『算术蜡梅』）时，你为什么把眼睛弯了又弯？你又为什么朝我撅嘴，亲爱的艾维斯？这样的种种，一个家伙只觉实在忘它不掉。还有，哦，亲爱的艾维斯，你若知道你撅嘴那会儿，险些儿出了什么事，该有多好！」

===Original===
I had come to the wall by this, but again I paused to lament.

===Chinese===
话说到这儿，我已走到了墙边，可我又停住脚，哀叹起来。

===Original===
“It is very inconsiderate of her, very thoughtless indeed. She might at least have asked my permission, before upsetting my plans in life. I had firmly intended to marry a rich woman, and now I am forming all sorts of preposterous notions—”

===Chinese===
「她这样做，太不体谅人了，实在欠考虑得很。打乱我一生的规划之前，她至少也该先问过我一声才是。我原本铁了心，要娶一位有钱的女人，而如今，我却在一茬一茬滋生种种荒唐透顶的念头——」

===Original===
Then, on the bench where I had first seen her, I perceived a book. It was the iron-gray book she had been reading when I interrupted her, and I now picked it up with a sort of reverence. I regarded it as an extremely lucky book.

===Chinese===
就在这时，在我初见她的那条长凳上，我望见了一本书。正是我打断她时她读着的那本铁灰色的书，此刻我怀着几分虔敬，把它拾了起来。在我眼里，这是一本福气大得不得了的书。

===Original===
Subsequently, “Good Lord!” said I, aloud, “what luck!”

===Chinese===
接着：「主啊！」我出声说道，「这运气！」

===Original===
For between the pages of Justus Miles Forman’s *Journey’s End*—serving as a bookmark, according to a not infrequent shiftless feminine fashion—lay a handkerchief. It was a flimsy, inadequate trifle, fringed with a tiny scallopy black border; and in one corner the letters M. E. A. H., all askew, contorted themselves into any number of flourishes and irrelevant tendrils.

===Chinese===
原来在贾斯特斯·迈尔斯·福尔曼（Justus Miles Forman）的《旅途尽头》（*Journey’s End*）的书页之间——遵照一种屡见不鲜、女人家没甚算计的做派，权充着书签——躺着一条手帕。那是一件轻飘飘、不顶事的小物什，缘着一圈细小的黑色扇贝滚边；一角上，M. E. A. H. 几个字母个个歪着身子，扭出数也数不清的花体涡卷和不相干的卷须。

===Original===
“Now M. E. A. H. does not stand by any stretch of the imagination for Avis Beechinor. Whereas it fits Margaret Elizabeth Anstruther Hugonin uncommonly well. I wonder now—?”

===Chinese===
「可是 M. E. A. H. 这几个字母，任凭你把想象抻到天边，也断然拼不出艾维斯·比奇诺；倒是同玛格丽特·伊丽莎白·安斯特瑟·休戈宁（Margaret Elizabeth Anstruther Hugonin），贴合得天衣无缝。我这会儿倒要琢磨琢磨——？」

===Original===
I wondered for a rather lengthy interval.

===Chinese===
我琢磨了相当长的一阵子。

===Original===
“So Byam was right, after all. And Peter was right, too. Oh, Robert Etheridge Townsend, your reputation must truly be malodorous, when at your approach timid heiresses seek shelter under an alias! ‘I have heard a deal of you, Mr. Townsend’—ah, yes, she had heard. She thought I would make love to her out of hand, I suppose, because she was wealthy—”

===Chinese===
「这么说来，拜亚姆到底没说错。彼得也没有错。哦，罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森啊，你的名声想必当真是发臭了的：你人还没到跟前，胆小的女继承人便要躲进一个假名字底下避难！『您的事我听了不老少，汤森先生』——啊，是啊，她是听过。我料想，她认定了我一照面便会不管不顾地向她求爱，只因她有钱——」

===Original===
I presently flung back my head and laughed.

===Chinese===
不多时，我便仰起头来，哈哈大笑。

===Original===
“Eh, well! I will let no sordid considerations stand in the way of my true interests. I will marry this Margaret Hugonin even though she is rich. You have begun the comedy, my lady, and I will play it to the end. Yes, I fell honestly in love with you when I thought you were nobody in particular. So I am going to marry this Margaret Hugonin if she will have me; and if she won’t, I am going to commit suicide on her doorstep, with a pathetic little note in my vest-pocket forgiving her in the most noble and wholesale manner for irrevocably blighting a future so rich in promise. Yes, that is exactly what I am going to do if she does not appreciate her wonderful good fortune. And if she’ll have me—why, I wouldn’t change places with the Pope of Rome or the Czar of all the Russias! Ah, no, not I! for I prefer, upon the whole, to be immeasurably, and insanely, and unreasonably, and unadulteratedly happy. Why, but just to think of an adorable girl like that having so much money!”

===Chinese===
「嗐，好吧！任凭什么铜臭的算计，也休想横到我真正利益的道上。这位玛格丽特·休戈宁，哪怕她有钱，我也娶定了。这出喜剧既已由您开了场，我的小姐，我便要把它演到收梢。是的，当我只当你是个无足轻重的路人时，我已经老老实实爱上了你。所以，只要她肯要我，我便要娶这位玛格丽特·休戈宁；她若不肯，我便上她家门槛去自尽，马甲口袋里揣一张楚楚可怜的小字条，以最崇高、最一揽子的气度原谅她——原谅她无可挽回地糟蹋了这样一幅大有可为的前程。是的，她若不识她这份天大的好运，我打算做的正是这个。而她若肯要我——嗐，便是拿罗马教皇来换、拿全俄罗斯的沙皇来换，我也不换！啊，不换，我才不换！因为权衡再三，我更乐意无限度地、痴痴癫癫地、蛮不讲理地、纯而又纯地快乐。哎呀，你只消想一想：这样一位可人儿，竟还揣着偌大一笔钱财！」

===Original===
All in all, my meditations were incoherent but very pleasurable.

===Chinese===
总而言之，我这一番冥想，前言不搭后语，却受用无比。

## XXV / 二十五

He Advances in the Attack on Selwoode / 他在进攻塞尔伍德中节节推进

### I / 一

===Original===
“Well?” said Peter.

“Well?” said I.
===Chinese===
「怎么样？」彼得说。

「怎么样？」我说。

===Original===
“What’s the latest quotation on heiresses?” Mr. Blagden demanded. “Was she cruel, my boy, or was she kind? Did she set the dog on you or have you thrashed by her father? I fancy both, for your present hilarity is suggestive of a gentleman in the act of attendance on his own funeral.” And Peter laughed, unctuously, for his gout slumbered.
===Chinese===
「女继承人眼下的最新行情如何？」布拉格登先生追问。「她待你残酷么，我的孩子，还是待你仁厚？她是放狗咬你了呢，还是叫她父亲使人把你揍了一顿？我看两样齐全，因为你眼下这份欢天喜地，活脱教人想到一位正出席自家葬礼的绅士。」彼得说罢油津津地笑了，因为他的痛风正睡得香甜。

===Original===
“His attempts at wit,” I reflectively confided to my wineglass, “while doubtless amiably intended, are, to his well-wishers, painful. I daresay, though, he doesn’t know it. We must, then, smile indulgently upon the elephantine gambols of what he is pleased to describe as his intellect.”
===Chinese===
「他这些卖弄机智的尝试，」我若有所思地对我那只酒杯倾吐，「用意虽则无疑是一片好心，可落在他那班盼他好的人眼里，却是一桩苦事。不过我料想，他自己并不知情。那么，我们也只好姑存宽容，笑看那头大象般的庞然大物——他美其名曰智力的那个东西——撒欢蹦跳了。」

===Original===
“Now, that,” Peter pointed out, “is not what I would term a courteous method of discussing a man at his own table. You are damn disagreeable this morning, Bob. So I know, of course, that you have come another cropper in your fortune-hunting.”
===Chinese===
「哎呀，这样的话，」彼得指出来，「照我看，可算不得在人家自家餐桌上谈论人家的客气法子。你今天上午别扭得实在要命，鲍勃。于是我自然明白，你那桩追求有钱女继承人的营生，又栽了一个跟头。」

===Original===
“Peter,” said I, in admiration, “your sagacity at times is almost human! I have spent a most enjoyable day, though,” I continued, idly. “I have been communing with Nature, Peter. She is about her spring-cleaning in the woods yonder, and everywhere I have seen traces of her getting things fixed for the summer. I have seen the sky, which was washed overnight, and the sun, which has evidently been freshly enamelled. I have seen the new leaves as they swayed and whispered over your extensive domains, with the fret of spring alert in every sap cell. I have seen the little birds as they hopped among said leaves and commented upon the scarcity of worms. I have seen the buxom flowers as they curtsied and danced above your flowerbeds like a miniature comic-opera chorus. And besides that—”
===Chinese===
「彼得，」我叹服地说，「你的睿智，有时简直近乎人类！不过话说回来，我这一天倒是过得再惬意不过，」我懒洋洋地接着说。「我一直在同大自然促膝谈心呢，彼得。她正在那边树林里忙她的春季大扫除，我一路行去，处处见着她为迎接夏天张罗打点的痕迹。我见了天空——那是一夜之间才洗濯过的；见了太阳——分明是新上了一层瓷釉的。我见了新生的叶子，它们在你这广袤的领地上空摇曳低语，每一颗汁液的细胞里，都警戒着春日的躁动。我见了小小雀儿，它们在上述叶子间蹦来跳去，议论着虫子的稀缺。我见了那些丰腴的花儿，在你的花床上屈膝行礼、婆娑起舞，活像一队小型轻歌剧的合唱队。除此之外——」

===Original===
“Yes?” said Peter, with a grin, “and besides that?”

“And besides that,” said I, firmly, “I have seen nothing.”
===Chinese===
「哦？」彼得咧嘴一笑，「除此之外呢？」

「除此之外，」我斩钉截铁地说，「我什么也没看见。」

===Original===
And internally I appraised this bloated Peter Blagden, and reflected that this was the man whom Stella had loved; and I appraised myself, and remembered that this had been the boy who once loved Stella. For, as I have said, it was the twenty-eighth of April, the day that Stella had died, two years ago.
===Chinese===
而我心里暗暗把这位大腹便便的彼得·布拉格登估量了一番，思忖道：这，便是斯特拉爱过的那个男人；我又把自己估量了一番，记起：这，便是当年爱过斯特拉的那个男孩。因为——如我已经说过的——那一天是四月二十八日，正是两年之前，斯特拉死去的日子。

### II / 二

===Original===
The next morning I discoursed with my soul, what time I sat upon the wall-top and smiled and kicked my heels to and fro among the ivy.
===Chinese===
第二天早晨，我又同我的灵魂讲论起来，一面讲，一面坐在墙头顶上，含着笑，两只脚跟在常春藤间前前后后地晃荡。

===Original===
“For, in spite of appearances,” I debated with myself, “it is barely possible that the handkerchief was not hers. She may have borrowed it or have got it by mistake, somehow. In which case, it is only reasonable to suppose that she will miss it, and ask me if I saw it; on the contrary, if the handkerchief is hers, she will naturally understand, when I return the book without it, that I have feloniously detained this airy gewgaw as a souvenir, as, so to speak, a *gage d’amour*. And, in that event, she ought to be very much pleased and a bit embarrassed; and she will preserve upon the topic of handkerchiefs a maidenly silence. Do you know, Robert Etheridge Townsend, there is about you the making of a very fine logician?”
===Chinese===
「因为，别看表面，」我同自己辩难道，「那手帕也未必就是她的，这层可能总是有的。她也许是向人借的，也许不知怎么阴差阳错地错拿了。倘若是这样，那么合理推想，她发觉遗失，自会问我见着没有；反过来，倘若手帕是她的，那么我把书还回去、却扣下这方手帕，她自然心里明白：我把这件轻飘飘的小玩意儿，犯下重罪也似地扣押了下来，权作一件纪念——不妨说，当作一件*爱情的信物*（gage d’amour）。果真如此，她该当十分受用、又带点儿难为情才是；往后在手帕这个题目上，她自会保持一副闺秀式的缄默。你可知道，罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森，你这副坯子里，大有造就一位顶出色逻辑学家的质料？」

===Original===
Then I consulted my watch, and subsequently grimaced. “It is also barely possible,” said I, “that Margaret may not come at all. In which case—Margaret! Now, isn’t that a sweet name? Isn’t it the very sweetest name in the world? Now, really, you know, it is queer her being named Margaret—extraordinarily queer—because Margaret has always been my favourite woman’s name. I daresay, unbeknownst to myself, I am a bit of a prophet.”
===Chinese===
接着我看了看表，随后做了一个苦脸。「玛格丽特压根儿不来，这也同样是有可能的，」我说。「倘若是这样——玛格丽特！嗳，这名字多甜？这岂不是全世界顶甜顶甜的一个名字？说真的，你可知道，她偏偏就叫玛格丽特，这事可真古怪——古怪透顶——因为玛格丽特一直是我顶顶喜欢的一个女人的名字。我料想，连我自己都不曾察觉，我竟颇有几分先知的道行。」

### III / 三

===Original===
But she did come. She was very much surprised to see me.
===Chinese===
可她到底来了。她见到我，好不吃惊。

===Original===
“You!” she said, with a gesture which was practically tantamount to disbelief. “Why, how extraordinary!”
===Chinese===
「你呀！」她说着，还配了一个几乎等于不肯相信的手势。「哎呀，这可真是稀奇！」

===Original===
“You rogue!” I commented, internally: “you know it is the most natural thing in the world.” Aloud I stated: “Why, yes, I happened to notice you forgot your book yesterday, so I dropped in—or, to be more accurate, climbed up—to return it.”
===Chinese===
「你这促狭鬼！」我在心里评点道：「你自己分明知道，这是全世界顶自然不过的一桩事。」出声的部分，我则申明：「哦，正是，我碰巧注意到你昨天把书忘了，便顺道过来一趟——或者，说得再准确些，爬了过来一趟——把它奉还。」

===Original===
She reached for it. Our hands touched, with the usual result to my pulses. Also, there were the customary manual tinglings.
===Chinese===
她伸手来接。我们的手碰到了一处，我的脉搏照例起了照例的动静。此外，指掌之间，也照例窜过那一阵熟悉的酥麻。

===Original===
“You are very kind,” was her observation, “for I am wondering which one of the two he will marry.”
===Chinese===
「您真和气，」这是她的评语，「因为我正纳闷着，那两位里头，他到底娶的是哪一位。」

===Original===
“Forman tells me he has no notion, himself.”
===Chinese===
「福尔曼告诉我，他自己也毫无主意。」

===Original===
“Oh, then you know Justus Miles Forman! How nice! I think his stories are just splendid, especially the way his heroes talk to photographs and handkerchiefs and dead flowers—”
===Chinese===
「哦，这么说，您认识贾斯特斯·迈尔斯·福尔曼！真好！我觉得他的小说写得实在漂亮，尤其是他笔下的男主角同照片、手帕和枯花说话的那一手——」

===Original===
Afterward she opened the book, and turned over its pages expectantly, and flushed a proper shade of pink, and said nothing.
===Chinese===
随后她翻开那本书，满怀指望地一页页翻过去，脸上泛起恰到好处的一层粉红，一句话也没说。

===Original===
And then, and not till then, my heart consented to resume its normal functions. And then, also, “These iron spikes—” said its owner.
===Chinese===
直到这时，也直到这时，我那颗心才肯重新履行它正常的职能。也是到这时，「这些铁刺——」它的主人开口了。

===Original===
“Yes?” she queried, innocently.
===Chinese===
「怎么啦？」她天真烂漫地问。

===Original===
“—so humpy,” I complained.
===Chinese===
「——这么疙疙瘩瘩的，」我诉苦道。

===Original===
“Are they?” said she. “Why, then, how silly of you to continue to sit on them!”
===Chinese===
「是么？」她说。「哎呀，那你岂不傻透了，还一味坐在上头不肯下来！」

===Original===
The result of this comment was that we were both late for luncheon.
===Chinese===
这一句评语的后果是：我们两个，午饭都误了。

### IV / 四

===Original===
By a peculiar coincidence, at twelve o’clock the following day, I happened to be sitting on the same wall at the same spot. Peter said at luncheon it was a queer thing that some people never could manage to be on time for their meals.
===Chinese===
说来也巧得蹊跷，第二天十二点整，我恰好又坐在同一堵墙的同一个位置上。吃午饭时，彼得发表议论说，有些人竟从来没法子按时赶上饭点，真是咄咄怪事。

===Original===
I fancy we can all form a tolerably accurate idea of what took place during the next day or so.
===Chinese===
接下来一天上下的光景，我想我们大家都不难想见个八九不离十。

===Original===
It is scarcely necessary to retail our conversations. We gossiped of simple things. We talked very little; and, when we did talk, the most ambitiously preambled sentences were apt to result in nothing more prodigious than a wave of the hand, and a pause, and, not infrequently, a heightened complexion. Altogether, then, it was not oppressively wise or witty talk, but it was eminently satisfactory to its makers.
===Chinese===
我们的谈话，实在不必逐一转售了。我们闲磕牙的，都是些简简单单的事。我们很少开口；偶尔开了口，那些气魄雄浑的开场白，到头来也往往不过落得挥一挥手、顿一顿——还不时添上一阵脸红——再无半点更了不起的下文。总而言之，那算不得叫人喘不过气来的睿智或俏皮的谈话，可对制造它的两造而言，已是心满意足到了极点。

===Original===
As when, on the third morning, I wished to sit by Margaret on the bench, and she declined to invite me to descend from the wall.
===Chinese===
比如第三天早晨，我想在长凳上挨着玛格丽特坐下，她却偏偏不肯赏脸请我从墙上下来。

===Original===
“On the whole,” said she, “I prefer you where you are; like all picturesque ruins, you are most admirable at a little distance.”
===Chinese===
「总的说来，」她说，「我情愿你待在原地不动；同一切景色如画的废墟一样，你须隔上小小一段距离，才最可钦佩。」

===Original===
“Ruins!”—and, indeed, I was not yet twenty-six—“I am a comparatively young man.”
===Chinese===
「废墟！」——说实话，我那年还不满二十六岁——「我可是个相当年轻的人。」

===Original===
As a concession, “In consideration of your past, you are tolerably well preserved.”
===Chinese===
作为一点让步：「念在你那段过往的份上，你保存得还算不坏。」

===Original===
“—and I am not a new brand of marmalade, either.”
===Chinese===
「——再说，我也不是什么新牌子的橘子酱。」

===Original===
“No, for that comes in glass jars; whereas, Mr. Townsend, I have heard, is more apt to figure in family ones.”
===Chinese===
「不是，因为橘子酱这东西，是装在玻璃罐子（glass jars）里来的；而汤森先生呢，据我所闻，更惯于抛头露面的地方，是家庭的『罐子』（family jars，意即家庭口角）。」

===Original===
“A pun, Miss Beechinor, is the base coinage of conversation tendered only by the mentally dishonest.”
===Chinese===
「双关语，比奇诺小姐，是谈话里的劣等铸币，唯有心智不老实的人，才肯把它支付出去。」

===Original===
“—Besides, one can never have enough of marmalade.”
===Chinese===
「——况且，橘子酱这东西，多少也不嫌多。」

===Original===
“I trust they give you a sufficiency of it in the nursery?”
===Chinese===
「但愿他们在育儿室里，给你配的份量足足的吧？」

===Original===
“Dear me, you have no idea how admirably that paternal tone sits upon you! You would make an excellent father, Mr. Townsend. You really ought to adopt someone. I wish you would adopt *me*, Mr. Townsend.”
===Chinese===
「哎呀呀，你真想不到，这副慈父的口吻安在你身上有多妥帖！你会是一位顶出色的父亲，汤森先生。你实在该收养个把人才是。我但愿你就收养*我*，汤森先生。」

===Original===
I said I had other plans for her. Discreetly, she forbore to ask what they were.
===Chinese===
我说，我对她另有别的安排。她乖觉得很，忍住了不问那是什么安排。

### V / 五

===Original===
“Avis—”

“You must not call me that.”

“Why not? It’s your name, isn’t it?”

“Yes—to my friends.”

“Aren’t we friends—Avis?”

“We! We have not known each other long enough, Mr. Townsend.”
===Chinese===
「艾维斯——」

「你可不许这么叫我。」

「为什么不能？这是你的名字，不是么？」

「是——对我的朋友们说来是。」

「那我们难道不算朋友么——艾维斯？」

「我们！我们相识得还不够久呢，汤森先生。」

===Original===
“Oh, what’s the difference? We are going to be friends, aren’t we—Avis?”

“Why—why, I am sure I don’t know.”

“Gracious gravy, what an admirable colour you have, Avis! Well—I know. And I can inform you, quite confidentially, Avis, that we are not going to be—friends. We are going to be—”

“We are going to be late for luncheon,” said she, in haste. “Good morning, Mr. Townsend.”
===Chinese===
「嗐，那有什么打紧？我们往后总会做成朋友的，不是么——艾维斯？」

「这个——这个么，我实在说不上来。」

「我的老天爷，你这气色多可人，艾维斯！这个——我可知道。而且我可以万分机密地奉告你，艾维斯：我们往后不会做成——朋友。我们要做成的是——」

「我们要做成的是午饭的迟到者，」她急急地说。「早安，汤森先生。」

### VI / 六

===Original===
Yet, the very next day, paradoxically enough, she told me:

“I shall always think of you as a very, very dear friend. But it is quite impossible we should ever be anything else.”
===Chinese===
然而恰恰第二天，事情偏这般乖张，她却对我说：

「我会永远把您看作一位非常、非常亲爱的朋友。可是，我们若要再做成别的什么，那是断断不可能的。」

===Original===
“And why, Avis?”

“Because—”

“That”—after an interval—“strikes me as rather a poor reason. So, suppose we say this June?”
===Chinese===
「那是为什么，艾维斯？」

「因为——」

「这个」——隔了片刻——「在我看，实在算不上一个像样的理由。那么，不妨便说定，就在今年六月？」

===Original===
Another interval.

“Well, Avis?”

“Dear me, aren’t those roses pretty? I wish you would get me one, Mr. Townsend.”
===Chinese===
又一阵间歇。

「怎么样，艾维斯？」

「哎呀，那些玫瑰多好看呀。请您给我摘一枝来，好不好，汤森先生？」

===Original===
“Avis, we are not discussing roses.”

“Well, they *are* pretty.”

“Avis!”—reproachfully.
===Chinese===
「艾维斯，我们眼下谈的不是玫瑰。」

「话虽如此，它们*确实*好看嘛。」

「艾维斯！」——声调里带着责备。

===Original===
Still another interval.

“I—I hardly know.”

“Avis!”—with disappointment.

“I—I believe—”

“Avis!”—very tenderly.

“I—I almost think so—and the horrid man looks as if he thought so, too!”
===Chinese===
又是一阵间歇。

「我——我说不准。」

「艾维斯！」——带着失望。

「我——我相信——」

「艾维斯！」——温柔到了极处。

「我——我差不多也是这么想——而这可恶的男人，瞧那副神气，仿佛他心里也这么想呢！」

===Original===
There was a fourth interval, during which the girl made a complete and careful survey of her shoes.

Then, all in a breath, “It could not possibly be June, of course, and you must give me until tomorrow to think about November,” and a sudden flutter of skirts.

I returned to Gridlington treading on air.
===Chinese===
跟着是第四回间歇。这间歇里，姑娘把自己的一双鞋，从头到尾仔仔细细考察了一遍。

随后，一口气地吐出：「六月嘛，自然是万万不能的；至于十一月，您总得容我到明天再考虑考虑。」说罢，裙裾倏然一闪。

我脚下踏着云，一路回了格里灵顿。

### VII / 七

===Original===
For I was, by this time, as thoroughly in love as Amadis of Gaul or Aucassin of Beaucaire or any other hero of romance you may elect to mention.
===Chinese===
因为到这时节，我已爱得同高卢的阿马迪斯（Amadis of Gaul）、博凯尔的奥卡森（Aucassin of Beaucaire），或随你点将点到的哪一位传奇英雄一样，彻头彻尾，无以复加。

===Original===
Some two weeks earlier I would have scoffed at the notion of such a thing coming to pass; and I could have demonstrated, logically enough, that it was impossible for Robert Etheridge Townsend, with his keen knowledge of the world and of the innumerable vanities and whims of womankind, ever again to go the way of all flesh. But the problem, like the puzzle of the Eleatic philosophers, had solved itself. “Achilles cannot catch the tortoise,” but he does. It was impossible for me to fall uncomfortably deep in love—but I had done so.
===Chinese===
换作两个来礼拜之前，这样一桩事情竟会落到我头上，我准会嗤之以鼻；而且我本可以摆出足够严整的逻辑，论证罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森这个人——凭着他对世事人情的洞明，凭着他对女人家那数不清的虚荣与反复无常的深知——是绝无可能再随一切血肉之躯的大流，走上那条老路的。然而这道难题，恰似爱利亚学派（Eleatic）哲人的那道谜题，自己解开了自己。「阿喀琉斯追不上乌龟」，可他偏偏追上了。要我深陷爱河、陷到神魂不安，原是不可能的事——可我偏偏陷了。

===Original===
And it pricked my conscience, too, that Margaret should not know I was aware of her identity. But she had chosen to play the comedy to the end, and in common with the greater part of trousered humanity, I had, after all, no insuperable objection to a rich wife; though, to do me justice, I rarely thought of her, now, as Margaret Hugonin the heiress, but considered her, in a more comprehensive fashion, as the one woman in the universe whose perfections triumphantly overpeered the skyiest heights of preciosity.
===Chinese===
再说，玛格丽特尚不知晓我已识破她的身份，这一点也时时刺着我的良心。可是这出喜剧，既由她自家择定要演到底，而我——同穿长裤的人类中的大多数一样——归根结底，对一位有钱的太太也并无无法逾越的异议；不过，平心而论，到了如今，我已很少再把她当作女继承人玛格丽特·休戈宁去想，而是以一种更其恢廓的眼光，把她看作天地间独一无二的那个女人：她的千般完美，得胜似的凌越了刻意造作所能堆砌到的九霄绝顶。

## XXVI / 二十六

He Assists in the Diversion of Birds / 他襄助群鸟的消遣

### I / 一

===Original===
We met, then, in the clear May morning, with what occult trepidations I cannot say. You may depend upon it, though, we had our emotions.
===Chinese===
于是，在五月那个晴朗的早晨，我们会面了；各人心里藏着怎样的隐秘悸动，我说不上来。不过你尽管放心：我们该有的心情激荡，是一样也不曾少的。

===Original===
And about us, spring was marshaling her pageant, and from divers nooks, the weather-stained nymphs and fauns regarded us in candid, if preoccupied, appraisement; and above us, the clipped ilex trees were about a knowing conference. As for the birds, they were discussing us without any reticence whatever, for, more favoured of chance than imperial Solomon, they have been the confidants in any number of such affairs, and regard the way of a man with a maid as one of the most matter-of-fact occurrences in the world.
===Chinese===
在我们四周，春天正在调遣她盛大的仪仗；各处角落里，那些经风雨剥蚀的仙女与法翁，正以坦率的——虽说略带心不在焉的——眼光把我们估量；我们头顶上，修剪齐整的冬青槠正要开一场心照不宣的会议。至于鸟儿们，它们议论起我们来更是毫无顾忌，因为比起帝王气派的所罗门王（Solomon），它们更得机缘的眷顾，当过不知多少桩这类韵事的知情人，早把男人亲近少女之道，看作天底下最平淡无奇的寻常事之一。

===Original===
“Here is he! here is she!” they shrilled. “See how they meet, see how they greet! Ah, sweet, sweet, sweet, to meet in the spring!” And that we two would immediately set to nest-building, they considered a foregone conclusion.
===Chinese===
「他来了！她来了！」它们尖声嚷道。「看他们会面，看他们见礼！啊，甜哪，甜哪，甜哪，春天里相会！」至于我们两个马上就要动手筑巢，它们认为那是板上钉钉、早成定局的事。

### II / 二

===Original===
I had taken both her firm, warm hands in salutation, and held them, for a breathing-space, between my own. And my own hands seemed to me two very gross, and hulking, and raw, and red monstrosities, in contrast with their dimpled captives, and my hands appeared, also, to shake unnecessarily.
===Chinese===
见面行礼时，我已把她那双结实而温暖的手都握在了手里，把它们夹在我自己两只手当中，握了有一息之久。于是相形之下，我自己这两只手，在我看来，便成了两只何其粗蠢、笨大、粗砺而又通红的怪物——被它们擒住的那两个带酒窝的俘虏正自相反——况且我的手还似乎抖得毫无必要。

===Original===
“Now, in a moment,” said I, “I am going to ask you something very important. But, first, I have a confession to make.”
===Chinese===
「听着，只消片刻，」我说，「我就要向你提出一件顶顶要紧的事了。不过在此之前，先有一桩供状要招。」

===Original===
And her glad, shamed eyes bemocked me. “My lord of Burleigh!” she softly breathed. “My liege Cophetua! *My* king Cophetua! And did you think, then, I was blind?”
===Chinese===
而她那双又欢喜又含羞的眼睛，分明在取笑我。「我的伯利勋爵（Lord of Burleigh，丁尼生诗中假扮穷画师下娶村姑的贵族）！」她轻轻吐声道。「我的科菲图亚（Cophetua，古谣中钟爱乞食少女的国王）王！*我的*科菲图亚国王！怎么，难道你当我是瞎子不成？」

===Original===
“Eh?” said I.
===Chinese===
「呃？」我说。

===Original===
“As if I hadn’t known from the first!” the girl pouted; “as if I hadn’t known from the very first day when you dropped your cigarette case! Ah, I had heard of you before, Peter!—of Peter, the misogynist, who was ashamed to go a-wooing in his proper guise! Was it because you were afraid I’d marry you for your money, Peter?—poor, timid Peter! But, oh, Peter, Peter, what possessed you to take the name of that notorious Robert Townsend?” she demanded, with uplifted forefinger. “Couldn’t you think of a better one, Peter?—of a more respectable one, Peter? It really is a great relief to call you Peter at last. I’ve had to try so hard to keep from doing it before, Peter.”
===Chinese===
「倒好像我打从头一天起就不曾知道似的！」姑娘嘟起了嘴；「倒好像你掉下烟盒的那个头一天，我就不曾知道似的！嗐，彼得，你的名声我早有耳闻！——听说过彼得其人如何厌弃女人，竟羞于以自己的真面目出去求爱！莫不是怕我图你的钱财才嫁你么，彼得？——可怜的、胆怯的彼得！可是，嗬，彼得呀，彼得，究竟是什么鬼使神差，叫你顶用了那个声名狼藉的罗伯特·汤森的名字？」她竖起食指，质问道。「你就想不出一个好一点的名字么，彼得？——一个体面一点的名字么，彼得？如今总算能开口叫你一声彼得，实在叫人如释重负。为了忍住不这么叫，我先前费了好大的劲呢，彼得。」

===Original===
And in answer, I made an inarticulate sound.
===Chinese===
作为回答，我发出了一声不成腔调的声响。

===Original===
“But you were so grave about it,” the girl went on, happily, “that I almost thought you were telling the truth, Peter. Then my maid told me—I mean, she happened to mention casually that Mr. Townsend’s valet had described his master to her as an extraordinarily handsome man. So, then, of course, I knew you were Peter Blagden.”
===Chinese===
「不过你那副一本正经的模样，」姑娘快快活活地接着说，「彼得，倒险些叫我相信你说的句句是真话。后来我的女仆告诉了我——我是说，她碰巧随口提起一件事：汤森先生的贴身男仆向她描绘过自家的主人，说他俊美得出奇。这么一来，那还消说么，我就知道你是彼得·布拉格登了。」

===Original===
