“I perceive,” said I, reflectively, “that Byam has been somewhat too zealous. I begin to suspect, also, that kitchen-gossip is a mischancy petard, and rather more than apt to hoist the engineer who employs it. So, you thought I was Peter Blagden—the rich Peter Blagden? Ah, yes!”
===Chinese===
「我看明白了，」我若有所思地说，「拜亚姆未免热心得过了头。我还开始疑心，厨房里的闲话原是一枚凶险的攻城炮，十之八九还要把雇用它那位工兵本人，轰上半天云霄。这么说，你当我是彼得·布拉格登——那位有钱的彼得·布拉格登了？嗬，正是！」

===Original===
Now the birds were caroling on a wager. “Ah, sweet! what is sweeter?” they sang. “Ah, sweet, sweet, sweet, to meet in the spring.”
===Chinese===
这时，鸟儿们像下了赌注似的赛着嗓子欢唱。「啊，甜哪！还有什么更甜的？」它们唱道。「啊，甜哪，甜哪，甜哪，春天里相会。」

===Original===
But the girl gave a wordless cry at sight of the change in my face. “Oh, how dear of you to care so much! I didn’t mean that you were *ugly*, Peter. I just meant you are so big and—and so like the baby that they probably have on the talcum-powder boxes in Brobdingnag—”
===Chinese===
可姑娘一见我脸色变了，发出一声没有字眼的惊呼。「嗬，你这样把它放在心上，多叫人疼爱！我不是说你*丑*，彼得。我只是说你生得这么高大，而且——而且活像大人国（Brobdingnag，《格列佛游记》中的巨人国）里爽身粉盒子上多半会印的那种胖娃娃——」

===Original===
“Because I happen to be really Robert Townsend—the notorious Robert Etheridge Townsend,” I continued, with a smile. “I am sorry you were deceived by the cigarette-case. I remember now; I borrowed it from Peter. What I meant to confess was that I have known all along you were Margaret Hugonin.”
===Chinese===
「因为我恰好当真是罗伯特·汤森——那个声名狼藉的罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森，」我含着笑接下去说。「那烟盒害你上了当，我很抱歉。我如今想起来了：那是我跟彼得借的。至于我本要招的供，是这个：我从头一天起就知道，你是玛格丽特·休戈宁。」

===Original===
“But I’m not,” the girl said, in bewilderment. “Why—Why I *told* you I was Avis Beechinor.”
===Chinese===
「可我并不是，」姑娘愕然道。「咦——咦，我可是*亲口告诉过*你的，我是艾维斯·比奇诺。」

===Original===
“This handkerchief?” I queried, and took it from my pocket. I had been absurd enough to carry it next to my heart.
===Chinese===
「那么这方手帕呢？」我发问，一面把它从口袋里掏了出来。我先前竟荒唐到把它贴着心窝揣着。

===Original===
“Oh—!” And now the tension broke, and her voice leapt to high, shrill, half-hysterical speaking.
===Chinese===
「嗬——！」这下子，绷紧的弦断了，她的声音一跃而起，变得又高又尖，半带歇斯底里。

===Original===
“I am Avis Beechinor. I am a poor relation, a penniless cousin, a dependent, a hanger-on, do you understand? And you—Ah, how—how funny! Why, Margaret *always* gives me her cast-off finery, the scraps, the remnants, the clothes she is tired of, the misfit things—so that she won’t be ashamed of me, so that I may be fairly presentable. She gave me eight of those handkerchiefs. I meant to pick the monograms out with a needle, you understand, because I haven’t any money to buy such handkerchiefs for myself. I remember now—she gave them to me on that day—that first day, and I missed one of them a little later on. Ah, how—how funny!” she cried, again; “ah, how very, very funny! No, Mr. Townsend, I am not an heiress—I’m a pauper, a poor relation. No, you have failed again, just as you did with Mrs. Barry-Smith and with Miss Jemmett, Mr. Townsend. I—I wish you better luck the next time.”
===Chinese===
「我就是艾维斯·比奇诺。我是个穷亲戚，一个不名一文的表姊妹，一个仰人鼻息的拖累，一个寄人篱下的食客，你明白了么？而你——嗬，这多——多好笑！要知道，玛格丽特*向来*把她穿剩的华丽衣裳赏给我，那些零头、剩料、她穿腻了的行头、不合身的物件——好教她带我出去不丢脸，好教我还勉强见得人。那样的手帕，她给了我八方。我原打算用针把上头的花押挑掉，你明白么，因为我自己可没有钱买这种手帕。我如今想起来了——就是那天她给我的——头一天给的，过后没多久，我就发觉少了一方。嗬，这多——多好笑！」她又喊道；「嗬，这可真是太好笑了！不，汤森先生，我不是什么女继承人——我是个穷光蛋，一个穷亲戚。不，你又失手了一回，正像你对巴里-史密斯太太、对杰米特小姐那两回一样，汤森先生。我——我祝你下一回交上好运。」

===Original===
I must have raised one hand as though in warding off a physical blow. “Don’t!” I said.
===Chinese===
想必我是举起了一只手，仿佛要招架一记实打在身上的重击。「别说了！」我说。

===Original===
And all the woman in her leapt to defend me. “Ah no, ah no!” she pleaded, and her hands fell caressingly upon my shoulder; and she raised a penitent, tear-stained face toward mine; “ah no, forgive me! I didn’t mean that altogether. It is different with a man. Of course, you must marry sensibly—of course you must, Mr. Townsend. It is I who am to blame—why, of *course* it’s only I who am to blame. I have encouraged you, I know—”
===Chinese===
她一身的女性的柔情，霎时间齐齐跃起，赶来护卫我。「嗬，不，嗬，不！」她央告道，两只手爱抚地落在我肩头；她朝我仰起一张悔罪的、挂着泪痕的脸；「嗬，不，饶恕我吧！我不是全然那个意思。男人家是另一回事。你自然得务实地娶亲——你自然非如此不可，汤森先生。要怪罪也只能怪我——嗬，*当然*咯，能怪的只有我一个。是我一直在撩拨你，我知道——」

===Original===
“You haven’t! you haven’t” I barked.
===Chinese===
「你没有！你没有！」我厉声喝道。

===Original===
“But, yes—for I came back that second day because I thought you were the rich Mr. Blagden. I was so tired of being poor, so tired of being dependent, that it simply seemed to me I could not stand it for a moment longer. Ah, I tell you, I was tired, tired, tired! I was tired and sick and worn out with it all!”
===Chinese===
「可是，有的——因为我第二天之所以又回去，正因为我当你是那位有钱的布拉格登先生。穷，我当腻了；仰人扶携，我也受腻了；腻到我简直觉得，再有一刻也支撑不下去了。嗬，我告诉你，我腻了，腻了，腻了！我腻透了，烦透了，这一场一场把我磨得身心俱疲！」

===Original===
I did not interrupt her. I was nobly moved; but even then at the back of my mind some being that was not I was taking notes as to this girl, so young and desirable, and now so like a plaintive child who has been punished and does not understand exactly why.
===Chinese===
我没有打断她。我心头涌起一片庄严的感动；可即便在那时，在我脑海的背处，另有一个并非我的存在，却正在给这位姑娘做笔记：这样年轻，这样惹人爱慕，如今又这样像一个挨了责罚、又不很明白究竟为何挨罚的哀哀切切的孩子。

===Original===
“Mr. Townsend, you don’t know what it means to a girl to be poor!—you can’t ever know, because you are only a man. My mother—ah, you don’t know the life I have led! You don’t know how I have been hawked about, and set up for inspection by the men who could afford to pay my price, and made to show off my little accomplishments for them, and put through my paces before them like any horse in the market! For we are poor, Mr. Townsend—we are bleakly, hopelessly poor. We are only hangers-on, you see. And ever since I can remember, she has been telling me I must make a rich marriage—*must* make a rich marriage—”
===Chinese===
「汤森先生，你不知道穷对一个姑娘家意味着什么！——你永远无从知道，因为你不过是个男人。我母亲——嗬，你不知道我过的是什么样的日子！你不知道我怎样被人四处兜售，怎样被摆出来，供那些出得起我身价的男人们验看，怎样被支使着在他们面前卖弄我那一星半点的小小才艺，像集市上随便哪匹马一样，被牵着在他们眼前遛蹄验步！因为我们穷啊，汤森先生——我们穷得精光，穷得没了指望。我们不过是寄人篱下的食客，你瞧。而打从我记事以来，她就一直训导我，说我务必要嫁一门有钱的亲——*务必要*嫁一门有钱的亲——」

===Original===
And the girl’s voice trailed off into silence, and her eyes closed for a moment, and she swayed a little on her feet, so that I caught her by both arms.
===Chinese===
姑娘的声音渐渐低微下去，终至悄无声息；她的眼睛闭了一会儿，脚下的身子微微一晃，我赶紧双手扶住了她的胳膊。

===Original===
But, presently, she opened her eyes, with a wearied sigh, and presently the two fortune-hunters stared each other in the face.
===Chinese===
但过了片刻，她睁开眼睛，疲惫已极地叹了一声；又过了片刻，这一对猎财的男女，便你看我、我看你，直瞪瞪地相起面来。

===Original===
“Ah, sweet! what is sweeter?” sang the birds. “Can you see, can you see, can you see? It is sweet, sweet, sweet!” They were extremely gay over it, were the birds.
===Chinese===
「啊，甜哪！还有什么更甜？」鸟儿们唱道。「你看见了么，看见了么，看见了么？真是甜哪，甜哪，甜哪！」它们为这件事快活得了不得，这些鸟儿。

===Original===
After a little, though, I opened my lips, and moistened them two or three times before I spoke. “Yes,” said I, “I think I understand. We have both been hangers-on. But that seems, somehow, a long while ago. Yes, it was a knave who scaled that wall the first time—one who needed and had earned a kicking from here to Aldebaran. But I think that I loved you from the very moment I saw you. Will you marry me, Avis?”
===Chinese===
不过过了一小会儿，我张开嘴唇，先把嘴唇润湿了两三回，方才出声。「是了，」我说，「我想我明白了。我们两个，都是做过寄人篱下食客的人。可那不知怎的，仿佛已是许久以前的事了。是的，头一回翻上那堵墙的，是个无赖——一个欠着、并且挣下了一顿脚踢——从这儿一路踢到毕宿五（Aldebaran）才公道——的无赖。可我想，我从望见你的头一眼起，就已经爱上你了。你肯嫁给我么，艾维斯？」

===Original===
And in her face there was a wonderful and tender change. “You care for me—just me?” she breathed.
===Chinese===
她的脸上起了一种奇妙而温柔的变动。「你心上在乎的是我——只我这个人？」她轻轻吐出。

===Original===
“Just you,” I answered, gravely.
===Chinese===
「只你这个人，」我郑重答道。

===Original===
And I saw the start, and the merest ghost of a shiver which shook her body, as she leaned toward me a little, almost in surrender; but, quickly, she laughed.
===Chinese===
我看见她猛地一惊，一记至轻至微、恍如魂影的战栗掠过她的身子；她朝我微微倾过来，几乎已是降心相从的姿态；可是转眼间，她笑了。

===Original===
“That was very gentlemanly in you,” she said; “but, of course, I understand. Let us part friends, then—Robert. Even if—if you really cared, we couldn’t marry. We are too poor.”
===Chinese===
「你这样做人，真够君子，」她说，「不过，我自然是懂的。那么，让我们做好聚好散的朋友罢——罗伯特。纵然——纵然你当真心上有我，我们也结不成婚。我们太穷了。」

===Original===
“Too poor!” I scoffed—and my voice was joyous, for I knew now that it was I she loved and not just Peter Blagden’s money; “too *poor*, Avis! I am to the contrary, an inordinately rich man, I tell you, for I have your love. Oh you needn’t try to deny it. You are heels over head in love with me. And we have made, no doubt, an unsavoury mess of the past; but the future remains to us. We are the earthen pots, you and I, who wanted to swim with the brazen ones. Well! they haven’t quite smashed us, these big, stupid, brazen pots, but they have shown us that they have the power to do it. And so we are going back where we belong—to the poor man’s country, Avis—or, in any event, to the country of those God-fearing, sober and honest folk who earn their bread and, just occasionally, a pat of butter to season it.”
===Chinese===
「太穷了！」我嗤笑一声——嗓音里满是欢畅，因为我如今知道了，她爱的是我这个人，而不单是彼得·布拉格登的钱财；「*太*穷了，艾维斯！恰恰相反，我告诉你，我是个阔得没边的富翁，因为我有了你的爱。哦，你也不必费劲抵赖。你爱我爱得脚朝天、头朝地。过去，无疑，我们已搅成了一锅不干不净的烂糊；可未来还留着，归我们所有。我们是那两只陶罐，你和我，偏要同那些铜罐一道下水漂流（伊索寓言《陶罐与铜罐》）。好罢！这些又大又蠢的铜罐倒还不曾把我们撞得粉身碎骨，可他们已叫我们领教了，他们是有这份蛮力的。所以我们这就回我们该在的地方去——回穷人的国度去，艾维斯——或者说，无论如何，回到那些敬畏上帝、清醒而诚实的百姓的国土去——他们在那里挣得面包，偶尔呢，还挣得一小块黄油，给面包添点滋味。」

===Original===
The world was very beautiful. I knew that I was excellent throughout and unconquerable. So I moved more near to her.
===Chinese===
世界美极了。我知道自己从里到外无一处不出色，无一处不可征服。于是我朝她挪近了些。

===Original===
“For you will come with me, won’t you, dear? Oh, you won’t have quite so many gowns in this new country, Avis, and, may be, not even a horse and surrey of your own; but you will have love, and you will have happiness, and, best of all, Avis, you will give a certain very undeserving man his chance—his one sole chance—to lead a real man’s life. Are you going to deny him that chance, Avis?”
===Chinese===
「因为你总肯跟我同去的，是不是，亲爱的？哦，到了这个新国度，你可不会有那么多身衣裳了，艾维斯，说不定，连自备的一驾马和四轮敞篷马车也未必有；可你会有爱情，你会有幸福，而顶顶要紧的是，艾维斯，你会给一个全然不配的男人他的机会——他唯一仅有的机会——去过一个真正男子汉的生活。你总不至于回绝他这个机会罢，艾维斯？」

===Original===
Her gaze read me through and through; and I bore myself a bit proudly under it; and it seemed to me that my heart was filled with love of her, and that some sort of newborn manhood in Robert Etheridge Townsend was enabling me to meet her big brown eyes unflinchingly.
===Chinese===
她的目光把我里里外外读了个通透；我在这目光底下，把身姿撑得有几分傲然；我只觉我心中盛满了对她的爱，又觉得罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森体内某一种新生的男子气概，正扶持着我，教我毫不畏缩地迎住她那双棕色的大眼睛。

===Original===
“It wouldn’t be sensible,” she wavered.
===Chinese===
「这样可就不明智了，」她动摇着说。

===Original===
I laughed at that. “Sensible! If there is one thing more absurd than another in this very absurd world, it is common sense. Be sensible and you will be miserable, Avis, not to mention being disliked. Sensible! Why, of course, it is not sensible. It is stark, rank, staring idiocy for us two not to make a profitable investment of, we will say, our natural endowments, when we come to marry. For what will Mrs. Grundy say if we don’t? Ah, what will she say, indeed? Avis, just between you and me, I do not care a double-blank domino what Mrs. Grundy says. You will obligingly remember that the car for the Hesperides is in the rear, and that this is the third and last call. And in consequence—will you marry me, Avis?”
===Chinese===
这话叫我笑了。「明智！在这个荒唐透顶的世界上，倘若还有一样东西比旁的一样更荒唐，那便是常识。明智罢，你就要受苦受难，艾维斯，更不用说招人嫌了。明智！嗐，这当然不明智。我们两个临到婚娶，竟不肯拿我们——姑且这么说罢——天生的本钱，去做一桩有利可图的投资，这是十足的、露骨的、明摆着的愚不可及。因为倘若我们不这么办，格伦迪太太会说什么呢？嗬，她究竟会说什么呢？艾维斯，这话只你我二人之间说说：格伦迪太太说什么，我一概不在乎——在乎的程度，还抵不上骨牌里那张双白皮的零点牌（double-blank domino）。还请惠予留意：开往赫斯珀里得斯（Hesperides，希腊神话极西处的金苹果圣园）的那节客车已经停在站后，这是第三遍、也是末一遍催乘了。既然如此——你可肯嫁给我，艾维斯？」

===Original===
She gave me her hand frankly, as a man might have done. “Yes, Robert,” said Miss Beechinor, “and God helping us, we will make something better of the future than we have of the past.”
===Chinese===
她爽爽快快向我伸出手来，像个男人办事的那样。「肯的，罗伯特，」比奇诺小姐说，「而且有上帝扶助，我们定要把未来经营得比过去像样些。」

===Original===
In the silence that fell, one might hear the birds. “Sweet, sweet, sweet!” they twittered. “Can you see, can you see, can you see? Their lips meet. It is sweet, sweet, sweet!”
===Chinese===
在随之落定的一片寂静里，人还听得见鸟儿的声音。「甜哪，甜哪，甜哪！」它们啁啾着。「你看见了么，看见了么，看见了么？他们的嘴唇碰上了。真是甜哪，甜哪，甜哪！」

### III / 三

===Original===
But, by and by, she questioned me. “Are you sure—quite sure,” she queried, wistfully, “that you wouldn’t rather have me Margaret Hugonin, the heiress?”
===Chinese===
可是过了不久，她来盘问我了。「你拿得稳么——十分拿得稳，」她怅惘地问，「你敢说你不情愿我还是玛格丽特·休戈宁——那位女继承人么？」

===Original===
I raised a deprecatory hand. “Avis!” I reproached her; “Avis, Avis, how little you know me! That was the solitary fly in the amber—that I thought I was to marry a woman named Margaret. For I am something of a connoisseur in nomenclature, and Margaret has always—*always*—been my pet detestation in the way of names.”
===Chinese===
我举起一只手，以示告止。「艾维斯！」我责备她；「艾维斯，艾维斯，你多么不了解我！那正是琥珀里那只孤零零的苍蝇——害我以为自己要娶的，是一位名叫玛格丽特的女人。要知道，在命名之学上，我不免算得上半个行家；而玛格丽特这个名字，向来是——*向来*是——名字这门学问里，我素来最珍爱的一桩憎恶。」

===Original===
“Oh, what a child you are!” she said.
===Chinese===
「嗬，你这个人，浑然是个孩子！」她说。

## XXVII / 二十七

He Calls, and Counsels, and Considers / 他造访，他进言，他思量

### I / 一

===Original===
"I am now" said I, in my soul, "quite immeasurably, and insanely, and unreasonably, and unadulteratedly happy. Why, of course I am."

===Chinese===
「如今我呀，」我在心里说，「可真是无限度地、痴痴癫癫地、蛮不讲理地、纯而又纯地快乐。嗐，我自然是快乐的。」

===Original===
This statement was advanced just two weeks later than the events previously recorded. And the origin of it was the fact that I was now engaged to Avis Beechinor though it was not as yet to be "announced"; just this concession alone had Mrs. Beechinor wrested from an indignant and, latterly, a tearful interview.… For I had called at Selwoode, in due form; and after leaving Mrs. Beechinor had been pounced upon by an excited and comely little person in black.

===Chinese===
这番宣告，是在前文所记诸事之后恰好两个星期发出的。而其由来是：我如今已同艾维斯·比奇诺订了婚——只是此事尚不到「公布」的时候；单是这一项让步，便是比奇诺太太从一场始而怒气冲冲、继而泪如雨下的会晤中，好不容易才强索到手的。……因为我已循着正经礼数，到塞尔伍德登门拜会过；而辞别了比奇诺太太之后，便被一个身着黑衣、情绪激昂的俊俏小个子扑上来缠住了。

===Original===
"Don't you mind a word she said," this lady had exhorted, "because she is *the* Gadarene swine, and Avis has told me everything! Of course you are to be married at once, and I only wish *I* could find the only man in the world who can keep me interested for four hours on a stretch and send my pulse up to a hundred and make me feel those thrilly thrills I've always longed for."

===Chinese===
「她说的那些话，你一个字也不必理会，」这位太太当时这样谆谆相劝，「因为她呀，就是加达拉猪群里*那头*猪（Gadarene swine，《圣经》中被鬼附身、闯下山崖投海的那群猪），而且艾维斯把什么都告诉我了！你们当然要立刻结婚；我只巴望*我*也能找到世上绝无仅有的那个男人：能让我一连四个钟头兴致不减，能把我脉搏撺上一百度，还能让我尝到那股子酥麻战栗、我朝思暮想的悸动。」

===Original===
"But surely—" said I.

===Chinese===
「可是，总不见得——」我说。

===Original===
"No, I'm beginning to be afraid not, beautiful, though of course I used to be crazy about Billy Woods; and then once I was engaged to another man for a long time, and I was perfectly devoted to him, but he *never* made me feel a single thrilly thrill. And would you believe it, Mr. Townsend?—after a while he came back, precisely as though he had been a bad penny or a cat. He had been in the Boer War and came home just a night before I left, wounded and promoted several times and completely covered with glory and brass buttons. He came seven miles to see me, and I thoroughly enjoyed seeing him, for I had on my best dress and was feeling rather talkative. Well! at ten I was quite struck on him. At eleven perfectly willing to part friends, and at twelve *crazy* for him to go. He stayed till half-past, and I didn't want to think of him for days. And, by the way, I am Miss Hugonin, and I hope you and Avis will be very happy. *Goodbye!*"

===Chinese===
「不，我如今倒开始担心没有了，美人儿——虽说从前我当然是迷比利·伍兹迷得要死的；后来又有过一回，我跟另一个男人订了很久的婚，我对他也可算死心塌地，可他*从来*不曾叫我身上起过一丝丝战栗。而且您说怪也不怪，汤森先生？——隔了一阵子他回来了，那做派俨然是一枚怎也甩不脱的坏铜板，或是一只去而复返的猫。他打过布尔战争，恰好在我动身前一夜回了家，挂了彩，升了好几回级，遍体披挂着荣光与铜纽扣。他赶了七英里路来看我，我看他来也着实受用，因为我正好穿着一身最体面的衣裳，话兴又浓。嗐！十点钟的时候，我还挺中意他。到十一点，便很情愿好聚好散；到十二点，就*发疯似的*只盼他走了。他一直赖到十二点半，之后接连好几天，我连想一想他都嫌烦。哦，顺便说一句，我是休戈宁小姐，我祝您和艾维斯幸福。*再会*！」

===Original===
"Goodbye!" said I.

===Chinese===
「再会！」我说。

### II / 二

===Original===
And that, oddly enough, was the one private talk I ever had with the Margaret Hugonin whom, for some two weeks, I had believed myself to be upon the verge of marrying; for the next time I conversed with her alone she was Mrs. William Woods.

===Chinese===
而说来也怪，同这位玛格丽特·休戈宁——我一度足足有两个星期，自以为眼看就要娶她为妻——我此生私下里谈过的，那一回竟是头一回，也是仅有的一回；因为待我下一次同她单独交谈时，她已是威廉·伍兹（William Woods）太太了。

===Original===
"Oh, go away, Billy!" she then said, impatiently "How often will I have to tell you it isn't decent to be always hanging around your wife? Oh, you dear little crooked-necktied darling!"—and she remedied the fault on tiptoe—"*please* run away and make love to somebody else, and be sure to get her name right, so that I shan't assassinate the wrong person—because I want to tell this very attractive child all about Avis, and not be bothered." And subsequently she did.

===Chinese===
「哦，走开啦，比利！」她当时不耐烦地说。「我还得告诉你多少回：一天到晚围着自己老婆打转，成什么体统？哦，你这打着歪领带的小亲亲！」——说着她踮起脚尖，把那处歪斜扶正了——「*劳您大驾*，跑到别处跟别人谈情说爱去吧，可千万把人家的名字记准了，省得我杀错了人——因为我打算把艾维斯的底细，讲给这位万分逗人喜欢的小孩儿听个透彻，不许有闲人打搅。」而事后她也果真讲了。

===Original===
But I must not forestall her confidences, lest I get my cart even further in advance of my nominal Pegasus than the loosely-made conveyance is at present lumbering.

===Chinese===
只是她的这些知心话，我不可抢先叙出，唯恐我把大车赶得更前，进一步抢到我那匹挂名的珀伽索斯（Pegasus，诗神的飞马）头里去——这辆结构松散的车驾，眼下本就已轰隆隆地抢得太前了。

### III / 三

===Original===
And meanwhile Peter Blagden and I had called at Selwoode once or twice in unison and due estate. And Peter considered "Miss Beechinor a damn fine girl, and Miss Hugonin too, only—"

===Chinese===
这期间，彼得·布拉格登和我已排开应有的仪从，成双成对地到塞尔伍德拜会过一两回。彼得认为「比奇诺小姐是个顶顶好的姑娘，休戈宁小姐也是，只是——」

===Original===
"Only," I prompted, between puffs, "Miss Hugonin keeps everybody, as my old Mammy used to say, 'in a perpetual swivet.' I never understood what the phrase meant, precisely, but I somehow always knew that it was eloquent."

===Chinese===
「只是，」我一面喷云吐雾，一面替他续道，「休戈宁小姐照我那位老奶妈的口头禅说，把人人都弄得『没时没刻不心慌意乱』。这话究竟什么意思，我始终不曾弄明白，可不知怎的，我向来就知道它雄辩有力。」

===Original===
"Just so," said Peter. "You prefer—ah—a certain amount of tranquillity. I haven't been abroad for a long while," said Mr. Blagden; and then, after another meditative pause: "Now Stella—well, Stella was a damn sight too good for me, of course—"

===Chinese===
「正是这话，」彼得说。「你偏爱——呃——几分清净。我已经许久不曾出国了，」布拉格登先生说；随后又寻思了半晌，才道：「要说斯特拉嘛——嗐，斯特拉配我，那真是好出十万八千里，这还用说——」

===Original===
"She was," I affably assented.

===Chinese===
「她确实是，」我和颜悦色地附和道。

===Original===
"—and I'd be the very last man in the world to deny it. But still you *do* prefer—" Then Peter broke off short and said: "My God, Bob! what's the matter?"

===Chinese===
「——而且，天底下头一个不肯否认这话的，就得数我。可你终究*确实*偏爱——」彼得说到这儿猛地收住话头，说道：「我的老天，鲍勃！你怎么啦？」

===Original===
So I think I must have had the ill-taste to have laughed a little over Mr. Blagden's magnanimity in regard to Stella's foibles. But I only said: "Oh, nothing, Peter! I was just going to tell you that travelling *does* broaden the mind, and that you will find an overcoat indispensable in Switzerland, and that during the voyage you ought to keep in the open air as much as possible, and that you should give the steward who waits on you at table at least ten shillings—I was just going to tell you, in fine, that you would be a fool to squander any money on a guidebook, when I am here to give you all the necessary pointers."

===Chinese===
于是我想，我当时准是趣味败坏，竟对布拉格登先生宽宥斯特拉那些小过错的宏量，失笑了一小阵。但我嘴里只说：「哦，没什么，彼得！我正要告诉你：旅行*确实*能开阔人的心智；瑞士的大衣是断乎少不得的；航程中你该尽量待在露天里；还有，侍候你用餐的侍者，你至少也该赏他十先令——总而言之，我正要告诉你：既有我在这里给你一切必要的指点，你还要糟蹋钱去买旅行指南，那可是傻子了。」

===Original===
"But I didn't mean to go to Europe exactly," said Mr. Blagden; "—I just meant to go abroad in a general sense. Any place would be abroad, you know, where people weren't always remembering how rich you were, and weren't scrambling to marry you out of hand, but really cared, you know, like she does. Oh, may be it *is* bad form to mention it, but I couldn't help seeing how she looked at you, Bob. And it waked something—Oh, I don't know what I mean," said Peter—"it's just damn foolishness, I suppose."

===Chinese===
「可我也不是存心非去欧洲不可，」布拉格登先生说，「——我只是泛泛地想出国走走。你知道，随便什么地方都算国外，只要在那里，人们不至于时时刻刻记着你多么有钱，不至于争先恐后当场就要嫁你，而是真心实意地关爱你——就像她那样。哦，提这事也许*确实*失礼，可她看你的那种眼色，我想不看也做不到，鲍勃。那眼色唤醒了一点什么——哦，我也不知自己说的是什么，」彼得说，「——我想，不过是些混账的傻念头罢了。」

===Original===
"It's very far from that," I said; and I was honestly moved, just as I always am when pathos, preferably grotesque, has caught me unprepared. This millionaire was lonely, because of his millions, and Stella was dead; and somehow I understood, and laid one hand upon his shoulder.

===Chinese===
「离傻字可远着呢，」我说；而且我这是真心动了感情——每逢哀婉之致、最好还兼几分怪诞的境味冷不防攫住我时，我一向如此。这位百万富翁是寂寞的，正因为他那几百万；而斯特拉已经死了；不知怎的，我竟体谅起来，便把一只手搭上了他的肩头。

===Original===
"Oh, *you* can't help it, I suppose, if all women love by ordinary because he is so like another person, where as men love because she is so different. My poor caliph, I would sincerely advise you to play the fool just as you plan to do—oh, anywhere—and without even a Mesrour. In fine go Bunburying at once. For very frankly, First Cousin of the Moon, it is the one thing worth while in life."

===Chinese===
「哦，*你*也是没法子的，我想——既然天底下的女人照例爱人，是因为他这般像另外一个人，而天底下的男人爱人，却是因为她这般与世上旁的人都不同。我可怜的哈里发，我诚心诚意奉劝你，就照你的盘算去当一回傻子吧——哦，随便什么地方——而且连一个梅斯罗（Mesrour，《一千零一夜》中哈里发微服出巡时随行的黑衣太监）也不必带。总而言之，赶紧去『邦伯里』（Bunburying，王尔德《不可儿戏》中的杜撰词，谓假托一位虚构亲友之名出门逍遥）走一遭吧。因为坦白说，月亮的堂弟，这可是人生里唯一值得一做的事。」

===Original===
"I half believe I will," said Peter.… So he was packing in the interim during which I pretended to be writing, and was in reality fretting to think that, whilst Avis was in England by this, I could not decently leave America until those last five chapters were finished. So, in part as an excuse for not scrawling the dullest of nonsense and subsequently tearing it up, I fell to considering the unquestionable fact that I was in love with Avis, and upon the verge of marrying her, and was in consequence, as a matter of plain logic, deliriously happy.

===Chinese===
「我倒有一半心思，真要这么办了，」彼得说。……于是，在我假装埋头写作的那段日子里，他便一直在打点行装；而我表面写着，心里却在犯愁：艾维斯此时人已到了英国，可最后那五章不脱稿，我怎么也体面不下、离不开美国。于是——一半也为了给自己找个借口，好不去涂写天下最乏味的胡话、回头再撕掉——我便转而思索起一个无可置疑的事实来：我爱着艾维斯，且已濒临娶她为妻的边缘，于是顺理成章、按明白的逻辑推论，我快乐得神魂颠倒。

===Original===
"For when you are in love with a woman you, of course, want to marry her more than you want anything else. In nature, it is a serious and—well, an almost irretrievable business. And I shall have to cultivate the domestic virtues and smoke cheaper cigarettes and all that, but I shall be glad to do every one of these things, for her sake—after a while. I shall probably enjoy doing them."

===Chinese===
「因为当你爱上一个女人，你自然想娶她，想得胜过世上任何一样东西。就天性而论，这是桩顶正经的——嗐，几乎是覆水难收的大事。往后我就得修持齐家的诸般美德，抽便宜些的香烟，如此等等；可这些事我一件一件都乐意去做，为了她——过上一阵子之后。多半还会做得津津有味呢。」

===Original===
And I read Bettie Hamlyn's letter for the seventeenth time.…

===Chinese===
而贝蒂·哈姆林的那封信，我又读到了第十七遍。……

### IV / 四

===Original===
For Bettie had answered the wild rhapsody which I wrote to tell her how much in love I was with Elena Barry-Smith. And in the nature of things I had not written Bettie again to tell her I was, and by a deal the more, in love with Avis Beechinor. The task was delicate, the reasons for my not unnatural change were such as you must transmit in a personal interview during which you are particularly boyish and talk very fast.

===Chinese===
要知道，贝蒂早已回过我一封信——回的是那封热狂如醉的抒情长信，我在信里告诉她，我何等深爱着埃莱娜·巴里-史密斯。而按事理推移，我却再不曾写信给贝蒂，告诉她我如今又爱上了艾维斯·比奇诺，而且爱得又深了许多。这桩差事精细微妙：我这番变心——原也顺理成章——的种种缘由，属于只能当面转达的那一类；转达的时候，你还得格外孩子气，话讲得飞快。

===Original===
Besides, I do not like writing letters; and moreover, there was no real need to write. I was going to Gridlington; what more natural than to ride over to Fairhaven some clear morning and tell Bettie everything? I pictured her surprise and her delight at seeing me, and reflected it would be unfair to her to render an inaccurate account of matters, such as any letter must necessarily give.

===Chinese===
再说，我本不喜欢写信；何况也实在没有写的必要。我反正要去格里灵顿；挑一个晴朗的早晨，骑马到费尔黑文走一遭，把一切原原本本告诉贝蒂，还有比这更顺理成章的么？我想象着她见我时的惊与喜，心想：任何信件管保都只能给出一篇走样的叙述，那样待她，未免不公道。

===Original===
Only, first, there was the garden of Peter's aunt—which sounds like an introductory French exercise—and then Avis came. And, somehow, I had not, in consequence, traversed the scant nine miles that lay as yet between me and Bettie Hamlyn. I kept on meaning to do it the next day.

===Chinese===
只是，头一桩，先冒出了彼得的姑妈的那座园子——这串字眼，听着活像初级法语课本里的造句练习——接着艾维斯又来了。于是不知怎的，横在我与贝蒂·哈姆林之间那段不足九英里的短短路程，我竟始终不曾走完。我一天到晚只想着，明天便去。

===Original===
And the next day after this I really did.

===Chinese===
而这样念叨过之后的第二天，我当真去了。

===Original===
"For I ought to tell Bettie about everything," I reflected. "No matter if the engagement is a secret, I ought to tell Bettie about it."

===Chinese===
「因为凡事我都该让贝蒂知道，」我思忖道。「婚约纵然还是秘密，我也该让贝蒂知道。」

### V / 五

===Original===
When I had done so, Bettie shook her head. "Oh, Robin, Robin!" she said, "how did I ever come to raise a child that doesn't know his own mind for as much as two minutes? And how dared that Barry-Smith person to slap you, I would like to know."

===Chinese===
待我讲完，贝蒂摇了摇头。「哦，罗宾哪，罗宾！」她说，「我到底是怎么养出这么个孩子来的，自己的主意连两分钟都拿不稳？还有，那位巴里-史密斯君怎敢掴你耳光，我倒要问个明白。」

===Original===
"Now you're jealous, Bettie. You are thinking she infringed upon an entirely personal privilege, and you resent it."

===Chinese===
「这会儿你吃起醋来了，贝蒂。你是在想，她侵犯了一项纯乎其纯、只属于你个人的特权，所以咽不下这口气。」

===Original===
"Well—but I've the right to, you see, and she hadn't. I consider her to be a boldfaced jig. And I don't approve of this Avis person either, you understand; but we poor mothers are always being annoyed by slushy, mushy Avises. I suppose there's a reason for it. She'll throw you over, you know, as soon as *her* mother has had an inning or two. That's why she took her to Europe," Bettie explained, with a fine confusion of personalities. "Only she just wanted any quiet place where she could take aromatic spirits of ammonia and point out between doses that she has given up her entire life to her child and has never made any demands on her and hasn't the strength to argue with her, because her heart is simply broken. We mothers always say that; and the funny part is that if you say it often enough it invariably works far better than any possible argument."

===Chinese===
「哼——可我有这个权利，你明白，她却没有。我看她是个老脸皮的轻佻女人。还有，你听好，这位艾维斯其人，我一样不赞成；可我们这些做母亲的可怜人，总免不了叫那些甜腻腻、软塌塌的艾维斯们搅扰。我想这总有个道理在。她呀，你等着瞧，一等*她*娘上场折腾那么一两回合，就把你甩了。她娘就是为了这个才带她去欧洲的，」贝蒂解释道，把各家人物张冠李戴得煞是漂亮，「她娘不过是想寻个清静地方，服她的芳香氨水，趁着一剂与一剂之间，数说她为了女儿豁出了一辈子的心血，从来不曾对她提过半点要求，如今连同她拌嘴的气力也没有了，因为她这颗心简直全碎了。我们做母亲的老是这一套；而有趣的是，这话只要说得够多，管保比天下任何道理都灵验。」

===Original===
I told her she was talking nonsense, and she said, irrelevantly enough: "Setebos, and Setebos, and Setebos! I don't think very highly of Setebos sometimes, because He muddles things so. Oh, well, I shan't cry Willow. Besides there *aren't* any sycamore-trees in the garden. So let's go into the garden, dear. That sounds as if I ate in the back pantry, doesn't it? Of course you aren't of any account any more, and you never will be, but at least you don't look at people as though they were a new sort of bug whenever they have just thought a sentence or two and then gone on, without bothering to say it."

===Chinese===
我说她在瞎三话四，她却牛头不对马嘴地答道：「塞特博斯，塞特博斯，又是塞特博斯！有的时候，我对塞特博斯实在评价不高，因为他把事情搅和得这样一塌糊涂。哦，得啦，我才不会去哭什么『杨柳』呢（《奥赛罗》中苔丝狄蒙娜的弃妇之歌，唱歌的人正坐在一棵梧桐树下悲叹）——再说，这园子里*压根儿*没有一棵梧桐树。所以，咱们进园子里去吧，亲爱的。这话听着，倒像是我平日在后头配膳室里用饭似的，是不是？当然喽，你如今已算不得什么人物，往后也永远不会算数了；可至少，人家刚在心头想过一两句话、随即又自顾想下去、懒得说出口的时候，你不会拿眼看人家，看得人家像一只新发现的虫子似的。」

===Original===
So we went into Bettie's garden. It had not changed.…

===Chinese===
于是我们走进贝蒂的园子。园子不曾变样。……

### VI / 六

===Original===
Nothing had changed. It was as though I had somehow managed, after all, to push back the hands of the clock. Fairhaven accepted me incuriously. I was only "an old student." In addition, I was vaguely rumoured to write "pieces" for the magazines. Probably I did; "old students" were often prone to vagaries after leaving King's College; for instance, they told me, Ralph Means was a professional gambler, and Ox Selwyn had lately gone to Shanghai and had settled there—and Shanghai, in common with most other places, Fairhaven accorded the negative tribute of just not absolutely disbelieving in its existence.

===Chinese===
什么都没有变。倒仿佛我终于想法子把时钟的指针拨了回去。费尔黑文漫不经意地接纳了我。我只不过是「一个老学生」罢了。此外还有含含糊糊的传闻，说我给杂志写「文章」。多半也确有其事；「老学生」们离开国王学院之后，往往容易出些怪状；譬如人家告诉我，拉尔夫·米恩斯（Ralph Means）做了职业赌徒，奥克斯·塞尔温（Ox Selwyn）新近去了上海，还在那儿落了户——而上海，同其余大多数地方一样，费尔黑文对它的存在也只肯奉上一笔消极的敬意：恰好在「并非绝对不信」这一步止住。

===Original===
Nothing had changed. The Finals were over; and with the noisy exodus of the college-boys, Fairhaven had sunk contentedly into an even deeper stupor, as Fairhaven always does in summer. And, for the rest, the unpaved sidewalks were just as dusty, the same deep ruts and the puddles which never dry, not even in mid-August, adorned Fairhaven's single street; the comfortable moss upon Fairhaven's roofs had not varied by a shade; and George Washington or Benjamin Franklin might have stepped out of any one of those brass-knockered doorways without incongruity and without finding any noticeable innovation to marvel at.

===Chinese===
什么都没有变。期末考试已经考完；随着大学生们闹闹嚷嚷地一哄而散，费尔黑文便心满意足地沉入了更深的昏睡，一如费尔黑文每逢夏天总要如此。至于其余，未铺砌的人行道照旧尘土飞扬，同样深深的车辙、同样永不干涸的水洼——连八月中旬也不干——点缀着费尔黑文唯一的那条街；费尔黑文屋顶上那层舒舒服服的青苔，深浅不曾变过一丝；便是乔治·华盛顿或者本杰明·富兰克林从哪一扇装着黄铜门环的门洞里踱出来，也不显突兀，也找不出什么可观可叹的新花样来。

===Original===
Nothing had changed. In the precise middle of the campus Lord Penniston, our Governor in Colonial days, still posed, in dingy marble; and the fracture of the finger I had inadvertently broken off, the night that Billy Woods and I painted the statue all over, in six colours, was white and new-looking. Kathleen Eppes had married her Spaniard and had left Fairhaven; otherwise the same girls were already planning their toilets for the Y.M.C.A. reception in October, which formally presents the "new students" to society at large; and presently these girls would be going to the germans or the Opera House with the younger brother of the boy who used to take them thither.…

===Chinese===
什么都没有变。校园正当中，殖民时代我们那位总督彭尼斯顿勋爵（Lord Penniston）依旧以暗淡的大理石之身摆着姿势；当年我和比利·伍兹连夜把那尊像通身涂了个遍、一共用了六种颜色，我不留神掰断的那根手指，断口至今还白得簇新。凯瑟琳·埃普斯嫁了她的西班牙人，离开了费尔黑文；除此之外，还是那同一群姑娘，已在张罗十月里青年会（Y.M.C.A.）招待会上的行头——那场招待会照例把「新学生」正式引见给大庭广众；过不多时，这些姑娘便要由当年常带她们赴会的小伙子的弟弟，领着去赴德国舞会，或上歌剧院去了。……

===Original===
Nothing had changed; not even I was changed. For I had soon discovered that Bettie Hamlyn did not care a pin for me in myself. She was simply very fond of me because, at times, I reminded her of a boy who had gone to King's College; and her reception of me, for the first two days, was unmistakably provisional.

===Chinese===
什么都没有变；连我也未曾变样。因为我很快就发现，贝蒂·哈姆林对「我自己」这个人，是半个子儿也不放在心上的。她不过是真心疼我，因为我不时叫她想起一个上过国王学院的男孩；而她起初两天对我的接待，明明白白是试用性质的。

===Original===
"Very well!" I said.

===Chinese===
「很好！」我说。

===Original===
And I did it. For I knew how difficult it was to deceive Bettie, and in consequence all my faculties rose to the challenge. I did not merely mimic my former self, I was compelled, almost, to believe I was indeed that former self, because not otherwise could I get Bettie Hamlyn's toleration. Had I paused even momentarily to reflect upon the excellence of my acting, she would have known. So I resolutely believed I was being perfectly candid; and with constant use those older tricks of speech and gesture and almost of thought, at first laborious mimicry, became well-nigh involuntary.

===Chinese===
而我也当真这么办了。因为我知道，要瞒过贝蒂是何等不易，于是我浑身的本领都被这一挑战激发了起来。我岂止是模仿从前的自己，我简直是被逼着——几乎可说是被逼着——相信我当真就是从前那个自己，因为不如此，我便得不到贝蒂·哈姆林的宽容。倘若我哪怕只停顿一瞬，暗自赞一声自己演技之高明，她便立刻识破了。于是我斩钉截铁地相信，自己坦诚得不能再坦诚；而那些旧日的言语做派、乃至几乎连思绪的路数，起初还是费力的模拟，用的久了，竟渐渐成了不由自主。

===Original===
In fine, we could not wipe away five years, but with practice we found that you would very often forget them, and for quite a while.…

===Chinese===
总而言之，那五年我们抹它不掉；但经了练习，我们发觉，你往往可以把它忘掉——而且一忘就是老长一阵子。……

===Original===
I had explained to Bettie's father I was going to board with them that summer. Had I not been so haphazard in the progress of this narrative, I would have earlier announced that Bettie's father was the Latin professor at King's College. He was very old and vague, and his general attitude toward the universe was that of remote recollection of having noticed something of the sort before. Professor Hamlyn, therefore, told me he was glad to hear of my intended stay beneath his roof; hazarded the speculation that I had written a book which he meant to read upon the very first opportunity; blinked once or twice; and forthwith lapsed into consideration of some Pliocene occurrence which, if you were to judge by the expression of his mild old countenance, he did not find entirely satisfactory.…

===Chinese===
我已向贝蒂的父亲说明，那年夏天我要在他们家搭伙寄住。倘若我这篇叙事行进得不至于这样颠三倒四，我本该早些交代：贝蒂的父亲是国王学院的拉丁语教授。他年事已高，神思恍惚，对宇宙万有的总态度，是一种远远的追忆，仿佛从前也留意过这路名堂。于是哈姆林教授告诉我，听说我要在他屋檐下盘桓，他很高兴；又试着冒猜了一回，说我写过一部书，他有意一得空便拜读；眨了一两回眼；随即又坠入对上新世（Pliocene）某一桩事变的沉思之中去了——若凭他那张温厚老脸的表情判断，那桩事变他是不怎么满意的。……

===Original===
So I spent three months in Fairhaven; and Bettie and I read all the old books over again, and were perfectly happy.

===Chinese===
就这样，我在费尔黑文住了三个月；贝蒂和我把旧日的书统统重读了一遍，快活得很。

### VII / 七

===Original===
And what I wrote in those last five chapters of my book was so good that in common decency I was compelled to alter the preceding twenty-nine and bring them a bit nearer to Bettie's standard. For I was utilising Bettie's ideas. She did not have the knack of putting them on paper; that was my trivial part, as I now recognised with a sort of scared reverence.

===Chinese===
而我那部书最后五章写出来的东西，好到这步田地：出于起码的体面，我不得不把前头二十九章也改上一改，让它们稍稍向贝蒂的标准靠拢。因为我借用的是贝蒂的意思。把意思落到纸面上，这诀窍她是没有的；那是我微末的本分——这一点我如今怀着几分战战兢兢的敬意，才算认清。

===Original===
"Of course, though, you had to meddle," I would scold at her. "I had meant the infernal thing to be a salable book. Today it is just a stenographic report of how these people elected to behave. I haven't anything to do with it. I wash my hands of it. I consider you, in fine, a cormorant, a conscienceless marauder, a meddlesome Mattie, *and* a born dramatist."

===Chinese===
「不过，你当然是插手了，」我便这样数落她。「我原打算把这该死的东西写成一册卖得起价的书。如今它成了一纸速记报告，专记这伙人自己拣定的行止。同它我再无干系。我撒手不管了。总而言之，我认为你是一只贪馋的鸬鹚，一名丧尽天良的劫掠者，一个多事的玛蒂（meddlesome Mattie，旧时童谣里专会多手闯祸的孩子），*外加*一位天生的戏剧家。」

===Original===
"But, it's *much* better than anything you've ever done, Robin—"

===Chinese===
「可是，它比你以往写过的随便什么都*强得多*呀，罗宾——」

===Original===
"That is what I'm grumbling about. I consider it very unfeeling of you to write better novels than I do," I retorted. "But, oh, how good that scene is!" I said, a little later.

===Chinese===
「我埋怨的正是这个。你写的小说比我写得好，我认为你这心肠实在狠，」我反唇相讥。「可是，嗐，那一场戏有多好！」过了一小会儿，我又说。

===Original===
"Let's see—'For you, dear clean-souled girl, were born to be the wife of a strong man, and the mother of his dirty children'—no, it's 'sturdy,' but then you hardly ever cross your T's. And where he goes on to tell her he can't marry her, because he is artistic, and she is too practical for them to be real mates, and all that other feeblemindedness? Dear me, did I forget to tell you we were going to cut that out?"

===Chinese===
「我且看看——『因为你，灵魂洁净的好姑娘，天生该做强人的妻子，做他那群脏孩子的母亲』——不对，原文是『结实』，可你写起字来，几乎从不给 T 加横。还有他接着告诉她不能娶她的那一段——理由是他是搞艺术的，她太讲实际，他们成不了真正的心灵佳偶，还有那一整套的弱智呓语？哎呀，我是不是忘了告诉你，那段我们是要删掉的？」

===Original===
"But I particularly like that part—"

===Chinese===
「可我偏偏顶喜欢那一段——」

===Original===
"Do you?" said Bettie, as her pen scrunched vicious lines through it. Then she said: "I only hope she had the civility and self-control not to laugh until you had gone away. And 'We irrelevant folk that design all useless and beautiful things,' indeed! No, I couldn't have blamed her if she laughed right out. I wonder if you will never understand that what you take to be your love for beautiful things is really just a dislike of ugly ones? Oh, I've no patience with you! And wanting to print it in a book, too, instead of being content to make yourself ridiculous in tête-à-têtes with minxes that don't especially matter!"

===Chinese===
「是么？」贝蒂说着，笔下一道道狠劲的杠子把那段涂了个透。随后她又说：「我只巴望那位姑娘懂几分客气、有几分克制，能忍到你走了以后再笑。还有那句『我们这些不识时务之辈，专造一切无用而美好的物事』——好一个『不识时务』！不，她要真当场笑出声来，我也怪不得她。我纳闷你是不是一辈子也明白不过来：你自以为是对美好事物的热爱，其实不过是对丑陋事物的厌恶？哦，我真对你没耐心！而且还想把它印进书里，倒不甘心只在同几个不甚要紧的狐媚子促膝密谈时出出洋相！」

===Original===
"Well—! Anyhow, I agree with you that, thanks to your editing and carping and general scurrility, this book is going to be," I meekly stated, "a little better than *The Apostates* and not just 'pretty much like any other book.' "

===Chinese===
「这个——！不管怎么说，我同意你的看法：多亏了你的编削、挑刺和一以贯之的刻薄，这部书将来会是，」我低声下气地申明，「比《背教者》略强一点的东西，而不至只是『同随便哪本书都差不太多』。」

===Original===
"Do you know that's just what I was thinking," said Bettie, dolefully. She clasped both hands behind her crinkly small black head, and in that queer habitual pose appraised me, from between her elbows, in that way which always made me feel I had better be careful. "Damn you!" was her verdict.

===Chinese===
「你可知道，我方才心里想的正是这句，」贝蒂凄凄惨惨地说。她把两只手扣在鬈曲的乌黑小脑瓜后面，摆出那个古怪的老姿势，从两肘之间把我估量了一番——那副估量的眼色，素来叫我自觉还是小心为妙。「该死的！」这便是她的判词。

===Original===
"Whence this unmaidenliness?" I queried, with due horror.

===Chinese===
「这份没个姑娘样的做派，是从何说起？」我应景地惊问道。

===Original===
"You are trying to prove to me that it has been worth while. This nasty book is coming alive, here in our own eight-cornered room, with a horrid crawly life of its own that it would never have had if you hadn't been learning things my boy knew nothing about. That's what you are crowing in my face, when you keep quiet and smirk. Oh, but I know you!"

===Chinese===
「你是想向我证明，这一切总算没有白费。这本难缠的书，就在咱们自家这间八角形的小屋里，正活起来了，活出一种虫豸般蠕蠕而动的可怕生命——若不是你学会了些我的男孩全然不懂得的东西，它一辈子也活不成这样。你闷声不响、嘴角挂着笑的时候，冲着我脸啼叫得意的，正是这个。哦，你那点子心思，我还不知道！」

===Original===
"You do think, then, that, between you and me, it is really coming alive?"

===Chinese===
「那么你当真以为，你我之间，它确乎是在活过来了？」

===Original===
"Yes—if that greatly matters to the fat literary gent that I don't care for greatly. Yes, the infernal thing will be a Book, with quite a sizable *B*. I am feeding its maw with more important things than a few ideas, though. The thing is a monster that isn't worth its keep. For my boy was worth more than a Book," she said, forlornly—"oh, oceans more!"

===Chinese===
「是——倘若这件事，对于这位我不怎么待见的胖文学绅士还算有几分要紧的话。是的，这该死的东西将成其为一部书（Book），那个 *B* 还相当不小。只不过，我如今喂进它那张利口的，可比几条念头要紧得多。这东西是头怪物，饲养它的花费抵不上它的用处。因为我的男孩，可比一部书值钱多了，」她凄然说道——「哦，值钱得海了去了！」

### VIII / 八

===Original===
All in all, we were a deal more than happy during these three very hot months. It was a sort of Lotus Eaters' existence, shared by just us two, with Josiah Clarriker intruding occasionally, and with echoes from the outer world, when heard at all, resounding very dimly and unimportantly. I began almost to assume, as Fairhaven tacitly assumed, that there was really no outer world, or none at least to be considered seriously.…

===Chinese===
总而言之，在这大热的三个月里，我们的快活，远不止快活而已。那是一种食莲人（Lotus Eaters，《奥德赛》中食忘忧之莲的民族）式的日子，只你我两个人共享，偶尔有乔赛亚·克拉里克（Josiah Clarriker）来打搅一场；外间世界的声息，纵然偶尔传来，也回响得极其微弱、无足轻重。我几乎渐渐信以为真——像费尔黑文心照不宣地信着的那样——外间世界其实并不存在，或者说，至少没有一个值得认真看待的世界。……

===Original===
For instance: Marian Winwood had come to Lichfield, and wrote me from there, "hoping that we would renew an acquaintance which she remembered so pleasurably." It did not seem worth while, of course, to answer the minx; I decided, at a pinch, to say that the Fairhaven mail-service was abominable, and that her letter had never reached me. But the young fellow who two years ago had wandered about the Green Chalybeate with her had become, now, as unreal as she. I glimpsed the couple, with immeasurable aloofness, as phantoms flickering about the mirage of a brook, throwing ghostly bread crumbs to Lethean minnows.

===Chinese===
举例说吧：玛丽安·温伍德到利奇菲尔德来了，还从那里写了封信给我，「盼望我们重续一段她记忆中何等愉快的旧谊」。给这狐媚子回信，自然犯不上；我打定主意，情急时便推说费尔黑文的邮政糟糕透顶，她的信压根儿没到过我手里。可是，两年前曾陪她在绿铁泉一带徜徉的那个年轻人，如今也已同她一样地不真实了。我隔着无限遥远的距离瞥见这一对儿，如同瞥见两条在小溪蜃景旁摇曳的幽灵，正把阴间的面包屑，撒给忘川（Lethe）里的小鲦鱼。

===Original===
And then, too, when the police caught Ned Lethbury that summer, it hardly seemed worth while to wonder about his wife. For she was, inexplicably, with him, all through the trial at Chiswick, you may remember, though you were probably more interested at the time by the Humbert trial in Paris. In any event, no rumor came to me in Fairhaven to connect Amelia Lethbury with Nadine Neroni, but, instead, a deal of journalistic pity and sympathy for her, the faithful, much-enduring wife. Still quite a handsome woman, they said, for all her suffering and poverty.… And when he went to the penitentiary, Amelia Lethbury disappeared, nobody knew whither, except that I suspected Anton von Anspach knew. I could not explain the mystery. I did not greatly care to, for to me it did not seem important, now.…

===Chinese===
还有，那年夏天警方拿住内德·莱思伯里的时候，为他那位太太操心，似乎也犯不上。因为你可以记得，在奇西克（Chiswick）全程庭审中，她都莫名其妙地陪在他身边——虽然你当时多半更关心巴黎的洪贝尔案（Humbert trial）。无论如何，在费尔黑文，没有任何风声传到我耳里，把阿梅莉亚·莱思伯里同娜丁·内罗尼牵到一处；传来的倒是新闻界对她——这位忠贞不渝、含辛茹苦的妻子——连篇累牍的怜惜与同情。据说她虽历经苦难、清贫潦倒，风韵犹自不小。……而待他下狱之后，阿梅莉亚·莱思伯里便失踪了，无人知其去向；只有一点——我疑心安东·冯·安斯巴赫是知道的。这桩谜，我解不开。我也懒得大解，因为在我如今看来，它已算不得要紧。……

### IX / 九

===Original===
Meantime, I meditated.

===Chinese===
这期间，我思前想后。

===Original===
"I am in love with Avis—oh, granted! I am not the least bit in love with—we will euphemistically say 'anyone else.' But confound it! I am coming to the conclusion that marrying a woman because you happen to be in love with her is about as logical a proceeding as throwing the cat out of the window because the rhododendrons are in bloom. Why, if I marry Avis I shall probably have to live with her the rest of my life!

===Chinese===
「我爱着艾维斯——哦，不否认！至于爱上——我们且婉转地说——『任何别人』，那可一丝一毫也没有。可是真见鬼！我渐渐得出这么个结论：只因为你碰巧爱上了一个女人便娶她，其合乎逻辑的程度，约莫同杜鹃花开得正盛便把猫扔出窗外差不多。嗐，我若娶了艾维斯，往后多半得同她共度余生！

===Original===
"What if that obsolete notion of Schopenhauer's were true after all—that love is a blind instinct which looks no whit toward the welfare of the man and woman it dominates, but only to the equipment a child born of them would inherit? What if, after all, love tends, without variation, to yoke the most incompatible in order that the average type of humanity may be preserved? Then the one passion we esteem as sacred would be simply the deranged condition of any other beast in rutting-time. Then we, with the pigs and sparrows, would be just so many pieces on the chessboard, and our evolutions would be just a friendly trial of skill between what we call life and death.

===Chinese===
「倘若叔本华（Schopenhauer）那套过了时的论调终究是真的呢——说什么爱是一种盲目的本能，对它所支配的男人女人的福祉毫不在意，只顾念他们将来生下的孩子所要承袭的那副资质禀赋？倘若爱终究一成不变地总要撮合最不相配的人，好教人类的平均类型得以保存呢？那么，我们奉为神圣的这唯一的激情，便不过是任何一头发情期中的牲口的癫狂状态。那么，我们连同猪猡和麻雀，统统不过是棋盘上那么多枚棋子，我们的进退周旋，不过是我们所谓的生与死二者之间一场友谊赛的斗技罢了。

===Original===
"I love Avis Beechinor. But I have loved, in all sincerity, many other women, and I rejoice today, unfeignedly, that I never married any of them. For marriage means a lifelong companionship, a long, long journey wherein must be adjusted, one by one, each tiniest discrepancy between the fellow-wayfarers; and always a pebble if near enough to the eye will obscure a mountain.

===Chinese===
「我爱艾维斯·比奇诺。可我也曾真心实意爱过许多别的女人；而今天，我为从不曾娶过她们中的任何一个，由衷地庆幸。因为婚姻意味着终身的伴侣，一段长而又长的旅程，同路人之间每一丝最细微的龃龉，都得在这段路上逐一调停；而一粒石子，只要凑得离眼睛够近，总能遮没一座大山。

===Original===
"Why, Avis cannot attempt a word of four syllables without coming at least once to grief! It is a trifle of course, but in a lifelong companionship there are exactly fourteen thousand trifles to one event of importance. And deuce take it! the world is populated by men and women, not demigods; the poets are specious and abandoned rhetoricians; for it never was, and never will be, possible to love anybody 'to the level of everyday's / Most quiet need by sun or candlelight.'

===Chinese===
「嗐，艾维斯每要试说一个四个音节的词，总免不了至少栽一个跟头！这当然是桩小事，可在一辈子的伴侣之间，恰恰是一万四千件小事对一件要紧事的比例。而且见它的鬼！这世界上住的是男人和女人，不是半神；诗人个个是貌似有理、自甘堕落的辞令家；因为要爱任何人爱到『以朝朝暮暮、最平静的需要为度，无论凭日光还是烛光』（勃朗宁夫人十四行诗语），从来不可能，往后也永远不会可能。

===Original===
"Or not to me at least.

===Chinese===
「——或者说，至少对我不曾可能。

===Original===
"In a sentence, when it comes to a lifelong companionship, I prefer not the woman who would make me absolutely happy for a twelvemonth, but rather the woman with whom I could chat contentedly for twenty years, and who would keep me to the mark. I am rather tired of being futile; and not for any moral reason, but because it is not worthy of *me*. In fine, I do not want to die entirely. I want to leave behind some not inadequate expression of Robert Etheridge Townsend, and I do not care at all what people say of it, so that it is here when I am gone. Oh, Stella understood! 'I want my life to count, I want to leave something in the world that wasn't there before I came.'

===Chinese===
「一句话，说到终身伴侣，我偏不要那位能叫我绝对快活上一年的女人，而要那位能同我称心闲谈上二十年、还能盯住我不让我懈怠的女人。我一无所成的日子，过得有些腻了；这倒不为任何道德的缘故，只因那样配不上*我*。总而言之，我不想死得干干净净。我要留下一点不太寒碜的东西，作为罗伯特·埃瑟里奇·汤森的表达；人家对它说长道短，我一概不在乎，只要我人走之后它还在世上。哦，斯特拉是懂的！『我要我的一生算得了数，我要在这世上留下一点我来之前不曾有过的东西。』

===Original===
"Now Bettie—"

===Chinese===
「至于贝蒂嘛——」

===Original===
I arose resolutely. "I had much better go for a long, and tedious, and jolting, and universally damnable walk. Bettie would make something vital of me—if I could afford her the material—"

===Chinese===
我毅然起了身。「我还是去走一趟长路的好，走一趟又乏味、又颠簸、又无一处不遭人咒骂的远路。贝蒂是能把我造就成个有生气的人物的——只要我供得起她这份料——」

===Original===
And I grinned a little. " 'Go, therefore, now, and work; for there shall no straw be given you, yet shall ye deliver the tale of bricks.' Yes, you would certainly have need of a miracle, dear Bettie—"

===Chinese===
说着我又咧嘴笑了笑。「『现在你们去作工罢！草是不给你们的，砖却要如数交纳。』（《圣经·出埃及记》语）是啊，亲爱的贝蒂，你准得行个神迹才成——」

### X / 十

===Original===
I started for that walk I was to take. But Dr. Jeal and Colonel Snawley were seated in armchairs in front of Clarriker's Emporium, just as they had been used to sit there in my college days, enjoying, as the Colonel mentioned, "the cool of the evening," although to the casual observer the real provider of their pleasure would have appeared to be an unlimited supply of chewing-tobacco.

===Chinese===
我动身去走那趟该走的路。可吉尔医生和斯诺利上校正坐在克拉里克商场门前的扶手椅里，同我上大学那些年他们惯常坐的模样分毫不差，照上校的说法，是在享用「晚间清凉」——虽然在随便哪个路人看来，他们那份快乐的真正供奉者，乃是取之不竭的嚼烟。

===Original===
So I lingered here, and garnered, to an accompaniment of leisurely expectorations, much knowledge as to the fall crops and the carryings-on of the wife of a celebrated general, upon whose staff the Colonel had served during the War—and there has never been in the world's history but one war, so far as Fairhaven is concerned—and how the Colonel walked right in on them, and how it was hushed up.

===Chinese===
于是我便在这儿耽搁下来，在从容不迫的吐痰声伴奏之下，收获了许多学问：秋收的年成啦；某位鼎鼎大名的将军夫人的那些行径啦——战争期间，上校曾在那位将军的幕下效力，而且照费尔黑文看来，世界史上从来也只有过那一场战争；还有上校如何不偏不倚一头撞进他们中间，这桩事又如何被捂了下来。

===Original===
Then we discussed the illness of Pope Leo and what everybody knew about those derned cardinals, and the riots in Evansville, and the Panama Canal business, and the squally look of things at Port Arthur, and attributed all these imbroglios, I think, to the Republican administration. Even at our bitterest, though, we conceded that "Teddy's" mother was a Bulloch, and that his uncle fired the last shot before the *Alabama* went down. And that inclined us to forgive him everything, except of course, the Booker Washington luncheon.

===Chinese===
随后我们议论了利奥教皇的病况，议论了关于那帮天杀的红衣主教人人皆知的那些事儿，议论了埃文斯维尔（Evansville）的骚乱、巴拿马运河那摊子事、旅顺那边风雨欲来的局面，并且——我记得——把这重重乱子一股脑儿归咎于共和党政府。不过，纵在我们骂得最狠的时候，我们也肯承认：「泰迪」的母亲是布洛克（Bulloch）家的人，他舅舅在「亚拉巴马号」（*Alabama*）沉没前打出了最后一炮。有这一层，我们便倾向于原谅他的一切了——当然，布克·华盛顿（Booker Washington）那次午宴除外。

===Original===
Then half a block farther on, Mrs. Rabbet wanted to know if I had ever seen such weather, and to tell me exactly what Adrian, Junior—no longer little Adey, no indeed, sir, but ready to start right in at the College session after next, and as she often said to Mr. Rabbet you could hardly believe it—had observed the other day, and quick as a flash too, because it would make such a funny story. Only she could never quite decide whether it happened on a Tuesday or a Wednesday, so that, after precisely seven digressions on this delicate point, the denouement of the tale, I must confess, fell rather flat.

===Chinese===
再往前走了半条街，拉贝特太太（Mrs. Rabbet）先要考考我可曾见过这样的天气，再要原原本本讲给我听，小阿德里安（Adrian, Junior）——早不是从前的小阿迪（Adey）了，可不是嘛，先生，再隔一个学期一开学就要进学院念书了，而且正如她常对拉贝特先生说的，你简直不敢相信——前两天观察到的一桩事，脑筋还转得飞快，因为这故事保管好笑。只是，那桩事究竟出在星期二还是星期三，她永远拿不大定主意；于是，在这个微妙的节骨眼上足足绕了七回弯子之后，恕我直言，故事的结局倒是落得相当平淡。

===Original===
And then Mab Spessifer demanded that I come up on the porch and draw some pictures for her. The child was waiting with three sheets of paper and a chewed pencil all ready, just on the chance that I might pass; and you cannot very well refuse a cripple who adores you and is not able to play with the other brats. You get instead into a kind of habit of calling every day and trying to make her laugh, because she is such a helpless little nuisance.

===Chinese===
接着，玛布·斯佩西弗（Mab Spessifer）便拉住我，硬要我上廊子去给她画几笔画。那孩子早备好了三张纸和一支啃得坑坑洼洼的铅笔，专赌着我或许会打这儿过；一个崇拜你、又没法子同别的小顽童玩耍的瘸腿孩子，你总不便回绝。反过来，你倒养成了每天登门、逗她发笑的习惯，因为她是那样一个叫人束手无策的小累赘。

===Original===
And tousled mothers weep over you in passageways and tell you how good you are, and altogether the entire affair is tedious; but having started it, you keep it up, somehow.

===Chinese===
而蓬乱着头发的母亲们，会在过道里对着你淌眼抹泪，夸你心肠真好；总而言之，这整个儿一摊子事乏味得很；可既已开了头，不知怎么，你也就一路维持了下来。

### XI / 十一

===Original===
In fine, it is a symbol that I never took the walk which was to dust the cobwebs from my brain and make me just like all the other persons, thick about me, who grow up, and mate, and beget, and die, in the incurious fashion of oxen, without ever wondering if there is any plausible reason for doing it; and my brief progress was upon the surface very like that of the bedeviled fellow in *Les Facheux*. Yet I enjoyed it somehow. Never to be hurried, and always to stop and talk with every person whom you meet, upon topics in which no conceivable human being could possibly be interested, may not sound attractive, but in Fairhaven it is the rule; and, oddly enough, it breeds, in practice, a sort of family feeling—if only by entitling everybody to the condoned and matter-of-course stupidity of aunts and uncles—which is not really all unpleasant.

===Chinese===
总而言之，这是一个象征：我始终不曾走成那趟本该掸净我脑中蛛网、把我变得同周遭稠密的人群一模一样的散步——那些人长大、交配、生养、死去，全凭牛马一般浑浑噩噩的路数，从不问一声这样做可有什么说得通的理由；而我这一路短短的行进，表面上看，同《讨厌鬼》（*Les Facheux*，莫里哀的芭蕾喜剧）里那个处处被纠缠的倒霉家伙几无二致。然而不知怎的，我竟受用。从不匆忙赶路，逢人必停下来交谈，谈的又尽是任何想得出来的人类都不可能发生兴趣的题目——这听上去未必动人，可在费尔黑文这是规矩；而且说来奇怪，实行起来，它竟培育出一种家族之情——哪怕仅仅是让人人都有资格享受姑舅姨婶们那种可以获得宽宥、被视作理所当然的愚昧——倒也实在并非全然不愉快。

===Original===
So I went home at half-past seven, to supper and to Bettie, in a quite contented frame of mind. It did not seem conceivable that any world so beautiful and stupid and well-meaning could have either the heart or the wit to thwart my getting anything I really wanted; and the thought elated me.

===Chinese===
于是七点半，我心满意足地回家吃晚饭、找贝蒂去了。这样美丽、愚蠢而与人为善的一个世界，居然会有那份心肠、或那份头脑，来阻挠我到手我真心想要的任何东西——这似乎是不可想象的；这个念头叫我意气昂扬。

===Original===
Only I did not know, precisely, what I wanted.

===Chinese===
只是，我究竟想要什么，我自己也说不大准。

## XXVIII / 二十八

He Participates in Sundry Confidences / 他参与了种种密谈

### I / 一

===Original===
I was in the act of writing to Avis when the letter came; and I put it aside unopened, until after supper, for I had never found the letters of Avis particularly interesting reading.

===Chinese===
那封信寄到时，我正给艾维斯写信；我把它搁在一边，原封不动，留到晚饭以后再拆——因为艾维斯的来信，我从来不觉得有多少趣味可读。

===Original===
“It will be what they call a newsy letter, of course. I do wish that Avis would not write to me as if she were under oath to tell the entire truth. She communicates so many things which actually happened that it reads like a ‘special correspondent’ in some country town writing for a Sunday morning’s paper—and with, to a moral certainty, the word ‘separate’ lurking somewhere spelt with three *E*’s, and an ‘always’ with two *L*’s, and at least one ‘alright.’ No, my dear, I am at present too busy expressing my adoration for you to be exposed to such inharmonious jars.”

===Chinese===
「这封信，不用问，准是他们所谓的『满纸新闻』。我真心希望艾维斯给我写信时，别摆出一副如宣誓庭上、非把全部实话倒尽不可的架势。她汇报的事千真万确、全然发生过，读上去倒活像外省小镇某位给星期日晨报写稿的『特派记者』——而且十有八九，信里某处潜伏着拼成三个 *E* 的 seperate（『分装』之误拼），一个两个 *L* 的 allways（『永远』之误拼），外加至少一处 alright（all right 之讹体）。不，亲爱的，我眼下倾吐对你的爱慕还忙不过来，可经不起这等不谐和的刺耳之声。」

===Original===
Then I wrote my dithyrambs and sealed them. Subsequently I poised the unopened letter between my fingers.

===Chinese===
随后我写完我的狂歌，把信封了口。接着，我把那封未拆的信夹在指间掂量。

===Original===
“But remember that if she were here to *say* all this to you, your pulses would be pounding like the pistons of an excited locomotive! Nature, you are a jade! I console myself with the reflection that it is frequently the gift of facile writing which makes the corespondent—but I *do* wish you were not such a hazardous matchmaker. Oh, well! there was no pleasant way of getting out of it, and that particular Rubicon is miles behind.”

===Chinese===
「可是要记住：倘若她人就在这儿，亲口对你说出这一切，你的脉搏管保会像一台发急的火车头的活塞似的砰砰猛撞！造化啊，你真是个喜怒无常的贱妇！我聊以自慰地想：造就『通信人』——也就是离婚案里的『共同被告』（correspondent/co-respondent 之双关）——的，往往正是那支下笔轻快的才情；可我*实在*巴望你别当这么个乱点鸳鸯的危险媒人。嗐，算了！当初本就没有什么痛快的脱身之路，何况那一条具体的卢比孔河（Rubicon），早已落在身后好几英里了。」

===Original===
I slit the envelope.

===Chinese===
我裁开了信封。

===Original===
I read the letter through again, with redoubling interest, and presently began to laugh. “So she begins to fear we have been somewhat hasty, asks a little time for reconsideration of her precise sentiment toward me, and feels meanwhile in honour bound to release me from our engagement! Yet if upon mature deliberation—eh, oh, yes! twaddle! *and* commonplace! and dashed, of course, with a jigger of Scriptural quotation!”

===Chinese===
我把信从头到尾又读了一遍，兴趣倍增，不一会儿便笑出了声。「这么说，她开始担心我们行事未免仓促了些，请求给她一点工夫，重新考虑她对我的那份确切情意，同时觉得在此期间，凭道义必须把我从婚约中释放出来！然而倘若经过成熟的深思熟虑——呃，哦，可不是嘛！全是空话！*外加*陈词滥调！当然，还兑上了一小盅《圣经》里的引文！」

===Original===
I paused to whistle. “There is strange milk in this coconut, could I but discern its nature.”

===Chinese===
我停下来吹了声口哨。「这椰子里准藏着古怪的奶水，只消我能辨出它的名堂才好。」

### II / 二

===Original===
I did, some four weeks later, when with a deal of mail I received the last letter I was ever to receive from Avis Beechinor.

===Chinese===
大约四个星期之后，我果然辨出来了——那天，随一大堆邮件，我收到了我此生从艾维斯·比奇诺那里收到的最后一封信。

===Original===
Wrote Avis:

===Chinese===
艾维斯写道：

===Original===
Dear Robert:

Thank you very much for returning my letters and for the beautiful letter you wrote me. No I believe it better you should not come on to see me now and talk the matter over as you suggest because it would probably only make you unhappy. And then too I am sure some day you will be friends with me and a very good and true one. I return the last letter you sent me in a seperate envelope, and I hope it will reach you alright, but as I destroy all my mail as soon as I have read it I cannot send you the others. I have promised to marry Mr. Blagden and we are going to be married on the fifteenth of this month very quietly with no outsiders. So goodbye Robert. I wish you every success and happiness that you may desire and with all my heart I pray you to be true to your better self. God bless you allways.

Your sincere friend,

Avis M. Beechinor

===Chinese===
亲爱的罗伯特：

多谢您退还我的那些信，也多谢您写给我的那封美好的信。不，我想您最好还是不要现在过来看我、照您的提议把事情当面谈开，因为那多半只会叫您不快活。再说，我也确信，有朝一日您会同我做成朋友，做成一位非常要好、非常忠实的朋友。我把您最近寄我的那封信，另装在一个信封（原文误拼作 seperate）里退还给您，但愿它安然无恙（原文误拼作 alright）地寄到；至于其余各封，我每每一读过便销毁，所以无法奉还。我已应允嫁给布拉格登先生，我们定于本月十五日成婚，一切从简，不请一位外客。那么再会了，罗伯特。我祝您得偿所愿、诸事顺遂、幸福美满，并且我全心全意地祈求您，忠于您那个更良善的自我。愿上帝永远（原文误拼作 allways）保佑您。

您真诚的朋友，

艾维斯·M·比奇诺

===Original===
I indulged in a low and melodious whistle. “The little slut!”

===Chinese===
我吹了一声低低的、悦耳的呼哨。「小贱人！」

===Original===
Then I said: “Peter Blagden again! I *do* wish that life would try to be a trifle more plausible. Why, but, of course! Peter meant to go chasing after her the minute my back was turned, and that was why he salved his conscience by presenting me with that thousand ‘to get married on.’ Even at the time it seemed peculiarly un-Petrine. Well, anyhow, in simple decency, he cannot combine the part of Shylock with that of Judas, and expect to have back his sordid lucre, so I am that much to the good, apart from everything else. Yes, I can see how it all happened—and I can foresee what is going to happen, too, thank heaven!”

===Chinese===
随后我说道：「又是彼得·布拉格登！我*真*希望人生这东西，多少再讲一点情理。嗐，可是——当然啦！彼得打的原就是这主意：等我一转身，便立刻追她而去；所以他才拿那一千元『结婚用』的厚赠来膏他的良心。即便在当时，这一手也显得格外地『非彼得作风』（un-Petrine，戏用与圣彼得同名之语作的双关）。嗐，不管怎么说，凭起码的体面，他总不能既扮夏洛克，又扮犹大，还指望把那笔肮脏钱收回去；这么着，撇开其余一切不谈，单这一项我便有赚。是的，这一切是怎么闹出来的，我看得明白——往后还要闹出什么来，我也预见得到，谢天谢地！」

===Original===
For, as drowning men are said to recollect the unrecallable, I had vividly seen in that instant the two months’ action just overpast, and its three participants—the thin-lipped mother, the besotted millionaire, and the girl shakily hesitant between ideals and the habits of a lifetime.

===Chinese===
因为——正如人说溺水的人会把平生想不起的事都想起来——就在那一瞬，方才过去的两个月的这出戏，连同它的三名演员，已历历在我眼前演过：那薄嘴唇的母亲，那如痴如醉的百万富翁，还有那在理想与半生积习之间哆哆嗦嗦拿不定主意的姑娘。

===Original===
“But I might have known the mother would win,” I reflected: “Why, didn’t Bettie say she would?”

===Chinese===
「可我本该料到赢的准是母亲，」我反思道，「嗐，贝蒂当初不就说过她会赢么？」

===Original===
I refolded the letter I had just read, to keep it as a salutary relic; and then:

===Chinese===
我把方才读过的那封信重新折好，留作一件有益身心的圣物；随后：

===Original===
“Dear Avis!” said I; “now heaven bless your common sense! and I don’t especially mind if heaven blesses your horrific painted hag of a mother, also, if they’ve a divine favor or two to spare.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的艾维斯！」我说，「现在愿上天保佑你的常识！至于你那位涂脂抹粉、可怕煞人的老妖婆母亲，上天若还匀得出一两桩神恩来，也一并保佑了她，我倒不特别反对。」

===Original===
And I saw there was a letter from Peter Blagden, too. It said, in part:

===Chinese===
我还看见彼得·布拉格登也来了一封信。信里有一部分这样说：

===Original===
I am everything that you think me, Bob. My one defence is that I could not help it. I loved her from the moment I saw her … You did not appreciate her, you know. You take, if you will forgive my saying it, too light a view of life to value the love of a good woman properly, and Avis noticed it of course. Now I do understand what the unselfish love of woman means, because my first wife was an angel, as you know … It is a comfort to think that my dear saint in heaven knows I am not quite so lonely now, and is gladdened by that knowledge. I know she would have wished it—

===Chinese===
鲍勃，你想我是什么，我便是什么。我唯一的辩白是：我实在情不自禁。从见到她的那一刻起，我就爱上了她……你并不赏识她，你知道。恕我直言，你对人生看得太轻飘，不能真正懂得一个善良女子的爱情有何可贵，艾维斯当然也看了出来。如今我可算懂得了女人无私的爱意味着什么，因为我的第一位妻子是位天使，这你是知道的……想到我那位天上亲爱的圣徒知道我如今不再那么孤单，并为此感到欣悦，实在是一桩安慰。我知道她是会愿意的——

===Original===
I read no further. “Oh, Stella! they have all forgotten. They all insist today that you were an angel, and they have come almost to believe that you habitually flew about the world in a nightgown, with an Easter lily in your hand—But I remember, dear. I know you’d scratch her eyes out. I know you’d do it now, if only you were able, because you loved this Peter Blagden.”

===Chinese===
我读到这里便不读了。「哦，斯特拉！他们都忘了。他们今天众口一词，硬说你是一位天使，而且几乎当真相信起：你惯常穿着睡袍、手擎一枝复活节百合，满世界飞来飞去——可我记得呢，亲爱的。我知道你会把她的眼睛抓出来。我知道你如今还会干这一手，只要你还有这本事，因为你爱过这个彼得·布拉格登。」

===Original===
Thereafter I must have wasted a full quarter of an hour in recalling all sorts of bygone unimportant happenings, and I was not bothering one way or the other about Avis …

===Chinese===
此后，足足一刻钟的工夫，想必都叫我荒废在追忆种种陈年的无关紧要的旧事上了；至于艾维斯，我心里已不大在意，好也罢歹也罢……

### III / 三

===Original===
In the moonlighted garden I found Bettie. But with her was Josiah Clarriker, Fairhaven’s leading businessman. He shook hands, and whatever delight he may have felt at seeing me was admirably controlled.

===Chinese===
在月光朗照的园子里，我找到了贝蒂。可陪着她的是乔赛亚·克拉里克，费尔黑文商界的头面人物。他同我握了握手，见到我时他心里无论翻起多大的欢喜，那份欢喜也被克制得令人钦佩。

===Original===
“Now don’t let me interfere with your eloquence,” I urged, “but go right on with the declamation.”

===Chinese===
「别让我搅了您的雄辩，」我怂恿道，「只管把这段朗诵继续下去。」

===Original===
“I make no pretension to eloquence, Mr. Townsend. I was merely recalling to Miss Hamlyn’s attention the beautiful lines of our immortal poet, Owen Meredith, which run, as I remember them:

===Chinese===
「我哪儿敢妄称雄辩，汤森先生。我不过是把我们不朽诗人欧文·梅瑞狄斯（Owen Meredith）的佳句，重新提请哈姆林小姐注意。依我的记忆，那几句是这样说的：

===Original===
“ ‘I thought of the dress she wore that time
That we stood under the cypress-tree together,
In that land, in that clime,
And I turned and looked, and she was sitting there
In the box next to the stage, and dressed
In that muslin dress, with that full soft hair
And that jessamine blossom at her breast.’ ”

===Chinese===
「『我想起那时她穿的那身衣裳
那时我们一同站在柏树底下，
在那片国土，在那方风土，
我回过头去望，她正坐在那里，
在紧挨着戏台的那个包厢里，穿着
那件细棉布衣裳，披着那一头丰软的头发，
胸前簪着那一朵素馨花。』」

===Original===
“But I am not permitted to wear flowers when Mr. Townsend is about,” said Bettie. “Did you know, Jo, that he is crazy about that too?”

===Chinese===
「可是汤森先生在跟前的时候，人家是不许我戴花的，」贝蒂说。「你可知道，乔，他在这一条上也是痴得可以？」

===Original===
“Well—! Anyhow, Meredith is full of very beautiful sentiments,” said Mr. Clarriker, “and I have always been particularly fond of that piece. It is called ‘Ox Italians.’ ”

===Chinese===
「这个——！不管怎么说，梅瑞狄斯满纸都是极美的情操，」克拉里克先生说，「而那一篇我一向格外喜爱。它的名字叫做《奥克斯·意大利人》（Ox Italians——原诗题实为法文 Aux Itaux，被这位店主人讹读成了『牛·意大利人』）。」

===Original===
“Yes, I have been previously affected by it,” said I, “and very deeply moved.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，我从前就叫它感动过，」我说，「而且感动得非常之深。」

===Original===
“And so—as I was about to observe, Miss Hamlyn—you will notice that the poet Meredith gowned one of the most beautiful characters he ever created in white, and laid great stress upon the fact that her beauty was immeasurably enhanced by the dainty simplicity of her muslin dress. This fabric, indeed, suits all types of faces and figures, and is Economical too, especially the present popular mercerised waistings and vestings that are fast invading the realm of silks. We show at our Emporium an immense quantity of these beautiful goods, in more than a hundred styles, elaborate enough for the most formal occasions, at fifty and seventy-five cents a yard; and—as I was about to observe, Miss Hamlyn—I would indeed esteem it a favour should you permit me to send up a few samples tomorrow, from which to make a selection at, I need not add, my personal expense.

===Chinese===
「而且所以——正如我方才要说的，哈姆林小姐——您当已注意到，诗人梅瑞狄斯给他笔下创造过的最美的女角之一，穿的就是一身白，并且着力强调：正是那件细棉布衣裳的雅洁朴素，把她的美貌烘托得无以复加。这种料子，实在宜于各式的脸庞与身段，而且还『经济』，尤其目下盛行的那种丝光棉女衫料、马甲料，正在大举进犯丝绸的疆土。我们商场现有此等美货大批，花色不下百余种，再隆重正式的场合也尽敷铺张，每码售价五角至七角五分；而且——正如我方才要说的，哈姆林小姐——您若肯容我明日送上几件样品，供您拣选，不必赘言，一应费用概由我私人奉垫，那我真要引为莫大的荣宠。

===Original===
“You see, Mr. Townsend,” he continued, more inclusively, “we have no florists in Fairhaven, and I have heard that candy—” He talked on, hygienically now. …

===Chinese===
「您瞧，汤森先生，」他接着说，这回把我也一并纳入了听众，「费尔黑文没有花店，而我听说糖果——」他一路讲了下去，这回讲的都是卫生之道了。……

### IV / 四

===Original===
“And that,” said I, when Mr. Clarriker had gone, “is what you are actually considering! I have always believed Dickens invented that man to go into one of the latter chapters of *Edwin Drood*. It is the solitary way of explaining certain people—that they were invented by some fagged novelist who unfortunately died before he finished the book they were to be locked up in. As it was, they got loose, to annoy you by their incredibility. No actual human being, you know, would suggest a white shirtwaist as a substitute for a box of candy.”

===Chinese===
「而这位，」等克拉里克先生走了之后，我说，「就是你当真在考虑的人！我一向相信，这个人本是狄更斯造出来，预备塞进《埃德温·德鲁德》（*Edwin Drood*，狄更斯未完成的小说）靠后某一章里去的。某些人，唯有这样才解释得通——他们本是某位才思枯竭的小说家笔下造出来的人物，不幸那小说家还没写完那本要把他们锁进去的书，就一命呜呼了。于是他们走脱出来，用他们那份叫人无法置信来烦你。你要知道，任何一个真实存在的血肉之人，都不会提出拿一件白色女衬衫去顶替一盒糖果。」

===Original===
“Oh, I have seen worse,” said Bettie, as in meditation. “It’s just Jo’s way of expressing the fact that I am stupendously beautiful in white. Poor dear, my loveliness went to his head, I suppose, and got tangled with next week’s advertisement for the *Gazette*. Anyhow, he is a deal more considerate than you. For instance, I was crazy to go to the show on Tuesday night, and Josiah Clarriker was the only person who thought to ask me, even though he is one of those little fireside companions who always get so syrupy whenever they take you anywhere that you simply can’t stand it. The combination both prevented my acceptance and accentuated his devotion; and quite frankly, Robin, I am thinking of him, for at bottom Jo is a dear.”

===Chinese===
「哦，更糟的我也见识过，」贝蒂若有所思地说。「这不过是乔的说法，用来表达这么个事实：我一穿白，便美得石破天惊。可怜的宝贝，我这份美貌大概冲昏了他的头脑，同下星期《公报》（*Gazette*）上的广告搅和在了一处。不管怎么说，他比你体贴得多了。比如，星期二晚上那场演出，我简直想看得要命，而乔赛亚·克拉里克是唯一想起来邀我的人——虽说他是那种炉边的小伴当，不管带你上哪儿，总腻甜得叫你没法消受。这两样凑在一处，既叫我没法应他的约，又抬升了他的痴心；而且坦白说，罗宾，我如今真有些把他放在心上了，因为归根到底，乔是个可人。」

===Original===
I laid one hand on each of Bettie’s shoulders; and it was in my mind at the time that this was the gesture of a comrade, and had not any sexual tinge at all. I wished that Bettie had better teeth, of course, but that could not be helped.

===Chinese===
我把两只手分别搭上贝蒂的双肩；当时我心里想的是，这是一个同志式的手势，不带一丝男女情爱的意味。我自然希望贝蒂的牙齿能长得好些，不过那是没法子的事。

===Original===
“You are to marry me as soon as may be possible,” said I, “and preferably tomorrow afternoon. Avis has thrown me over, God bless her, and I am free—until of course you take charge of me. There was a clever woman once who told me I was not fit to be the captain of my soul, though I would make an admirable lieutenant. She was right. It is understood you are to henpeck me to your heart’s content and to my ultimate salvation.”

===Chinese===
「你就要嫁给我了，一有可能便嫁，」我说，「顶好就在明天下午。艾维斯把我甩了，上帝保佑她，我自由了——当然，直到你把我接管过去为止。从前有位聪明的女人告诉我，我不配当自己灵魂的船长，却做得一名极出色的大副。她说得对。讲明白了：你只管对我行你的雌威，管到心满意足，也管到我最终的得救。」

===Original===
“I shall assuredly not marry you,” observed Miss Hamlyn, “until you have at least asked me to do so. And besides, how dared she throw you over—!”

===Chinese===
「我断断乎不会嫁给你，」哈姆林小姐评说道，「除非你至少先向我求过婚。再说，她怎么竟敢甩了你——！」

===Original===
“But I don’t intend to ask you, for I have not a single bribe to offer. I merely intend to marry you. I am a ne’er-do-well, a debauchee, a tippler, a compendium of all the vices you care to mention. I am not a bit in love with you, and as any woman will forewarn you, I am sure to make you a vile husband. Your solitary chance is to bully me into temperance and propriety and common sense, with precisely seven million probabilities against you, because I am a seasoned and accomplished liar. Can you do that bullying, Bettie—and keep it up, I mean?”

===Chinese===
「可我并不打算求你，因为我拿不出半点贿赂。我不过打算娶你。我是个不成器的东西，一个酒色之徒，一个贪杯之徒，一部你想列举什么恶德便有什么恶德的大全。我对你说不上有一丝一毫的爱，而且随便哪个女人都会预先警告你：我准会叫你守上一个顶恶劣的丈夫。你唯一的机会，就是把我欺压节制、改邪归正、逼出常识来——而赔率恰好是七百万比一，全不利于你，因为我是个老练而登峰造极的说谎家。这份欺压的差事，你干得了么，贝蒂——我是说，还能长年累月地干下去么？」

===Original===
And she was silent for a while. “Robin,” she said, at last, “you’ll never understand why women like you. You will always think it is because they admire you for some quality or another. It is really because they pity you. You are such a baby, riding for a fall—No, I don’t mean the boyishness you trade upon. I have known for a long while all that was just put on. And, oh, how hard you’ve tried to be a boy of late!”

===Chinese===
她沉默了片刻。「罗宾，」她终于说，「女人为什么喜欢你，你一辈子也明白不了。你总要以为，是因为她们钦佩你这一点那一点品性。其实是因为她们可怜你。你就是这样一个小娃娃，骑着马专等摔下来——不，我说的不是你拿来招摇撞骗的那套孩子气。那一套全是装出来的，我早就知道了。哦，这些日子你为装个男孩，下过多大的苦功啊！」

===Original===
“And I thought I had fooled you, Bettie! Well, I never could. I am sorry, though, if I have been annoyingly clumsy—”

===Chinese===
「我还当把你瞒过去了呢，贝蒂！唉，我从来就瞒不过你。不过，倘若我笨手笨脚得惹人厌烦，那我抱歉——」

===Original===
“But you were doing it for me,” she said. “You were doing it because you thought I’d like it. Oh, can’t you understand that I *know* you are worthless, and that you have never loved any human being in all your life except that flibbertigibbet Stella Blagden, and that I know, too, you have so rarely failed me! If you were an admirable person, or a person with commendable instincts, or an unselfish person, or if you were even in love with me, it wouldn’t count of course. It is because you are none of these things that it counts for so much to see you honest with me—sometimes—and even to see you scheming and playacting—and so transparently!—just to bring about a little pleasure for me. Oh, Robin, I am afraid that nowadays I love you *because* of your vices!”

===Chinese===
「可你那么做是为了我，」她说。「你那么做，是因为你以为我会喜欢。哦，你难道不明白么：*我早知道*你一钱不值，知道你这辈子不曾爱过任何一个活人，只除了斯特拉·布拉格登那个没头没脑的轻浮女人；我也知道，你是何等难得让我失望！倘若你是个可敬的人，或是个禀性可嘉的人，或是个无私的人，甚或你当真爱上了我，那这一切当然都算不得什么。恰恰因为你一样都不是，看见你待我坦诚——偶尔的坦诚——甚至看见你耍心眼、演戏——还演得那么透明！——只为给我张罗一点小小的欢喜，这才格外值钱。哦，罗宾，我怕如今我爱你，正是爱你的那些恶德！」

===Original===
“And I you because of your virtues,” said I; “so that there is no possible apprehension of either affection ever going into bankruptcy. Therefore the affair is settled; and we will be married in November.”

===Chinese===
「而我爱你，却是爱你的美德，」我说，「所以这两份感情，谁也无破产之虞。如此，事情便定了；我们十一月成婚。」

===Original===
“Well,” Bettie said, “I suppose that somebody has to break you of this habit of getting married next November—”

===Chinese===
「唉，」贝蒂说，「我想总得有个人出来，治一治你这个老把婚期搁在『下一个十一月』的习惯——」

===Original===
Then, and only then, my hands were lifted from her shoulders. And we began to talk composedly of more impersonal matters.

===Chinese===
直到这时，也直到这时，我的双手才从她肩上放了下来。于是我们开始心平气和地谈起些更不带个人色彩的事来。

### V / 五

===Original===
It was two days later that John Charteris came to Fairhaven; and I met him the same afternoon upon Cambridge street. The little man stopped short and in full view of the public achieved what, had he been a child, were most properly describable as making a face at me.

===Chinese===
两天之后，约翰·查特里斯来到了费尔黑文；当天下午，我就在剑桥街（Cambridge Street）上遇见了他。这小个子猛地站住，当着大庭广众，摆出了一副——他若是个孩子，最名正言顺的形容便是『冲我做鬼脸』——的表情。

===Original===
“That,” he explained, “expresses the involuntary confusion of Belial on re-encountering the anchorite who escaped his diabolical machinations. But, oh, dear me! haven’t you been translated yet? Why, I thought the carriage would have called long ago, just as it did for Elijah.”

===Chinese===
「这一副脸，」他解释道，「表达的是彼列（Belial，《圣经》中的魔君）再遇上那位从他的魔爪计谋底下逃脱的隐修士时，不由自主的慌乱。可是，哎呀呀！你还没有蒙召升天么？嗐，我还当接你的那辆车早该登门了，就像当年接以利亚（Elijah）的那样。」

===Original===
“Now, don’t be an ass, John. I *was* rather idiotic, I suppose—”

===Chinese===
「行了，别当蠢驴了，约翰。我想我先前——是够蠢的——」

===Original===
“Of course you were,” he said, as we shook hands. “It is your unfailing charm. You silly boy, I came from the pleasantest sort of house-party at Matocton because I heard you were here, and I have been foolish enough to miss you. Anne and the others don’t arrive until October. Oh, you adorable child, I have read the last book, and every one of the short stories as well, and I want to tell you that in their own peculiar line the two volumes are masterpieces. Anne wept and chuckled over them, and so did I, with an equal lack of restraint; only it was over the noble and self-sacrificing portions that Anne wept, and she laughed at the places where you were droll intentionally. Whereas I—!! Well, we will let the aposiopesis stand.”

===Chinese===
「你当然是蠢的，」我们握着手，他说道。「这正是你那从不含糊的魅力。傻孩子，我本在马托克顿（Matocton）赴一场再惬意不过的庄园聚会，听说你在这儿，便赶了来，而且痴得够可以，竟一直惦着没见着你。安妮和其他人要到十月才到。哦，你这招人疼的孩子，你那最后一部书我读了，那些短篇我也一篇不落地读了，我要告诉你：就它们各自那条独家的路数而论，这两卷都是杰作。安妮读得又哭又笑，我也一样，同样毫无节制；只不过，安妮哭是在那些高贵自我牺牲的段落，笑是在你故意逗趣的地方。至于我嘛——！！好了，咱们就让这半截话（aposiopesis，修辞学所谓话到中途戛然而止的『跳脱』格）原样站在这儿吧。」

===Original===
“Of course,” I sulkily observed, “if you have simply come to Fairhaven to make fun of me, I can only pity your limitations.”

===Chinese===
「当然喽，」我怏怏地说，「你若专程到费尔黑文来拿我打趣，那我只好可怜可怜你的见识短浅了。」

===Original===
He spoke in quite another voice. “You silly boy, it was not at all for that. I think you must know I have read what you have published thus far with something more than interest; but I wanted to tell you this in so many words. *Afield* is not perhaps an impeccable masterwork, if one may be thus brutally frank; but the woman—modeled after discretion will not inquire whom—is distinctly good. And what, with you only twenty-five, does *Afield* not promise! Child, you have found your métier. Now I shall look forward to the accomplishment of what I have always felt sure that you could do. I am very, very glad. More so than I can say. And I had thought you must know this without my saying it.”

===Chinese===
他换了一副全然不同的嗓音说话。「傻孩子，压根儿不是为了这个。我想你一定知道，迄今你发表的每一行字，我读时怀着的东西远不止是兴味；可有些话，我要一字一句地当面告诉你。《旷野》（*Afield*）——若容我这样毫不留情地直说——算不得一部无懈可击的杰作；可是书中那位女子——她的原型是谁，出于审慎我不打听——写得确实出色。何况你才二十五岁，《旷野》还有什么许诺不了的！孩子，你找到你的本行（métier）了。往后我便可以指望你做成那件我一向确信你能做成的事了。我非常、非常高兴。高兴得说都说不出来。我原以为，这话不必我说你也必定知道。」

===Original===
The man was sincere. And I was very much pleased, and remembered what invaluable help he could give me on my unfinished book, and what fun it would be to go over the manuscript with him. And, in fine, we became again, upon the spot as it were, the very best of friends.

===Chinese===
这人是一片真心。我也非常欢喜，想起他那无可估量的本事，正可帮我收拾那部没写完的书，想起同他逐字推敲手稿该是何等有趣。总而言之，我们可以说当场又成了最要好的朋友。

### VI / 六

===Original===
It was excellent to have Charteris to talk against. The little man had many tales to tell me of those dissolute gay people we had known and frolicked with; indeed, I think that he was trying to allure me back to the old circles, for he preoccupied his life by scheming to bring about by underhand methods some perfectly unimportant consummation, which very often a plain word would have secured at once. But now he swore he was not “making tea.”

===Chinese===
有查特里斯在旁边可供抬杠，实在是妙事一桩。这小个子有许多故事讲给我听，讲的是我们从前结识、一同厮混过的那班放浪快活人；说实在的，我看他是想诱我回到旧日那些圈子里去，因为他平生的要务，便是用迂回隐蔽的手段，去促成某件全然无关紧要的结局——而这种结局，往往一句开门见山的直话便手到擒来。不过这一回他赌咒发誓，说他并没有在『沏茶』。

===Original===
That had always been a byword between us, by the way, since I applied to him the phrase first used of Alexander Pope—“that he could not make tea without a conspiracy.” And it may be that in this case Charteris spoke the truth, and had come to Fairhaven just for the pleasure of seeing me, for certainly he must have had some reason for leaving the Musgraves’ house-party so abruptly.

===Chinese===
顺便说一句，『沏茶』向来是我们之间的一句口头禅，因为当年我把人家最初形容亚历山大·蒲柏（Alexander Pope）的那句话用在了他身上——说他「连沏一壶茶也非要密谋一场不可」。而这一回，查特里斯说的兴许倒是实话，他到费尔黑文来，就只为见我一面的乐趣；因为不客气地说，他离开马斯格罗夫家那场庄园聚会走得那样突然，总得有个缘故。

===Original===
“You are very well rid of the Hardresses,” he adjudged. “Did I tell you of the male one’s exhibition of jealousy last year! I can assure you that the fellow now entertains for me precisely the same affection I have always borne toward cold lamb. It is the real tragedy of my life that Anne is ethically incapable of letting a week pass without partaking of a leg of mutton. She is not particularly fond of it, and indeed I never encountered anybody who was; she has simply been reared with the notion that ‘people’ always have mutton once a week. What, have you never noticed that with ‘people,’ to eat mutton once a week is a sort of guarantee of respectability? I do not refer to chops of course, which are not wholly inconsistent with depravity. But the ability to eat mutton in its roasted form, by some odd law of nature, connotes the habit of paying your pew-rent regularly and of changing your flannels on the proper date. However, I was telling you about Jasper Hardress—” And Charteris repeated the story of their imbroglio in such a fashion that it sounded farcical.

===Chinese===
「你把哈德雷斯夫妇甩脱了，甩得大妙，」他断言道。「那家子男性那一位醋劲大发作，去年那一出，我同你讲过没有！我可以向你担保，那家伙如今待我的那份感情，同一我一向待冷羊肉的感情丝毫不差。我此生真正的悲剧在于：安妮在道义上做不到让一个星期过去而不吃一顿羊腿。她并不是特别爱吃羊腿，说实话，爱吃这东西的人我生平一个也没遇到过；她只不过是从小被人灌输了这么个观念：『人家』每星期总要吃一回羊肉。怎么，你从没留意过么——在『人家』那里，一星期吃一回羊肉，简直是体面的一种保票？我当然不是说羊排，羊排同堕落败德并非全然不能相容。可是有本事把羊肉以烘烤之形吃下去，依自然界某条古怪的法则，便意味着按时缴纳教堂座租、按节气如日更换法兰纶内衣的习惯。不过，我方才是要给你讲贾斯珀·哈德雷斯——」于是查特里斯把他们那场纠葛又讲了一遍，讲得像一出闹剧。

===Original===
“But, after all, John, you *did* make love to her.”

===Chinese===
「可是说到底，约翰，你*确实*是向她求过爱的。」

===Original===
“I have forgotten what was exactly the last observation of the lamented Julius Caesar,” Mr. Charteris leisurely observed—“though I remember that at the time it impressed me as being uncommonly appropriate—But to get back: do you not see that this clause ought to come here, at the end of the sentence? And, child, on all my ancient bended knees, I implore you to remember that ‘genuine’ does not mean the same thing as ‘real.’ …”

===Chinese===
「已故的尤利乌斯·恺撒临终那句名言的确切措辞，我忘了，」查特里斯先生慢条斯理地说——「不过我记得，当时它给我的印象，是贴切得无以复加——还是言归正传：您难道看不出，这个从句应该搁在这儿，搁在句子末尾么？还有，孩子，凭我这一双古老而屈下的老膝，我恳求您记住：『genuine』（真材实料）同『real』（确有其事）可不是一回事。……」

### VII / 七

===Original===
Meanwhile he and Bettie got on together a deal better than I had ever anticipated.

===Chinese===
这期间，他同贝蒂相处得极好，远出我意料之外。

===Original===
Charteris, though, received my confidence far too lightly. “You are going to marry her! Why, naturally! Ever since I encountered you, you have been ‘going to marry’ somebody or other. It is odd I should have written about the Foolish Prince so long before I knew you. But then, *I* helped to mould you—a little—”

===Chinese===
不过查特里斯听我吐露心事，态度未免太轻飘飘。「你要娶她啦！嗐，这还用说！打从我认识你那天起，你就一直在『就要娶』这个或那个。也真古怪，《愚蠢的王子》（The Foolish Prince）我竟在认识你之前那么久就写出来了。不过话说回来，*我*也帮你塑了塑型——塑了那么一点——」

===Original===
And resolutely Bettie said the most complimentary things about him. But I trapped her once.

===Chinese===
而贝蒂也斩钉截铁地说了他一大堆顶恭维的话。可有一回，我逮着了她的话把。

===Original===
“Still,” I observed, when he had gone, and she had finished telling me how delightful Mr. Charteris was, “still he shan’t ever come to *our* house, shall he?”

===Chinese===
「话说回来，」等他走了、她也讲完了查特里斯先生何等讨人喜欢之后，我说，「话说回来，他可永远不许进*咱们*家的门，对不对？」

===Original===
“Why, of course not!” said Bettie, who was meditating upon some cosmic question which required immediate attention. And then she grew very angry and said, “Oh, you *dog*!” and threw a sofa-cushion at me.

===Chinese===
「那是自然，当然不许！」贝蒂说——她正琢磨着某个亟待处理的宇宙大问题。随后她勃然大怒，说道：「哦，你这*狗东西*！」说着朝我摔过来一只沙发靠垫。

===Original===
“I hate that wizened man,” she presently volunteered, “more bitterly than I do any person on earth. For it was he who taught you to adopt infancy as a profession. He robbed me. And Setebos permitted it. And now you are just a man I am going to marry—Oh, well!” said Bettie, more sprightlily, “I was getting on, and you are rather a dear even in that capacity. Only I wonder what *becomes* of all the first choices?”

===Chinese===
「我恨那个干瘪老头，」她过了一会儿自顾说道，「恨得比世上任何人都狠。因为正是他，教会了你把一身孩儿态当作职业来操持。他把我抢了。而塞特博斯竟容许了这事。如今你不过是一个我要嫁的男人罢了——哦，得啦！」贝蒂说到这儿，口气轻快了些，「我这也眼看一年大似一年，就算充这个数，你也还算个可人。只是我纳闷，那些头一批的人选，后来都打发到哪儿去了呢？」

===Original===
“They must keep them for us somewhere, Bettie dear. And that is probably the explanation of everything.”

===Chinese===
「他们总归是替我们把那些收存在什么地方呢，亲爱的贝蒂。而这，大概就是万物的解释了。」

===Original===
And a hand had snuggled into mine. “You do understand without having to have it all spelt out for you. And that’s a comfort, too. But, oh,” said Bettie, “what a wasteful Setebos it is!”

===Chinese===
而一只手已悄悄偎进了我的掌心。「你果然不用人家把话一个字母一个字母拼给你听，就全明白了。这也叫人安慰。可是，嗐，」贝蒂说，「塞特博斯这个家伙，何等挥霍的败家子啊！」

## XXIX / 二十九

He Allows the Merits of Imperfection / 他承认不完美自有其可贵之处

### I / 一

===Original===
I was quite contented now and assured as to the future. I foreknew the future would be tranquil and lacking in any particular excitement, and I had already ceded, in anticipation, the last tittle of mastery over my own actions; but Bettie would keep me to the mark, would wring—not painlessly perhaps—from Robert Townsend the very best there was in him; and it would be this best which, unalloyed, would endure, in what I wrote. I had never imagined that, for the ore, smelting was an agreeable process; so I shrugged, and faced my future contentedly.
===Chinese===
如今我心满意足，对未来也已笃定。我预知这未来必是一片安宁，谈不上半点特别的刺激，而且我已预先让渡了支配自己行动的最末一丝权柄；可是贝蒂会看住我、不让我离了准头，会从罗伯特·汤森身上——那滋味或许并不好受——榨出他身上顶好的东西来；而正是这顶好的东西，将不掺一丝杂质地，留存在我写下的文字里。矿石之须冶炼，这过程从来算不得一桩惬意事——这一点我从不曾存过妄想；于是我耸一耸肩，心满意足地迎向我的未来。

===Original===
One day I said, “Tomorrow I must have holiday. There are certain things that need burying, Bettie dear, and—it is just the funeral of my youth I want to go to.”
===Chinese===
有一天我说：「明天我必须歇一天工。有些东西该入土埋葬了，亲爱的贝蒂，还有——我想要去参加的，正是我青春的葬礼。」

===Original===
“So it is tomorrow that we go for an admiring walk around our emotions!” Bettie said. She knew well enough of what event tomorrow was the anniversary, and it is to her credit she added: “Well, for this once—!” For of all the women whom I had loved, there was but one that Bettie Hamlyn had ever bothered about. And tomorrow was Stella’s birthday, as I had very unconcernedly mentioned a few moments earlier, when I was looking for the Austin Dobson book, and had my back turned to Bettie.
===Chinese===
「这么说，明天我们要绕着咱们的感情，作一番游赏的散步了！」贝蒂说。明天是哪一桩事的周年，她知道得再清楚不过；而她还肯添上一句：「好吧，就只这一回——！」这实在是她的好处。因为在我爱过的所有女人当中，贝蒂·哈姆林真正放在心上的，前后只有一位。明天是斯特拉的生日——这是片刻之前、我翻找那本奥斯汀·多布森（Austin Dobson）的书、背朝着贝蒂的时候，用最不经意的口气顺嘴提过的。
